# Decisions

One line per judgment call, newest last.

- Phase 0: The spec arrived as RTF at `/SPEC.md`. It is converted to Markdown at `docs/SPEC.md` with the text unchanged.
- Phase 0: Added `@types/three` (dev, types only). `three` ships no TypeScript types and strict TS needs them.
- Phase 0: "Every effect responds to the slider": each effect has a `[calm, mad]` ramp. §2 defines warp, displacement and lens; pixel crush, snap grid, affine exaggeration, fog distance, isolation strength and dither spread got small ramps.
- Phase 0: The FX intensity cap clamps stress (`min(1 − sanity/100, cap)`) for every sanity effect. It is a debug slider until the Phase 6 settings menu.
- Phase 0: The post pass renders into a 400×225 canvas that CSS upscales nearest-neighbour (`image-rendering: pixelated`), letterboxed to 16:9.
- Phase 0: Colours are display sRGB end to end (`ColorManagement` off, custom `ShaderMaterial`s only), so palette hex values are exact on screen.
- Phase 0: The quantisation palette has 58 colours: a 24-step bone/sepia ramp, 8 sea-grey, 8 rust, and 6 shades per anomaly colour. The post shader picks the nearest colour.
- Phase 0: Chromatic split samples are colour-isolated per channel before recombining, so low sanity leaks colour fringes into the world.
- Phase 0: `anomalyProximity` = camera distance to the test anomaly (1 at 4 m, 0 at 30 m). Phase 3's FX controller takes this over.
- Phase 0: Vertex displacement is zero within 7 m of the camera and full at 32 m, keeping combat range readable.
- Phase 0: Render and FX numbers also live in `src/data/tuning.ts`, so every tunable is in one place.
- Phase 0: Test-scene camera is a debug orbit (auto-orbit with breathing radius, drag/wheel, O). Phase 1's third-person camera replaces it.
- Phase 1: The training dummy and the Deep One are placeholder primitive figures with a simple chase/attack brain (`placeholders.ts`, `brain.ts`). Phase 2's registry, sprites and archetypes replace them. The player is a tweened-primitive investigator.
- Phase 1: Moves are data (`moves.ts`): 60 Hz frame windows for hits, i-frames, parry, cancel and interrupt. Each combatant owns a moveset, and poses are derived from those fields.
- Phase 1: Controls follow Elden Ring on PC: LMB light, Shift+LMB heavy, RMB block, Shift+RMB parry, Space dodge, F revolver, Q lock-on, ←/→ or a mouse flick to switch. The pad uses the Souls layout.
- Phase 1: Dodge button, Dark Souls style: released within 14 frames it rolls toward the stick (or backsteps with no direction), held longer it sprints. The dodge fires on release.
- Phase 1: Parry is its own action (8-frame window, frontal melee only). The revolver interrupts inside an attack's `interrupt` wind-up. Both leave the foe `parried`, and the next hit is a ×2.5 riposte.
- Phase 1: "Hitting zero causes a guard break" applies to blocked hits that empty stamina. Spending on an action only needs stamina above zero (Dark Souls rule). Enemies have no stamina.
- Phase 1: Lock-on score = distance + 12 m/rad × |horizontal angle from the camera forward|. A held lock breaks beyond 27 m (2 m hysteresis) or after 0.5 s out of sight.
- Phase 1: Hitstop freezes only the attacker and the victim (per entity), 2–4 frames from move data. The event bus dispatches synchronously.
- Phase 1: Only one Echo drop exists; dying again before touching it loses it (Souls rule). Drops glow Void Green: Echoes are eldritch, and colour means wrong.
- Phase 1: The training dummy is immortal (hp floors at 1 and refills after 3 s without damage). The Deep One's kill pays 150 Echoes.
- Phase 1: The camera boom hangs 0.45 m over the right shoulder and tilts down on close lock targets, so the target isn't hidden behind the player.
- Phase 1: The Elder Sign is only the respawn point for now; resting, discovery and fast travel are Phase 4.
- Phase 1: The arena is the default page; the Phase 0 look test moved to `?look`, and `?debug` exposes `window.game` for scripted checks.
- Phase 2: Added two entities §4 misses that are clearly Lovecraft's: the Gnoph-keh (Polaris) and Zkauba the Wizard of Yaddith (Through the Gates of the Silver Key, with E. Hoffmann Price). 100 entities in all.
- Phase 2: One file per tier stays under 300 lines by keeping each entry to ~6 lines with the helpers in `entities/kit.ts`.
- Phase 2: An entity lists its attacks in `behavior.attacks` (§3C names only archetype + params); boss scripts carry their own weighted attacks.
- Phase 2: The attack library compiles into Phase 1 MoveDefs scaled by `stats.damage` and body size. Ranged attacks are hitscan until Phase 5 projectiles; teleport, summon, gaze, darkness and sanity damage are data until Phases 3/5.
- Phase 2: Archetypes are parameter sets for one shared perception-driven FSM (hidden, idle, engage, return, follow), not separate code. Hidden ambushers and burrowers can't be hit or locked; flight is drawn (hover), not simulated.
- Phase 2: Bosses fight with their script's first phase; phase changes, summons, arena changes and reality hooks run in Phase 5.
- Phase 2: Elder Shoggoth and Polyp Swarm are `bossVariant` overrides; Nyarlathotep's true form is its `eldritchVariant`. A region boss must have a script directly or through its boss variant.
- Phase 2: Sprites are 16 body plans drawn by a pure software rasteriser into 64×64 cells (idle 2, move 2, attack 3, hurt 2 frames), one 2048-wide atlas of 103 sprites built in ~0.2 s. One view per sprite, mirrored by facing; no 8-way rotations. Atlas alpha marks glow pixels.
- Phase 2: Colossi are assemblies: Cthulhu, Dagon, Hydra, Ghatanothoa, the Dunwich Horror, the Colossus Beneath the Pyramids, Azathoth, Yog-Sothoth, Shub-Niggurath. Their capsule is as wide as their body.
- Phase 2: Region fits where §3D has no home: Egyptian and Arabian stories (Under the Pyramids, The Nameless City) go to Pnakotus; Exham Priory, Cool Air, The Alchemist and The Outsider to the hub; Venus (In the Walls of Eryx) to Yuggoth.
- Phase 2: Phase 1's placeholder Deep One stays as the default arena's sparring partner (its tests pin its frame data). A `?spawn` creature replaces it, or fights beside it when it is an ally.
- Phase 2: Insight on first sight is 0 for lesser and greater (their first sight costs sanity instead) and at least 1 for named and above; the validator enforces it.
- Phase 2: The Being from Beyond carries `hidden.maxSanity: 15` (Unmoored); the Phase 3 HiddenLayer hook will hide it, so for now it is visible.
- Phase 2: The ambient Blind Albino Penguin is a skirmisher with `flee: 1` and no attacks: it waddles away.
- Phase 2 review: The arena has no moon. Its only light is the investigator's lantern (warm, 6 m, inverse-square decay cut off steeply from 4.5 m, carried at the hip 0.3 m ahead) plus a faint cold ambient. The `?look` test keeps its moonlight.
- Phase 2 review: Characters (figures, colossi, sprites) take a fixed 0.65 share of the lantern without N·L, so their value blocks hold as they turn and stay darker than the lit floor. The placeholder Deep One's hide is darkened for the same reason.
- Phase 2 review: The rim light is a post effect. Characters write fog/2 into the scene target's alpha (only when a pipeline turns the marks on; the bestiary's direct canvas stays opaque). The post pass lightens a character pixel when its left, right or upper neighbour is backdrop that isn't clearly brighter.
- Phase 2 review: The grade is split toning by luma: cold grey-green (#4e5b56) at or below 0.03, warm bone (#d6c4a0) from 0.2. It replaces the single bone tint, and the palette's 24-step ramp follows the same curve. The fog colour is a near-black grey-green.
- Phase 2 review: The floor uses a new `slab` texture (1 m slabs in staggered rows, grout at ×0.55, hairline cracks on half the slabs), tiled every 2 m for 32 px/m like the pillars. The floor, walls and Elder Sign are tessellated to 0.5 m so the vertex-lit pool stays round.
- Phase 2 review: World textures use NearestMipmapNearest with mipmaps. The sprite atlas stays Nearest, since mips would bleed between cells.
- Phase 2 review: Figures use a near-flat `cloth` texture (4-texel folds, 8-texel patches); their value blocks come from vertex colours. The investigator: dark coat, hat and trousers, pale face and hands, a lighter belt, and a self-lit lantern at the belt.
- Phase 2 review 2: The arena night has a very dim, cold, low moon. Being low, it finds walls and pillars more than the floor, so architecture beyond the lantern reads as faint shapes (bricks within ~25 m) and the lantern pool stays the brightest area.
- Phase 2 review 2: The lantern hangs at the player's left hip, so the pool is brightest on that side. Its 4.5–6 m edge band wanders ±12% with the angle around the lantern and is ordered-dithered per pixel (Bayer 4×4), so the pool is no longer a smooth oval.
- Phase 2 review 2: The rim tells the player from creatures and also fires where the two meet; creatures get a stronger rim (0.6 against 0.4). It fades out 9–15 m from the camera, so distant creatures read as dim moonlit silhouettes with self-lit eye glints (a new muted sprite pixel kind) instead of outlines.
- Phase 2 review 2: Enemies use mid or pale values: the placeholder Deep One's hide is now a pale grey-green (this replaces its darkened hide), and its eyes are self-lit. The investigator's sleeves are one step lighter than the coat, so attack and block poses read.
- Phase 3: Bands fall at their floors (70/40/15) and climb back only 3 points past them (73/43/18). A jump across several bands is one `SanityBandChanged`.
- Phase 3: Auras need no line of sight. Each is whole within 3 m of the creature's body and fades to nothing at 12 m; every present creature's aura adds up (one lying in ambush counts, a hidden-layer one does not).
- Phase 3: A landed blow (damage > 0) costs the attacker's `sanityDamage`. Roar and gaze take `sanity × sanityDamage` over their active frames within their range bracket; a gaze needs line of sight, a roar carries through walls. Blocks and i-frames stop blows, not roars.
- Phase 3: First sight means within 30 m, inside 50° of the camera's forward and in line of sight of the player's eye, once per roster id. It costs 6/10/18/25 sanity for greater/named/great old one/outer god and grants `insightOnSight`. Hallucinations never count.
- Phase 3: Damage multipliers by band (Lucid → Unmoored), applied in `strike`: dealt ×1, 1.1, 1.2, 1.35; taken ×1, 1.1, 1.25, 1.5.
- Phase 3: "Restored at checkpoints" = respawning at the Elder Sign refills sanity and Laudanum; resting comes with Phase 4's checkpoints. Laudanum is the consumable: 3 doses of +30 sanity on R / pad Y, spent as the vial comes up (like a Souls flask).
- Phase 3: Insight upgrades: Vigour (+20 hp) and Endurance (+15 stamina) at 1 insight a level, Resolve (every sanity loss −15% per level, 4 levels) at 2. The debug panel buys them until Phase 4's Elder Sign.
- Phase 3: The arena has one tome, the Pnakotic Manuscripts (+1 insight), read by touch like an Echo drop.
- Phase 3: `hidden.maxSanity` must be a band floor (the validator checks). A layer shows once its band lies wholly at or below it, so it keeps the band hysteresis and changes only on `SanityBandChanged` / `InsightChanged`.
- Phase 3: Hidden things are absent (`isAbsent`): they neither act, collide, draw nor get targeted, and have no aura or first sight. Hidden-layer geometry is data (boxes); its colliders leave the world while hidden (`CollisionWorld.off`). The arena has a door that needs 1 insight and "Wrong Angles", standing and floating stones shown at Fractured. Bridges wait for Phase 4's dungeon kit.
- Phase 3: VariantSwap rebuilds the whole combatant from the variant (look, name, body, moves, brain, dread) and keeps the health and poise fractions. A requested `?variant` never swaps. The placeholder Deep One (a figure) never swaps, but weighs on the mind like the roster Deep One.
- Phase 3: Hallucinations are lesser sprites with pack_hunter, brute or skirmisher brains: after a 2 s onset one every 4–8 s, at most 3, 6–9 m from the player within 60° of straight behind the camera, fading after 30 s. Their blows deal no damage or poise and cost 5 sanity; any hit makes one vanish (no Echoes). Allies cannot see them, and they hunt only the player.
- Phase 3: The FX controller feeds the game's sanity to the FX (the debug sanity slider now sets the game's sanity), takes anomalyProximity from the nearest glowing creature, Echo drop or shown hidden stone, and adds a 0.8 s pulse of ripple and chroma, within the FX cap, when the band worsens.
- Phase 3: Its audio half is one WebAudio chain (lowpass → waveshaper → gain) carrying a low 55 Hz sawtooth drone, silent while lucid; gain, detune (to −70 cents), wobble and distortion ramp with stress. It starts on the first click or key press; Phase 6 routes its sounds through it.
- Phase 3: HUD: the sanity bar has ticks at the band floors and turns Cosmic Purple at Fractured and Eldritch Magenta at Unmoored; below it the band, sanity and Laudanum doses; insight sits above Echoes. Notices for band changes, costly first sights, tomes and upgrades.
- Phase 4: The world map is a grid of 256 m region tiles (4 × 4 chunks of 64 m). The waking world is one landmass around the hub; every realm beyond (the Mountains of Madness, Pnakotus, K'n-yan, the Dreamlands, Mu & R'lyeh, Yuggoth, Beyond the Gate) is an island of its own. Off the land lies dark sea or, for the realms, the abyss; both push the investigator back at the edge.
- Phase 4: Realms are reached through paired gates standing at sites (the Miskatonic expeditions to Antarctica and Australia, Akeley's brain cylinder, the Mound, the Alert, the Ultimate Gate). The Dreamlands open only by resting at the hub's Sleeper's Sign, which leads down the sealed Stairs of Slumber.
- Phase 4: Terrain is each region's seeded fBm heightfield, blended across a 32 m band at region borders and sinking to the sea floor over 24 m beyond the coast. Sites flatten it under circular pads, and dungeon footprints under rectangles.
- Phase 4: Legacy dungeons are room graphs (corridor, hall, stair, pit, bridge, well) laid out on 16 m cells, with walls, floors, steps, decks and chasms as kit parts that are both colliders and meshes. Hidden rooms and bridges are Phase 3 hidden-layer pieces with a seal (the wall a door opens, the chasm edge a bridge spans).
- Phase 4: Every other roster entity sits in its best-fitting region's spawn table or waits at a lair (optional bosses) or an ally location. `validateWorld` checks the layout, pairing, walkable spots, region reachability and that every roster id is placed.
- Phase 4: Population keeps at most 60 creatures awake, nearest chunks first; creatures beyond the 7 × 7 are let go. Killed foes return on resting or death; bosses and lairs stay slain for good (`Vanquished`).
- Phase 4: An Elder Sign is found within 6 m. E within 3.2 m rests there: health, sanity and Laudanum refill, it becomes the respawn point and foes return. Resting is refused while a foe hunts the investigator within 18 m. Resting opens the sign's menu, which spends insight on upgrades (replacing Phase 3's debug buttons in play), fast-travels to any sign found, and descends to the Dreamlands at the Sleeper's Sign.
- Phase 4: The menu is mouse-driven with E/Esc to leave; pad navigation waits for Phase 6's menus. The investigator takes no input while it is open.
- Phase 4: Chunk meshes (terrain at 1 m vertices, merged props) and site meshes are built by generator jobs under the 2 ms slicer. After a long jump (a new game, a load, fast travel, a gate, a respawn) the 3 × 3 chunks under the investigator are finished at once, like a loading screen, so they never stand over nothing.
- Phase 4: The ground uses the arena's texel density (2 m per texture repeat, 32 px/m) and the figures' ×2 tint gain, so the lantern pool reads as it does in the arena. The investigator rises 4.6 m in front of a sign, so the camera behind them clears the slab.
- Phase 4: Save/load is one localStorage JSON (version 1; every field is checked on parse; a bad save is ignored). It is written every 20 s while alive, after resting, travel and respawn, and when the page hides. Killed-since-rest foes are not saved, so a reload brings them back, as a rest would.
- Phase 4: The open world is now the default page; `?arena` (or `?spawn`) is the Phase 1–3 combat arena, and `?fresh` forgets the save.
- Phase 5a: Boss fights (`systems/bossFight.ts`): any creature with a boss script, whatever its archetype, holds a fight over its arena (an arena site's ring or its dungeon room; the whole arena for `?spawn`). It begins when the boss engages. Phases start as health falls below `hpBelow`, never going back even if the boss heals; each swaps in its weighted attacks, refills poise, applies its arena change once, and raises its summons unless the phase calls them with its own summon attack. The investigator's death or the boss giving up resets the fight; the boss's death ends it; summons, decoys and props go either way.
- Phase 5a: The attack library works as parameterised data. Projectile, fan and spit are real bolts: spit lobs and leaves a pool, a roll's i-frames let a bolt through, a guard blocks one, nothing parries one. The beam stays hitscan. Pool spreads under its target. Wind shoves 3.5 m even through a guard. Grabs can't be blocked or parried. Teleport lands 3.5–8 m from the target inside the arena. Summon calls from the phase's summons (non-bosses from `behavior.summons`), at most 3 alive, worth no Echoes. Gaze fills a buildup meter: full, −12 sanity and a stagger. Darkness darkens the arena for 7 s.
- Phase 5a: Pool and void ticks hurt but are not blows: they take no per-hit sanity.
- Phase 5a: All ten reality hooks come with 5a, as the library they share. petrify_buildup kills when full, like a Souls curse. hidden_platforms makes the floor a void (8 hp and 1 sanity every 0.5 s) except on platforms shown to insight ≥ 1, so spending insight hides them. time_skip puts the boss within reach mid-wind-up, with a flash. control_swap walks the body to the nearest foe, swinging, for 3 s every 10–14 s. Decoys are copies of the boss under the hallucination rules.
- Phase 5a: "Great Race: control_swap" is the control_swap hook itself. It seizes the body in Ephraim Waite's fight and in the Polyp Swarm's second phase, since the Archives are the Great Race's.
- Phase 5a: Boss numbers live in `data/bossTuning.ts`, re-exported by `tuning.ts`, which is at its 300-line limit.
- Phase 5a: The Colour heals 30 hp/s while the world has colour, draining it over 75 s of healing; the post pass greys the world and dims anomaly hues as it drains. Its sprite's `outside` flag marks its pixels (alpha ~0.31) for the post pass to leave ungraded and unquantised, a crawling iridescence no palette holds.
- Phase 5a: The Dunwich Horror's script is `unseen`: it is not drawn, locked on to or beheld until the Powder of Ibn Ghazi reveals it (3 doses, E within 14 m, 15 s each). Below 12% health, blows can't take its last hit point (a health `floor`, not the dummy's self-healing `immortal`). Only the incantation (E, a 3 s chant a stagger breaks) destroys it, within 40 m.
- Phase 5a: The Haunter's arena lights four lamps. Within a lit lamp's 6 m it burns (12 hp/s) and takes ×1.5 damage; in the dark it takes ×0.1. Its darkness attack snuffs the nearest lamp, E relights one, and with every lamp out the arena goes dark.
- Phase 5a: Keziah Mason summons rats (Brown Jenkin already fights beside her); Curwen, Orne and Hutchinson raise reanimated corpses; the Dagon Priest calls Deep Ones.
- Phase 5a: The Elder Shoggoth and Polyp Swarm (boss variants of greater entries) are scripted with the named batch.
- Phase 5b: The great old ones fight by two- or three-phase scripts composed from the library. The sea colossi (Dagon, Hydra, Bokrug, Cthulhu) flood their arenas. N'kai and the Mound go dark (Tsathoggua, Nug, Yeb). The Great Ones' Kadath floor becomes a void with hidden platforms. Hastur's last phase skips time.
- Phase 5b: Cthulhu has a health floor of 1 from the moment its fight begins. Below 33% the Alert comes to the arena's edge. E within 5 m takes the helm and she sails at 14 m/s straight at it, the investigator on deck (a `helm` move with i-frames). The ram bursts it (a stagger, down to 1 hp). Over 2.5 s it reforms, then R'lyeh sinks: Cthulhu plays its death throes as a sinking and counts as vanquished, its Echoes paid. `Title` events mark each beat.
- Phase 5b: Hastur's script is `called`: its arena stays empty until it has been called. Each time the sanity band worsens in Yuggoth, its name flickers across the screen, a little longer each time. The third time calls it: it rises 12 m ahead of the investigator, and from then on its arena holds it. The count and the calling are saved (two optional save fields; still version 1).
- Phase 5b: Ghatanothoa's arena raises five 7 m monoliths at half its radius; they block feet and sight, and its petrifying gaze sees from its eye height. They come down with the fight.
- Phase 5c: The outer gods fight by phase scripts; the Daemon Pipers, the Other Gods and the Ancient Ones (optional lairs) get two phases each, like the named lairs.
- Phase 5c: Shub-Niggurath's arena raises four spawning roots: fixed bodies with 700 hp that can be locked on to and struck, and never strike back. Every 6 s each standing root bears a Thousand Young, with at most 6 alive. While any root stands, Shub-Niggurath takes only a fifth of each blow.
- Phase 5c: Yog-Sothoth's arena floats five spheres. Touching one carries the investigator through to the next, and every 9–13 s the arena leaps, throwing them through one at random. Its teleport moves the god itself among them.
- Phase 5c: Nyarlathotep's avatars are the Black Man and the Haunter of the Dark (a notice marks their fall). In the open world, each time a region boss falls, its black pharaoh stands watching for 4 s: a phantom seen by the investigator alone. Below 30% it takes its true form for good (no swapping back) and borrows up to six of the most-used attacks of the bosses slain before it (its avatars' in the arena), compiled with its own stats. Nodens, once beheld, answers E once per fight as an ally.
- Phase 5c: Azathoth takes no damage. Its health bar is the song: it drains over 90 s of piping, which also drives its phases. It hears the investigator's noise: 45 m for a shot, 28 sprinting, 20 dodging, 16 striking, 7 walking, and nothing standing still or creeping behind a guard. 0.75 s after it hears, the court erupts at the spot (a short, deadly pool). When the song ends it slumbers: its death throes, its Echoes, and a title.
- Phase 5c: The three endings are data (`data/endings.ts`), chosen through `systems/endings.ts`. 'Umr at-Tawil yields below 25%: it stops striking, and E beside it passes through the Gate with it. Once Azathoth slumbers, resting at the Court's Elder Sign offers the other two: wake and seal the Gate, or kneel as Nyarlathotep's herald. The ending card closes the dream; the investigator may walk on or begin anew, and the ending is saved.
- Phase 5c: A boss fight's first step runs its signature too, so a boss's shield or invulnerability holds from the moment it engages.
- Phase 6: Sounds are data, like creatures: `data/sounds.ts` holds the stingers and `data/voices.ts` the creature voices and drones. A recipe is layers, each an oscillator or generated noise with a pitch glide, an attack-and-fade envelope, an optional filter sweep and vibrato. `render/audio/synth.ts` plays them; there are no samples.
- Phase 6: An entry names its `voice` (validated) or speaks with its tier's: lesser growl, greater bellow, named murmur, great old one abyss, outer god choir, allies silent. 21 entries have a voice from the text: polyps whistle, the shoggoth pipes "Tekeli-li", Elder Things pipe, Mi-Go buzz, Deep Ones and hybrids croak, ghouls (and Pickman) meep, ghasts cough, zoogs flutter, rats scurry, cats meow, Zann's window plays a viol, the Daemon Pipers and Azathoth pipe and drum, the Voice in the Tomb and Hastur whisper, the Hound bays, penguins squawk. Night-gaunts are silent.
- Phase 6: A creature calls at random within its voice's interval while it is in the world and not lying hidden. It also calls at once when it turns on the investigator, at most every 2.5 s. Ambushers stay silent, but invisible stalkers and the unseen Dunwich Horror can be heard. Loudness falls off squared from 3 m to the voice's range; the pan follows the bearing from the camera.
- Phase 6: Stingers answer events. Blows sound by outcome where they land; shots, summons and lamps also sound in place. The mind's events play inside the head: band changes, first sights (pitched lower for greater horrors), insight. Signs, travel, boss beats, every reality hook and signature beat, titles and endings get one too. Pool and void ticks are silent. Past 24 one-shots at once, new ones are dropped.
- Phase 6: Drones: one bed per region (plus the arena and the title), crossfading over 4 s. A boss fight swells a darker bed beneath it, and Phase 3's sanity drone stays. Everything passes the sanity waveshaper, then a compressor, then the volume; drones and new one-shots take the FX detune and wobble.
- Phase 6: Settings are the spec's three plus volume: FX intensity cap, sensitivity (scales mouse, stick and arrow look), resolution scale (0.5–2 × the 400×225 target; unused while pixelation is toggled off) and volume. They live under their own localStorage key and are clamped and snapped on load.
- Phase 6: The default page opens on a title screen: Continue (when a save exists), New game (asks before forgetting a save), Settings, Controls. `?fresh`, `?arena` and `?spawn` skip it. It shows `document.title` ("[WORKING TITLE]") rather than an invented name, under a line from Supernatural Horror in Literature.
- Phase 6: Esc, pad Start, or losing the mouse capture or window focus pauses when no other menu is open. The world stands still: no sim steps, the last step drawn, creature voices hushed; drones play on. Resume recaptures the mouse. Quit to title reloads without parameters; autosave runs on pagehide.
- Phase 6: One menu kit serves the title, pause, Elder Sign and ending screens: arrows/Enter/Esc or pad d-pad/stick/A/B, with sliders moved by left/right. An open menu swallows key presses, so the game never hears them. The Elder Sign menu gets the pad navigation Phase 4 deferred. A back key in a screen's first 250 ms is ignored, since it is the key that opened it.
- Phase 6: The debug panel shows only with `?debug` or in the arena, and `?debug` also exposes the audio engine as `window.audio`. The play hints moved to the Controls page.
- Phase 6: Performance pass, measured in headless Chromium by touring every Elder Sign. The sim costs ~0.3 ms a step, and ≤60 AI already held. The draw-call hog was the colossi at 40–86 draws each (Azathoth's Court: 106), now rigid-batched per material on the CPU (`rigidBatch.ts`, ≤3 draws). The busiest sign is now 44 draws, and frame script time fell from 3–6 ms to 1–2.5 ms. The HUD writes the DOM only on change; creature views allocate nothing per frame. `tests/budgets.test.ts` pins colossus draws, the AI cap at every sign, and fog/far plane inside the streamed chunks.
- Playtest round 1 (feedback): The characters' rim light is gone; it broke immersion. The post pass keeps only the Colour's out-of-palette mark.
- Playtest round 1: The lantern is lit per pixel with inverse-square decay windowed smoothly to nothing at 11 m (no hard edge, no dithered band).
- Playtest round 1: Self-lit eyes and markings sink into the dark between 6 and 13 m, so a creature is not spotted from afar by its eyes.
- Playtest round 1: World textures are 128 texels (twice the old repeat at the same density) with new ground and building kinds (grass, mud, sand, snow, cobble, brick, clapboard, shingle). World materials vary tone in world space, and organic ground blends in a turned second sample by a noise mask, so no ground visibly repeats. Regions got fitting grounds (grass for New England, mud for Innsmouth, snow, sand).
- Playtest round 1: Each blow of the chain has its own swing (render/swings.ts): slash, rising backhand, lunging thrust, two-handed overhead chop, full spin. The dodge roll is a dive, a tucked somersault and a crouch.
- Playtest round 1: The healing item is West's Reagent (Herbert West's luminous solution, injected): 4 doses, 45% of full health each, refilled on resting and rising; Silver Vials add doses (up to 9). R heals and T drinks Laudanum; on a pad, Y and d-pad down.
- Playtest round 1: A blow taken no longer flashes the investigator: the screen's edge darkens toward red on the side the blow came from, the camera jolts, and the health bar's lost part drains away after a moment.
- Playtest round 1: Ordinary foes show a name and health bar over their heads while hunting the investigator, wounded or locked on (within 22 m, in sight); bosses keep the bottom bar. The old bottom bar for any lock target is gone.
- Playtest round 1: The title screen plays the supplied track "Subterranean Pulse" (public/music, the only audio file: the user asked for it), looped at the volume setting and faded out when play begins. It replaces the title drone.
- Playtest round 1: Particles (render/particles.ts): one instanced batch of dithered discs for blood, ichor, sparks, dust, smoke and glows.
- Playtest round 1 (world): Region tiles are 512 m (8 × 8 chunks) and every site's coordinates doubled, so places, bosses and lairs lie twice as far apart. Saves are version 2; a version 1 save (the old, smaller world) is ignored.
- Playtest round 1 (world): Each region has a seeded plan (world/regionPlan.ts). Roads (a minimum spanning tree of wandering polylines, textured cobble, mud, sand, slab or snow) join its Elder Signs, legacy dungeons, gates, towns and the middles of shared borders, so they run on across regions. Lesser dungeons, lairs and boss arenas are not road stops: they are found by leaving the roads.
- Playtest round 1 (world): Towns (data/regionFeatures.ts) line radiating streets with houses facing them (clapboard with gambrel roofs, brick, hill hovels, stone), street lamps and lit windows; decayed towns (Innsmouth, K'n-yan) have ruins among them. New England field walls follow country roads.
- Playtest round 1 (world): Open ground holds ordered features instead of random scatter: groves (dead hardwoods or pines, with undergrowth), walled graveyards with a gate and graves in rows, standing-stone circles about an altar, ruined houses, rock outcrops and camps around a fire pit. The biome's own scatter is a third of what it was.
- Playtest round 1 (world): Foes are posted, not strewn: around every camp, often in ruins, groves, circles and graveyards, guarding every dungeon door and the approach to every boss's ring, in pairs along the roads every ~95 m, wandering town streets, plus a thin scatter. None within 30 m of an Elder Sign or gate. About 60-90 per region (220 in the Dreamlands), so the 60-awake budget now does real work.
- Playtest round 1 (world): Thirteen lesser dungeons (data/lairs.ts) hide optional bosses from the stories at their far end behind two or three rooms of foes: Muñoz's rooms, the castle's cellars and crypt, the old churchyard, the deserted house, the swamp tomb, the Shunned House, Red Hook's vaults, the old captain's house, the Whateley farmhouse, the Waite house, the sculptor's tower and the Rue d'Auseil. Ten hold a Silver Vial (a Reagent dose; at most 14 doses).
- Playtest round 1 (world): Colliders gained a turned box (obox), so walls, fences and houses stand at any angle.
- Playtest round 1 (bosses): A boss is bound to its ring, not a leash (systems/bossArena.ts). It wakes as the investigator steps into the ring, never loses them while they stay within 8 m of it (sight or no sight), and gives up only after they have been beyond that for 2.5 s; so its health resets only when the investigator leaves or dies. It keeps 2.5 m inside its rim, backs off only from 4 m and at 60% pace, and circles once near the rim.
- Playtest round 1 (bosses): Quick foes slip blows: casters, mind-thieves and skirmishers (bosses among them) may sidestep a blow as it winds up within 3.5 m, a 3 m step aside with a moment's i-frames that never leaves the ring (30–40%, at most every 1.25 s). Colossi and brutes never do.
- Playtest round 1 (bosses): Ten attacks for a dodging game (systems/strikes.ts): the eruption marks the ground under and around the investigator and bursts spot by spot; the quake sends a ring racing out along the ground (roll through it or guard); the sweeping beam crosses an arc; the barrage pours bolts out along four turning arms; the vortex draws its prey in, then bursts; a three-blow chain (sweep, backhand, delayed overhead) and a long-delayed slam that punishes an early roll. Chains are moves that run on into the next (`then`); a stagger breaks them. Every boss script mixes several in by what it is: sorcerers get barrages, marked ground and beams; beasts and brutes chains, delayed slams and quakes; colossi quakes, eruptions and vortices; outer gods most of all.
- Playtest round 1 (bosses): Every boss attack shows on the ground while it winds up (render/telegraphs.ts, one instanced batch of dithered decals): magenta where a blow lands (a swing's arc, a slam's disc, a charge's lane, a pool's circle, marked ground), green where a bolt or beam runs, bone for a quake's band, purple for what works on the mind or pulls. Each fills as the blow comes and flashes as it falls. Beams draw as white-hot ribbons; eruptions, quakes, teleports, summons, darkness and phase changes throw particles, and bolts leave glowing trails. Ordinary foes show none: they are read by their bodies.
- Playtest round 1 (bosses): The higher a boss stands in the Mythos, the less its body holds together (render/eldritch.ts, shaders/eldritch.ts): named horrors writhe a little and their sprites slip now and then; great old ones writhe hard under an oily sheen, with patches of flesh open onto a starry void; outer gods also slide bands of their bodies out of place, wear a turning halo of shards and lag two dithered echoes of themselves. Sprites of the higher tiers open their darkest pixels onto the void and lag a faint double.
- Playtest round 1 (bosses): The boss bars sit at the bottom centre; interaction prompts moved down to make room.
- Playtest round 1 (map): The ground the investigator has seen stays charted for good (systems/exploration.ts): 16 m cells within 56 m of where they have walked, region by region, saved as base64 bits. A save from before the map loads with nothing charted.
- Playtest round 1 (map): The minimap (top right, round, north up, 110 m to its edge) and the full map (M, pad Select, or Map in the pause menu; the world stands still while it is open) draw only what has been seen; the rest lies under a dark survey sheet, its edge soft. The full map shows the realm the investigator is in (regions joined edge to edge: the waking world is one realm, each far realm another) with how much of it is charted; wheel or +/− zoom, drag or WASD pan, C centres.
- Playtest round 1 (map): The map's art is an investigator's survey drawn from the world data (ui/mapArt.ts): ground tinted by region and shaded by the lie of the land with contour lines every 6 m, water, roads, houses, walls, groves, graves and stones, dungeon plans and boss rings. It is sampled a few rows a frame (1.5 ms a frame while playing, 6 ms with the map open), so no frame stalls.
- Playtest round 1 (map): Marked where they have been seen: Elder Signs (dim until lit, named once lit), gates, dungeon doors (with an eye once a boss inside has been beheld), boss rings (an eye, struck through once the boss is slain), and the investigator's dropped Echoes.
- Playtest round 1 (story): The frame is a 1928 sleeping sickness in Arkham. A new game opens on a telegram from Armitage and four notebook entries (data/intro.ts): eleven sleepers who talk of a stair, a key and a tall man in a good coat; the investigator lies down in the Miskatonic reading room and wakes in the dream. The title screen names the time and place instead of quoting anyone. Lovecraft is never named in the game; the stories show through their people, places and books.
- Playtest round 1 (story): Eight people from the stories stand beside Elder Signs (data/npcs.ts; E talks): Peaslee on the quad, Gilman in Arkham, Kuranes in the dream lands, Zadok Allen in Innsmouth, Wilmarth in Vermont, Dr. Willett in Providence, Curtis Whateley in Dunwich and Dyer at the Antarctic camp. What they say depends on the quests; the world stands still while they talk. Nothing hunts or hurts them.
- Playtest round 1 (story): Quests (data/quests.ts, systems/quests.ts): the main line runs Peaslee → Gilman → the Witch House (Keziah Mason) → down the Sleeper's stair to Kuranes → the Threshold, where the three endings already wait. Five favours send the investigator after Curwen, the Dunwich Horror, the thing in Akeley's chair and the shoggoth, and Zadok trades his story and a Silver Vial for a swallow of Laudanum. Goals are talk, give, slay a boss or find an Elder Sign; rewards are Echoes, insight or a vial. Stages are saved.
- Playtest round 1 (story): Every tome now has a text, and ten field notes (clippings, letters, a police report, a memorandum) lie on crates near Elder Signs (data/documents.ts). Picking one up opens it to read; the journal (pause menu) lists the quests under way and done, and every document read to reread. The map marks people once their spot is seen, named once met.
- Playtest round 1 (story): All text is plain prose, written as the people in it would say it. The rest message, the new-game warning and the three endings were rewritten in that voice.
- Playtest round 1 (detail): The investigator is rebuilt in detail (render/investigator.ts): lapels, shoulders and a flared coat skirt, shirt and tie under a rust scarf, belt and buckle, a satchel on its strap, a fedora with its band, brow, nose and ears, gloves, the cane's silver grip and ferrule, the revolver, shoes, and the lantern in an iron cage. The people met share the build with their own clothes (render/npcFigures.ts).
- Playtest round 1 (detail): Sprites stay 64 px (the game renders at 400×225, where more pixels would not show); detail comes from skins and anatomy instead. Each creature's palette and body plan give it a skin worked into its shading (render/sprites/skins.ts): scales on sea things, fur on beasts, chitin plates, veins, warts, cracked stone, wrinkles, cloth folds, trailing wisps; wet skins take a hot highlight. Humanoids gained feet, knees, shoulders, a neck, a belt line and a mouth (and gills, a head fin and claws on the sea-born and the ghoulish); hunched things show ribs, spine knobs and teeth; beasts show ribs, a ridged back, paws, an ear and teeth as they bite.
- Playtest round 1 (detail): Every body stands on a soft dithered blob shadow (render/shadows.ts); a hovering creature's shrinks and fades with its height.
- Playtest round 1 (detail): Ground cover, render-only and seeded by chunk (render/groundCover.ts): grass tufts and October leaf litter on turf, reeds on mud, dry grass on sand, pebbles on stone and snow, nodules on flesh; never on roads, sites or dungeon floors, never collided with. About 45% of the hardwoods keep clumps of rust and ochre leaves.
- Playtest round 1 (detail): Dungeon walls gained plinths, cornices, pilasters every few metres, iron sconces with lit flames on some, and rubble at their feet; pillars gained bases and capitals. All render-only: the colliders are unchanged.
- Playtest round 1 (self-playtest): Scripted runs walked the road from the quad to Arkham and fought the Colour Out of Space in its ring (no errors; the fight held without a reset; the busiest views drew 48–74 calls). Beyond the lantern the world fell to black and hid its towns, so the fog was lifted a shade off black (still near black) and the moon brightened a little: houses, trees and stones now stand out as shapes against it.
- Playtest round 1 (self-playtest): The new attacks and story beats were silent. Marked ground now hisses as it is marked and bursts with a thump, a quake rumbles, a sweeping beam hums as it begins, and picking up a document or moving a quest on has its own sound.
- Playtest round 1 (self-playtest): A new investigator gets one-time hints at the upper left as things first come up (moving, a fight, a wound, a failing mind, an Elder Sign, dropped Echoes, the map, a quest, a boss, insight), remembered in the browser rather than the save.
- Playtest round 2 (feedback): A foe's health is restored only when the investigator rests or dies. A foe that gives up the chase walks home keeping its wounds; a boss the investigator leaves keeps them too and, woken again, takes up at once the phase they bring (its arena change and summons return; Azathoth's song resumes where it broke off). A wounded foe let go by streaming comes back as wounded (its health fraction kept by spawn id), and a reload keeps the wounds (an optional `wounds` save field; still version 2).
- Playtest round 2 (feedback): Walls and houses swam up close because the PS1 affine mapping bends a large face's texture across its two triangles. The affine coordinates now stray at most 1.25 texels from the true ones when calm (up to 10 as madness sets in, a sanity effect under the FX cap), and mip levels come from the true mapping's gradients. Far faces keep their wobble; near ones only shiver.
- Playtest round 2 (feedback): Figures gained elbows, wrists, knees and ankles. Walking, running and sprinting are key poses of one leg over a stride (render/gait.ts), blended by speed, with a cadence fitted so a planted foot keeps pace with the ground; the arms swing bent against the legs, the pelvis twists and dips, a run leaves the ground between steps and the body leans into its pace. Every pose then settles: a knee gives where its foot would sink (on slopes too, from the ground under each foot), and feet lie level. The coat's skirt is two panels that swing with the thighs, so legs no longer cut through it; the lantern hangs low at the left side of the belt, clear of the swinging arm.
- Playtest round 2 (feedback): The title's music could start only on a first key or click (the browser's rule), and choosing at once faded it out as it began, so it was hardly heard. The title now opens on "press any key", which starts it; it plays on through the menu, under the veil and through a new game's opening, and fades as the world first shows.
- Playtest round 2 (feedback): Long jumps pass under a veil (ui/veil.ts, ui/journeys.ts): ink creeps in from the edges along noise with a dithered Cosmic Purple fringe, and once it covers everything Lovecraft's branch-like Elder Sign glows over the name of where the investigator is bound. It covers a game begun or continued (the world is built out of sight), fast travel, the descent into dream, gates, waking after death (the fall shows for 0.9 s and the death throes wait for the dark) and quitting to the title. The world stands still while it covers, takes one step on arrival so its creatures rise, then waits while the chunks about it are built at 14 ms a frame (at least 0.9 s, at most 4 s), and moves again as it lifts. On the title it only haunts the edges.
- Playtest round 2 (feedback): Sanity lost now reads on its bar without adding to the screen's distortions (ui/mindHud.ts): the lost part lingers in Eldritch Magenta and drains after 0.9 s, as a wound does on health's bar; a loss of 1.5 or more at once (a blow, a first sight, a gaze's burst; not an aura's slow drain) is announced as `SanityLost`, which jolts the bar, lights its frame and sounds a soft thud and ringing inside the head. The threshold for the call to drink is the Fractured band: crossing into it, the band's notice adds "T LAUDANUM" (or "REST AT AN ELDER SIGN" with none left), and while it lasts the Laudanum count reads "T · LAUDANUM" and pulses.
- Playtest round 3 (feedback): The roll was over too fast to read (its somersault took a quarter of a second, the whole move half a second). It now lasts 48 frames (0.8 s): the same 4.2 m over frames 0–34, a somersault of about 0.4 s, then the investigator lands in a squat and rises through a lunge without a dip, standing just as the move ends. Its i-frames still begin on frame 2 and now run to 24 (0.37 s, was 0.22 s); another action may cut in from frame 36 (was 22); the stamina cost is unchanged. The landing dust comes from the roll's data, not a fixed frame.
- Playtest round 3 (feedback): Raising the guard calls off an attack. A block pressed (or a parry) while a blow winds up or recovers, or a shot before or after it fires, ends the move at once: the guard is up that step (a parry press parries; with no stamina to parry it calls nothing off), the stamina spent is lost, and a chain begins again at its first blow. Never while a blow is landing (its hit window): held through it, the guard comes up as the window closes. Never a roll, a backstep, an item or a reaction. A blow struck with the guard already held plays out and the guard returns after it (only a fresh press calls one off), so attacking from behind the guard works as before.
- Playtest round 3 (feedback): Figures ease from one pose into the next over 0.12 s when their move or guard changes (render/poseBlend.ts, render only: joints slerp the short way round from the pose last drawn), so a swing called off by the guard, a blow cut short by a stagger, a chain's next strike or a guard raised mid-stride no longer jumps.
- Playtest round 4 (self-playtest): Echoes were earned, dropped and recovered but bought nothing, so dying cost nothing that mattered. They now buy levels at an Elder Sign (systems/levels.ts): Vigour (+12 health), Endurance (+8 stamina) and Might (+4% to the investigator's blows and shots), up to 20 each, the next level costing 250 + 90n + 9n² Echoes (n levels bought). Insight keeps the mind's upgrades: Resolve, and a new Draught (+1 Laudanum carried, up to 2, 3 insight each). An older save's insight Vigour and Endurance become the levels nearest them (same health and stamina); the save stays version 2 with an optional `levels` field. The HUD marks a level within reach (ECHOES n ▲), a one-time hint says to rest, and a bought level sounds an open chord.
- Playtest round 4 (self-playtest): Searching a dungeon now pays: an Echo cache (an iron-bound casket of Void Green shards) waits in each dead end holding nothing else, or in the deepest free room where there is no dead end (world/caches.ts), 28 in all. Each holds three times the richest bounty among its dungeon's creatures (150 to 6000). Taken for good (kept with the tomes read).
- Playtest round 4 (self-playtest): The night sky was the fog's flat near-black, so outdoor views had no depth and nothing to steer by. A sky is now drawn first, behind everything (render/sky.ts): the fog's colour at and below the horizon, a moonlit haze rising off it that is strongest on the moon's side (far roofs, trees and hills stand against it as silhouettes), twinkling stars, slow cloud silvered near the moon, and the moon itself (maria, halo) along the moonlight's own direction. Each realm has its own (SKY: sea mist over Innsmouth, clear stars over the Mountains and Pnakotus, a near moon in the Dreamlands, no moon over Yuggoth or Beyond, nothing under K'n-yan), eased over 3 s when walked into and at once after a journey; inside a dungeon's walls it closes off to the fog's colour.
- Playtest round 4 (self-playtest): Lit windows barely existed: each window drew once to be lit and again, separately, for the lit colour, so a "lit" window was almost always dark glass (about 2% glowed). One draw now decides both, and 30% of windows glow, brighter, so towns show life at night.
- Playtest round 4 (self-playtest, marketability): The title read "[WORKING TITLE]" in plain monospace over black. The game is proposed as **Seventy Steps** (one constant, `GAME_NAME` in data/intro.ts, and index.html's <title>): the Seventy Steps of Light Slumber are its way down into the Dreamlands and the stair the sleepers dream of; a web search found no game by that name, while "Unwaking" sat too near an existing *Unawake*, and anything with "Arkham" in it collides with Batman's and Arkham Horror's. The name is set as a logo in a period book face over a small stair of seven treads ending in a Cosmic Purple glow; the page gained a description, theme colour and an inline SVG favicon of the same stair.
- Playtest round 4 (self-playtest, marketability): The title now plays over a live backdrop through the game's own pipeline (ui/titleBackdrop.ts, world/titleStair.ts): the investigator walks down an endless stone stair cut into the earth, lantern at the hip, between walls with pilasters and a row of sconce flames going down into the fog, toward a gate glowing purple that keeps its distance however far they go, under a low moon. The stair repeats every eight steps, so the walk loops without a seam. The moon's light is cheated higher and brighter than its disc so the descent reads. The logo and menu moved to a left column over a dark gradient so the scene shows to the right. The backdrop stops and its canvas goes when the world begins.
- Playtest round 4 (self-playtest): Menus, dialogue, documents and the ending card are set in a period book face (system fonts: Iowan Old Style, Palatino, Book Antiqua, Georgia; no font files), a size larger; the telegram in the intro is typewritten (Courier New). The HUD keeps monospace, for its numbers.
- Playtest round 4 (self-playtest): Boss fights had only a darker drone bed beneath the region's. They now have music (render/audio/bossMusic.ts, scores in data/music.ts): a low bowed ostinato (a sawtooth and its sub-octave through a closing low-pass), timpani, and a choir of detuned saws through an "ah" formant holding a chord a bar, scheduled a moment ahead on the audio clock into the drones' bus (so failing sanity bends it too). Each phase quickens it and adds to it: a second drum, a roll every fourth bar and a shaker on the off-beats; from the third phase a bell line above. The deeper in the lore, the stranger the mode: the named in D Phrygian, the Great Old Ones lower in B Locrian, the Outer Gods in whole tones. It swells in with the fight over 2.5 s and fades over 3.5 s when the fight ends or another begins. Offline renders put it at a peak of about 0.4 and a level near the vanquished stinger's, then trimmed to 0.45 so blows cut through it.
- Playtest round 4 (self-playtest, onboarding): A new investigator woke with nothing to do but find someone to talk to ("Nothing yet" in the journal). The main line now always leads somewhere (systems/lead.ts): its open stage's goal (the person to speak with or give to, the boss's ring or room, the Elder Sign to reach) or, before a main quest begins, whoever asks it. The journal says it when nothing is under way ("Speak with Wingate Peaslee, by the Elder Sign at Miskatonic Quad."), and the map and minimap mark it with a breathing hollow diamond; on the minimap, a pointer on the rim when it lies beyond. Only within the realm on show: another realm is reached by its gate or stair. A one-time hint explains the mark.
- Playtest round 4 (self-playtest): There was one weapon, so nothing to find but Echoes and nothing to choose. Arms now (data/weapons.ts, systems/arms.ts): each weapon is a light and a heavy chain over the shared swings, taken up from a new Arms page in the pause menu (anywhere, like a soulslike's equipment screen; the chains swap, the rest of the moves stay). Besides the sword-cane: a Straight Razor on a trestle in the Witch House parlour (four quick cuts that land from frame 6, short reach, a lunging heavy) and a Woodsman's Axe in the Whateley farmhouse kitchen (slow overhead chops that stagger, a heavy that bites for 74). Picking one up says where to take it up; owned and in-hand weapons are saved (optional fields; an unknown one falls back to the sword-cane). Only the weapon in hand shows on the investigator.
- Playtest round 5 (feedback): The title's theme seemed to start late. Browsers allow sound only after a click or key press (the title asks for one), and on that press the track's first three seconds are a near-silent lead-in (loudness 0.001 to 0.05 against 0.14 for the whole track; its first hit lands at 3.85 s). The theme now begins where it first swells, at 3.3 s (a loop still plays it whole), is buffered while the title waits (`preload = 'auto'`) and fades up over a quarter second so starting mid-phrase does not click. Where the browser allows sound on opening, it plays at once with no press; checked headless both ways (blocked: it sounds from 3.31 s the moment of the key press; allowed: from 3.30 s half a second after the page opens).
- Playtest round 5 (feedback): The investigator stopped dead after a roll: its travel ends on frame 34 but the move ran to 48, and moves ignore the stick. A move may now name a `release` frame from which the stick ends it, so a roll run out of lands straight into the stride (34; the backstep's is 14); let go of the stick and the roll still comes up through its recovery.
- Playtest round 5 (feedback): The backstep could be spammed: it cancelled into itself every 16 frames for 2.4 m, about 9 m/s (faster than a sprint) with i-frames a third of the time. A move may now `rest`: the backstep cannot start again while it lasts or for 10 frames after it ends (a press then is dropped), so spammed it is slower than walking. It still turns into an attack or a roll in its cancel window.
- Playtest round 5 (feedback): The sound grew louder as sanity fell, which put players off. Two causes, both gone: the sanity drone (a rough sawtooth chord rising to 0.06) is removed, and the sanity waveshaper, whose soft clip gave quiet sounds a gain of 1 + 50 × amount (over +30 dB at its worst, lifting the whole ambience), is now a blend of the clean signal with tanh(g·x)/g: its slope at silence is always 1 and it never exceeds its input, so madness squashes loud sounds into grit (full scale to about 0.5) and never turns anything up. Madness still sags and drifts the pitch.
- Playtest round 5 (feedback): Street lamps, fires, dungeon torches and lit windows glowed but lit nothing: the lantern was the night's only light, and self-lit surfaces were dimmed by their texture. The chunk and dungeon builders now report each as a light spot (propShapes' lamp glass and fire pits, houseMesh's lit windows half a metre out from the glass, siteMeshes' sconce flames), and render/worldLights.ts makes the nearest 12 to the camera the world shader's point lights (the lantern's falloff; per pixel, N·L for the world, a fixed share for characters and sprites). The chosen set's reach is the 13th nearest's distance (at most 42 m), and a light fades out over the last 30% of it, so none pops as another takes its slot; flames flicker, windows and lamps hold steady. Every spot within 75 m also wears an additive halo that fades only 55% as far as the fog does, so a town reads by its lights at night, and the lantern wears one too. A self-lit surface now shines through its texture (60% toward white).
- Playtest round 5 (feedback): The investigator's face was brighter than their lantern: pale skin (bone × 2) took a full character's share of a lantern hanging a hand's breadth away, while the flame was drawn through a dark texture. The skin and shirt are now bone × 1.3, the investigator takes 45% of a character's share of their own lantern (`LANTERN.self`), and whatever lights them (moon, lantern, a street lamp overhead) is held so their lit colour's brightest channel stays at most 1 (`LANTERN.selfMax`); with the flame's brightening this keeps the flame the brightest thing on them (headless, at luminance: face 0.21 in the dark and 0.43 under a street lamp, flame 0.93).
- Playtest round 6 (feedback): The title's stair flickered. Each step's pale nosing was a thin box laid over the front of its tread, their top faces coplanar and overlapping; vertex snapping (spec §2) moves each face's own corners to the low-res pixel grid, so the two depths disagreed by centimetres to decimetres at a grazing angle and the faces took turns, a band of flashing strips down the stair as the camera walked. The nosing now sits flush in front of a tread shortened to make room, sharing its corners (so both snap alike). Measured frame by frame under a paused clock: pixels flipping back and forth six or more times in 48 frames fell from 14,430 to 525, the stair floor from 10.4% to 0.2%; a test keeps tread tops from overlapping.
- Playtest round 6 (feedback): "The music should start as soon as you open the game." Browsers refuse sound until the page's first key press or click (Chromium, headless, under its default desktop policy: refused on opening, after a reload and in a new tab; it sounds only once there has been an input). No page can get round that, so the game now opens with its first input: it opens in the dark, and where the browser refuses the theme the veil shows the Elder Sign over "press any key"; that press starts the theme and draws the dark back from the stair together. Where the browser lets sound play on opening (a returning player, a host page that delegates autoplay, a permissive browser), the theme sounds and the title opens by itself, with no press (checked headless both ways: sounding 1.1 s after opening, or 0.07 s after the key).
- Playtest round 6 (feedback): Spawning in, the theme faded linearly over 2.5 s by the element's volume, stepped each animation frame: loud until the last moment, then dropping and stopping at 2% volume, and stalling when building the world held up the frames. The theme now plays through the audio engine (a media element source into a bus after the compressor, under the volume setting, beside the sanity FX), so its fades run on the audio clock. As the veil lifts on the world it sinks away over 7 s: an exponential fade, even in loudness (about −11 dB each quarter, −43 dB at the end, where it stops), under a low-pass closing from 20 kHz to 280 Hz, while the region's ambience, already fading in under it, takes over.
- Playtest round 6 (feedback): "Improve the overall sound design ... use good and fitting audio assets from the web (not overused by a lot of other games)." This overrides spec §0's "no asset files" for sound, as the title theme already had (CLAUDE.md now says so). The recordings are all from Freesound and all CC0 (public domain: free to use, change and ship, with no attribution required; each is credited anyway, with its cut and treatment, in public/audio/CREDITS.md). To keep them from being the stock sounds heard everywhere, they are mostly from Craig Smith's library of vintage film and radio effects (57 of 67 sources, typically tens to a few hundred downloads each) and a few field recordists, never the popular game packs; and each is cut and treated for the game (monsters slowed to tape speed, a toad an octave down for the Deep Ones, whales an octave down for the Great Old Ones, big cats slowed for the lesser things).
- Playtest round 6: What is recorded now: blows (light swishes and heavy whooshes, heard a moment before they land; a creature's lower the bigger it is), impacts (blade into flesh, clangs on the guard, a bright ring for a parry, heavy thuds, a stab and slide for a riposte, body falls), a heavy revolver, the investigator's grunts when hurt and their dying groan (under the old doom chord), a roll's tumble, a backstep's scuffs, the Laudanum's cork, pages and a struck match; footsteps a stride apart on what is underfoot (the sea's shallows, a dungeon's stone, a road, open ground); and the creatures' voices for every tier default and most named voices (growls, with a snarl when one turns on the investigator; bellows, groans, whales, toad croaks, pig grunts for the ghouls' glibbering, bees for the Mi-Go, howls for the hounds, cats, rats, whispers, wings, and a new hiss for the Children of Yig); a creature cries out as it dies. The pipers, Zann's viol, the choirs and the Tekeli-li keep their recipes. Recorded stingers keep a share of the recipe beneath where it gives weight (a hit's low thump, the parry's ring).
- Playtest round 6: Every region has recorded ambience under its drone: looped beds (wind; crickets in Dunwich and Vermont; surf and marsh frogs at Innsmouth; slowed great waves and a singing wind at R'lyeh; a howling cold wind in the Mountains; leaves in Vermont; a cave's roar and drips in K'n-yan and every legacy dungeon; an alien buzz on Yuggoth; a distant murmur in the Dreamlands), each played twice half a loop apart and panned apart so a mono file surrounds the listener, crossfading as the investigator crosses a border; and spot sounds now and then, far off and dulled (owls, a dog howling, Dunwich's whip-poor-wills, thunder, an old hull creaking at Innsmouth, gurgles under the water, chains, creaking timbers). The mix was measured in the browser at the master: beds sit near −28 dB, the drones (their levels lowered for it) near −32 dB, a dread beneath the wind and sea rather than the wind and sea; recorded voices match the loudness of the recipes they replace (+2 dB).
- Playtest round 6: Sound files are mono MP3 (every browser decodes it): one-shots 96 kbps, beds 32 kHz 64 kbps and looped by crossfading their end into their start (and skipping 60 ms at each end, where a browser may keep an encoder's padding). 4.8 MB in all, one-shots loaded when WebAudio starts, beds when their place is reached; until a file has loaded (or if it fails) its recipe plays.
- Playtest round 7 (feedback: "attacking with the razor the enemy just disappears, and you can't be attacked; switching weapons it persists"): the weapons are sound (in the simulation and in the browser the razor's cuts land and the investigator takes blows as with the cane). What was described is the Unmoored band's hallucinations (spec §3A): lesser horrors that look and fight like the real thing, vanish when struck and wound only the mind; the Witch House, where the razor lies, is hard on sanity. They now read as what they are: a hallucination slips and glitches like an eldritch body (wrongness 0.45), comes apart into dark smoke and violet motes with a whisper when struck, and a hint explains them the first time one is struck or lands a blow.
- Playtest round 7 (feedback: "you can't even attack smaller enemies"): blows sweep at chest height (1.1–1.2 m), and passed over the shortest bodies (Zoogs, the Cat from Saturn, Brown Jenkin: 0.5–0.66 m tall), missing by up to 0.21 m at arm's length. A blow now comes down to a body shorter than it (to just under its top, never below the attacker's knee); a test checks every blow of every weapon reaches every creature in front.
- Playtest round 7 (feedback: "sometimes when blocking the attacks go through"): three causes. Grabs are unblockable by design (19 creatures have one) but gave no warning: a creature now flares crimson about the head as a grab winds up, with a warning sound, and a hint says to roll the first time one lands. The guard covered only 120° in front: now 180°. And without a lock-on the investigator turned to face where they walked, so backing away with the guard up turned their back on the blow: while guarding they now face where the camera looks, and strafe or back away behind the guard.
- Playtest round 7 (feedback: walk while drinking; smoother, more responsive combat): a move may name `walk`, the share of the walking pace kept through it: a swallow of Laudanum and a shot of Reagent keep 40% (the legs stride under the arm's pose), and moving walks out of their tails once the dose is down (`release`). The same `release` now ends every blow's late recovery (two frames after its cancel frame) when the stick is held, so a fight flows into movement. A dodge may cut a blow short three frames after it lands (COMBAT.evadeAfter), before the cancel window opens for the rest. The input buffer holds a press for 250 ms (spec §3B's 150 ms dropped a press made early in a swing before it could cut in). Without a lock, a blow turns to the nearest foe within 3.6 m and 70° of its line (a shot: three times as far, a third as wide), so blows stop whiffing past a foe just off the line.
- Playtest round 7 (feedback: potions should stop damage over time): a swallow of Laudanum steadies the mind for 10 s (auras, roars, gazes, a hallucination's blows and the void take no sanity) and a shot of Reagent mends the body for 8 s (a pool's and the void's lingering ticks do no harm). There are no stacking debuffs in the game to purge: its sanity drains are standing near horrors, and its health drains are standing in their pools; so a dose now stops them for a while, which is what purging them would do. The HUD shows it: the sanity bar glows violet and reads STEADY, the health bar glows green and the Reagent line reads MENDED.
- Playtest round 7 (feedback: fast travel by clicking lit waypoints on the map): a lit Elder Sign of the realm on show is chosen by a click (or in turn with Tab or the pad's shoulders, nearest first), glows in a breathing ring, and a second click (Enter, pad A) makes the journey under the veil, as from a sign's menu. It is refused, in words under the title, while a foe hunts the investigator close by (the same rule as resting) or a boss fight is on. Only the realm on show: the map shows one realm, and another realm's signs stay a sign's menu away.
- Playtest round 7 (feedback: the minimap should turn with the camera): it turns so what the camera looks at is up, the investigator's arrow showing where they face against it, and an N rides the rim toward north. The full map stays north up (a map to read and plan on).
- Playtest round 7 (feedback: the game should not pause while talking): the world goes on while someone talks; the investigator stands to listen (no input reaches them, as in a sign's menu), and a blow landing on them ends the talk. Reading a tome or note still stops the world (a page to read at leisure).
- Playtest round 7 (feedback: "when sanity goes down the game should not be further pixelated, but keep the wavy morphing"): madness no longer coarsens the picture. The post pass's extra pixel size (0 → 1 more low-res pixel) and the vertex snap's growth (1 → 4 low-res pixels) are gone; the low resolution and a one-pixel snap hold at every sanity. The ripple, chromatic split, space displacement, affine swim and lens breathing still grow as sanity falls (spec §2's pixel crush is overridden).
- Playtest round 7 (feedback: better waypoints, "thematically fitting juice", the sigil glowing like the loading screen's once activated): an Elder Sign is now a shrine (world/shrine.ts): a stepped plinth, a tapered standing stone of weathered rock (a new `rock` texture) carved with the branch-like sign on both faces, a ring of runes on the ground, and a broken ring of lesser stones behind it (they collide; the way in front stays open, and each rest point stays clear). A sign not yet found stands dark. Found, its carving glows as the veil's does, a bone core in a Cosmic Purple glow, breathing; the runes glow and turn slowly; candles burn on the plinth; motes rise; and a purple light (a new `sigil` light kind) lights the ground and glows through the fog from afar. Found, it flares: the sign blazes, a ring of light runs out over the ground and motes burst up; resting there, a softer flare. The glow is drawn by its own additive shader, displaced and snapped like the world (shaders/world.ts's SPACE_GLSL), so it keeps to its stone.
- Playtest round 7 (feedback: "improve the visual look of all assets drastically, including the player character"): figures are rounded out. The investigator and the people met in the dream are built from tapered, smooth-sided prisms and lumps (meshKit's `round` and `lump`) instead of boxes, so they take the light as bodies do; the investigator gains a turned-up collar, an Inverness cape over the shoulders, a coat to mid-calf, a snap-brim fedora, a moustache, round-toed shoes and a hurricane lantern, and a mid-dark coat (it was near black) so the form shows. Characters also get a soft light from above and a cold rim light where they turn from the eye (world shader, LIGHT.rim), so dark figures read against the dark.
- Playtest round 7: the default internal resolution is now 1.5× (600×338; still 0.5–2× in the settings). At 400×225 the figures' new detail (face, cape, hat, lantern) was lost in a handful of pixels; at 1.5× it reads while the picture stays chunky and pixelated. This overrides spec §2's default of 400×225; a player's stored setting is kept.
- Playtest round 7 (all assets, continued): the creature sprites are shaded as a pixel artist would — six bands of tone dithered only where they meet, a small highlight and a cool rim on the edge turned from the light — and a shape drawn over another is edged with a dark contour where it overlaps (limbs part from bodies); outlines take a dark shade of the body they ring rather than one flat colour (sprites/raster.ts; near-black pixels grey, so eight bits never round them into false colour). World props and houses have light baked into their vertices, darker toward the ground (meshKit's `groundShade`); houses' windows gain mullions and sills, and clapboard houses shutters; street lamps are Victorian (a fluted base, a collar, a ladder bar, a four-sided lantern under a pyramid cap).
- Playtest round 8 (feedback: "I don't like the new character and NPC models at all, either revert to their old models or find free and perfect ones online"): reverted to the round-6 investigator and people. Models from the web would break spec §0 and CLAUDE.md (no imported models; the figures are tweened primitives, no skeletal animation), so the old ones return as they were, and the rim and top light made for the rounded ones goes with them. The 1.5× default resolution stays (it serves everything on screen, and the setting still offers 0.5–2×).
- Playtest round 8 (feedback: remove the new effects on the health and sanity bars when drinking a potion): the bars' violet and green glows and their STEADY and MENDED words are gone. The potions still hold the mind and body as before (10 s and 8 s).
- Playtest round 8 (feedback: remove the stair-walking title screen, keep the purple rim; the menu as before): the live stair is gone (titleBackdrop.ts, world/titleStair.ts and its test). The title is again the name and its two lines centred over black, the buttons under them, as before round 4 (the name in the menus' book face, and without round 4's small stair motif); the only thing added is the veil haunting the edges in Cosmic Purple once the title opens.
- Playtest round 8 (feedback: the title theme cut off abruptly when it looped): the track ends in a five-second decay and begins with a three-second lead-in, so looping the file left about two seconds of near silence (the level fell to −60 dB). It now loops as a segue: two passes take turns, the next starting from its very beginning 4.75 s before the one playing ends, so its rising lead-in swells under the old ending's decay and its first hit lands as that fades out (measured offline: the joint stays within about −15 dB of the peak).
- Playtest round 8 (feedback: enemies' eyes should glow faintly out of the dark when near enough): a sprite's eyes (and glowing marks) wear a faint additive halo in their own colour, placed where the atlas found them in each frame, and the eyes themselves now hold out to 12 m and sink into the dark by 22 m (they were gone by 13). Near enough, a creature beyond the lantern shows as a pair of faint lights; from afar, nothing. The halos share the world lights' halo batch code (render/halos.ts), one draw for every creature.
- Playtest round 8 (feedback: "enemies should have a further and more logical aggro detection range, and way better AI overall"): sight reaches 24–30 m (it was 12–24, all or nothing at 16) in a 150° cone with a clear line; within 12 m a creature knows at once, farther a glimpse stirs it and it turns and stalks toward what it saw until it is sure (about a second, twice that at the edge of its sight); beside or behind it, it glimpses only what is within 6 m. It hears the investigator: footsteps 5 m, running 14 m, a roll 10 m, a blow 12 m, a shot 34 m, half as far through a wall. Struck, any creature turns on whoever struck it at once. Taking up a hunt, it rouses its kind: those within 8 m join at once, those within 16 m come to look. Losing its quarry for 1.5 s, it goes to where it last knew it and looks about for 7 s before going home; walking home, what it sees plainly within its leash (now 45 m) it turns on again. Idle ones look about now and then. In a fight only two close in on the investigator at once (the nearest; the rest keep off beyond their reach, circling and waiting their turn); a hunter strikes into an opening (a swallow, a blow's recovery, a stagger) once half its cooldown has passed; quick ones fall back after a blow; hunters keep apart, and one pushing against something steps aside to get round it. Bosses and allies keep their own ways (the arena ring, following the player). Split: perception.ts (senses), brain.ts (states), tactics.ts (the fight).
- Playtest round 9 (feedback: "the title song doesn't even loop, and just stops entirely"): round 8's segue started a second pass of the theme on a second audio element from a timer. Browsers that grant sound per element, and only inside a key press or click (Safari), refuse that second element, so the first pass played out and the theme fell silent (reproduced in Chromium under a strict per-element rule: the second pass was refused; Chrome itself allows it, which is why round 8's checks passed). The theme is one element again, looping natively as a safety net, and it loops by a leap: 1.1 s before its end, where the ending has decayed to about −20 dB, it jumps back to 3.2 s, where the lead-in has swelled to the same level a moment before the first hit, under a 30 ms dip on either side so the jump cannot click (measured offline: the joint never falls below −20 dB; the file's own loop fell to −66 dB). Should the timer miss the leap, the file loops by itself and the leap then skips its lead-in.
- Playtest round 10 (feedback: "many of the lanterns are placed very weirdly (e.g. right in front of the doors)"): a street's lamp stood at the very spot along the street where the house on its side stood, a metre off the kerb, so 45 of the town's 80 lamps were a couple of metres before a front door. Lamps now stand at the kerb just past a house's corner, and are placed only once every house stands, never within a house's width of its door out to 3.5 m before it, nor on a road (0 of 71 before a door; a test keeps it so).
- Playtest round 10 (feedback: the "hair" block on the back of the investigator's head): it was one dark slab, the hat's colour, stuck 2.5 cm out from the back of a bare pale head. The investigator now wears a short back and sides in dark brown, apart from the hat: it wraps the skull close from the brim down, round the back and sides above and behind the ears, tapers in to the nape, and leaves short sideburns before the ears (four boxes, in the figures' blocky style; the NPCs' hair was never a slab and is unchanged).
- Playtest round 10 (feedback: "when pressing continue or new game there is about 5-10 seconds pure black without any loading indicators"): the world was made in one piece right after the veil covered the title (the sprite atlas alone took about half a second here), and the frames under the veil drew the world anyway, compiling its shaders on the spot (seconds on some GPUs) and forcing the chunks under the investigator in one frame. The veil could not draw a frame through any of it, and its Elder Sign had not yet faded in, so the screen stayed black. Now the world is made in steps with a frame drawn between them (loading.ts), and the veil shows the sign, the name of where the investigator wakes and a line beneath it that fills as the making goes, through the startup and then the chunks. The sprite atlas is drawn a slice at a time, begun in idle moments while the title waits. Nothing is drawn while the veil covers everything; the shaders compile in parallel where the browser can (KHR_parallel_shader_compile) and each program is taken into use, with one frame drawn, before the veil lifts, so the first frame shown does not stall. New game shows its opening at once and the world is made behind it. Here, from Continue to the world took 3.8 s (was about 5), with no pause in the veil's drawing longer than 0.2 s before it lifts (was 0.6 s of black, then stalls under the sign). Keeping the name of the place and a line beneath it, rather than a loading bar or a percentage, keeps the loading in the veil's own look.
- Playtest round 11 (feedback: "the title music is gone"; "booting the game or refreshing the theme doesn't play, but when quitting out of a run back to the main menu the theme suddenly starts"; "it should start playing after booting the game and landing in the main menu"; asked which route to take: "You decide. My goal is to release the game on Steam soon"): nothing in the theme had broken. A browser holds a page's sound until its first key press or click (Chrome, Safari and Firefox all do, and no page can get round it), so on a fresh load the title waits for one; quitting a run reloads the page carrying the click that chose Quit, which the browser honours, so the theme sounded at once. Since the game is bound for Steam, it now has the desktop shell a web game ships in there: Electron (`desktop/`; its one new dependency, on the user's go-ahead), whose window lets the game sound from the start, so the title's theme plays as the game boots and the title opens straight into its menu (the game's own code already did this wherever sound was allowed). The shell serves the built game on an `app://` scheme rather than from files, so its modules, fetches and media load as from a web server; its responses carry Content-Length, without which the media element took the theme for unseekable and began in its silent lead-in and could not leap back to loop. On the web (`npm run dev`) the title still asks for a key, as it must. Steam's own parts (the Steamworks SDK, packaging) are left for when the release is prepared.
- Playtest round 12 (self-playtest; the user asked to fix everything it found): those passing a gate now stand 5.5 m out from their twin (was 2.6 m), so the camera behind them no longer sits inside the gate's glowing surface (every arrival opened on a flat purple screen), and a stray E cannot send them straight back.
- Playtest round 12: near an Elder Sign the world went saturated purple, worst when madness sets in. Two causes: the sign's purple halo sits 1.4 m before the stone, where the camera stands at the rest point, so it washed the whole screen; and its light, Cosmic Purple, counted as an anomaly hue for colour isolation. Halos now fade out within a few of their radii of the lens, and the sign lights its ground a pale violet too grey to count as an anomaly (its halo and carving keep Cosmic Purple).
- Playtest round 12: a short foe close in (a rat swarm, a troglodyte) vanished under the investigator. When the line from the lens to the foe they fight (the lock target, else the nearest hunter within 4 m) passes through their body, their figure is dithered 60% away (the eldritch shader's `uGhost`), and the lock-on camera looks down at most 0.8 rad (was 1.05).
- Playtest round 12: the ending card's Begin anew went to the `?fresh` test route: no question, no opening, and every reload wiped the new save again.
