It now asks first, then reloads to the plain page with a session flag that opens a new game with its opening. The endings reached (and dreams finished) are kept beside the settings (`systems/records.ts`), so beginning anew never forgets them; the card counts them.
- Playtest round 12: the desktop shell has a preload bridge (`desktop/preload.cjs` → `window.desktop`: quit, fullscreen), and the title (Quit) and pause menu (Quit to desktop, after saving) offer the way out there; the web build offers neither, as a tab is closed by its own hand.
- Playtest round 12: when the dream breaks it says so instead of freezing on black: no WebGL 2 at start, a lost graphics context (saved first), or a frame that throws (the loop stops and autosave halts, so nothing unsound is written) each show a plain screen with Wake again (and Quit in the shell).
- Playtest round 12: the Powder of Ibn Ghazi is not offered while the Dunwich Horror still shows (more than 3 s of showing left), so pressing E again no longer wastes a dose; and Armitage's telegram says Peaslee was found Monday (October 1st, as the Advertiser and Peaslee have it), not four days before the 3rd.
- Playtest round 12 (progression; the self-playtest found nothing gating the endgame: the Dreamlands open from the start, gates open mid-fight, so it walked past the Great Ones and Nyarlathotep to the Ultimate Gate, and an ending wanted no boss slain): the stair into dream opens only once Keziah Mason falls, and the descent quest then begins by itself (Gilman still explains it). No gate opens while a boss fight holds the investigator; it says why, and no veil falls. Kadath's door is sealed until four of the waking world's great horrors fall: each of its ten regions and realms holds a seal, broken by any of its bosses (Keziah's counts). The Gate quest counts them in the journal and marks the nearest horror still holding one, and the door, a glyph-banded slab, says the count. Four of ten, like a soulslike's few great runes of many: it proves the waking world faced without prescribing a route. Beyond the Gate the horrors wake in order ('Umr at-Tawil, then Yog-Sothoth, then Azathoth): each arena waits empty until the one before falls, and says who keeps the way. 'Umr must fall, not only yield, so a player who takes the Silver Key ending there needs no more.
- Playtest round 12 (controls; every prompt named keyboard keys with a pad in hand, and nothing could be rebound): whichever device was used last is the one the prompts name (`core/device.ts`; a key, a click or a pad's press or stick switches it, and an open menu is drawn again), in the standard layout's names as Steam shows them (A, B, RB, LT, View, Menu; `ui/glyphs.ts`). Hints, the HUD's prompt, the minimap and map, the dialogue, the sign menu and the menus' Back all fill `{act}` tokens this way. The keyboard's eleven actions can be rebound from Controls (a key taken from another action swaps with it; Esc, Enter, Tab and the arrows stay the menus'), kept in localStorage apart from the save and the settings (`core/bindings.ts`). The mouse's buttons and the pad's layout stay fixed: rebinding them is a later step, and Steam Input can remap a pad already.
- Playtest round 12 (settings): brightness (a gamma in the post pass, 70–160%), text & HUD scale, screen shake (the hurt shake and the hitstop's), invert look, and music, effects and ambience volumes beside the master (the engine's music and boss-score buses, effects, and the ambient bed), and a Fullscreen button (the shell's window, else the browser's). Settings are grouped Video, Controls, Sound. Hitstop's freeze frames stay: they are the combat's timing, not a comfort effect. There is no frame cap or vsync switch: the browser and the shell draw in step with the display.
- Playtest round 12 (UI scale; the HUD and menus were 9–16 px type, a tenth of the screen at 2560 × 1440): the HUD and the menus' panels are laid out in a layer the screen's size divided by the scale and drawn scaled up (CSS `--ui`), so everything anchored to an edge stays there. The scale is the window's height over 720 px (1 to 2.5) times the setting (0.75–1.5). The map keeps its canvas at the screen's size and scales only its words.
- Playtest round 12 (UX): coming back to a page finds the focus where it was left, and a choice that redraws a page keeps it. The Elder Sign's menu puts the Court's two endings first and the stair next; travel is one button that opens the regions with signs found (a region with one sign travels at once), then the signs of the region chosen: the list of 40 no longer scrolls off the screen. Elder Signs are rested at from 5.2 m (gates and people keep 3.2 m), so E rests at once where travel and death set the investigator down, 4.75 m out. E takes what the investigator faces (a thing's distance, lengthened up to three times the further it lies from straight ahead), so a person by a sign no longer takes it from their side. Talking turns the investigator and the one who speaks to each other, and the camera frames them turned 0.5 rad aside, the speaker clear to the right, until the investigator moves, looks away or acts. The map's names are set so none overlaps another or a mark (right, left, above, below, else left out; the regions', then the signs', then the people's first), with a dark edge. The Arms page shows each weapon's first light and heavy blows with Might counted, how soon the light blow lands, its reach and its stamina.
- Playtest round 12 (balance; a Great Old One took 400–900 light cane blows): every boss's and optional boss's health is now 0.9 × hp^0.9, rounded to 50 (Keziah 1600 → 700, the Colour 2400 → 1000, Cthulhu 20000 → 6700, Nyarlathotep 15000 → 5150), so the order holds and the top is compressed most. Keziah falls to 32 light cane blows with nothing bought, and the hardest to at most 90 at full Might and reinforcement (a test holds both).
- Playtest round 12 (reinforcement; Might's +80% was the only growth): a boss slain for good leaves star-stones by tier (named and greater 1, Great Old Ones 2, Outer Gods 3; an older save is paid for the bosses it slew), and resting at an Elder Sign sets them into a weapon owned: five levels at 1, 1, 2, 2 and 3 stones, each +20% to its blows (melee only, not the revolver). The name is Derleth's; the stones are Lovecraft's, the five-pointed greenish soapstones of At the Mountains of Madness.
- Playtest round 12 (stagger-lock): once staggered, the investigator cannot be staggered again for 90 frames from the stagger (its 34, then time to roll away); blows in that time still hurt. Foes get no such respite, so the axe still holds them.
- Playtest round 12 (sanity pacing): landed blows take the mind once per 75 frames, only the greatest of them counting (each of Keziah's barrage bolts took 8: 71 sanity in 3.5 s at arm's length, now 23). Horrors first seen within 4 s of each other cost only the greatest (Keziah and Brown Jenkin took 20). A roar through a wall takes 35% of its toll, and a roar weighs 1 × sanityDamage (was 1.5, so a Moon-Bog Wraith's took 7.5).
- Playtest round 12 (Azathoth; its script found a silent investigator 23 times, and two boss-tier adds joined): its phases aim at nothing now, only roar and darkness and no summons (the Daemon Pipers and the Other Gods keep their lairs), so its blows fall only where it hears. Its swells come every 4–6 s at 5 sanity (a still mind was gone in 15 s); across the song that is about 105 without Laudanum, and a mind that drinks its three doses stays Lucid or Uneasy. Engaging it says it is blind and hears, and a first-time hint says what carries.
- Playtest round 12 (foes; Dunwich spawned one kind, Vermont, Yuggoth and the Beyond two): creatures gained homes where their stories allow, so each table holds at least four kinds where it was thin. Herbert West's corpses walk the Miskatonic grounds, ghouls Arkham's burying ground, Martense degenerates, rats and a stray Mi-Go the Round Hills, rats and a Moon-Bog Wraith Vermont's hollows, the Yekubian and Beings from Beyond Yuggoth, Cats from Saturn, Beings from Beyond and a Flying Polyp the Beyond, and Esoteric Order priests and hybrids R'lyeh. Dunwich's density rose from 0.6 to 0.8. Innsmouth keeps its three canon kinds.
- Playtest round 12 (people; Pnakotus, K'n-yan, R'lyeh, Yuggoth and the Beyond had no one): Nathaniel Wingate Peaslee at the desert camp (his own account in the Archives, then the Polyp Swarm), Pánfilo de Zamacona in the Mound's depths (Tsathoggua; he gives his espada), Gustaf Johansen on the Alert's deck (Cthulhu), Henry Akeley, as the Outer Ones keep him, at the Mi-Go landing (the Fungoid Cities, then Rhan-Tegoth in the hall of cylinders), and Randolph Carter at the Threshold (Yog-Sothoth). Their thanks are star-stones, a weapon, a vial and insight (quest rewards gained `stones` and `weapon`). They live in `data/npcsFar.ts`, since npcs.ts neared 300 lines.
- Playtest round 12 (the Cavern of Flame's priests stood silent): an NPC may be spoken for by a roster ally (`creature`), with no figure of its own. The ally for Nasht & Kaman-Thah is talked with where it stands, and the priests warn, as in the Dream-Quest, that seeking Kadath will be the death of the soul.
- Playtest round 12 (arms): Obed Marsh's cutlass lies in Y'ha-nthlei's cells (wide cuts, a heavy spin), and Zamacona's espada (long, narrow thrusts and a lunging heavy) is his thanks. The hand's and the rack's weapon meshes are one table (`render/armsMeshes.ts`).
- Playtest round 12 (the lead's sequence break, talking to Kuranes before going back to Gilman): already mended by the descent beginning as Keziah falls. A test now holds it: the lead goes to Kuranes, and talking with him goes straight on to the seals.
- Playtest round 12 (a merchant; Echoes bought only levels): Dr. Francis Morgan, who carried the sprayer at Dunwich, stands on the Miskatonic Quad. His talk ends on his wares (an NPC's `shop`, data/wares.ts): flasks of lamp oil at 150 Echoes (five carried at most), three star-stones at 3,500 and one Silver Vial at 2,500 across the whole dream. What he has sold is saved (`sold`).
- Playtest round 12 (a throwable): a flask of lamp oil (G, pad D-pad ↑; a rebindable action) is lobbed with the off-hand at the foe locked on, else ahead. It bursts for 16 and leaves a burning pool that hurts only the investigator's foes, drawn as pale flame like the game's other fires (the grade keeps rust near sepia). The HUD counts the flasks once any are carried, and the first purchase brings a hint.
- Playtest round 12 (staples: backstabs): the investigator's melee blow into a foe's back (within its rear 100°) lands as a riposte does, 2.5× and reeling. That excludes bosses, anything taller than 2.9 m, and a foe already reeling. It reuses the riposte's spray and cue.
- Playtest round 12 (staples: fog gates): once the investigator is inside an engaged ring, the fog closes behind them. They are held within it until the boss falls or they do, and warm grey mist rises all round the ring, thickest where they stand. A boss woken from outside its ring (within its margin) may still be walked away from, and it gives up as before.
- Playtest round 12 (staples: shortcuts and levers, skipped): every legacy dungeon has an Elder Sign within two rooms of its boss, and the lairs run two to four rooms, so a shortcut back to the mouth would save almost nothing.
- Playtest round 12 (staples: NG+): after an ending, the confirm also offers "Begin journey N, carrying your strength". The carry (levels, arms, weapon, reinforcement, star-stones, the mind's upgrades, Silver Vials, Echoes) is kept beside the records until the reload's new game takes it, before the world fills. Each journey's foes have +60% health, +35% damage and +50% Echoes (up to the 8th); the world, quests and bosses begin again, and the sign menu names the journey.
- Playtest round 12 (endings; each was two paragraphs on black): the card now goes on to "Afterwards", a line for each person met on what became of them (data/epilogues.ts: the ending's own, else their favour done or left undone). Then the credits roll, then the dream's numbers (journey, time in the dream, level, horrors put down of all 51, foes killed, deaths, Echoes earned, endings reached), with Walk on and Begin anew. The run's tally lives in the overworld and is saved (systems/tally.ts); time counts only while the world steps.
- Playtest round 12 (credits and notices): an in-game credits page (data/credits.ts, ui/credits.ts), rolling by itself, from the title, the pause menu and after an ending. It names Lovecraft and his revision collaborators, the eight Freesound recordists (CC0; every file is credited in public/audio/CREDITS.md), three.js and Electron with their licences, and the title theme by name only: its author and licence are unknown, and must be settled before release.
- Playtest round 12 (the Seventy Steps; a 30-second walk through nine empty rooms, and the priests silent): the stair now says itself as it goes. Rooms of the legacy dungeons may have `words`, said as a title the first time they are entered after a rest or death (systems/roomWords.ts): the Seventy Steps, the Cavern of Flame, the Seven Hundred Steps, their two, four and six hundredth, and the Gate. The Dream-Quest's lesser things wait on the flights (zoogs, night-gaunts, ghasts), and the seven hundred steps run six flights instead of four. The priests speak (see above).
- Playtest round 12 (found while checking the stair): a dreamer arrived in the Stairs of Slumber facing the threshold's back wall. A sealed entrance's "outside" side is the one its stair leaves from, so its axis pointed away. The arrival now faces the first room that opens off it.
- Playtest round 12 (environments: every legacy dungeon was one stone-brick kit): each dungeon and lair has a kit (data/kits.ts) of wall and floor textures, a tone, a trim, and the share of pilasters bearing a flame. Wood and dark timber posts for the Witch House, Akeley's farm and the lairs' houses; brick and wood for the Library; rough rock and mud, untrimmed, for Sentinel Hill; drowned rot for Y'ha-nthlei; pale slab for the Elder Thing City; basalt for the Archives; blue-grey stone for Tsath; warm, flame-lit stone for the Stairs of Slumber; onyx for Ulthar to Kadath; green-black slab for R'lyeh; flesh and rot for the Fungoid Cities; near-black slab for the Void. Only the look changes; the shapes stay the kit's (world/dungeonParts.ts).
- Playtest round 12 (environments: the realms were plains of the shared props, with no landmark to know them by, and K'n-yan had New England houses and street lamps): six landmark kinds (render/landmarkShapes.ts), each a region-plan feature of one great landmark with two or three lesser ones about it. R'lyeh has leaning cyclopean spires of slab on slab, each set off the one below; Yuggoth black windowless towers in terraces; the Mountains of Madness the Elder Things' cones on star-shaped footings; K'n-yan stepped pyramids; Pnakotus the Great Race's half-sunk basalt blocks; the Beyond globes hanging in the air, lit purple and magenta. K'n-yan lost its town and its lamps, and its road is slab. Foes gather about some landmarks, well clear of their bulk.
- Playtest round 12 (found while placing the landmarks): a foe could be posted in a rock. The spawn planner filed each prop's collider only under the 8 m cells its bounds touch, then checked the one cell a foe stood in, with a 0.8 m margin, so a rock just over a cell's edge was missed. Colliders are now filed under every cell their margin reaches.
- Playtest round 12 (environments: no Kadath, no cyclopean R'lyeh, no Yuggoth towers to see): the far realms have a skyline (data/skylines.ts, render/skyline.ts): unknown Kadath's peak with its castle of towers north of the Dreamlands, the Mountains of Madness with cubes on their heights, R'lyeh's city of leaning spires, Yuggoth's towers, the Round Hills with stones on the highest, and the Green Mountains. The far plane is 90 m, so each is a flat silhouette drawn just inside it after the sky, a shade darker than the fog, at the bearing of where it truly stands and at the angle it would fill from there. It fades with the realm's sky and is hidden within a dungeon's walls.
- Playtest round 12 (the Witch House was six rooms): it gains a cellar stair and vault below the hallway, where the rats come from (the vault keeps an Echo cache), and the lodgers' passage off the landing, nine rooms in all.
- Playtest round 12 (Steam readiness: saves): three save slots (systems/save.ts). The first is under the old key, so an earlier save is slot 1's, and the slot in use is remembered. The title's New game chooses a slot (asking before one is overwritten) and Load lists each slot's place, level, time in the dream and journey. In the desktop shell the storage is files in the user's data folder, `saves/<key>.json`, written whole or not at all, where Steam Cloud can keep them. They are served to the page by synchronous IPC, as the Web Storage API it stands in for is synchronous, and what the shell's browser storage held before is taken over on first reading.
- Playtest round 12 (Steam readiness: the shell): the window keeps its size, place, maximised state and fullscreen between runs (window.json, dropped if it no longer falls on a screen). It wears an icon drawn in code, the Elder Sign's five strokes in bone on near-black with a Cosmic Purple glow (desktop/icon.js, PNG-encoded with node's zlib); `npm run icon` writes it to build/icon.png (ignored by git) for packaging. The version is 0.12.0, and the README says what the game is and how to run and play it.
- Playtest round 12 (Steam readiness: achievements): 21 of them (data/achievements.ts), earned from the dream's own state once a second, so a loaded save earns what it holds. They are kept with the records beyond any save, announced on the HUD, listed on a pause-menu page, and passed to the shell (`desktop:achieve`), which answers false until Steamworks is carried.
- Playtest round 12 (Steam readiness: not done, for the owner to decide): packaging (electron-builder or Electron Forge) and Steamworks (e.g. steamworks.js, for achievements, cloud and the overlay) are both new dependencies, which the rules say to ask about first. The title theme's author and licence are unknown (THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md says so). The UI's strings stay inline: no second language is planned.
- Playtest round 12 (licences): THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md carries the three.js and Electron licences, says that a package must keep Electron's LICENSE and LICENSES.chromium.html beside the executable, and points to the recorded sounds' CC0 credits.
- Playtest round 13 (the pad in the desktop shell): Chromium can list a phantom device (a virtual pad, a headset's buttons) before the real pad, and both the game and the menus read the first connected one. They now read the standard-mapped pad used most recently, else any pad used most recently (`core/pads.ts`).
- Playtest round 13 (Start, then B to leave the pause menu, and the investigator stepped back): the game polled nothing while the menu was open, so the B that closed it arrived as a fresh press of dodge (a backstep standing still). A menu closing now mutes the pad's buttons held at that moment until they are let go, so neither B nor A reaches the game.
- Playtest round 13 (resolution): the internal resolution starts at its most (2 × 400 × 225); settings kept before this round are raised to it once (a version in the stored settings), and a later choice is kept. The desktop shell's first run opens maximised and fullscreen; after that it keeps the window as it was left.
- Playtest round 13 (the minimap turned the wrong way): the map was the world's mirror. The world has +z north and the camera's right at (−cos yaw, sin yaw), so facing north +x lies on the left; the map drew +x on the right. The map, the minimap, travel picks, the lead's mark and panning now draw +x on the left (`screenX`), the regions' art mirrored to match, so what lies to the right lies to the right on the map, and the minimap turns with the camera.
- Playtest round 13 (the HUD half on the letterbox): on a screen taller than 16:9 the HUD's layer is the picture's own box (`PICTURE_LAYER`, CSS variables set with the UI scale), and the UI scale follows the picture's height. The bars stay; the HUD sits inside the picture.
- Playtest round 13 (the investigator dithered away beside a short foe, round 12): removed; it broke immersion. The lock-on camera's lower tilt limit (round 12) stays.
- Playtest round 13 (window lights hung before the window): a lit window's point light still stands half a metre out (it lights the wall and the ground before it), but its glow sits on the glass, smaller (0.55 m), and fades as the window is seen edge on and from behind.
- Playtest round 13 (the heavy blows looked silly): the heavies are key poses (`render/heavySwings.ts`): a two-handed overhead cleave with the blade dropped behind the head, a flat forehand or a backhand from over the left shoulder (the arc says which), a fencer's lunge, and a crouched whirl. The weapon hand is placed by a two-bone reach (`render/armReach.ts`) with the blade's way and the elbow's pole; the off hand joins the grip for the cleave. The axe's second heavy is now a forehand (its arc flipped). The light blows keep their swings.
- Playtest round 13 (dungeons: no ceilings, so every dungeon read as a roofless ruin, and every one dripped; wooden floors in the Mi-Go's tunnels, a church and the library): a kit now says its roof (a vault of its stone, boards on dark beams, or the sky), its sound and its outside. Roofed rooms get a solid ceiling at their walls' tops (the lens stays under it) and close the sky; the ruins (Sentinel Hill, the Elder Thing City, Tsath, Ulthar to Kadath, R'lyeh, the Mi-Go cities, the Ultimate Void) keep the sky, their walls broken off at uneven heights, and the region's own ambience. Inside, the ambience follows the room's kit: stone and drips below ground, boards and wind in a house, water and gurgling in the drowned places.
- Playtest round 13 (room kits): a room may name its own kit (Akeley's Mi-Go tunnels are a mine of rock and mud), and a room sunk below the entrance takes its dungeon kit's `below` (a house's cellar is brick and earth, the library's and the church's depths a crypt). The library and the church are floored in stone flags; boards stay only in houses. Muñoz's rooms are a townhouse, the Shunned House a cellar, the sculptor's tower marble.
- Playtest round 13 (dungeons sat in the map as bare mazes): outside, a mound kit heaps lumpy rock over its roofs and steep earth against its outer walls, leaving only the mouth; a 2 m band outside those walls stops feet (the heap is steep) and keeps foes planned in the open clear of it. A building kit raises a pitched roof over each room at ground level or above (its cellars are heaped over). Ruins stand bare.
- Playtest round 13 (dungeons: bigger, with more foes, secrets and ways): every legacy dungeon now runs 10 to 17 rooms (was 4 to 10), with side branches, hidden rooms behind insight or madness, and new tomes to find (data/documents.ts: the Liber Ivonis, Gilman's dream-diary, Old Whateley's ledger, Obed Marsh's log and others). Every lair runs 6 to 9 rooms (was 2 to 4).
- Playtest round 13 (dungeons sat in the map, easy to find): roads no longer run up to every legacy dungeon's door (only to signs, gates, towns and borders), and a thicket of the biome's trees and rocks rings each dungeon in two loose rows, leaving its mouth open. A dungeon is come upon, or found by the lead's mark. The Old Man's house moved 50 m to sit clear of its neighbours.
- Playtest round 13 (found while doing so): a street lamp could stand on an Elder Sign's pad, because the occupancy cells are coarser than a lamp. Lamps now keep a metre clear of every site's pad.
- Playtest round 13 (boss arenas alike, some too small): an open arena is at least 24 m in radius (was as small as 10), and each boss's ground is dressed by `data/arenaStyles.ts` from the world's prop kinds (no new meshes): the heath's dead trees about the Gardners' well, monoliths and an altar with braziers for the sabbat, the black grove's close trees and fires for Shub-Niggurath, R'lyeh's leaning spires, Carcosa's towers and a tall obelisk, Yog-Sothoth's globes, cones about the Court of Azathoth. The ring stands outside the fight's circle and is thinned so a 2.5 m way through always remains; the heart stands to the far side of the boss. Every dungeon boss room is a wide hall (3 × 3 cells) with its style's heart and up to four corner braziers; the Haunter's has none (it lives in the dark). Foes posted by an arena keep clear of its dressing too.
- Playtest round 13 (self-playtest, headless): walked the start, fought, died, opened the map and pause, visited seven arenas and four boss halls, filmed both heavy blows close up (they read: the arm gathers overhead, the body turns into the strike), and measured draw calls (60 to 100 in the heaviest views). No page errors. The one risk worth a test: the new thickets could shut a dungeon in, so a flood fill now proves every door has a way out past them. Open, left as is: on screens wider than 16:9 the picture is pillarboxed, since its low resolution is fixed at 16:9.
- Playtest round 14 (self-playtest, kept short at the user's request; "make it award-winning": juice, immersion, retention, UI, font, sound): the great moments are drawn in the period face across a dark band that fades in while the words slowly widen (`ui/banner.ts`): UNMADE in dark red above the veil (the fall now shows 2.6 s before the dark comes, where it had 0.9 s and the words were lost under the veil within a second; they fade as it falls), HORROR VANQUISHED in gold over the horror's name, and a region's or place's name. The HUD's words are the period face too, with even-width figures, and so are the map's names and the veil's. Foes' and bosses' bars show the damage a run of blows adds up to (held 1.5 s after the last, then fading) and a pale chip that lingers 0.45 s before draining (`ui/damageTally.ts`). Below 30% health the heart is heard and the screen's whole edge pulses red with it, faster below 15% (a recipe, `STINGERS.heartbeat`; no new recording). Under the veil a line is read beneath the place's name: Lovecraft's own words, from stories published by 1928, or one of the dream's lessons (`data/loreLines.ts`). A journey taken up again from a save first says where the story had led, in the hints box.
- Playtest round 15 ("continue": the two gaps round 14 left, animation and story): resting at an Elder Sign, the investigator goes down on one knee facing the stone, head bowed, forearms on the raised knee, eased in and out over about a third of a second, until they move or act (`Pilot.kneeling`, set by `rest` and cleared by any input, as `listening` is; the pose is `kneel` in `render/poses.ts`). Each of the 48 great horrors (the named, the Great Old Ones, the Outer Gods) has an epitaph of its own, read in italics under HORROR VANQUISHED and its name (`data/epitaphs.ts`; the banner now holds 5 s); a boss version of a common creature has none. The sprite atlas's slicing test builds two whole atlases and ran past Vitest's 5 s limit whenever the suite ran in parallel; it has 20 s.
- Playtest round 16 (feedback: "add volumetric fog everywhere it fits, to increase immersion dramatically"): the post pass now marches each pixel's ray, from the lens to what it struck (the low-res target gained a depth texture) or 48 m, through a mist that pools about the investigator's ground (eased as they climb or descend) and thins with height, gathered in drifts of two octaves of slow value noise carried by each place's wind (`render/shaders/fog.ts`, `render/volumetricFog.ts`). Ten samples, their start jittered by the picture's own 4×4 ordered dither; past 48 m the old far fog takes over. The mist is lit by the moon's grey, and the lantern and the twelve nearest lamps glow in it in closed form along the whole ray (the integral of 1/(1 + k·r²), windowed to each light's reach and dimmed by the mist before it), since ten samples would miss a lamp's halo; a lamp behind a wall can glow a little through it, which reads as light spilling round. Each region has its own mist (`data/fogs.ts`): heavy ground fog on the Blasted Heath and the moon-bog, sea fog off Innsmouth and R'lyeh, valley fog in Dunwich, blowing snow on the plateau, dust at Pnakotus, K'n-yan's blue murk, violet dream-mist, Yuggoth's spores, the void's haze; a dungeon's roofed rooms a low crypt-mist; the mist turns from one to the next over a few seconds. A failing mind thickens it (up to 1.6× at full stress, capped by the FX setting as every sanity effect is). A Volumetric fog slider in Video settings scales it, 0 turning it off, for slower machines.
- Playtest round 17 (a comprehensive self-playtest, the autopilot of round 12 walking the main line): (1) a key held down through a talk or a menu was lost when it closed (the menu swallows its press, and the input skipped the key's repeats), so the investigator stood still under a held W; repeats now mark a key held (without pressing any button again). (2) E mashed through a talk rested at the Elder Sign behind the speaker (the foes back, the sign's menu open): for 350 ms after any talk or menu closes, E is held back. (3) Sanity: on the quad a scrap with three vermin cost about a point a second, the whole bar in minutes. Auras no longer stack as their sum: the strongest weighs whole, each other 35%; and the lesser tier's weigh 60%. (4) The first main-line boss was a pair from the first blow: Brown Jenkin now waits in the walls, unseen and untouchable, until Keziah is half spent, then comes out with a notice (`joins` on a boss script: a boss hidden until another falls below a share). (5) A barrage's bolts kept landing along one arm on an investigator standing in it (22 bolts, 380 damage, a full bar in seconds): after a bolt lands on the investigator, the same caster's bolts pass for 20 frames, and the barrage's arms turn 17° a burst (was 13°). Two heavy tests (every legacy dungeon's lights; the sprite atlas sliced) run past Vitest's 5 s when the suite runs in parallel on a busy machine; they have 20 s.
- Playtest round 17, second pass (realms, menus, save, ending): every region toured with the fog on, no page errors, 59–98 draw calls; the simulation steps in about 0.5 ms (1.7 ms at worst) with 60 creatures awake. (6) A long menu page with one button (Achievements) opened scrolled to that button at its foot, and a pad could not scroll back up to read the rest: a page first opened is now read from its top, the arrows past its last choice scroll it on before they wrap, and the pad's right stick scrolls it (`menuKit.ts`). (7) The descent, the game's title moment, took a random lore line and lifted as fast as any journey: it now says its own line (`DESCENT_LINE`) and holds until it can be read (a journey given a line falls in 1.6 s and holds at least 3.4 s). (8) The ending card was a silent block of text with full-width buttons (the menu style overrode its own): its words now come out of the dark one after another, its buttons sit centred at their own width, and the title's theme sounds again under it until the investigator walks on. (9) The Arms page's numbers were the last monospace line in the menus: now in the book face with lining figures.
- Playtest round 17 (10): the lead's mark hid a goal in another realm (only the journal's line named the way), so from Keziah's fall to meeting Kuranes, the whole descent, the map and minimap marked nothing; inside the sealed Stairs of Slumber it pointed through the walls; and from the dream the seals' horrors of the waking world went unmarked. A goal in another place is now marked where the way to it begins (`systems/leadWay.ts`): a gate, or the stair at the Sleeper's Sign once it opens, on the fewest crossings, the nearest first (a sealed dungeon counts as a place of its own, left by its gate); else a lit Elder Sign where the investigator stands, since a sign's travel crosses realms. The seals' lead prefers a horror in the investigator's own realm.
- Playtest round 17 (11): down in the dream, the descent's line still read "Rest at the Sleeper's Sign … and go down the stair", as if the investigator were still above; it now reads well on either side of the stair (find Kuranes: down the stair from the Sleeper's Sign, and on through the Gate of Deeper Slumber). The quest keeps its one stage, so saves keep their place in it. The stairs themselves played well under the autopilot: the flights' zoogs, night-gaunts and ghasts, the priests fighting beside the investigator, the Cavern of Flame's sign and its achievement.
- Playtest round 18 ("make the whole open world way more lively and fun to explore; playtest it and find out what I mean"): walked Arkham, Dunwich and Innsmouth out from their Elder Signs. What made the world feel dead: (a) nothing moved of itself: the 60 to 90 creatures of each region stood frozen at their posts, turning their heads now and then, until they saw the investigator (the brain's idle branch; the tuning's `wander`, "an idle creature strays this far from its post", was never used); (b) the world's sounds (owls, dogs, whippoorwills, gulls, thunder) had nothing to be seen making them; (c) between the towns and features lay dark, empty ground, and the things worth finding were small and dark, noticed only underfoot; (d) places had no names, so exploring added up to nothing. First, (a): idle in the open world, a foe now makes its rounds (`systems/roam.ts`): it ambles at about a third of its pace to a spot within its archetype's `roam` of its post (12 m for pack hunters, 14 for swoopers, 6 for brutes; 0 for ambushers, burrowers, invisible stalkers, stationary horrors, bosses and allies), lingers 3–9 s looking about, and ambles on; a blocked stroll ends in good time. It still notices what it sees on the way, and hunts, searches and goes home as before. Outside the open world (the arena, the tests' scripted foes) a foe keeps its post.
- Playtest round 18 (b): the world's small lives, drawn over the simulation and touching nothing in it (`data/fauna.ts` which and where, `world/haunts.ts` each chunk's critters placed on its props, `render/critterLife.ts` their ways, `render/sprites/critters.ts` 16-pixel frames, `render/fauna.ts` one instanced billboard batch plus a glow batch: two draw calls). Crows on dead trees, graves, crosses and obelisks (Arkham, the hub, Providence, Vermont's woods), whippoorwills in Dunwich, gulls on Innsmouth's roofs and ruins (and a few over R'lyeh), bats looping about trees and ruins (K'n-yan's pillars too), rats running about the feet of houses and walls, moths circling every street lamp, fireflies drifting and blinking over bushes and stumps, and Ulthar's cats, black or tabby, on the Dreamlands' walls with eyes that catch the lantern. A bird takes wing at the investigator's approach (8 m for a crow, twice as far at a run), from a shot within 32 m or a blow within 10, flies off climbing and banking until the dark has it, and comes back to its perch once the investigator is well away; rats bolt into a crack, cats slink off; the first of a startled flock cries (a new synthesized `caw`; the recorded wings, squeaks and yowls for the rest). They are drawn larger than life (a crow 0.8 m), as the creatures are: at true size the dark and the fog swallowed them. The mountains, the desert, Yuggoth and the Beyond keep their silence. Critters live only in the 3 × 3 chunks about the investigator (at most 20 a chunk) and are drawn within 70 m; none under a dungeon's roof.
- Playtest round 18 (c): the sky and the finding. Now and then a flock of the region's birds (crows over Arkham, the hub, Providence and Vermont, whippoorwills gathering over Dunwich, gulls over Innsmouth and R'lyeh) or one of the realm's great winged things (Mi-Go over Vermont and Yuggoth, night-gaunts and, rarely, a shantak over the Dreamlands: their roster sprites, sharing the creatures' atlas texture rather than a second 15 MB copy) comes out of the dark, crosses low over the investigator, 5 to 18 m up and 10 to 24 m off at its nearest so it passes before the horizon's haze where the camera sees it, and is gone, crying as it passes nearest (`render/skyLife.ts`, `SKY_VISITORS` in `data/fauna.ts`). Fliers take only 0.4 of the world's fog: fogged fully, a bird against the dark sky was a pale fleck like a star, not a dark shape. Lightning rolls over Dunwich, Innsmouth, R'lyeh and the mountains (`render/lightning.ts`): three quick pulses over half a second light the land, the sky's haze and the mist nearly white for an instant (added after the night's light is set each frame, so nothing accumulates), and the thunder follows 0.6–3.2 s later, quieter and duller the farther the strike; the ambience's own unsynced thunder spots in Dunwich and R'lyeh are gone. Under a dungeon's roof only the thunder is heard. Things worth finding (tomes, notes, caches, vials, arms not yet taken) wear a faint warm glimmer that breathes, with now and then a brighter twinkle, seen through the fog from 32 m (`render/glints.ts`): they had been small and dark and found only underfoot. All three hang off `render/worldLife.ts`, one line in the frame loop, updated after `lightReality`.
- Playtest round 18 (d): the world's lesser places have names, and finding them counts. Every feature of every region's plan (about 240: groves, graveyards, stone rings, ruins, outcrops, camps, the far realms' landmarks) is named in its realm's own words, "The {adjective} {noun}" (New England's Hanging Copse and Shunned Mill, Innsmouth's Tide-Worn Barn, the plateau's Frozen Scarp, R'lyeh's Wrong-Angled Portal, the Beyond's Endless Throne), the stories' names given first (Christchurch Cemetery and Nahum Gardner's Farm in Arkham, the Devil's Hop Yard in Dunwich, the Marsh Refinery in Innsmouth, Swan Point in Providence, the Zoogs' Ring in the Dreamlands); regions that share a realm's words are named together, in order, so no name repeats across them (`data/placeNames.ts`, `world/namedPlaces.ts`). Stepping within four fifths of a place's footprint for the first time finds it (`systems/places.ts`, a few looks a second): the banner names it with "N OF M PLACES · REGION", a lower, softer Elder Sign chime sounds, it is saved (`places`, optional, so older saves load), the map marks and names the places found and counts them in its heading, the ending card counts them, and fifty found earns a new achievement, "Searcher After Horror".
- Playtest round 18 (e): lit windows are lived behind. A town's 30% of lit windows glowed on and on and nothing stirred behind them; now each lit pane has a seed (from where it sits on its house, so the houses' own random draws are left as they were) and by it about two in five are put out for a spell every minute or few and lit again, some dim for a moment every half minute as someone passes the lamp inside, and half waver a little as a candle does (`render/paneLife.ts`). The rule is written twice and kept alike: in TypeScript for the window's point light and halo (`worldLights.ts`), and as GLSL for its glass, which now has a material of its own (`pane`, a world material with the PANES define and an `aPane` attribute), so glass, halo and the light on the street go out together.
- Playtest round 19 ("some textures glitch out; some look extremely weird, you can see through them or see the 2D", with screenshots and a recording): (a) the far silhouettes (`render/skyline.ts`: the Mountains' range, Kadath, Yuggoth's towers, the Round Hills) were transparents with no depth test, so they were drawn after the whole world and painted over it, the investigator and the houses too, the fog ghosting what they hid (the huge flat dark shape of the first screenshot, and the dark wedge over the investigator's legs). They are now drawn with the opaque, their render order putting them after the sky and before the world, blended over the sky by hand. Judgment calls: each is held to at most 34° of sky (`SKY.farAngle`; Kadath rose 50° over the Dreamlands' north edge, a black wall), and its foot fades into the haze (`SKY.farFoot`), so no hard straight line runs along the horizon.
- Playtest round 19 (b, "glitching" textures): faces lying within a couple of centimetres over others and facing the same way, which the PS1's snapping (half a low-res pixel, a depth error that grows as a face is seen aslant) flipped in front and behind as the view turned: a pit's walls rose flush with the floor slabs' own faces along its rim (the recording: dark jagged triangles along the Library's charnel pit), a window's glass stood 1.5 cm before a slab of frame and 2 cm behind its mullions (whole windows flicked lit and dark from across a street), the Elder Sign's strokes 2.7 cm proud with their glow 0.8 cm before them, the glyph bands on the Mu monolith and the hidden layer's stones 1.5–2 cm proud, and a bridge's deck laid flush over the floors' edges. Now: the pit's walls stop under the slabs; the window's frame stands about the glass (nothing behind it but the wall, 7 cm back) with its mullions 7 cm before it; the strokes stand 5.5 cm proud and their glow is drawn with a polygon offset; the bands stand 5 cm proud; the planks lie 5 cm over the stone, and a hidden bridge runs brink to brink. `tests/depthFights.test.ts` holds the rule: nothing lies within 5 cm over another face, facing the same way.
- Playtest round 19 (c, "see through them"): a dungeon well's rim was an open cylinder with no inner face, so over its far side one saw the floor beyond and the Elder Sign's rune ring (screenshot: the Library Archive). It has its inner face now, from the lip down to the water.
- Playtest round 19 (d, the giant crossing sheets): a mound-shelled dungeon's earth banks (`render/dungeonShell.ts`) ran two metres straight on past every wall's end, so at each corner two banks crossed in the air like a tent's flaps (seen close, flat sheets with paper-thin edges), along a row of rooms each bank lay over the next, and beside the way in the bank's end was a bare sheet. `world/moundBanks.ts` now says how each end meets what lies past it: a corner the dungeon ends at (one of its two walls sweeps the bank round it, in four steps), the next wall in line (the banks meet at the cells' edge), or a doorway or another room's wall (the bank stops, its end closed down to the ground). The earth over the roofs meets the banks' brim level (it stood up to half a metre off it), and the unseen band that stops feet now covers the corners too.
- Playtest round 19 (e, "the flying green orb doesn't attack me; check the other enemies"): the orb is the Light-Being from Algol, an ally (it follows the investigator and fights what threatens them), but nothing said so. An ally falling in beside the investigator is now announced once ("… · AT YOUR SIDE") and wears a name tag marked ✦ with a sea-coloured bar; the HUD's notice line now holds each notice 0.7 s before the next takes its place (the ally's was overwritten by the first-sight line in the same frame). The audit the user asked for (every hostile roster creature before an investigator standing still, `tests/foesStrike.test.ts`) found two real faults: skirmishers hold a 2.5–5 m ring, and those whose blows reach less (the Innsmouth hybrid, the zoog, the K'n-yan dweller, Brown Jenkin) never struck at all; a foe ready to strike whose blows reach only nearer now darts in (the fall-back after the blow was already there). And a big body's melee (its reach grown with its size) swept only an arc far out, so one who stood close to Hastur, Cthulhu or Father Dagon was never touched; a blow reaching past 2.5 m (`COMBAT.limb`) now sweeps all it passes over from the body out. Judgment call: the bosses hit harder up close than before (that spot was a safe dead zone). By design and exempted in the test: the albino penguin (harmless), the being from beyond (seen only below 15 sanity), and the moon-bog wraith, the voice in the tomb, Azathoth and the Ancient Ones, which work on the mind at that range.
- Playtest round 20 (bugs, "when kneeling at a sign, the walking animation is broken afterwards until you restart"): `kneel` in `render/poses.ts` assigned the legs' and arms' rotations outright, scaled by the eased kneel; the ease in `actorViews` only approached 0 and never reached it, so after rising a hair of kneel (10⁻³ and less) still overwrote the stride's legs and arms every frame. The pose now eases each joint toward the kneeling one by the kneel (`toward`) instead, and the view snaps the kneel to 0 once it is up and under 0.004. Two pose-level tests (`tests/kneel.test.ts`) fail on the old code.
- Playtest round 20 (bugs, "going back to the title the music doesn't play"): after Quit to title the page reloads, and the theme's `<audio>` may play at once while the WebAudio context (its route to the speakers) is still suspended, so the menu opened over silence and never asked for a key. `music.ts` now counts the theme as sounding only once the context is running (it waits `THEME.wake`, 0.35 s, for it to wake, else pauses the element and asks for the key as with a refused autoplay). The exact browser state on the player's machine is inferred; the four cases are in `tests/menuMusic.test.ts`.
- Playtest round 20 (bugs, "the Mi-Go sometimes does the gun sound"): the `Shot` event, emitted by the revolver and by any creature's beam (`move.shot`), always played the revolver's bang. It now plays the bang only for the investigator's shot and a new `beam` stinger (a falling, warbling whine and a hiss, no crack) for a creature's (`render/audio/cues.ts`, `data/sounds.ts`).
- Playtest round 20 (bugs, "some resting signs are halfway in a wall"): 16 of 41 signs, all in dungeons, had their shrine reaching into the rooms' walls: the ring of lesser stones (3 m out behind the stone) ran into the entry wall of a 16 m hall (the sign stood 2.5 m from it), the plinth stood 0.4 m in the lane wall of every corridor, and a pit's ledge or a well room's corner cannot hold a ring at all. (a) A hall's and a well room's sign moved 1.5 m into the room, its rest point with it (`roomSpots`); a corridor's plinth now stands 10 cm off the lane's wall. (b) The shrine adapts to its place: a lesser stone that would stand within 40 cm of a wall, a pillar, the chasm's edge or a doorway's opening (`doorwayClear`: no collision, but nothing is set in one) is left out (`standingStones`, kept on the sign as `stones`, in its colliders and in its model: `shrineGeometry(stones)`, one merge per distinct set). The ring is broken anyway, so a shrine set against a wall keeps the stones that flank it; a corridor's has none. The open world's 25 signs keep all five. `tests/shrine.test.ts` scans every sign against every collider about it (props included) and the doorways.
- Playtest round 20 (found while doing so): moving the halls' rest points shifted the roads planned to them, and a critter's perch in Dunwich came out 8.7 cm above the ground beside a low wall, failing the fauna test's "up on its prop" rule. A perching critter is now placed only where its prop's top stands 12 cm above the ground there (`haunts.ts`); nothing else in the stream of draws changes.
- Playtest round 20 ("finding a resting seal or a new place sounds too arcadey"): the sign's `found` was a run of bright triangle chimes (E5, B5, E6) and a place found played it lower. A sign found now tolls a great church bell (a CC0 recording, two tolls cut and slowed to a low 130 and 218 Hz; `bell`), and a place found sounds a gong from far off (`ghost`); under each the recipe keeps a share (half), rewritten as a bell's inharmonic partials with a beating twin, a sub swell and a breath over stone (`found`), and a distant gong's wash (`place`). Nothing above 600 Hz. The recovered Echoes' arpeggio (`echoes`) is a swell and a breath now too.
- Playtest round 20 ("Echoes should drop from foes and float to the player"): the sim still pays a bounty at once (saves, tests, levels), so the drama is render-only (`render/echoFx.ts`, `echoPath.ts`, `echoLook.ts`; numbers in `data/fxTuning.ts`). A foe the investigator kills lets its bounty go as 3 to 12 wisps (more for each 1.6 doublings) of Void Green, the colour of the dropped shard, with a bone-white heart: they unspool upward in a slow circle, hang and bob a moment, then are drawn to the chest along a spiral that tightens and quickens (2.5 to 13 m/s, faster than a sprint, and taken in anyway after 4.5 s or on a long journey), leaving a trail of green-purple motes. Each carries a share of the Echoes; the HUD counter shows the count less what is in flight (`createHud(…, held)`), so it climbs as they land; each landing is a breath drawn in (`absorb`, each a little higher) and the release is a soft exhale over a recorded whisper. Echoes recovered from where the investigator fell stream up out of the drop the same way; dying or resting clears the flight.
- Playtest round 20 ("more impact on the player's attacks, visually and audible"): a landed blow now has a weight (0.5 to 1.4: its damage, a heavy chain's, a stagger, a riposte, a kill) that drives every part. Audible (`render/audio/impact.ts`, a pure table): flesh always (5 takes of a CC0 "blunt force trauma" library, deeper for a bigger body), a smack over a hard blow or an axe's bite, bone and a wet burst for a kill or a riposte, a low boom for a great foe's fall or a crushing heavy; the layers start a few milliseconds apart (the sampler gained `delay`). Seen (`render/impactFx.ts`): a flare at the wound, streaks of sparks flung all about it (three discs along the flight), clots of ichor, a ring of light on a kill or riposte; the camera is punched toward the blow and shaken for a fifth of a second (scaled by the Screen shake setting). The sim hangs a killing blow or a riposte of the investigator's 3 frames longer (`IMPACT.finisher`).
- Playtest round 20 (recordings, sourced from Freesound by searching its CC0 filter, as in round 6; each page's licence checked): Aeonemi's "Creepy church bell" (`bell1-2`), electra's "ghost gong 2" (`ghost1`); TheFilmLook's "Blunt Force Trauma (Clean and Juicy)" (`flesh1-5`), BarryTheWhite's "Smacks And Thwacks" (`smack1-3`), Gammelsmurfen778's "Bones Breaking 1 and 3" (`crunch1-4`, slowed ×0.85), gprosser's "splat" and "splat2" and ThefitzyG's "goreSplat" (`splat1-3`), harrisonlace's "trailer boom impact" and phluidbox's "super low bass hit" (`boom1-2`), Evcy's "Axe Chop" and birdswkaren's "Axe Cut #9" (`chop1-2`). All peak-normalised with headroom for the MP3's overshoot (–3 to –4 dB before encoding), credited in `public/audio/CREDITS.md`.
- Playtest round 20 ("make the name Seventy Steps more appealing and stylized on the title menu, thematically fitting"): it was 28 px of spaced capitals. The name is now a wordmark of coarse pixels drawn in code, two screen pixels each (`ui/logo.ts`, `logoPlan.ts` the timing and geometry, `logoArt.ts` the drawing): SEVENTY over a larger STEPS cut in the period face's capitals as stone (a lit lip along the top and left, a dark one below and right, grain, pits, chipped edges, a shadow and a purple halo), above a flight of seventy treads running down and in to a point in the dark, as the seventy steps of light slumber do in the Dream-Quest, and a small Elder Sign crowning it all. When the title opens (with the veil's purple drawing back to the edges) a light sets out down the flight, lighting each tread as it passes (4.6 s), the letters take fire one after another with a flash as it goes, the sign comes alight last and motes climb the flight out of the dark; every 9 s the letters shudder, their colours parting. Everything is a function of the seconds since it opened, made once and kept (a page of the title built again takes the same canvas, so the descent is not played twice); the lines under it fade in after the descent; the menu is there from the first moment. `tests/logo.test.ts` checks the plan (the treads, the light's descent, the order the letters take fire).
- Playtest round 20 ("different sound effects and design for every enemy; search the web for fitting ones"): a creature's voice was its tier's, so two dozen lesser horrors growled alike, every named one murmured and every great old one sounded the abyss, and a foe made no sound when struck. Now every voiced entry of the roster (`data/creatureSounds.ts`, 95 of them; the night-gaunt, Nodens and the other silent allies stay silent) is its own mix of 40 recorded families: the call it gives as it goes about, another as it turns on the investigator, its cry when struck (about one blow in four goes unanswered, and never twice within 0.8 s) and its dying, all at its own pitch (the great old ones a half to two thirds of the lesser's rate), and its blows may sound their own way. No two enemies share all four cries (`tests/creatureSounds.test.ts` checks it against the roster). The families, sourced from Freesound's CC0 filter and each page's licence checked: Troll (the brutes' grunts), monster growls, dragon roars (the great beasts', slowed), zombie moans, goblin snarls and heavy breathing, monkey and squirrel chatter (the ghouls), a chant and a Latin demon chant (the cults), witch and evil laughs, gargling and a throat gurgle (the deep ones), frogs, a penguin colony of the Antarctic (the shoggoth's Tekeli-li and the Elder Things' piping, slowed), raven, bat, slime and tentacle squelches, deep rumbles (the great old ones), rattlesnakes, ghost wails, screams, eerie choruses (the outer gods), pig squeals and dog yelps and male pain grunts (cries at a blow), monster and male deaths. The pipers, the viol and the polyps' whistle keep their recipes. Attacks: the library's blows now sound over the whoosh where they land (`ATTACK_SOUNDS`: jaws snap, claws rip, slams boom, tentacles lash, a gust for a charge or a dive, a spit, a roar), and a creature may have its own (a shoggoth's are wet, a Mi-Go's grab clicks, a Deep One's lunge snaps); the frame is read off the move (`strikeFrame`).
- Playtest round 20 ("add beautiful cutscenes to everything: intro, endings, bosses"): a scene is data (`data/cutscenes.ts`): a run of shots (a camera that stands off someone at a bearing from the line to the other party, and moves from one stand to another over the shot: yaw, distance, height, what it looks at, field of view, roll, drift) and beats laid over them (a line said in the bottom bar, a fade, a stinger or a recording, a burst of ash, motes, embers or stars, a tremor, the investigator rising). `render/cinemaPlan.ts` is the pure camera and timing; `render/cinema.ts` plays it: it takes the lens over from the follow camera (blended in and out, the same pose `placeCamera` has just made), pulls the lens in from anything between it and its subject, and either stops the world (`frozen`) or runs it at 0.28 of its pace (`slow`, the world's steps and its interpolation both metered, checked at 0.285). `ui/cinemaUi.ts` lays bars, fades, lines and the horror's name over it (under the pause menu, the HUD hidden). Scenes: the wake of a new game (the investigator on one knee before the sign they wake at, the bell tolls, three lines, they rise); a horror's arrival, the first time it turns on the investigator only (`overworld.watched`, saved; the arena test keeps it for the page) in one of four treatments by height (person under 3.2 m, large under 8, giant under 18, colossal), each three shots (a stand off its line, a close-up on its face, the view over the investigator's shoulder), with its name and a line of its story (`data/bossCards.ts`); a horror's fall, slowed, with a white flash and a circle about the body (not for the Dunwich Horror, unseen, nor Brown Jenkin, who joins another's fight); and the three endings, each ending on black, the card coming up out of it (`endingCard` no longer hears `Ending` itself: `render/cinemaDirector.ts` plays the scene and then opens the card, and opens it at once if the scene is off). Esc, Enter, Space, the interact key, the pad's A, B or Start, or the mouse being let go skip it after 0.6 s (fading to black first; Esc is heard before the pause menu, which is held off while a scene plays: `held`); Settings › Controls › Cutscenes turns them all off. The intro's cards bring their lines out of the dark one after another. Ambiguity: the hint that a key skips shows only after 1.2 s, and the sim is frozen rather than slowed for arrivals so that nothing strikes the investigator while the camera is away.
- Playtest round 21 ("there are still texture glitches, make sure there are none throughout the whole game", a recording of the University Library's brick hall): flat near-black wedges lay across its walls about two metres up, changing shape as the camera moved. Cause: `ceiling()` (`world/dungeonParts.ts`) gave each roofed room a ceiling slab reaching the walls' outer faces, so the library's cellar rooms, which stand beside its upstairs rooms, had dark stone slabs (5.06 to 5.56 m) lying in the plane of the upstairs walls' inner faces (z = 343.5 in the periodicals and the restricted stacks), and the PS1's snapping, which moves a face's depth by more than 12 cm on a wall seen aslant, flipped stone and brick in front of one another. A slab now reaches the walls' middles. Found and kept found by a new kind of test: view rays (`tests/rayAudit.ts`, `dungeonView.test.ts`) leave open spots in every room of every dungeon; a ray may not meet the back of a face first (seen through), leave a roofed room where a wall should be, or meet a face with another of another look facing the same way within 12 cm behind it. The same audit found, and this round fixed: (a) a mound bank's closed end lay on the inner face of the next room's wall (new bank end `neighbour`, `world/moundBanks.ts`, buried at the wall's middle); (b) at a corner where walls of two kits met (a timber parlour beside a brick cellar, a farmhouse and its mine tunnel) each ran half a wall past the cell's edge, one over the other, or an end came out on the other's far face: `world/wallJoins.ts` draws the wall that lies within the other's height cut back there (the whole stub in line, to the other's middle across), colliders unchanged; (c) what stands 4 to 15 cm off a wall (a dungeon's plinths, cornices, pilasters, timber beams and bridge decks; a house's frames, glass and door) is drawn nearer than it is by polygon offset (factor -1, units -4: a pixel's slope of depth, however slanted the view; the groups `brick+` etc. in `render/siteMeshes.ts`, the prop materials `trim` and `pane`), as no gap under 12 cm is safe on a wall seen aslant; (d) dungeon boxes take their UVs from where a face is in the world (`meshKit.worldUv`, counted from the dungeon's origin on the texture's grid so the affine mapping keeps its precision), so a pilaster, a lintel and the wall by them show one set of bricks and a box's ends are no longer stretched over its length; (e) a well's lip and water were rings of ten corners a half step off the cylinders they join, ten 12 cm slivers of open air about every well; (f) houses: the roof was one-sided so the eaves were open sky from below (now two-sided), the gable began 20 cm under the wall's top and lay in the wall's own plane over that band (now from the top), and it stood 3% inside the roof's edge, a slit along each slope (now 0.5%); (g) rocks were displaced by a fresh random per vertex, tearing the sphere's seam and poles open (now a scatter of the vertex's place); an altar's stained cloth stood 2 cm proud of the slab (now 7). `tests/objectAudit.ts` and `objectView.test.ts` do the same for props and houses. Accepted, small: a stone-style house's flat lid is hollow under its parapet, and the finest details (a headstone's base, the bars of a fence, a fire pit's ring, a bush) lie under 12 cm of one another; both are left out of `objectView`. A black-box hunt (each mesh drawn as one flat colour with and without vertex snapping, `window.pipeline.grab()`; scratch, not kept) confirmed the wedge and showed that ordinary wobble of a near face flips a strip along its edge, which is not a glitch. Debug additions: `window.scene`, `camera`, `pipeline` (`grab()`), and texture names on the world materials.
- Playtest round 22 (feedback: the wake's rise was too quick to be cinematic, and its yellow title should go): the "THE SLEEPERS" title beat is gone from the wake (`WAKE`, `data/cutscenes.ts`; the caption lines stay). A `rise` beat now carries the seconds the get-up takes (`WAKE_RISE`, 3.4; `cinema.ts` hands them to `ActorViews.rise`, which drives the kneel's amount by that clock instead of the exponential ease of play, whatever the frame rate), the wake's three shots crane up with it (low behind the kneeling figure, up and back as it rises, then a slow settle), and the kneel pose gives its parts stretches of their own (`poses.ts`): the head lifts first, the hands rest on the knee to half way, the legs and body unfold the whole way (together, so the feet stay down). A wake skipped before the beat lets them up at once under the fade. Resting at a sign still rises as fast as one moves.
- Playtest round 22 (feedback: the title music does not loop well): the leap (a jump from 1.1 s before the end to 3.2 s, under a 30 ms dip either side) was heard; measured offline, the level jumped by 5 dB in a tenth of a second and the spectral centroid went from 200 to 540 Hz. The track ends on its last hit (202.76 s) ringing on a low C and opens on a lead-in swelling on the same C to its first hit (3.85 s), so a pass now gives way to the next by an equal-power crossfade of 3.6 s (`THEME.overlap`; `render/audio/themeLoop.ts`): the ending's decay fades out under the next pass's lead-in, whose first hit lands as the fade ends (offline through the real modules: the level runs -20, -22, -23, -26, -29, -29, -27 dB into the hit, and no sample steps more than the music does elsewhere). It plays from the whole track decoded, its passes scheduled on the audio clock three ahead, because the element cannot be crossfaded with itself and a second element was refused in round 9 (Safari). The element plays the first pass as ever (the theme still sounds the moment the browser allows) and the decode (about 75 MB, freed with the title) begins only `THEME.prepare` (30 s) before the ending, so a title left sooner pays nothing; a pass not yet decoded there still leaps, as before. `tests/themeLoop.test.ts`.
- Playtest round 22 (feedback: stamina recovers too quickly and rolling on and on is abused): "levelling the recovery rate" is read as both, a slower start that Endurance raises. Regeneration was 40 a second for everyone with a 36-frame delay, so a roll's 18 came back inside half a second and a chain of rolls at their cancel frame ran to eight seconds. It now starts at 24 a second (`STAMINA.regen`), each Endurance level adds 0.8 (`LEVELS.endurance.regen`; twenty levels return the old 40; `Stamina.regen`, set by `applyLevels`), and the delay after any spending is 48 frames, the length of a roll (`STAMINA.regenDelay`), so a chain of rolls draws on the bar alone: 100 stamina is six rolls, and a bar that empties takes over four seconds to fill at the start. The sign menu says Endurance's level makes it "return faster".
- Playtest round 22 (feedback: the revolver is overpowered because it can be spammed; give it limited ammunition found about the map or at traders, harder hits used wisely, upgrades, and a strong fall-off in accuracy and damage): the revolver has a cylinder of six (`GUN.chamber`) and spare rounds carried (`GUN.carry`, 24; a new investigator has six spare), shown on the HUD as "REVOLVER 6/6 · 6" and on the Arms page. A shot spends a round when it leaves the barrel (`systems/revolver.ts`); pressing the revolver's button on an empty cylinder reloads if there are spare rounds, and clicks (`DryFire`, "NO ROUNDS") if there are none; a new key, V (D-pad left), reloads by choice (`gun.ts`; the `reload` move, 80 frames, loaded on frame 48, walkable at a third of the pace, a blow or a roll before that loses nothing; it is no swallow of Laudanum, `sanity.ts`). Resting at a sign and rising again load the cylinder from the spare rounds for nothing. Rounds are found (four in a box beside each of the ten notes, or by a region's first Elder Sign where it has none; four beside the casket in each dungeon's dead ends, the same rooms as the Echo caches: about 300 in the whole dream; taken for good; a box that will not fit the pocket stays where it lies) and bought (a box of six for 180 Echoes from Dr. Morgan, Curtis Whateley in Dunwich and William Dyer at Lake's Camp, so that a long way from Arkham is not a long way from cartridges; no stock, the pocket is the limit). A shot is thirty up close, where it was seven (`shoot`, `data/moves.ts`), whole to three metres and then falling to a floor of an eighth by fifteen, by the 1.6th power of the way there (a third at nine metres), and the bullet strays in a cone that widens with the range to what it is aimed at (half a degree to three metres, then 0.9° a metre): against the tall placeholder Deep One it hits every time to six metres, seven in ten at nine, a quarter at twelve and one in ten at fifteen, against a small mark (radius 0.2, the height of a Zoog) a third at nine, and from twelve metres a body that keeps under a fifth of its damage; a shot that keeps under half its damage no longer interrupts a wind-up, and its poise falls with it. The odds were measured by running 300 shots at each distance, size and level (`tests/gun.test.ts` keeps their shape). Levels of the gun are bought at an Elder Sign with star-stones, as a weapon's are (one, one, two, two, three; five levels): +15% damage, the fall-off put 1.5 m further off and the scatter a tenth narrower each, so a fully set gun is a mid-range weapon (53 up close, 42 at twelve metres) and still a limited one. Saves keep the cylinder, the spare rounds and the gun's levels (an older save has a full cylinder and six spare), a new journey carries the levels and starts the rounds again; the arena has a full pocket and no boxes.
- Playtest round 22 (feedback: he holds the gun in his hand all the time; the lantern is not attached at its top; the smoke comes out of him): the revolver rides in a holster on the belt, at the front of the left hip outside the coat's skirt, and is in the hand only while it is used (a shot or a reload: `ActorViews` shows the hand's and hides the holstered grip, and back), the reload has a pose of its own (drawn up to the chest, the head bent to it, the hand ticking with each round, a snap of the wrist as the cylinder shuts), and its sounds (the cylinder swung out and six rounds in one by one; a snap; a click). The lantern hangs from the belt by a leather loop on the belt's end, a brass clip and a strap running out to its bail ring (it hangs 9.5 cm lower than it did, so the flame does too). Smoke is put out at the next frame drawn, at the barrel's mouth wherever the arm has it (`ActorViews.muzzle`, the flash's world position), curling forward and up (it was at the ray's start, in the chest), and the tracer begins there too.
- Playtest round 22 (feedback: sanity should restore slowly when not fighting, faster in lit places, and a very low sanity should not distort the screen so much but have other fitting effects): the mind mends by itself (`sanity.ts`, numbers in `SANITY.mend`). It is in a fight for six seconds after a real blow struck or taken against a real foe (`Mind.fought`, set from the `Hit` event: a swing at the air, a shot that misses, a hallucination's blow and a pool's lingering tick are none), and while any foe that has the investigator as its target is engaged or searching within 45 m (aggro); otherwise sanity returns at a quarter of a point a second (a full mind in under seven minutes), and up to 1.6 in the light of a lamp or a fire, in proportion to how well lit the ground is. The light is the world's own (`WorldLights.lightAt`, handed to the simulation as `Game.lit` so the simulation keeps to its rules and imports nothing of the renderer): each lamp, fire, torch and lit window gives what its kind is worth at its foot (1, 1, 0.85, 0.4) falling off to nothing at 0.6 of its range, and the Elder Signs' violet light, an Echo's glow, the lantern in the investigator's own hand and the eldritch effects give none, as asked. It stops while the mind is being drained and while the investigator lies dead; a swallow of Laudanum steadies the mind as before, and it may mend meanwhile. The HUD shows an arrow after the band (▲ mending, ▲▲ by a lamp or fire), and the hint for the mind says how. The picture's warp is capped at 0.4 of the stress (`FX.warpCap`: the ripple, split, swimming walls, breathing lens and shear had grown all the way to madness and the screen tore); past it the picture stops growing more wrong and dread takes over, on a toggle of its own (`dread`, key 0 of `?debug`, which the debug panel's keys now run 1 to 0): the edges of sight darken from a stress of 0.25, breathing slowly, and blur, so the middle stays clear and readable (`render/shaders/post.ts`), pale eyes open at the edge of sight from 0.5 (one, two, then three at madness, opening and closing), the stars of the sky crawl, crowd and flicker (`uWrong`, `render/shaders/sky.ts`), and now and then a whisper is heard at one ear (`gameAudio.whispers`, the recorded whisper: every 22 s at a stress of 0.6, every 9 s at madness, at a quarter to less than half the volume, from one side, nothing else added to the sound). All of it keeps to the accessibility cap (`FX` and `AUDIO` with `sanityStress`). Tests: `tests/sanity.test.ts`, `fx.test.ts`, `worldLights.test.ts`; two older tests that asserted an exact hold on the mind now allow it to mend (`combatFeel`), or say they are in a fight (`gun`).
- Playtest round 23 (feedback on the sanity overhaul: the pale eyes look cheap, the warp was tuned down too far, and the darkened edges should go, or be subtler and not flicker): the eyes are gone (`dread()` in `render/shaders/post.ts`, `FxParams.watch`, `FX.watchFrom`), and so is the darkening of the edges (`tunnel`), which breathed; of what round 22 called dread there stay the soft focus at the edges (`FX.blur`, up to 1.1 low-res pixels, and static) and the wrong stars, both from `FX.dreadFrom` (0.25 of the stress) on. The warp is no longer flat at 0.4 of the stress from there: it grows in step with it to `FX.warpCap` (0.5) and past it `FX.warpSlope` (0.3) as fast (`warpOf`, `render/fx.ts`), so at sanity 0 it is 0.65 of what it was before round 22, at the edge of Unmoored (15) 0.6, and it still grows as the mind goes; the ripple, split, swimming walls, breathing lens and shear all keep to it. The edge of the picture now means one thing: a wound, or near death (below).
- Playtest round 23 (feedback: when Unmoored the investigator should take more damage, do less, recover stamina slower and take damage over time, but not overstated, the game being hard enough): the fourth band, and only it, now costs the body (`SANITY.unmoored`, `SANITY.dealt`/`taken`; `systems/sanity.ts`, `stamina.ts`, `vitals.ts`). The multipliers by band were 1, 1.1, 1.2, 1.35 dealt and 1, 1.1, 1.25, 1.5 taken (a failing mind hit harder and was hit harder, spec §3A), so Unmoored had been the strongest state there was, and was felt as no penalty at all. Uneasy and Fractured keep that bargain; Unmoored now deals 0.85 and takes 1.35 (the taking eased from 1.5 because three more penalties join it: the whole is worse, not more than the game can bear), stamina comes back at 0.65 of its pace (`tickStamina`'s `pace`), and the body wears away by 0.25% of full health a second (0.4 a second of 160): never to death (it stops at the last point), and none while a shot of Reagent holds the body (8 s, as it holds a pool's tick off). It is a wearing and not a wound: no hit, no flinch, no toll on the mind, no red at the edge; the health bar drains behind it as ever. As the mind mends by itself out of a fight (round 22), and in seconds by a lamp, it is a state to leave. A hint says so the first time it comes (`unmoored`, `ui/hints.ts`).
- Playtest round 23 (feedback: make being close to death more noticeable, but not too obvious or cluttered): since round 14, below 30% of full health, the heart sounded (a pair of 62 and 55 Hz sines, which small speakers cannot play) and the edge flashed red for a tenth of a second at each beat, which a player looking at the middle of the picture rarely sees. The heart is now one for the frame (`HEART`, `render/feel.ts`) that the sound, the picture and the health bar keep to: it beats at once on crossing the line, and quickens smoothly from 0.95 to 1.6 a second as health falls to nothing (`beatRate`; it had two rates), each beat a stroke and a softer one 0.26 s after (`swellAt`; the sound's own second stroke is at `HURT.dub`). The picture's edge stays a dark red, up to 0.16 of the way to red at the last, and swells at each stroke by up to 0.34 more; the colour drains a little, up to 0.3 at the last (the post pass's `faint()`, `uFaint`; `hurtFx.ts`); the health bar throbs toward a brighter red with it; the world's sound dulls, a low-pass on it from 16 kHz at the line to 2.4 kHz at death's door (`AudioEngine.setMuffle`; the title's theme is not on that bus), while the heart itself is low and stays; and the heartbeat has a knock (a triangle at 150 Hz) and a click over its low body, so it is heard on a laptop, and is louder as death nears. Nothing is added to the HUD but that throb, and none of it shows above 30% of health.
- Playtest round 23 (feedback, with screenshots: the volumetric fog reduces the atmosphere, is poorly designed and does not even look grey; find the best solution): compared on and off in every realm and in the dungeons, through a harness that moves the investigator about a running page. What was wrong: (a) it was a blanket. The mist scaled 2.4 to 12 m and reached 0.085 a metre, the camera stands at 3 to 4 m, so every ray, the horizon's and the roofs' included, was marched through it and lifted from black to a flat murk, and the sky's own moonlit haze was doubled; (b) its colour came after the grade and was then snapped to the palette, whose entries at that brightness were the graded ramp's khaki and sepia ((0.135, 0.134, 0.115), (0.178, 0.167, 0.138)) and eight blue-green sea-greys, so a grey mist of (0.13, 0.14, 0.138) came out olive (measured: (98, 91, 77) where the same street without it was (66, 62, 52)); (c) the investigator's lantern, standing in the thickest of it, washed the stones about them, and the mist began at the lens, so the ground of a fight lost its contrast. Now: the palette has six cool neutral greys (`MIST_LEVELS`, `palette.ts`: 64 entries, the most), and a mist's colour snaps to one of them (`tests/palette.test.ts`); the mist is a low one, scale heights 1.3 to 3 m (`FogDef.height`, most 1.3 to 2.2) and denser at the ground (0.09 to 0.14 a metre), under a thin haze that reaches the lamps' height (`FogDef.haze`, `FOG.hazeHeight` 9 m): the sky and the upper parts of things keep their dark, the ground fades into it with distance, and roofs, trees and hills stand out of it as silhouettes, and lamps glow in the haze; it gathers from 5 m of the lens to whole at 24 m (`FOG.start`, `FOG.full`), so the ground about a fight keeps its contrast; the lantern shines in it at 0.35 (`FOG.lantern`) and the lamps at 1 (`FOG.glow`, it was 0.55 for both, of a mist that met them more); its own light is brighter toward the moon and dimmer away from it, as the sky's haze is (`FogDef.moon`, `fogTint` in `shaders/fog.ts`), and even in the realms with no moon. A test keeps every mist one the camera can see over: at 3.5 m, the whole reach of the march lets more than a sixth of the light through (`tests/fog.test.ts`). The Fog setting scales all of it (0: none). `window.settings` joins the `?debug` hooks, to try that live.
- Playtest round 24 (a, "playtest it again and fix every bug or inconsistency you find; note down what may need improvement"): played through the sim more than the picture, since the headless browser draws two to seven frames a second and cannot judge the feel of a fight. `tests/soak.test.ts` plays random inputs for thousands of frames across every realm with the roster conjured about the investigator (any tier, some eldritch or boss variants), teleports, rests and sudden wounds, checking that every position, health, stamina and sanity is a number in its range, ammunition is whole, the entity count stays bounded, and a save loads as it was written (`SOAK_SEEDS`, `SOAK_FRAMES`, `SOAK_FIRST` widen it; ten seeds of 5000 frames ran clean). `tests/botHelpers.ts` is a bot that fights whatever stands in the arena (closes to the body's edge, strikes, dodges, shoots from afar, does what E offers in a boss fight); `bossBot.test.ts` sets it on every scripted boss and `foeBot.test.ts` on every other creature in every variant it has. In the browser: the title and every menu at five window sizes, a new game through the intro and the wake, all thirteen realms, a look into a dozen dungeons, and a monkey (random keys, clicks and resizes) listening for console errors. Its findings and what may need a second look are in `docs/PLAYTEST.md`.
- Playtest round 24 (b, a crash the bot found): a melee blow that killed a boss with decoys or summons (the Black Man's, the Terrible Old Man's servants, Tsathoggua's spawn) threw `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'pos')` in `aimAt`. The list of who a blow may touch is made before the loop, and a boss's fall clears its minions at once from inside the killing `strike` (`Died` → `endFight` → `clearArena`), so the loop came to a body that was gone. `live()` (`systems/targets.ts`) walks such a list and skips whoever has gone; the melee, burst, spread and sweep loops and the pool and bolt loops use it. A test kills each kind of boss with its summons standing (`bossFight.test.ts`), and `bossBot.test.ts` would have found it in a second.
- Playtest round 24 (c, the colossi could not be struck, so the main line could not be finished): the same bot dealt no damage at all to Cthulhu, Ghatanothoa, Yog-Sothoth or Shub-Niggurath. A hurt capsule stands on a sphere centred one radius up, which for a man is nothing, but a colossus's body (`ASSEMBLY_RADIUS`: 0.3 to 0.5 of its height, "nothing walks inside a colossus") keeps the investigator a whole radius from its axis, and there the capsule was a few metres across at the height of a blade (Cthulhu: 5 m across at a metre, against 12.4 m to stand off), so no blow of any weapon reached it. Cthulhu's and Yog-Sothoth's falls are quest goals, and the Beyond's order puts Yog-Sothoth before Azathoth, so the Court's endings were shut behind a horror no blade could touch; the revolver could hit them, but from the middle of the body outwards (below). The lower cap of the capsule is now centred no higher than `COMBAT.foot` (0.5 m), so a wide body stands on a flat foot and is struck along its whole base; a capsule of a radius under that is what it was, and no capsule shrinks. `tests/reach.test.ts` holds the sword-cane against every creature, in every variant, from as near as its body lets the investigator stand: 100 of the 105 landed a blow, the five that did not being the colossi (Azathoth's blows count, though it is warded whole), and now all do.
