With the bot pressing E and given two and a half minutes, 44 of the 47 scripted bosses fall (Azathoth by outlasting its piping); the other three ask for what the bot does not do: to take the Alert's helm (Cthulhu), to keep behind a monolith from a gaze that turns flesh to stone in four seconds (Ghatanothoa), and to cut the four roots, 700 health each, that ward Shub-Niggurath to a fifth (roots can be struck: checked).
- Playtest round 24 (c2, the two bosses of Innsmouth were not in their room): the same bot, set on every boss in its own place in the open world (`tests/bossStand.test.ts`, a fresh world for each), found Father Dagon and Mother Hydra 8 m outside their arena a step after they appeared, and neither could be reached. Their room, Y'ha-nthlei's temple, is roofed (the 'drowned' kit's vault, level with the walls' tops, six metres up: `ceiling()` in `world/dungeonParts.ts`), the ceiling is a solid box, and `pushOut` treats any box that overlaps a body's height as a wall: bodies fifteen and sixteen metres tall were pushed clear of the room's whole footprint, through the wall, to the nearer side. Nothing else in the game stands taller than a roof (checked over every creature of every room). The temple has a new kit, `sunken` (the drowned look, the vault fallen: an open roof, the sky over it; `data/kits.ts`), so they stand in it with their heads in the stars, and a test (`kits.test.ts`) fails for any creature taller than the roof over it. `bossStand.test.ts` asks of all 47 bosses that they stand in their arena a second after the world puts them there, and of the wide ones that a bot meets them from some side within twenty seconds (Tsathoggua's hall keeps its dressing across one approach); it fails for exactly these two without the kit.
- Playtest round 24 (c3, the Whisperer could be cut to half untouched): set at the edge of every creature's body, the bot found 86 of 87 that strike back within twenty seconds. The one that did not is the Whisperer in Akeley's Chair, which cannot move: its blows that wound (a bolt from 4 m, a sweeping beam from 3, its summons from 4) are chosen only within their range brackets, its gaze reaches but takes the mind, not the body, and so one who stood at its chair was in reach of nothing for the whole of its first phase. `chooseAttack` (`systems/tactics.ts`) takes a creature that cannot move to be able to strike what stands nearer than its blows like as well as what stands at their range (the ones that can move step back to theirs, as before). The other immobile ones, Azathoth and Yog-Sothoth, are unchanged in effect (blind, and with blows of every reach). `foesStrike.test.ts` sets each foe against an investigator at the edge of its body.
- Playtest round 24 (c4, the espada could not touch anyone at arm's length): every weapon against a body of every size, from as near as the bodies let the investigator stand, found thirty-four creatures, every one of a man's size or less (a cultist, a ghoul, Brown Jenkin, a Zoog, the Black Man…), that Zamacona's espada could not hit at all. A thrust (`pierce`, `data/weapons.ts`) is a hitbox of 0.32 m at its reach of 1.75, straight ahead, so its point passes beyond whatever stands nearer than about a metre from the investigator's axis, and a man's body at arm's length is 0.8 m away; it reached them only at a step back, and its heavy lunge (2.8 m of it, then a point) only from far off. A hit may now be a `thrust` (`HitDef`): swept from the hand out to the point as a long limb's is (`COMBAT.limb`, round 19), so what stands between the two is struck as well. Only the espada's blows are thrusts. `tests/reach.test.ts` holds every arm against a mite, a man, a Deep One, a Shoggoth, Father Dagon and Cthulhu, light and heavy.
- Playtest round 24 (c5, attacks in a boss's script that no fight could choose): an attack is chosen only when the investigator stands within its distance bracket, and a bracket scaled with the body's height (`attackRange`, `data/attacks.ts`: a human's 1.9 m is 1) scaled its minimum too. For a colossus a minimum of four or five metres became forty or sixty: Yog-Sothoth's beam and teleport began 53 and 63 m from its centre in an arena 34 m across its heart, Shub-Niggurath's summon and charge 38 m (arena 32), the Colossus beneath the Pyramids' charge 30 (26), Azathoth's darkness 63 (44). The script of each lists them with weights (Yog-Sothoth's beam is a third of its first and last phases) and they were never used. A scaled minimum is now at most 20 m (`LEAST_CAP`); reaches scale as before. `bossStand.test.ts` fails for any boss attack whose bracket starts beyond its arena. The colossi now use the scripts they were written with (none of these fights had been fought, since no blade reached them: they want a human's balance pass).
- Playtest round 24 (d, found beside it): a shot's range, which sets its damage, its stray and where the bullet ends, was measured to the middle of the body it hit. Against a colossus the whole radius counted: standing two metres off Ghatanothoa's side was eight metres for the revolver, and the bullet ran on to its axis. It is measured to the near side now (`firstHit`, `systems/revolver.ts`; a man's radius makes it a third of a metre nearer, which the fall-off table's metres hardly feel), as `assistTarget` already did. A test in `gun.test.ts`.
- Playtest round 24 (d2, and there was nothing to see when a colossus was struck): the sparks, the flare and the spray of a landed blow (`render/impactFx.ts`, `combatFx.ts`) were put a quarter metre in from the struck body's axis at the height of its heart, which for a man is where his chest is; on a colossus that is a dozen metres inside the body (where the mesh hides it) and several above the blade. Now one place decides it (`render/wound.ts`, pure: `woundPoint`): on the near side of a body wider than a metre, at the height of the blow (the least of the target's heart and a little above the attacker's own). A man's and a brute's wound is where it was. `tests/wound.test.ts`.
- Playtest round 24 (e, small things the pass found): the title's Settings and Controls pages were cut off at the top of a short window (800 × 600: their heading off-screen); the title panel scrolls now, with a margin, at 86% of the window's height (`ui/titleScreen.ts`). `AudioEngine.setMuffle` was asked every frame and told the lowpass filter's target every time, an automation event a frame; it says so only when the amount changes. A merchant's button went grey with no word when the pocket was full (`hasRoom` in `systems/trade.ts`: "no room to carry it"). The rest text at an Elder Sign did not say the revolver is loaded from the spare rounds. The arms page said "4 more lie somewhere in the dream" under the revolver's lines, which reads as four more revolvers; it says weapons. Two lines of the veil's rotating words say what the round-22 and 23 systems are (the revolver's six and where to find more; what an unmoored mind costs). What was not changed and may want a look is in `docs/PLAYTEST.md`.
- Playtest round 24 (f, upkeep): four files had grown past 300 lines since the last round and are split along their seams (CLAUDE.md's rule): `data/playTuning.ts` → `data/lifeTuning.ts` (the hunt, the small lives, lightning; re-exported by `tuning.ts` as before), `world/placements.ts` → `world/roomFurnish.ts` (what stands in a dungeon room), `data/dungeons.ts` → `data/dungeonsFar.ts` (R'lyeh, the Mi-Go cities, the Void) with the room helpers shared in `data/roomBuild.ts` (`lairs.ts` had its own copy), and the tests `sanity.test.ts` → `sanityMending.test.ts`, `gun.test.ts` → `gunStock.test.ts` and `gunHelpers.ts`. Nothing else in them changed. `docs/PLAYTEST.md` is where this round's notes for the next pass live.
- Round 25 (the notes of `PLAYTEST.md` taken up; a: small things): Shift is kept from the keys (`FIXED_KEYS` in `core/bindings.ts`, with Escape, Enter, Tab and the arrows), because it is the heavy blow's and the parry's modifier and "Move back" on Shift made every step back a heavy blow; a stored layout that bound it falls back to that action's default, and the Controls page says the key is kept when one is pressed (note 12). The shop line under each ware says what is carried ("Carried: 2 of 5 flasks"; `carried()` in `systems/trade.ts`; note 10). The line under the veil is the first lesson that has come up and has not been said yet (`pickLore` in `data/loreLines.ts`, each lesson tied to the hint that must have been shown; what has been said is kept in this browser beside the hints, `ui/loreLine.ts`) and only then one of Lovecraft's at random, so a new game's first line is never about fog closing before a horror (note 11). `?spawn=<colossus>` stands the investigator a few metres off the body's near edge, facing it (`standAtEdge`, `systems/game.ts`; note 7). `npm run check` is the everyday set and `npm run audit` the slow one (the soak, the bots, the walks of every dungeon and prop, the balance table; `vitest.config.ts`, `AUDITS`); `npm run check:all` runs both and is what CI should (note 14). Note 13 (how it sounds, how it plays on a pad, how fast it draws) needs a person at a real screen and is still open.
- Round 25 (b: bodies shove by their weight, note 3): two overlapping bodies give way by the share the other weighs, a body weighing its footprint (radius², at least 0.3 m) instead of half and half (`separate`, `systems/movement.ts`): a colossus no longer moves at half the pace of the man who bumps it, nor 17 m when one begins inside it, and two bodies of a size still give way alike.
- Round 25 (c: a colossus has zones, notes 2 and 6): a body that is an assembly and 12 m tall or more (`ZONES.height`; eight of them) is no longer one column struck alike along its height. A lathed or mounded one is three bands (`data/assemblyShape.ts`, from the outline the model is drawn with): legs (up to a third, taking the blow whole), torso (soaking it to 0.6) and head (the weak spot, ×2.5), which on its feet is far above a blade; the revolver aims for it where it is in range (`weakSpot`, not above 18 m: Cthulhu's is not). After each blow of its own a colossus recovers stooped (`MoveDef.open`, set by `compileAttack` for every attack that is not a special; `refreshStoops` holds it 24 frames past the recovery's end; a stagger or a parry stoops it too) and its head comes down in front of its face within the blade's reach (`systems/hurt.ts`): stand before the face in its recovery and a blow hurts it two and a half times over, from behind or on its feet it takes the legs' share. The model leans forward to show it (`render/creatureViews.ts`). A body of spheres (Yog-Sothoth) is struck where its spheres are, placed from the creature's seed alike in the picture and in the fight (`globes`; eight of its fourteen rest in a ring low about its foot, so a blade reaches it from any side, and the rest float above). Blows to a colossus go through `capsuleGap2` as before, which now names the share a place takes (`damage`) and is given the blow's own radius (`reach`); melee and the revolver multiply by it. Bodies that are not colossi keep their one capsule. The short-body reach test leaves colossi out (`reach.test.ts` strikes them from where they let one stand).
- Round 25 (d: hints and the gaze, notes 4 and 5): what each boss asks besides striking is said once before the fight, as Azathoth's was: Cthulhu's ship, the Dunwich Horror's powder and incantation, Ghatanothoa's stone, Shub-Niggurath's roots, Yog-Sothoth's spheres, and a colossus's stooping (`bossHints`, `ui/hints.ts`). Turning to stone now shows in the picture (the post pass's `uStone`, driven by `g.reality.petrify` in `render/hurtFx.ts`): the colour drains to grey as it fills, the edges close in pale, and past half the picture cracks, so it is a rule the eye learns and not a surprise (the four-seconds-in, five-seconds-out numbers are as they were). The monoliths were proved to be cover (`tests/stoneCover.test.ts`): behind each, a little way out from the ring, the boss's eye cannot see a man, and from anywhere in the ring the nearest is less than four seconds of sprinting away.
- Round 25 (e: the balance bot and the numbers it moved, notes 8 and 1): `tests/balance.test.ts` (`docs/BALANCE.md`) fights every scripted boss with a bot at the standing a person has when they reach it (`tests/balanceModel.ts`: bosses met in order of health; 60% of the world's lesser Echoes in step with them and 85% of each boss's own, spent as Vigour, Might, Vigour, Endurance, Might; the stones the bosses leave set into the sword-cane), reading the wind-ups and rolling from 55% of blows, in cover from the gaze and at the Alert's helm, unkillable, and tallying what it took against the health and Reagent a person has ("lives"). Its findings: the early and middle bosses fall in 7 to 20 s at 0.05 to 0.3 lives (as the design of round 12 has it: a first boss within 35 blows, none past 90 at the most), the late ones take 1 to 3 lives in 30 to 90 s, and three were out of line: Tsathoggua (3.3 lives), Yog-Sothoth (7.6) and Cthulhu (9.3 lives in 188 s). Changed: Tsathoggua's damage 70 → 52, Yog-Sothoth's 120 → 62, Cthulhu's 120 → 35 and its health 6700 → 4300 (it cannot be killed: its health is how long the Alert takes to come), each to fewer than 3 lives (a colossus, 6) and under 260 s. The test holds every boss to that (Azathoth, who cannot be wounded, and the Haunter, who is hurt only by lamplight, are exempt and said to be; Ghatanothoa is fought with its gaze waived and its seconds scaled by the half of a fight in sight, Shub-Niggurath with its roots down and forty seconds added). Left as they were: the health of the early bosses (a test keeps Keziah within 35 cane blows), and the economy of note 9 (a box of cartridges is 180 Echoes): the note makes that change conditional on the pass finding nobody uses the gun, and this pass has no player to find it; the prices are `WARES.rounds` and the found boxes `GUN.find` when a person does.
- Round 26 (a, "make the game more lively, increase awe and retention", all of the list taken up; Awe): a colossus's arrival, its steps, its dust and its tremor, the horizon's reveals (a titan's silhouette at R'lyeh, a false landmark for a failing mind), the dream's answer to a horror's fall (mist thins, stars rise, life returns, rumours), dread's silences and distant bellows, and what sanity does to the world (watchers in the dark, the map drawn wrong, buildings leaning in, flames wavering harder) are render-side and read the simulation only; none change a number in a fight.
- Round 26 (b, Life): the people walk rounds of their own by the hour (`systems/npcLife.ts`; not saved, a load finds them at home), weather (rain, gales, the dream's motes) hushes the investigator's noise, files of creatures cross the dream (`systems/wanderers.ts`, only with an overworld, so the arena and the balance bot are unaffected), the night turns (the moon's course, windows going out, a grey last hour). Not done: dogs barking at strangers, a lamplighter, shutters closing on a horror's roar.
- Round 26 (c, Retention): an early omen at about a minute and then another (`systems/hook.ts`, kept in `told`) tells a new player where the main line leads; pale columns stand over the Elder Signs not yet found (`render/beacons.ts`), and reading a sign's first page uncovers a little of the map about it (`systems/survey.ts`); a death names the blow that took the investigator and how it is met (`data/deathLines.ts`: one line per attack), else a fragment of the place, never the same twice running; a second journey is told once what has changed.
- Round 26 (d, build variety): relics (`data/relics.ts`), one left by each horror slain for good, all worn at once, each a gift with a price in the mind or the purse (no equip screen: a menu was more than the round could hold, and a choice between them is made by whom to kill first); they live in `told` (`relic:<id>`) so the save needs no new field, and are not carried into a new journey. Two weapons have tempers: the cutlass takes more Echoes, the espada steadies the mind at a kill. Multipliers are read in `combat.ts`, `death.ts` and `sanity.ts`'s `mendRate`.
- Round 26 (e, shareability): P keeps a picture (`ui/photo.ts`: the canvas is read the frame it is drawn, so the grade and sanity's warp are in it and the HUD is not; scaled whole-number times, hard-edged, downloaded as a PNG), and the ending card keeps a plate of the run's numbers (`ui/keepsake.ts`). A gallery of moments was left out: it would need a viewer and storage the game does not have, and the downloads are the gallery.
- Round 27 (a, "thematically fitting doors to all dungeons where it is fitting"): each kit has a look of door by the room it leads into (`data/doors.ts`): oak planks with iron bands for houses (brass in the library, white and gilt in the sculptor's), barred iron for vaults, crypts and the mine's gate, a slab that sinks into the floor for tombs and ruins (pale and glyphed for the Elder City, dark for the archives, black with gold for Kadath), a slab that slides into the wall, hung out of true and green-lit, for R'lyeh, bronze with a green serpent for Tsath, wet valves for the Mi-go's cities, drapes for the dream. The hill's circle, the drowned temple and the void have none: they are open ruins. 222 doors in all (99 plank, 55 grille, 48 slab, 10 drape, 10 valve). Doors are render-only (`render/doorViews.ts`): the doorway's clearance and collision are unchanged, so no walk, bot or audit is touched; a door opens as the investigator or any foe nears, swinging away from whoever opened it, and shuts behind them, and some stand ajar until first approached. Hidden-layer doors and Kadath's sealed door keep their own seals. Their colours are lifted 1.9× in `doorShapes.ts`: at the kits' tones the doors were black against the lantern-lit walls.
- Round 27 (b, "a thematically fitting boss fog that you need to pass"): the fog gate of round 12 closed only after the investigator was inside the ring, as motes; now there is a wall to walk through first (`render/bossFog.ts`, `world/gatePlan.ts`): in the doorway of every boss room (in place of a door) and in a ring 1.5 m outside every open arena's ring of stones, so that it is always crossed before the boss wakes (a boss wakes within its ring's radius; a room's doorway is a metre past it). It is not solid and needs no button: the audits and bots pass through it, and a player may still turn back before the horror wakes. It thickens while a fight holds the investigator, billows and sounds when it is crossed and the horror wakes, and dissolves over 3.5 s once every horror it keeps is slain (kept in `slain`, so it stays gone). Sixteen themes (`data/fogThemes.ts`) by boss, else by realm: grey churchyard mist, Keziah's violet swirl, Curwen's green steam, Sentinel Hill's torn storm, Innsmouth's green brine, R'lyeh's heavy deep, bone-cold grave mist, the dark that eats the lantern, Hastur's yellow rags, the colour out of space (its hue will not hold still), desert dust, frost, embers, the black grove's rot, the Mi-go's spores, the dream's gold haze, the Beyond's stars, Kadath's gold-flecked onyx. The engaged-fight motes (`fogGates.ts`) take the same theme. Not done: a fog to interact with (a prompt, a walk-through animation), as in Elden Ring; a solid gate would stop the bots and the audits.
- Round 27 (c, playtest: walking under a streetlight spawned flies around the investigator): the round 26 lure (moths of any lamp within 14 m, about half of them, left the lamp and circled the lantern) moved them to the investigator in one step, so they appeared out of nothing. Removed, with its test: moths circle their own lamps only, as before round 26.
- The voices (request: "use ElevenLabs' new v4 to voice all the NPCs and bosses that talk, and the [] tags to really sell the emotions", then "redo the takes already collected, and make them better with v4"): the people of the realms and the horrors that speak are voiced (`docs/VOICES.md` has the whole of it). (a) Who: the fifteen people met at an Elder Sign, every line of every topic (123), and the twenty-eight horrors that speak in their own stories, which until now only had a name, a card and an epitaph: each was given a line as it arrives and a last as it falls (54, `data/speechBosses.ts`, kept to 135 shown letters to be heard over a scene); the Dunwich Horror (unseen) and Brown Jenkin (joins a fight begun) have no scene and say only a last, as a notice. The rest of the roster stays wordless, as their stories leave them. (b) The words are not touched: a performed line is the shown line with tags and capitals (`data/speechNpcs.ts`, `speechFar.ts`, `speechBosses.ts`), tests hold the words equal, and a boss's caption is its performed line with the tags out and the leaned-on capitals let down (`shown`); a person's caption is `npcs.ts` as it was. (c) Performance follows v4's own guidance: tags say how a line is spoken and never what the speaker does, sit where the delivery changes and stack; a capital for the word leaned on; ellipses and `[pause]`s; no hyphen, comma or digit in a tag (a test: v4 reads them aloud); three names (`Yog-Sothoth`, `Dunwich`, `Pawtuxet`) are respelt in IPA between slashes in the request only. (d) A recording is `public/voice/<speaker>-<hash of the words>.mp3`, listed in `index.json`: a changed word names a new recording and a changed tag or capital keeps the name, so a performance can be tried again; a missing recording is shown, not heard, and the folder is the one more place besides `public/music` and `public/audio` where a file may be kept (CLAUDE.md). (e) Casting by description, since nobody has listened: premade voices for ordinary speakers (they work on any plan), library voices for the monstrous, old and foreign (Creator plan, which was bought during the work); a `rate` below 1 lowers and slows a great voice and an `echo` puts a hall behind it, both at playback (`render/audio/speech.ts`), so a recast needs neither again. The free plan allowed only premade voices and refused the rest, so the first set was made with them; every recording now in the folder was made again on the Creator plan, with v4 free at the time. (f) Playback: one voice at a time (the next cuts the last off in 0.14 s), a talk's next five lines fetched ahead, ten decoded recordings kept; the speech bus joins before the limiter and after the sanity shaper and muffle, so a failing mind and near death do not smear what is said, with a make-up gain of 1.5 (`AUDIO.speech`: speech averages about 20 dB under full scale, 10 over the bed); a Voices slider joins Settings › Audio; skipping a cutscene silences its voice; a horror's last shot is held until its line is said (`speaking()` in `render/cinemaDirector.ts`). (g) Checked: every file an MP3 of the duration ElevenLabs reported (3.4 to 20.7 s, at most 350 KB, 25.7 MB in all), and an offline speech-to-text pass over all 177 (faster-whisper `base.en`, with `small` for the doubtful: no tag was spoken as a word, 97 match the words to the letter and 172 to 0.9 or better, and the rest are a chant, a number written in digits and names; five takes that misheard plain words were made again and the new ones read back closer); not heard by ear, and ElevenLabs' own transcribe node was no use for it (given a speech node it hands back the prompt). (h) The credits say the voices are synthetic and whose (`data/credits.ts`), and `public/voice/CREDITS.md` says to check the plan's and each voice's licence before the game is sold; Steam asks for AI-generated content to be disclosed. This work was cut from round 24; main has since added rounds 25 to 27, which touch `render/audio/engine.ts`, `gameAudio.ts`, `data/cutscenes.ts`, CLAUDE.md and this file, so the two want a deliberate merge (the speech bus beside main's hush, the speech beats beside main's scene changes).
- Round 28 ("implement all the background soundtracks to every realm", the Drive folder "Backgrounds by realm", fifteen tracks, an MP3 and a WAV of each; seamless loops, crossfades, normalised, neither too loud nor tiring): the fifteen MP3s are in `public/music/realms/<title with dashes>.mp3` (listed in `public/music/CREDITS.md`; 79 MB in all; only the MP3s, the 46 MB WAVs of each are left on Drive). What is built (`data/realmMusic.ts`, `render/audio/loudness.ts` + `realmLoop.ts` + `realmMusic.ts`): the track of the realm the investigator is in plays on the music bus (so the music setting is its level) and gives way to the next realm's by a six-second equal-power crossfade; the Seventy Steps and the Cavern of Flame with the seven hundred steps have their own two (by room of the `slumber` dungeon); none in the arena or at sea. **Loudness** is read from the decoded samples as a track loads: its body (the half-second windows within 85% of the median level) is brought to −27 dBFS RMS, never past a 0.8 peak, so any take comes out as loud as any other and a new one needs no editing. **The loop** has no seam: it is the body, each pass fading out (equal power) over its last ten seconds as the next fades in over its first ten, scheduled on the audio clock three passes ahead, as the title theme's is (`themeLoop.ts`); the body is chosen so both sides of the seam are steady stretches, not a fade-in and a fade-out. **Not tiring**: the level breathes by 14% over 97 s, so a loop of four minutes is not heard to turn; it gives way to a boss's score (2.5 s) and returns, and to a murmur while the ground has gone quiet before a horror (follows the engine's hush); it is quiet beside the effects by design (−27 dBFS) and tuned in `REALM_MUSIC`. Memory: two decoded tracks are held (about 90 MB each for a four-minute stereo track). Measured on the real tracks in headless Chromium: all decode (44.1 kHz stereo, 194 to 240 s), their bodies run from 0 to 18 s in and end 5 to 8 s before the file's end, their bodies sat at −15 to −18 dB RMS (gains of 0.26 to 0.37) and come out at −27, and the level of the last ten seconds of each loop against the first ten differs by at most 4 dB (the seam's crossfade holds the sum steady). For comparison the title theme is −17 dB RMS at full and the ambience beds −22 dB before their own gains (about −28 net): −27 puts the backgrounds with the beds, about 10 dB under the theme. In the game, the hub's track loaded and looped with its passes overlapped and a change of region fetched the next and crossfaded, no errors. Nothing has been heard by ear. The boss themes (the "Boss themes" folder) are not taken up: the request was the realms' backgrounds, and the bosses already have scores in code (`data/music.ts`).
- Round 28b (playtest: "the songs don't play at all"): measured at the output (an analyser on the destination, power averaged over 25 s, music bus alone against the ambience bus alone), the realm music at the first target of −27 dB came out 2.5 dB under the ambience, so quiet beside two ambience beds and the drones that it was not noticed (a first, one-window reading had said 9 dB: too noisy to trust). The master volume (0.7), the limiter and the slow swell take about 6 dB off what the track is normalised to. Target is now −21 dB (about 2 dB over the ambience, 5 dB under the title theme). Not reproduced: a failure to start. In headless Chromium, from the title through a new game, the track is fetched, decoded and scheduled and the context runs, so if it is silent in another browser the cause is there: `realmMusic.ts` now catches a refusal while starting (it was an unhandled rejection, retried every frame) and says what happened in the console (`realm music: <track> (loop …)` on success, `could not be loaded` / `could not start` with the error otherwise). Also dropped a redundant `setValueAtTime` at the start of each fade's curve, which WebKit may be stricter about than Chromium.
- Round 29 (playtest notes: Morgan's voice, the flask's effects, the theme through the intro, kneeling): **a** Dr. Morgan (the trader, four lines) was cast as Adam, a firm premade voice, and sounded generic for a man who trades lamp oil and "odd things from the drawers" for Echoes and says not to ask what he does with them. He is now `Josef Hammer – Deep & Expressive` (a library voice: mature, resonant, a little smoky, intimate rather than polished), played unchanged, and his four lines are recorded again with Eleven v4 (same words, so the same file names): the delivery is lower and slower (`[low voice] [measured]`, more `[pause]`s, the last line whispered). Nobody has listened to it; to recast, change `speechCast.ts` and make the four again (docs/VOICES.md). The takes are in https://elevenlabs.io/app/flows/BPzg1ScZYxhFbvfljL6V. **b** the flask of lamp oil flew as the same spinning shard as a horror's bolt and landed as a pale disc: it is now a bottle tumbling with its rag alight, trailing embers and smoke; a flash, a ball of fire, a ring of flame run along the ground and flung glass where it lands; and while it burns tongues of flame, embers and smoke, a breathing glow, and all of it dying down (and the disc shrinking) as the oil is spent (`render/flaskFx.ts`, numbers in `FLASK`); the game's grade pales dull colours to grey, so the flames and the disc are bright orange to read at all. **c** under a new game's intro cards the first realm's music and ambience already sounded, though the theme was to play on until the spawn: the world's own sound (`audio.update`: ambience, drones, creatures, the realm's music) now waits for the spawn (`reveal` in main.ts), so the theme plays on alone through the cards and the loading, sinks away over seven seconds as it did, and the realm's track and ambience come in under it; the realm's track is fetched and decoded meanwhile (`audio.warm()`) to come in at once. This holds for Continue as well: the ambience used to start under the theme while the world was made. **d** fast travel to a sign now arrives on one knee before the stone, already down as the veil lifts (`Travelled` snaps the pose), and rises on the first step as after a rest; leaving the Elder Sign's menu now lets the investigator rise (`Screen.onClose`), where before they knelt until they next moved.
- Round 30 (playtest notes: torches, dialogue boxes, loud crickets, the water, tentacles, the dungeons): **a** torches (`render/sconces.ts`): they were a dice roll per pilaster, on both faces of every wall (outer faces too), at a height above the wall's lowest point (buried on stairs); now evenly spaced along each stretch of wall (the kit's `flames` sets the spacing), on faces that look into a room, at 1.95 m above that room's floor, a bigger flame and a stronger light (2.3, range 9.5). **b** dialogue has no box: text under the speaker in a soft shadow, each word revealed as the voice says it (`core/pace.ts`: the recording's energy is cut into windows, flat ones skipped, so a word after a pause lands where the voice returns; the `Speaking` event carries the curve), words rising from a blur in one beat; with no recording the pace is the old fixed rate. **c** the insect and frog beds are quieter, lowpassed, and breathe (a slow LFO) so they do not fatigue; the numbers are `BED_MANNER` in `data/samples.ts`. **d** the sea is its own mesh (`render/sea.ts`, `seaWaves.ts`): exp-sine waves with analytic normals, rings of whole cells following the eye, foam as thin streaks and a washed line where the terrain meets it; the chop follows weather and region; the wave table is shared with the CPU (`seaHeight`). **e** tentacles (`render/tentacles.ts`): after twenty seconds of looking at open sea, one to three rise 40–66 m off (brighter than the haze so they read; at 56–84 m they vanished in the fog), writhe, and sink; spray, a low sound, more often as the mind goes. Render only, never a creature. **f** the dungeons (`render/dungeonDressing.ts`, `dungeonFurniture.ts`; render only, nothing solid, nothing in the sim, so no layout, spawn or audit of play moves): vault ribs or joists under every roof; banners hung from the ribs of masonry halls; a border, a runner (carpet kits) and a medallion inlaid in each hall's floor; by kit, shelves, coffins, statues, stelae, pews, casks, crates and growths along the walls (kept 2.8 m from doorways, 1.5 m from torches, 2.2 m from the sign, gate, tomes and spawn spots) with rubble and bones strewn between; braziers (fires) in a hall's far corners; boss halls get floors and ribs only (their own style dresses them). Relief (books, runes, a coffin's cross, ribs, banners) is drawn in the `+` groups so the depth audit treats it as relief; bodies keep to one tone per cluster so intersecting pieces do not fight. Not done: new room shapes or graphs (they would move spawns, gates and the walks), so the "drastic" here is in how the rooms look and feel.
- Round 31 (playtest notes): **ambience**: the winds, sea, leaves, cave and drips are played at half their listed level (`BED_TRIM`), softened and breathing like the crickets (`BED_MANNER`), spots at 0.6. **dungeons**: the hanging banners are gone (stone-textured cloth made no sense); the ribs stay. **desktop**: leaving the window on a loading screen opened the pause menu under the veil and the load waited on Escape: the pause menu keeps out while the world is made, a journey, an arrival or a fall is under way (`held`, `journeys.busy`); the shell no longer throttles a window behind another and gives the page the focus on show and on return (Chromium reports pads only to a focused page). **pad**: a pad is not heard until used (a new button down, or a stick well off where it lay); buttons already down at first sight are ignored until let go once; an axis that lay off centre is read from there; so a phantom device, or a trigger read as a stick, can neither hold an attack down nor walk the player; keys and mouse are let go on blur, focus and visibility change; the Controls page names the pad heard. This is what could be told from the code: the desktop shell was not run with a real pad. **lights**: a corridor of torches and a hall of braziers added up past white and washed the walls to cream: the lamp sum is eased toward a ceiling in the shader, torches 2.3→1.9 (range 8.5), fires 3→2.6. **UNMADE**: a layered sound (the heart's last strokes, a fall into the floor, a backward swell, a cold wavering cluster, a thin ringing; `unmade`), whispers behind it, and the world's high end closes over a few seconds and opens again on the waking (`hush`); the old doom chord stays under. **respawn**: up on one knee before the sign, as after fast travel. **talk**: its screen keeps the pointer captured (`keepLock`), so leaving it needs no click. **walking**: the people walk at 1.15 m/s (0.9 was a shuffle), getting up to pace and easing off at each turn; the run and sprint lean less, swing the back leg less far and lift more between steps, so the stride is shorter and less of a lunge.
- Playtest round 32 (feedback, seven points: the realms look the same, it is too dark and foggy, the ground is weak, props are sparse and the character large, the dungeon rooms look alike and the boss halls are plain, the sanity effects start too hard and two nameplates overlapped, the menus are plain boxes and the HUD bars thin and close in colour). Numbered 32: `main` went on to rounds 28 to 31 (realm music, the dungeons dressed, the sea, tentacles, the ambience) while this was in progress; PR #34 (the voices) has since been merged, and this round was rebuilt on `main` after it, its conflicts resolved by hand (below: where the two rounds touched the same things).
- Playtest round 32 (1, "every realm is dark grey-sepia under dark grey haze"): found by reading the picture's path. One cold-to-warm split-tone grade (`GRADE`) and one fixed 64-colour grey/sepia palette were applied to every realm, and `FX.desaturate` took 85% of the rest of the colour; what the lamps and the moon lit with was one grey too. A realm now has its own look (`data/looks.ts`, 13 of them; `render/realmLook.ts` eases from one to the next in `SKY.fade`, or at once on a leap): a gradient-map grade of four colours (ink, shade, mid, high; each normalised to brightness 1, so a pixel keeps its light and takes the realm's hue: `gradeTint` in `shaders/post.ts`, mirrored by `render/realmPalette.ts`), a `native` share (0.52–0.62) of what each pixel was lit as, kept over the grade, two accent hues, a mist, a horizon and a zenith, the ambient and moon light, and the colour its far silhouettes stand in. The post pass's 64-colour palette is built per realm from the same grade (24 graded steps, 2 × 7 of the accents as they come out of the grade, 6 of the lantern's own warm light, 5 × 3 of the anomaly colours, 5 of the mist) and swapped as the look is. Hubs: Miskatonic indigo and amber, Arkham khaki, Dunwich russet and plum, Innsmouth teal, Providence slate, Vermont pine, the Mountains ice and lavender, Pnakotus terracotta, K'n-yan cobalt, the Dreamlands lilac, R'lyeh viridian, Yuggoth wine, the Beyond indigo. Judgment calls: (a) the colour isolation reads anomaly hues (magenta, purple, green) by hue and saturation and leaves them out of the grade, so any colour that enters the scene (ambient, moon, horizon, zenith, far) must not be one with more than 0.3 saturation and some brightness, or the realm's own light would flood as "an anomaly": `tests/palette.test.ts` enforces it, and the strong hue of a realm (R'lyeh's green, Yuggoth's wine) is carried by the grade stops, which are applied after the isolation; (b) the anomaly palette entries cost 0.006 more to match (`ANOMALY_FROM/TO`), and the lantern's warm ramp is in every palette: without them a lantern pool on blue snow snapped to mint and pink and the Mountains' ground was a rainbow; (c) no two realms may grade alike (a distance over 0.1 between their tints at three brightnesses, a test), and each keeps 0.3–0.8 of a pixel's own colour.
- Playtest round 32 (2, "too dark and foggy; towns, mountains and R'lyeh's towers never show as silhouettes"): the world shader's linear fog closed to near-black from 4 m to 80 (`FX.fogNear/Far`) over a far plane of 90, and the volumetric mist was 0.09–0.14 a metre and a dark grey. Now the land fades into its realm's haze from 22 m to 132 (the far plane 140 and the stream of chunks reach as far), the mist is 0.025–0.07 a metre in the realm's colour (`data/fogs.ts`, `lookOf(region).mist`), the moon stands higher (`LIGHT.nightMoonDir`) so the ground beyond the lantern is lit, light halos are drawn out to 120 m (a town's windows are seen across a field), and the sky is a gradient from the realm's horizon to its zenith (`shaders/sky.ts`; lightning flashes it through `uFlash`). The skyline gained kinds (and kept round 26's titans, their halos and eyes, and a failing mind's false city: `render/skyline.ts` draws each silhouette with its own material, `skylineShapes.ts` holds the outlines): town (gabled roofs, chimneys, steeples, and windows lit by a hash over its cells), reef, dunes and minarets (`render/skylineShapes.ts`), with a haze and a glow per silhouette; each waking-world realm sees its neighbours' towns where they really stand (so walking to one arrives at it), the Mountains' ranges and Pnakotus's towers show over their haze. `tests/landmarks.test.ts` now holds a silhouette of a town to stand where a real town does (and over 200 m from any sign), the rest over 300 m.
- Playtest round 32 (3, "the big fields are flat and repeat one texture; the pink-white blotches on dirt look like litter"): the ground textures were built of the base greys, rust and bone, so a heath had pale mottling on red-brown earth. `render/textureGround.ts` replaces them: grass, dirt, mud, rot, sand, snow (fine grain over a few soft fields at several scales, no pale hard-edged patch anywhere) and, as scattered overlapping pieces, gravel and leaf litter. The world material mixes a second ground in by patches (`uMap3`, `uPatch`; the region's `biome.patch`: leaves in the hub, Providence and Vermont, gravel in Innsmouth and the Mountains, dirt in Dunwich…) by world noise and not on a road, and varies the tone across the land at three scales with a hue drift, so a field is not one tile; ground cover counts raised by two thirds or more (fifty to a hundred and thirty tufts, stones and leaves to a chunk). The clapboard's paint peel (dark blobs that read as camouflage on Innsmouth's houses) is long, soft and low in contrast.
- Playtest round 32 (4, "props are sparse and the character is large on screen; there is little to walk toward"): `world/planFill.ts` (split from `regionPlan.ts`, which was at 299 lines) lays over a biome's scatter a fill of the ground's own kinds by slow noise (`strewn`: thick where the land is wooded or strewn, thin in a clearing; bushes, trees, rocks, stumps and logs on grass, rock on snow and gravel, broken pillars on slabs), copses, wayside stones with their lamps and a farmstead with its fenced yard (`world/features.ts`: `FILLER_KINDS`, drawn from `layout.copses/waymarks/farms`, in `data/regionFeatures.ts`), and every thirty to fifty metres along a country road, on alternate sides, what the realm's roads are lined with (crosses and lamps in New England, pillars in the Dreamlands, standing stones elsewhere: K'n-yan has no street lamp, a test). Fillers are not named places and no foes camp in them, so the places and the foes are as before (about 900 to 2,000 props a region, 6,000 in the Dreamlands). The camera is further and wider (`CAMERA.distance` 4.2 → 5.6, `pivotHeight` 1.7, `RENDER.fovDeg` 60 → 66): the investigator was a third of the picture's height and is about a quarter; `WORLD.gateArrive` rose to 7.2 so the boom clears the gate, and the gate test now steps back toward it a pace and not half the way.
- Playtest round 32 (5, "dungeon rooms look alike; each repeats the same inlay ring and box shape; boss halls are the plainest"): a hall had four pillars (or a ring of eight) and a bare floor wherever it stood; a boss hall had its heart and four braziers, and a lair's boss room (13 of the 27 boss halls) no dressing at all. `world/roomStyle.ts` gives a room a plan from its dungeon's name and its place among its dungeon's rooms: a hall's pillars (four, pairs at its doors, two rows down a nave, a ring of ten), its floor (a carpet runner, a framed border, a cross, a chequer, nested squares; corridors' thresholds; a ring about a well), cross-shaped halls (the corners filled solid) and buttress bays along the walls; eight small-hall plans taken three apart, so no two halls in turn in a dungeon alike (a test), and a boss's hall is a great hall with one of three emblems (rings, squares, a disc with four about it) under its heart, a carpet in from the door, bays, and two rows of the boss's own pieces down the sides (`ArenaStyle.hall`; a style for every lair's boss). Inlays are flat relief on the floor (the part look `inlay`: four centimetres, not solid, drawn nearer by polygon offset like a wall's trim so the ray audit's depth-fight rule passes, in the wall's stone paler or darker, or a carpet or gilt cloth, board floors take carpets only, earth floors only a boss's markings). Pillars keep a pace from every spot a thing is put and out of a doorway's lane; corners and bays only where nothing stands. Round 30 had meanwhile dressed every hall with the same border, runner and three rings in its floor (`render/dungeonDressing.ts`): that was the "same inlay ring" of the playtest, so those floors are gone from the dressing, and each room's plan lays its own (the carpet colours round 30 chose for each kit are kept for the plans' carpets); the rest of round 30's dressing (ribs, furniture, debris, braziers) stays, and now keeps clear of a plan's pillars, filled corners and bays. Rejected: raised daises and sunken floors (the ground under a character is read from the room's own level, bosses spawn at it), rotated walls (parts are axis-aligned boxes). The wide-hall plans for halls that are no boss's (the story has none) were dropped as dead code, one fallback is kept.
- Playtest round 32 (6, "at 76 sanity the screen fringing and shimmer already hide the scene; two nameplates overlapped into an unreadable word"): the warp (ripple, split, swimming walls, breathing lens, shear) grew in step with the stress from nothing, so 76 sanity (stress 0.24) was already 1 px of ripple and 0.8 px of split everywhere. `FX.calm` (0.2) holds the picture whole until a fifth of the stress is spent, easing in (`onsetOf`, a smoothstep to `FX.warpCap`, where it is whole again, so every value from the cap on is as it was): at 76 the ripple is a twentieth of a pixel, at 70 (Uneasy) a third of one, at 50 as before. The fog's closing, the colour drained, the dither and the boost of anomaly hues wait for it the same way (`seen = stress × onset`). The edges' blur and the stars' crawl already began at 0.25. Nameplates (`ui/plateLayout.ts`): a plate lay at its foe's head whatever stood there, and a long name ran off its 74-pixel box to the right, so two foes together wore names on one another; plates are laid out in turn (the foe locked on to, then the nearest), one lying over another is lifted above it, and one that finds no room in four lifts is left out (the locked one never); a plate is as wide as its name and centred.
- Playtest round 32 (7, "menus are plain boxes; the HUD bars are thin and close in colour"): health, stamina and sanity were rust, slate and bone, six pixels each. They are 16, 9 and 12 pixels, with a lit top and a shaded foot, in blood red, leaf green and a blue that runs through violet to magenta by band (`ui/hudKit.ts` `bar`, `BLOOD`, `LEAF`; `mindHud.ts`); the boss's bar is 14, the foes' 5, in the same red. A panel is framed (`ui/menuKit.ts` `frame`): a deep ground lit from above, a gold border held off by a dark one, corner brackets, a heavy shadow; its buttons are plaques with a bar of gold at the left that brightens under the focus; a page's title (`title`) is pale capitals over a rule fading out either side of a small diamond, a section (`heading`) small gold capitals over a faint rule; the reading panel wears the frame too (round 30's talk has no box, and keeps none).
- Playtest round 32 (tests changed, and why): `tests/palette.test.ts` is rewritten for the looks (it tested the old grade and the one palette); `fog.test.ts` (the mist is the look's colour, at most 0.075 a metre), `skyline.test.ts` (the new kinds, lit towns, silhouettes out of reach), `textures.test.ts` (saturation below 0.72), `budgets.test.ts` (the far fog ≥ 125), `lighting.test.ts` (the moon's height), `landmarks.test.ts`, `checkpoints.test.ts` (a gate), `fx.test.ts` (the calm and the warp's onset) follow the numbers above; new: `roomStyle.test.ts`, `plateLayout.test.ts`.
- Round 33 (playtest: the castle drawn over the hills, the new HUD "awful", the pad still unheard): **skyline**: every far silhouette is drawn at one distance with no depth test, so which lay over which was only the order they were made in (a far castle over a near hill); they are now sorted each frame, the farthest drawn first, and paler with distance (`uFade`), a titan's halo and eyes keeping their places about it. **HUD**: the round 32 bars were thick saturated slabs; the vitals are now a 196 px cartouche (`ui/gauges.ts`): a hairline brass frame, a 7 px health gauge with a diamond at each end, a 3 px stamina line, a 5 px mind gauge, muted fills; Reagent as diamonds and the cylinder as six rounds, full or hollow, with the spare rounds after; the counters at the right small and dim. **pad**: still not reproduced (no pad here); the Controls page now says what the browser itself reports (devices listed, connect and disconnect events, or why it refused), so the next report can name the cause.
- Playtest round 34 (a second comprehensive playtest, asked to raise the visual fidelity of the whole game, find every bug and inconsistency, and improve balance, lore, cutscenes, liveliness and immersion; the sweep: a screenshot of every realm, 35 rooms of 28 dungeons, the title, the intro and the wake, creatures set before the investigator, the raw low-res picture beside the final one): (1, "creatures and people lose their colour") the raw picture (`pipeline.grab()`) has the Mi-Go pink, the Deep One teal, the corpse brown; the final picture had them all grey with white heads, because the realm's grade is a gradient map on brightness and the 64-colour palette is a single ramp with two accents. Characters now carry alpha 0.75 out of the world and sprite shaders (`uMarkCharacters`), and the post pass takes only `FX.charShare` (0.3) of the realm's grade for them and posterises them to `FX.charLevels` (9) levels a channel in square-root space, dithered, instead of snapping to the palette (`shaders/post.ts` `posterize`): the world stays in its realm's colours, what lives in it keeps its own. The Colour Out of Space's mark (0.31) is untouched.
- Playtest round 34 (2, "nothing is shadowed"): trees, houses and people stood on the ground without a shadow but a disc under the feet. The moon casts shadows (`render/moonShadow.ts`): one 1024² depth map drawn from the moon each frame over the investigator (`FX`-style numbers in `SHADOW`: 34 m either side, a texel of 7 cm, the camera held to the map's own texel grid across the rays so the shadows slide with the ground and do not shimmer: `render/moonFrame.ts`, tested), of what stands on the CASTER layer. To keep the pass to about fifteen draws (the first version, every mesh, cost 87 in the hub, past the 150 budget) a chunk's props are merged into one position-only mesh that only the moon's camera sees (`shadowMesh`, `propMeshes.ts`; ground cover does not cast) and a person is one merged shape, drawn a little inside the real figure so it never shadows itself (`personShadow`, `actorViews.ts`); terrain only receives. The world shader splits the moon's term out of the vertex light (`vMoon`) and takes `SHADOW.strength` (0.9) of it from what lies behind something in the map (`shaders/shadow.ts` `moonLit`: normal offset, a slope bias, four turned taps, a fade toward the map's edge); the sprite shader does the same for a creature's centre. Under a roof, in a dungeon, with the Moon shadows setting off (Settings › Video), or with the moon under 8° nothing is drawn. The moon moves through the night (`nightFx.ts`), so the shadows are never baked.
- Playtest round 34 (3, "the land is still"): leaves and boughs bend in the wind (`SWAY` in `shaders/world.ts`, `aSway` per vertex, `propMeshes.ts` `addSway`: trees, pines and bushes lean up to 3 cm a metre at the top and nothing at the foot; blades of grass lean at the tip, `groundCover.ts`), by gusts that roll across the land and shiver in it; `uWind` is 1 on a calm night and rises in rain and a gale (`weather.ts`). The vertex is moved after its light and shadow are found where it stands. Chimneys send up smoke (`render/chimneys.ts`): the tops are reported by `houseMesh.ts` (`Piece.smoke`) and `propMeshes.ts` for the chunk, kept in a registry the chunk's build and unload feed, and drawn as grey dithered puffs while the night keeps its windows lit (`windowShare`), most hearths lit and not all.
- Playtest round 34 (4, "the sky is a gradient with a few stars"): the Milky Way (a band across the sky broken by lanes of dust), the aurora (curtains of cold cyan light low in the north, over the Mountains of Madness, the Dreamlands and the Beyond; cyan, because the anomaly hues' windows are green and purple and a colour there would be boosted as a wrong thing) and falling stars (a head and a tail across the upper sky in a share of the turns that come every eleven seconds) are in the sky shader (`shaders/sky.ts`), each realm's strength in `SKY` (`playTuning.ts`): none under K'n-yan's earth, the most over Yuggoth, whose sky has no moon.
- Playtest round 34 (upkeep): `shaders/world.ts` had grown to 299 lines; its shared GLSL chunks (the lantern, the lamps, noise, space) moved to `shaders/common.ts` and are re-exported from it, so no importer changed. This work was numbered round 33 while it was made; the other session's round 33 (skyline, HUD, pad) landed first, so it is round 34.

- Playtest round 34 (5, "the lore is thin outside the first towns"): six of the thirteen realms had no document to find (Providence, Vermont, the Mountains, Pnakotus, K'n-yan, R'lyeh) and the journal said nothing of what the investigator had met. Seventeen more notes (`data/documents.ts`, 53 in all: a surveyor's book, a receipt in the Archivist's hand, a radio operator's last log, a dated letter, a ship's manifest) are set by `NOTE_SITES` in region-local metres through the same placements as the first thirty-six, and every realm now has at least two. The Journal gains a bestiary (`ui/bestiaryPage.ts`, `data/bestiaryNotes.ts`, `data/bestiaryFacts.ts`; `Mind.seen`, the saved list of what has been beheld, which first sight already keeps): the creatures met, by tier, each page a field note written for it (all 100 roster ids; a test holds that none is missing), its tier, the source it comes from, its habit (read off its archetype: "stalks in packs", "waits buried"), what it is hurt by and what turns the blade or the revolver (read off its resistances), and the realms it is met in. A creature not yet beheld is not listed; the heading counts "n of 100".
- Playtest round 34 (6, "every horror arrives the same way"): `arrival(scale, name, epithet)` (data/cutscenes.ts) chooses a scene by the horror's size alone, so the witch, the Haunter, Cthulhu and Nyarlathotep all came the same. Ten of the story's horrors now have an arrival of their own (`data/cutscenesBoss.ts`; `BESPOKE`, picked by boss id before the size's, `render/cinemaDirector.ts`): the witch by a corner of the room that tilts, the Haunter as the lamps go out one by one, the Colour as a drain of the world's colour toward it, Cthulhu as a door opening on the whole of the sky, Father Dagon as the sea coming up into the temple, Hastur under the yellow sign, Yog-Sothoth as the sky fills with spheres, Azathoth unseeing, Nyarlathotep as a tall man in a good coat with a key on his watch chain, Shub-Niggurath as the woods breathing. Each is data like any scene, `speaking()` lays the horror's recorded line over it as it does over the rest, and no spoken line's words changed. A test (`tests/cinemaBoss.test.ts`) holds that each is a boss, its beats lie inside the scene, its name card has over 2.4 s to be read, and the lens is finite throughout. Played in the browser (each horror set before the investigator in the hub, Shub-Niggurath at its own arena): the camera, the fades and the name cards read as written.
- Playtest round 34 (7, soak finding, "a bolt at 665 m"): the wide soak (seed 103, frame 6180) found a spit bolt over the Dreamlands at y = 665. A lobbed bolt is aimed to come down where its target stands, with the arc computed for the whole distance; a spitter's wind-up had run out after the investigator travelled far away, and an arc for a kilometre rises at a kilometre a second. A lob is aimed no further than it can be thrown (`reach = min(distance, range)`, the target's height scaled with it, `systems/projectiles.ts`): the bolt now comes down at the end of its throw. This also saves the oil flask, which lifetime-expired in mid-air without bursting when thrown at a foe beyond 24 m. `tests/bolts.test.ts` (3 tests, failing without the fix at 2446 m); sixty seeds of nine thousand frames now pass.
- Playtest round 34 (8, "the rain does nothing to the ground"): rain fell as streaks over ground that stayed dry. The ground takes the wet (`render/wetness.ts`, one shared object that is the world shader's `uWet` and that the weather eases: 0.3 a second to soak, 0.04 to dry, so puddles last about a minute after a spell, and none under a roof): everything under the sky is darkened by it, and on flat ground the heaviest rain lays puddles (a mask of the land's own noise that shrinks as the ground dries) that throw back the lamps and the lantern, the moon at a glancing look and the haze, and ring where drops fall (one drop to a cell in its turn, each time elsewhere in it: the first version rang every cell at once and read as a lattice). Characters, lit things and the sea are left as they were. Footsteps on soaked ground splash (the water-step recording, quietly, in proportion to the wet, and five drops thrown up under the foot at each stride, `render/weather.ts`; none from a leap, a standing still or under a roof). The hub keeps its clear skies by design (round 26's tests say a region that knows no weather stays clear).
- Playtest round 34 (9, "the people stand about like furniture"): the fifteen people met in the dream spoke only when talked to. Passing near one in their sight, a line of what is on their mind is now overheard (`data/overheard.ts`: three each, written to their voices and to nothing the story has yet to reach: Peaslee's lost count of the days, Gilman's corners, Johansen saying the seven names so they are said somewhere; `systems/overheard.ts`; the HUD's line above the prompt, `ui/hud.ts`, the name in small gold capitals and the words in italic for six seconds). Rules, all in `HEARD` (`data/lifeTuning.ts`): within 6.5 m and in sight (a ray at head height, so a wall hushes them), the nearest first, a line chosen from those not yet said by a hash of the frame (not the game's own random stream: the first version drew from it, and so changed what the world rolled next, which made the soak's runs from before it impossible to replay; a replay hears the same), each once until the next rest or death (it shares `Overworld.said` with the dungeon rooms' words, which those two events already clear), one in 25 s whoever it is, and none in a talk, in a boss fight, with a foe hunting close by or while the investigator lies dead. Text only: no recording stands behind it, so no spoken line was added or changed (the voice tests tie recordings to exact words).
- Playtest round 34 (10, soak invariants, "stuck in a church wall"): the soak now also asserts that the investigator stands on the ground (within 0.75 m of it) and is not held for sixty frames inside something solid (the capsule resolve would move them more than 0.3 m). Ten seeds found one case: a boss's fog closes behind the investigator and holds them in its ring by setting them on the rim, ignoring walls; the soak teleported them 1250 m away in a fight (a journey is barred from the map in one, `travelBar`, which the soak now respects) and the fog dragged them back across the world, onto a rim that lay in the corner of the Starry Wisdom church's walls, where the wall pushed them out and the fog pulled them in and they were held fast in the wall. `holdInVeil` (`systems/bossArena.ts`) now lets go of one carried more than `BOSS.margin` past the ring (the boss lets them go as it does one who walks away) and does not set them down on a rim that lies in something solid. Two tests (`tests/bossArena.test.ts`) fail without it. A body in its death throes is exempt from the check (a second sample, eight horrors the soak had set about the investigator at once, shoved the dying body into a pillar until the respawn, which is not a thing a person can reach). Forty seeds of nine thousand frames pass with the new invariants.
- Playtest round 34 (11, "the story's people are one pale figure in five colours"): the bestiary's cards showed Dr. Muñoz, Hypnos, the Terrible Old Man, the Outsider and Lilith on one body, told apart by a tint, and a dozen more named people on the same three bodies (the upright, the hunched, the robed). A recipe's `gear` (`data/schema.ts` `GEAR`, `render/sprites/gear.ts`) gives each what the story gives them, drawn about each body plan's own anchors in three passes (what lies behind, what hangs from the hips, what is put on last): the witch her pointed hat; the Black Man a tall hat and coat; Curwen a tricorn and coat; the Terrible Old Man a hat, a beard and a cane; Dr. Muñoz a coat and the cooling pipes of "Cool Air"; Hypnos a halo; the Outsider tatters; Lilith horns; the High Priest his yellow silk mask; the cowled sorcerers and Umr at-Tawil hoods and staves; Nyarlathotep "a tall man in a good coat" with the key on its watch chain. Nothing stands more than six pixels above a head (its cell would clip it), the golds are held muted (the sprite tests keep every unlit pixel under 0.6 saturation), and the gear is data like the rest of the recipe, so the variants that spread a base recipe keep it. `tests/gear.test.ts`: each piece changes the picture on each body that carries it and in each pose, draws the same each time, is on no body that cannot carry it, and each of the five once-alike figures now wears something different.
- Playtest round 34 (12, "the lantern lights through walls"): the moon's shadows (round 34 (1)) are drawn only in the open night, so in a dungeon, which is lit by the lantern alone, a pillar or a wall's end threw no shadow and the light lay on whatever it reached in a straight line through them. The lantern now has its own map (`render/lanternShadow.ts`): a perspective depth map of 1024 texels drawn each frame from the investigator's chest in the way the camera looks (130°, as far as the lantern shines and two metres more), by a single draw a chunk. What blocks the light is not drawn a second time as geometry: `render/colliderShadow.ts` builds, from each chunk's colliders (the walls, pillars, trunks, house walls and stones the simulation already stops the investigator with), one mesh of closed solids on its own layer, and the pass draws their far faces only, so the face a room shows is never in its own shadow and no bias has to hide it (a bias of 6 cm, and 1 cm more for each metre, covers the map's texels). The world and sprite shaders take the shadow out of the lantern's share of the light, not the lamps' (a lit window is not dimmed by a wall in front of the lantern) and not the investigator's own (they carry it), at 0.88 so a wall is not black. Settings › Shadows (once "Moon shadows") turns both off, for a slower machine. Doors and people cast none (a door opens as bodies near; the moon's shadows already give people theirs). `tests/lanternShadow.test.ts` checks the solids look outwards, that with back-face rays a surface a room shows is lit and what lies behind it is not, and that the depth the shader reads is the metres it was drawn at.
- Round 35 (playtest notes, thirteen points): **fog** (`systems/fogGates.ts`, `render/bossFog.ts`): the fog before a horror was a picture walked through, and the horror saw across it. It is a wall while its horrors live: nothing crosses it (`fogBlockSystem` undoes a step that would, the investigator's too, leaps over three metres excepted), nothing sees or hears across it (`sight`, `hears`), and the shader is opaque from its foot to near its crown (the visible range is out to 138 m, so it does not thin away and show the horror). The one way through is E within 2.6 m (`interactable` kind `fog`, the prompt "pass through the fog"): the investigator is turned and walked through at 1.5 m/s, input theirs only for the camera, the fog parting about them in a ragged round, widest as they cross, mist torn off the wall in curls, a low sound, a billow as it closes behind (`FogPassing`, `FogPassed`). A fight that holds them (`veiled`) is not to be left by it. **shadows**: the lantern's depth map was aimed afresh at every frame's look, so its texels crawled across every edge as the camera turned; its aim now steps in 10° notches and only when the look is 17° off the one held, with eight fixed taps in place of four; the nearest three torches, braziers and street lamps each have a depth map of their own (`render/lampShadows.ts`: 512², drawn from the flame toward the investigator, a lamp keeps its map while it is among the nearest and its shadow fades in over a third of a second; `shaders/lampShadow.ts`). **menus**: gilt frames, brackets and plaques read as a modern game; a panel is now a leaf of a field journal (a near-black warm ground, one fine rule inside the edge), its choices lines of print, the chosen one marked by a small lozenge, sliders a hair line and a lozenge; `GOLD` is an old brass, the stamina's `LEAF` a drab olive (the green was too much). **night**: a journey opens at `CLOCK.start = 0.3`, the deep, not the gloaming; `NIGHT_DARK` takes the sky, mist, far land, ambient and moon down to about a quarter or a third at the deepest (`darkOf`, `render/realmLook.ts darkened`; the palette is built from the undimmed look, so it does not rebuild as the night turns); the minimap carries a hair line with a moon on it and the hour's name (GLOAMING, THE DEEP, THE WANING). The clock did not change: it is still one long night that turns (the dream has no day); the tests that opened in the gloaming now name their phase. **people**: they were never seen to walk: the movement system overwrote their previous position (`prev`) after `npcLife` had set it, so every step drew as standing; it now leaves those on a round alone (`managed`). Their rounds are strolls: a place chosen near home each time (within the hour's reach, 8 m in the gloaming, 5 in the deep, 0 in the last hour), along a curve (they turn at 1.7 rad/s, on the spot if far off the way), slowing through a bend and toward a stop, sometimes stopping only a breath. `render/npcFigures.ts`: coats with collars, lapels, buttons, belts and swinging tails, waistcoats, ties, faces with brows, eyes, ears, moustaches and beards, five cuts of hair, hats with bands, gloves, boots, and what each person is (spectacles, a watch chain, a satchel, a doctor's bag, a cane, a pipe, a flask, a shawl, a morion and cuirass, a knitted cap, a staff). **houses** (`render/houseMesh.ts`): each its own paint (eight for boards, six for brick), roof colour, pitch and form (gable, gambrel, saltbox), with wings and bays and a window in each gable and doors of five colours; the gable's UVs were four times the wall's and began afresh at the eaves (the bricks that looked wrong under the roof) and now run on from the wall; a soffit under each eave closes a slit between wall and roof that steep roofs left open (the view audit finds it on seeds it had not been run on: it fails the old code on twelve of twenty-four); now and then, in one lit window in five, a head and shoulders cross the glass and are gone (`paneFigure`, a few seconds in a round of a minute and a half or more). **water**: the sea is drawn again from a camera mirrored in its surface (`render/reflection.ts`, half size, only when there is sea about), read through its ripples by the sea's shader as much as the glancing angle makes a mirror, with the lamps' and the lantern's glitter; the world shader cuts what lies under the surface in that pass (`uClipY`). **foliage and woods**: ferns, tall seeding grass, wildflowers, toadstools and brambles join the ground cover by ground (more of it: 190 pieces a chunk on grass, 170 under leaves); the fill is half as much again and the trees stand closer in a thicket; the mist thickens with how wooded the land is (`woodOf`). The wind already moved trees and blades (round 34). **eyes**: in a mist, a creature that plainly sees the investigator shows its eyes glowing out of it from up to 38 m further off than usual, easing in over a second, with a blink now and then (`FOG_EYES`). Not verified in motion: the passing animation's timing, the sea's reflection beside a lit shore, how the eyes read at distance, and the walks by eye.
- Round 36 (torches glow through walls): two causes. A torch's halo is pulled toward the eye so it is not buried in its own wall, which drew it out through a dungeon's outer wall to anyone outside (`render/lightSight.ts`: a flame in a roofed room is shown only from its own room, or where a collision ray from the eye reaches it, redone every 8 frames per light, not each frame); and the world shader gave a face turned from a lamp half its light (`uLanternFacing`), so a wall's outer face took the torch's light through the stone. Lamps now light only faces turned toward them (0.92, none for the figures, whose own light stays). A halo is hidden by the ray only for roofed lights: under the sky the depth test already knows the land.
- Round 36 (menus, "worse in design, colour and cleanliness", "settings way too big", "cluttered"): one panel's look (`menuKit.ts`): ash-black ground, pale-brass hairlines, a chosen line lit by a soft wash and a lozenge (as the souls-likes do), sliders with a filled track; no colour but brass and bone (the neon green of the map's dropped Echoes is now pale brass). Pages are shorter and sorted: **tabs** (`tabs`; PageUp/PageDown or the bumpers, or ← → on a tab) for Settings (Display, Sound, Play, Controls: the keys' table is the last tab, so the pause menu and title lose their Controls line) and the Journal (Under way, Documents, Bestiary); every page ends in a **foot** (`footer`: a hint line saying what the chosen line is, from `data-hint`, and the keys it answers to for the device in hand: `menuKeys.ts`). The pause menu groups its lines under hairlines; Arms lists the weapons as lines and shows the chosen one's note and numbers in the hint instead of stacking every weapon's text. Judgement: tabs, not a second screen or scaling the panel down, so no page needs a scroll at 960×540.
- Round 37 (the torches "still" glow through the walls and are seen on the outside: a night street beside a brick building, four round halos in a row on its outer wall and a warm patch on its bricks): round 36 hid a roofed light's halo by a ray to the flame and took the lamps' light off faces turned from them (0.92), which left the cause and some of the effect. (a) Where a torch hung. `torchStops` sets them on a four-metre grid along each wall, which is also the grid of the pilasters (the 1.4 m buttress blocks that stand out from a wall), and a stack of shelves (the 6 m blocks of the library and the townhouses) is built against a wall with its face inside the wall's own thickness. 1124 of the 1694 lights of the dungeons (1654 torches and 40 braziers) hung inside such a solid, 66%; their bracket was out of sight, their halo (drawn toward the eye by 0.8 of its radius, so the wall it hangs on does not cut it) came out of the facade's outer face, and their light lay on the brick outside. `render/wallTorches.ts` moves a torch to the bay beside (2 m, then 1 m, either way), or to the wall's other face where a room lies on each side, or hangs none: 1002 hang now, none inside anything (`tests/sconces.test.ts` asserts it, and that every dungeon with flames keeps at least twelve). (b) The sight itself, which the halo alone had. The twelve lamp slots went to the nearest twelve whatever stood between (the library from its street filled all twelve with torches inside it), and the mist's glow of a lamp is the slots'. `render/lightSight.ts` (round 36's, replaced) gives each torch and fire a share, eased (a tenth of a second) toward what a ray from the camera to a point 10 cm before it and 40 cm above it finds in the colliders and the ground (above, so a flame is seen over the ring of stones its embers lie in); its halo, its point light and the mist's glow of it take the share, and the slots (and the lamps that cast shadows, which are chosen from them) go to the lights that show, so a hall's torches no longer lose theirs to those in the next room. Street lamps, lit windows (which fade by the angle they are seen at) and Elder Signs (which stand among their own stones) are not looked at: nothing hides them but the land, which the depth test knows. Ten lights are looked at a frame, the longest unlooked first (a ray is a walk over twelve points of ground and the colliders about it), and an eye pressed into a wall leaves what was seen as it was. Not changed: `lightAt` (the mind still mends by a lamp behind a wall: a gameplay number, left alone), and a door is no collider, so a light on the line of an open doorway shows along it. `tests/lightSight.test.ts`: a torch behind a wall is dark and one before it whole, a flame above its ring shows, the share eases and does not pop, a light out of reach starts afresh, lamps and the like are shown without a ray, and from the street at six places round each, the library, the townhouse and the cellar light nothing of what is inside them (their doors shut), while the library's foyer is lit to whoever stands in it; without the eye's sight the same test finds the twelve slots full.
- Round 36 (controllers in the desktop build): `navigator.getGamepads()` lists only nulls in Chromium/Electron on macOS (Safari's works), so the shell reads pads natively with `gamepad-node` (the Gamepad API over SDL2; added to `dependencies` at the owner's request, which the rules ask about). Judgement calls: it runs in a **utility process** (`desktop/padWorker.js`, started by `main.js`), not the main process, so SDL gets a plain Node (as gamepad-node's own tester has) apart from Electron's NSApplication, and a fault in the native module stops only the pads (restarted up to 3 times, then the browser's list alone). It sends plain snapshots (`padSnapshot.js`: standard layout, as gamepad-node gives every pad; sent only when they change) to the main process, which pushes them to the page's preload (`window.desktop.pads()`). `core/pads.ts` reads them **as the browser's own list is read** (the same rest/stuck/used rules, the standard-mapped pad used most recently in hand), the browser's first; where both list the same pad it stays with the one in hand for 4 s rather than flip between two readings. The web build has no bridge and is unchanged. Not done: a packager does not exist yet (round 12), so when one is chosen, `@kmamal/sdl`'s native binding and `desktop/padWorker.js` must be unpacked from the asar (`asarUnpack`) and the binding rebuilt or fetched for each target; rumble (gamepad-node has it) is not wired.
- Round 38 (playtest; foe damage setting): the game had no way to make the dream kinder or harder, so a player who finds it too much, or too little, had only the NG+ journeys. Settings › Play gains **Foe damage** (50–150%, by quarters; `SETTINGS.foeBlows`): a weight on every enemy's blow, laid over the journey's own (`systems/cycles.ts` `foeDamage`, read from `Game.assist`, which `main.ts` fills from the settings each frame). Judgement: the Echoes foes leave and their health are unchanged (it is an assist and a harder road, not a second economy), the investigator's own blows are untouched, and the setting is not in the save (it is the player's, like the sensitivity).
- Round 38 (menus that ran off the panel): a long page (53 tomes, 100 creatures, 22 achievements, the Elder Sign's fourteen lines) pushed its foot and Back out of the panel at 960 × 540, and the mouse had no way back from the pages that had lost their Back button. `footer()` now pins a page's title and tabs over a body that scrolls (`.scroll`, `menuParts.ts`) and the foot under it; the foot's Back is clickable. A page with nothing to choose (Achievements) is scrolled by the arrows. The Elder Sign's menu is three tabs (Grow: levels and the mind's upgrades; Arms: star-stones into weapons and the revolver, no longer a page of its own; Travel: the regions) under one line of what is held (level, Echoes, insight, star-stones), with the court's ending and the Sleeper's stair pinned above them when they are there; the rest's blurb is the foot's line until a choice has its own. The merchant's wares are plain lines, the chosen one's note in the foot, and a ware that cannot be bought is still a line (dimmed): choosing it says why. Documents are listed under Tomes and Notes; creatures under their tier with a count. `menuKit.ts` was 342 lines: its style sheet and builders are `menuParts.ts` (re-exported).
- Round 38 (what the playtest weighed, and left alone): the Straight Razor's chain did 60 in 96 frames at 1.5 damage a stamina against the cane's 41 a second and 1.6, with the shortest reach: nothing a person would keep it for. Its cuts are 15, 15, 16 and 22 now (68 in the same time: 42 a second, 1.7 a stamina), its reach and its speed as they were. Left as it is: the lesser foes die to a bot of the first level in 3–13 seconds and deal it 0–60 of its 160 health (the greater ones, met later, many times that: Shoggoth, Gug, Star-spawn and Dhole each take 7–10 lives from a level-0 bot), which is the shape a first region wants. A test now holds that every creature of the roster, and every entry of every region's spawn table, is placed somewhere in the world (`tests/coverage.test.ts`; a first reading of the fixed spawns alone said five were never met, and was wrong: the open ground's are in the region plans). The test clock is 30 s for every test (`vitest.config.ts`): the world-building tests fail by the clock when the machine is busy. Menu panels are `border-box`, bounded to 90% of the window with the padding in it (they were 90% plus 40 px), so at the smallest window the shell allows (640 × 360) the foot is still on screen.
- Round 38 (coming back to the game): the two places a returning player looks first now say where they stand. The pause menu says what the story asks next under its title (`PauseOptions.next`, from `systems/lead.ts`; one line, trimmed with an ellipsis, the whole of it on hover), and the title's Continue has the slot's line under it (where, level, time played: `titleSlots.ts`). The pause list was tightened to keep all eight lines on screen at 960 × 540 with it.
- Round 38 (retention: the first hours and the collectors): the first achievement came with a second Elder Sign and the next with a slain boss, so a person who plays an hour had earned one thing, and a collector had nothing to chase but 50 places. Six more (28 in all): First Growth (a first level bought), A Reader of Dangerous Books (ten tomes and notes read), Field Notes (25 creatures beheld), A Room of Arms (every weapon found), Natural History of the Dream (75 beheld), The Whole Library (every tome and note read). They pay nothing (a recognition, not an economy). Judgement: 'read' counts only what is a document (what is taken for good, a vial or a cache, is kept in the same set); 'beheld' is the bestiary's own count, short of the 100 because a creature seen only at the edge of madness (the Being from Beyond) and the allies are not met in an ordinary run.
- Round 38 (the Foe damage setting becomes a difficulty): a slider that could be moved at any hour was a way to lower a fight's cost after losing it. Three difficulties are chosen once, on a new game, after the slot (and after the question of overwriting one): Light Slumber (foes' blows ×0.6), Deep Slumber (×1: the dream as made, where the choice begins) and Nightmare (×1.5). It is `Pilot.difficulty`, kept in the save and carried into a second journey (`Carry.difficulty`), and there is no way in the game to change it afterwards: not in Settings (the slider is gone), not on load (a save brings its own, and one from before it is Deep Slumber, whatever the title would have chosen), not at an ending. Only the weight of enemy blows changes (health, Echoes and the journeys' own weight are laid over it unchanged); the slot's line and the Elder Sign's menu name a difficulty that is not the default. `?fresh` begins at Deep Slumber.
- Round 38 (the menus' look, chosen): five skins were built and shown (burnt paper with glowing edges, a gothic window, a standing stone, a shroud, mist), each with its own shape, moving art and opening; the owner chose the **mist** and asked for no edge and no box at all. The others are deleted. The mist's ground is a dark rectangle faded out by gradients on its sides and corners (it was a stack of rounded rectangles whose steps showed as a box), with a drift of purple-cast mist beyond it; the thread of light along its foot is gone.
