Pages and tabs now come in with a quick blur-fade (190 ms, each part 24 ms after the one above; a tab changes only the body, 150 ms), and not at all for a player who asks the system for reduced motion. The title: the flight of seventy treads is gone from the wordmark (it read as cheap), the name and its two lines stand in a layer of their own centred in the upper half, the menu in the lower, and Settings and Credits stand at the middle with the name faded; the wordmark's canvas is clear where it is black, so it is no box on the dark. The NPCs' spectacles are thin rims, a bridge and arms round eyes with a white and a dark to them (they were two solid lenses and a bar across the whole face, which read as sunglasses).
- Round 38 (menus, again): the mist's edge was still seen in two places: banks of mist cut off by the canvas's edge, and the rules, hint line and focus bar ending hard. The canvas now reaches 200 px past the words and is masked to fade out before its edge; the rules and the focus bar fade out at both ends. The mist is one weather kept across pages (it no longer re-seeds when a page changes size, which read as the menu opening again), and the ground eases to each page's size.
- Round 38: choosing a page (Settings, Arms…) or coming back lifts the old page away (a copy drifts off, blurring) as the mist stirs and the new page drifts in from the other side, ~280 ms; tabs keep their quick in-place blur-fade. The pause menu's NEXT line is gone (the Elder Sign menu keeps its own).
- Round 38 (pause menu reopening): Esc, a page, Esc, Esc reopened the menu. The page's click is a user gesture, so resuming by Esc takes the mouse in the same press the browser takes it back with (Esc lets a locked mouse go), and that let-go was read as leaving the game. A let-go within 600 ms of resuming is now ignored and the mouse taken again 150 ms later; any later let-go still pauses. Not reproduced here (headless has no real Esc exit); found by reading the order of events.
- Round 39 (the mouse after a menu): only the pause menu and the map took the mouse back when they closed; the Elder Sign menu, a shop, a reading, the ending card all left it free until a click on the world. Now `menuKit` says when the last menu has closed (and none opened in its place) and `main.ts` captures at once. Where the browser refuses (a menu left with Esc and no click or key since: Esc is not a gesture), a key press or a click takes it, and till then the mouse looks about anyway (`createInput`'s `free`). The world's canvas has no pointer. The menus draw their own cursor (`ui/cursor.ts`: a slit eye in a turning diamond, lit over what can be pressed), only while a menu is open and the mouse is free, hidden in the same moment the last menu closes; not shown for a pad. Pointer lock stays released in menus (kept only for a talk), rather than a virtual cursor over a locked mouse: a locked mouse cannot hover or click, and Esc lets it go in the browser regardless.
- Round 39 (pause menu after Esc in a secondary screen): the Elder Sign menu and the shop took the mouse back on closing (new this round) in the same Esc press the browser lets a captured mouse go with, and the pause menu read that let-go as leaving the game. Every ask for the mouse is now noted (`core/mouseLock.ts`), and a let-go within a second of one is ignored and the mouse asked for again once, 150 ms on.
- Round 39 (the wake's crane): it was three shots, each eased to a stop and away again, so the camera halted twice on its way up and the rise read as steps. It is now one shot with a `glide` ease (smootherstep: no jolt at either end); the lens's pull-in short of a wall lets out slowly rather than popping, its floor is a soft maximum, and a cutscene that stands the world still runs the render clock by the frame, not by the 60 Hz step (the rise and the sway were stepped on a display faster than that). `tests/cinemaWake.test.ts` holds it to no stop and no step.
- Round 39 (the people): each has one act of their own from the story (`data/npcActs.ts`: Peaslee reads, Gilman sets down sums, Morgan holds a vial to the light, Kuranes gazes up, Zadok drinks, Wilmarth smokes, Willett consults his watch, Curtis whittles, Dyer studies a chart, Zamacona polishes a blade, Johansen splices rope, Akeley sits too still, Carter turns the silver key, Nathaniel leans on his cane) and is found at it where they rose, sitting on a crate or stump where it sits. A breather every 45-100 s (stand, a few paces, look about, back) replaces the constant pacing; near the investigator they stand and turn as before, and sit again once alone; the last hour is dozing on their feet as before. Fishing was planned for Zadok and dropped: no sign stands within 300 m of water (Innsmouth's square is inland), so there was no shore to give him; it is a kind that can be added where a sign stands by water. No one is in view through the wake (`render/npcVeil.ts`: they are held back from the new game's start, and come in by the dither the ghosts use, a second after it ends); they stand beside the sign, 4 m from where the investigator rises, so they are the first thing found.
- Round 39 (the cursor): the lantern mote was chosen from five (a mote of lantern light, three motes in orbit about it, a wisp of fading light behind the hand, swelling over what can be pressed and bursting softly on a click). The orbits sat off the mote in the mock-up because CSS turns an SVG group about the viewBox's (0,0) as the top left of its reference box, not its middle; they are turned by SMIL about the origin now, which is the mote, and the old bobbing (which moved the mote off the point by a pixel) is a breathing of the glow instead. Checked in a browser at six moments of the orbits: each stays at its own radius from the mote's middle, and the middle is the mouse's point. Reduced motion: the orbits stand still, with no wisp.
- Round 39 (the figures): the player and the people were built of plain boxes, every edge a right angle. They keep their rig (the same joints, so every pose and act still fits) and their blockiness, but the forms are now `render/bodyKit.ts` hulls with every edge chamfered: tapering sleeves, thighs and shins; a waist that gathers, a chest that broadens and shoulders that slope; a skull broad at the brow and narrow at the jaw, with a nose bridge, cheekbones, mouth and chin; fists with curled fingers and a thumb; boots and shoes with soles, heels and toe-caps; a shawl, a breastplate and a robe's hem shaped rather than flat. The investigator gains tall leather boots, a turned-up collar, epaulettes, pocket flaps and a knotted scarf; each person has their own height and girth (`WHO.build`, applied as the figure's scale). Blender was not used: not reachable from the cloud session, and the project makes its meshes in code.
- Round 39 (shadows and light): the shadow maps (moon, lantern, three lamps) were already strong, so what was missing was what they cannot give. The post pass now takes a contact shadow (eight-tap screen-space occlusion over the scene's depth, 0.75 m about a point: the feet of things, the bases of walls and pillars, creases), bloom (what shines, flames and windows and the lantern, bleeds seven low-res pixels into what is about it) and a vignette; surfaces take a hemisphere's ambient (the sky's on what faces up, the dark ground's on what faces down) instead of one flat share; floors catch the lantern and the lamps in a damp streak (stronger on pale stone and in rain, a gloss that varies with the noise of the ground, the lantern's streak held to 40% so it is not a hot spot); and the lantern's flame breathes by 4.5%. Every number is in `LIGHTING` (data/fxTuning.ts). Seen in stills in the hub, a town street and the library; not seen moving, not on a real GPU, so the cost of the occlusion and bloom taps is unmeasured there (about 20 more texture reads a pixel, at the low-res size).
- Round 39 (the opening and the keys): the telegram's capitals and its "STOP" are gone and the four dense cards are five short ones in sentence case (at most 45 words each; the old ones ran to a hundred), kept to what the player needs: someone asleep in Arkham, a sleeper's dream of a stair and a key and a tall man, Armitage's errand, the Elder Sign where they may rest, the lamp, and the waking. The panel is the menus' mist skin on black, each card out of the mist with its lines fading in in turn, and a turn is the same ghost-drift as any page. Each card is read aloud by a narrator (Isaac, a library voice: resonant and mellow, with a faint hall) from `data/intro.ts`'s performed `say`, shown with its tags taken out (`shown`); it is chosen unheard, like the cast before it. The card turns by itself 1.4 s after its voice ends (E turns it at once, Esc skips all); with no recording to hear (no sound, no clip) it waits as long as the words take to read. The journal has a key of its own, J, rebindable with the rest (leaving it by J or Esc returns to the game, not the pause menu); the pad has no button to spare, so its prompt reads Menu. The HUD names the two keys in the one small line it already had under the minimap (`M MAP · J JOURNAL`, or the pad's words), rather than a new strip. Those who have something for the investigator wear a small five-branched star over their heads (`systems/questMarks.ts`, `ui/questMarks.ts`): pale violet to begin a quest, bone-white with a lit heart where a quest under way waits on a word with them; shown in plain sight within sixty paces, gone as the investigator comes within four (the talk prompt is enough).
- Round 39 (the narrator, recast): Isaac was too generic and not deep enough, so the author chose Finley - Articulate Anchor from the library. The intro's lines use [solemnly], [calmly], [thoughtfully] and [ominously], never a whisper, and the clips were made again (their names are unchanged: a clip is named by its speaker and words, not its tags or voice). The licences of the cast's other library voices could not be read through the connector, and are listed in docs/VOICES.md to be checked in the app.
- Round 39 (Peaslee and Morgan, continuous presence): the wake used to hide everyone behind a veil and dither them in once it ended, which is a fade-in. The veil (`render/npcVeil.ts`) is gone. Peaslee and Morgan are now simply elsewhere, each with a place of their own (`Post` in `data/npcActs.ts`, in world coordinates: a data table, with tests that the street lamp each stands by is still there and that they lie more than 100 degrees from the way the investigator faces on waking, behind the camera): Peaslee seated in a dark-wood armchair with a small table, books and a cup, beside the eastern lamp of the quad (11.7 paces from the sign, in its light), reading, and Morgan leaning back against the southern lamp's post (14 paces), one foot crossed over the other, an arm across his chest, the pipe in the other hand: held at the chest, now and then to the mouth for a long draw, and the breath let out (`pipeBeat`). The pipe smokes faintly: a thread from the bowl and puffs with the breath (`render/pipeSmoke.ts`, on the existing particles). Those with a post keep to it all night (no breathers, no dozing at the sign), and when the investigator comes near or talks to them they stand and turn, stepping clear of the chair or the post (the chair set is the world's: it is counter-transformed in `actorViews` so it stays as the figure turns), then take it up again when the investigator has gone. `npcPlace` returns the post, so the lead, the quest star, the map and the talk prompt all find them where they are. The other eleven keep their acts by their signs. The other hub NPCs' acts are unchanged; Morgan's vial is dropped for the pipe (`vial` stays as an act kind, unused).
- Round 39 (A and B swapped on a pad): reported as A backing out and B choosing. Nothing in the game's own pad code (A is button 0 and chooses, B is button 1 and goes back) had changed, and no hardware was to hand, so this is a diagnosis, not a reproduction: the desktop shell reads pads with SDL, which names a pad's face buttons by their printed labels where it can (a Nintendo-style pad's A is the one on the right) while the Gamepad API's standard mapping, which the game reads, is by place. The shell's worker now sets SDL_GAMECONTROLLER_USE_BUTTON_LABELS=0 before SDL starts, and where the browser lists the same pad in its raw (non-standard) order and the shell in the standard one, the standard one is read (it used to be the browser's first). Both are no-ops for an Xbox-style pad on the web.
- Round 39 (the opening, watched through untouched): five defects found. (1) The menus' cursor stood in the middle of the screen, on the words, from the first card to the last, though nobody had touched the mouse: it now shows only once the hand has moved since a menu opened (every menu, not only the intro). (2) The cards were pages of their own, each a different height, so the panel re-centred and the words jumped as they changed: the intro is one page now, with a stage of one height, the heading always on the same line. (3) A page change drifted the old words away over the new while a second, slower fade of the intro's own ran under it (the words came in, vanished and came in again): the intro changes its cards itself, the words of one sinking into the mist (1.3 s), a breath of mist alone, the next coming out of it (1.6 s, a line after the heading). (4) 'Go on' was a button, focused, so it wore the menus' lit focus box: it is a faint line now (E, Enter, Space, a click or the pad's A turn the card; Esc or B skip). (5) The end was a cut: now the last card's words and its key line fade, then the mist fades to the loading veil. The telegram's take was brighter than the other four (pitch higher and moving more: a median of 100 Hz and a spread of 4.5 semitones, against 95 to 100 Hz and 3.6 to 4 for the deeper ones); four new takes in graver tags ([somberly], [gravely], [heavily], no whisper) were measured and the steadiest kept (95 Hz, 3.5 semitones). Measured, not heard.
- Round 39 (the people at their act, the opening's corners and mist): a person at their act (`atAct`) no longer turns to the investigator when near, nor when spoken with, and does not put the act down: they keep to it (npcLife.ts, npcs.ts); a stroller still stands and faces them. The intro's keys ('skip' left, 'go on' right) sit in the screen's lower corners, and the menus' mist now gathers over the middle (`Screen.stir`, a radial surge in `skins/mist.ts`) as a card's words sink and as the next comes, so it is never bare. `docs/SUNO_SPEECH.md` holds the cards in plain words and one delivery prompt for Suno Speech.
- Round 39 (the narrator in a room): the Suno narration was tried and sounded worse, so the Eleven recordings stay, treated at playback (`render/audio/voiceRoom.ts`, `room` in the cast): high-pass 85 Hz, +2.5 dB low shelf, +1.8 dB at 3.2 kHz, a 2.5:1 compressor, and a convolution reverb (generated impulse, 2.1 s to -60 dB, 32 ms pre-delay, darkened at 4.2 kHz, wet 0.3) replacing the narrator's slap-back echo. Numbers were set by ear-less judgement (restrained values), not listened to: `CHAMBER` is the one place to tune them.
- Round 40 (dungeon light): a roofed room's brightness swung with the hour: the moon's shadow is switched off under a roof but its light was not, so the stone took the unshadowed moon whose direction followed the night's course (the floor's N·L from 0.6 to 0.9: about twice the light at the moon's peak, a flat grey-blue wash: the 'overcast'), the clock's darkness curve scaled the ambient, the last hour's grey lift (nightFx) was added indoors, and `enclosed` (the camera's cell) was a step in the light at every door. Measured by opening the library at five hours in a headless browser: ambient, moon and direction were the same at no two. Now `DUNGEON_LIGHT` (lifeTuning): under a roof the moon is held at its slant at the night's start, at 0.55 of its strength, with the darkness at the start's (x0.35), the dawn grey off, and all of it eased at 1.3/s through a door (`nightFx` and `realmLook` ease; main.ts gives the fixed dark). Also fixed in passing: `nightFx` read `uHazeColor`, which the realms' skies (round 32) no longer have, so the game threw on the first frame of the last hour (about 7.5 minutes into a night): it lifts the sky's horizon and zenith instead (sky.ts sets those anew each frame). Not listened to nor played by hand: a headless render at 1 to 2 fps, so judged by the uniforms (equal across hours) and stills.
- Round 40 (sound): the sound libraries (Freesound and the like) are not reachable from the sandbox this was written in (the egress policy denies them), so no recording could be searched for or downloaded. What the game lacked is made in code instead (the project's own rule), each recipe measured by an offline render and kept in a loudness window by tests; and each sound a recording would improve on is listed with a Suno Sounds prompt (`data/plannedSounds.ts` → `docs/SUNO_SOUNDS.md`), the game ready for the files (they play in front of their recipe once present, fit to their motion where it has a length). Doors: one thud at the start of opening, for every door, at no place, the whole length of its sample, and none on closing, became a recipe made for each swing (`doorSound`), by material, open and shut, placed and dulled by distance, drawn anew each time; a 'none' creak (the dream's curtains) now rustles. Menus: one triangle blip for everything became seven sounds; the opening mutes them. Everything that plays a recipe is varied (`vary.ts`; `STINGER_VARY`). The recordings' takes (cut to one peak) were up to 13 dB apart within a set: levelled to their set's mean at play (`takeTrim`), the set's gain unchanged. The fog wall's 4.5 s rumble sample at the start of a 0.7 to 3 s walk became a swell as long as the walk (`FogPassing` now carries its `frames`) with a closing exhale; `Echoes` carries `on: 'ware' | 'level'` so a ware's coins are heard and a level has its own sound. Not listened to: all judged by offline renders (levels, lengths, where the loudest moment falls) and by a headless run that played them without error.
- Round 40 (recordings found): with Freesound reachable, the planned sounds were searched on its CC0-filtered search, the previews downloaded and cut by their silences into single events (`tools` kept out of the repo: a scratch script; each cut named in `public/audio/CREDITS.md` with its source and range): 55 files for 21 of the 22 planned sets, mono 96 kbps MP3 at a −1.5 dBFS peak, played in front of their recipes (a third of the recipe stays beneath), gains set so each lands near its recipe's level (a recorded set's gain is scaled by the recipe's class gain, capped at 1). Cut blind, by title, length and silence structure, not listened to: a take that is not what its title says is replaced by editing one line of the pick and re-cutting. `pipeDraw` found nothing it could be cut from reliably (the pipe's inhale is in long files with ember crackle and no clean single draw) and stays a Suno prompt (`docs/SUNO_SOUNDS.md`, now only unrecorded sets). Any take 15 dB off its set's level was replaced (a creak with a click for a peak).
- Round 41 (voices): only premade or Voice-Design voices are cast, because a library voice's licence cannot be told through the tools. Peaslee, Morgan, Kuranes, Zadok, Zamacona, Johansen, Akeley, Nasht and 23 horrors were given designed voices and their 112 lines made again (Eleven v4, one take each, picked among the voice's three previews by what could be measured: nobody can listen here). The workspace holds only 30 custom voices, so Yog-Sothoth, 'Umr at-Tawil and Shub-Niggurath share the Great Ones', the priests' and Hydra's voices with their own rate and hall. The narrator (Finley, a library voice the author chose) is left as it is and flagged in docs/VOICES.md.
- Round 42 (voices): Peaslee and Morgan measured alike (median ~107 Hz) and ran slow. With no free voice slot and no premade old voice, their clips were processed offline (pitch by resampling, tempo by WSOLA, pauses over 0.4 s cut to 0.22 s): Peaslee to ~95 Hz and 12% brisker, Morgan to ~122 Hz, +4 dB above 2.5 kHz and 10% brisker (clips 15–25% shorter; names unchanged). Every horror now has a hall (`echo` 0.15–0.7 in `speechCast.ts`; a test keeps it so). Cleaner fix when slots allow: delete the unused library voices in the ElevenLabs workspace and design Morgan a voice of his own.
- Round 43 (licence, credits): LICENSE is a custom source-available, view-only licence (read, study, evaluate; no copying, redistribution, modification, commercial use, trademark use or model training), matching the README. The holder is Alessandro A. Foddis (DigitalDro). Credits open with his name; the AI tools (ElevenLabs, Suno, Claude Code) are named truthfully but in a small, dim `TOOLS` block at the foot, after the thanks, instead of among the names. Steam's AI-content disclosure is unaffected: the Steam page must still state it. Not legal advice: have the wording checked before release.
- Round 44 (boss themes): forty-eight Suno themes, one per horror or pair, in `public/music/bosses` (the Drive folder "Boss themes": 3 tier folders, 48 boss folders, one MP3 each, none empty, no duplicates, the titles as given; fetched with the Drive connector to disk, decoded by script, each size equal to Drive's, the bytes never printed; the MP3s untouched, 206.97 MB, 48 kHz stereo, 118 to 240 s, about 185 to 198 kbps, so the repository grows by 198 MiB as the realms' 78 MB did, MP3s only). The recorded theme plays while any engaged fight maps to it and the procedural score (now `bossScore.ts`) stays the fallback; `createBossMusic(e)` chooses. Every number, and how it was got: **Where it plays** (`tools/boss_music.py` -> `bossMusicMap.ts`; PyAV and numpy): 24 log bands (50 Hz to 14 kHz) of a 2048/512 STFT; onset flux; tempo from its autocorrelation (prior 100 bpm +-0.7 octave, 55 to 190 bpm, a pulse if the peak is at least 0.10: 47 of 48 have one; Dripping Stone, a drone, has none); beats by Ellis's dynamic programme (tightness 100) so the take's drift is followed; a bar of 3 or 4 beats by the autocorrelation at its multiples (the waltzes come out in 3; where it is a close call, a loop is whole twelves of beats); loops on bar lines, whole pairs of bars, inside the body (windows of 0.5 s at 0.85 of the median, as `loudness.ts` reads it), at least 25 s (45 s tried first); candidates ranked by how alike the music is across the seam, the best 24 tried by sound (the real crossfade mixed, its level step and flux jump measured) and accepted at a step of at most 1.5 dB and a flux of at most 1.5 of the loop's own 99th percentile; the crossfade whole bars (two, else one) when within 3 s, else whole beats (only then do the two passes' drums fall together in it: first made 3 s flat, which put the beats 0.1 s apart in 8 tracks; found by thinking, not by ear); `entry` 0 where the drive is within 10 s (45 of 48; the others start 6 s before it, on a bar line: 6.6 s, 10.1 s, 6.3 s); `hot` the most intense bar-aligned stretch (12 to 40 s, after the entry, inside the body, mean of z(level), z(onset density) and z(brightness) at least 0.3 sigma and 1 dB over the loop, its seam judged as the loop's): 13 tracks (the others have no stretch that stands clear: none is forced). The measurement was first misread twice (a 3.5 s lead made the "6 s before" median a single window; a different flux floor and band edges made the browser read 7 to 40% more): both ends now measure the same windows, and the browser, rendering the real scheduler, has the last word. **Seams** (Chromium, `OfflineAudioContext`, two passes of each loop): 41 loops cut on the beat step at most 1.40 dB with a flux at most 1.35; the 13 hot loops 1.34 dB and 1.49; every move to a hot stretch (13) lands on a bar line (0.000 of a bar, to 0.002). **Weakened:** 7 tracks have no cut within those limits (the best of each stepped 1.53 to 2.54 dB) and loop the realms' way, a ten-second crossfade of the body found as they load (`canal_blood_ritual`, `dripping_stone`, `fragile_defiance`, `the_alchemists_oath`, `the_hollow_bell`, `the_pendulums_last_waltz`, `the_swarm_awakens`; steps 3.8 to 20.8 dB spread over ten seconds, flux 1.2 to 3.3): the likeliest to sound wrong, and mended with `OVERRIDES.<id>.loop`; none skipped, none empty. **Level:** the gain brings the body of what plays (the first pass and the loop) to `BOSS_MUSIC.target` with no peak over 0.8 (the hot stretch alone is trimmed to the ceiling and to 3 dB over its loop: it measured +0.7 to +3.6 dB); the target is **-23 dBFS**, measured at the output in headless Chromium over 25 s with the effects and ambience of four fights (Cthulhu, Brown Jenkin, the Dunwich Horror, Hastur): the procedural score's total is -21.8 dBFS (it is gappy at headless's low frame rate, so only the totals are compared; its music alone reads -44 dBFS RMS at the scores' bus); a theme at -18 made the total -17.7 (+4.1 dB), at -21 -18.9 (+2.9), at -23 -19.6 (+2.3 dB: +1.7 to +2.8): within about 2 dB, and the limiter (-10 dB, 8:1) is barely touched (mean reduction at most 0.012 dB, worst 0.17 dB), so nothing pumps. Each file through the real scheduler: -22.3 to -23.3 dBFS (the trimmed -24.7 to -24.9), peaks at most -7.8 dBFS. **Trims:** Azathoth, the Haunter and the Ancient Ones are -2 dB, as the author asked for the thin and dynamic; the numbers do not quite agree with the description (the Ancient Ones are the steadiest, 2.6 dB between the 10th and 90th percentile of their level, and with the Haunter the brightest, 1116 and 1052 Hz; Azathoth the darkest, 479 Hz; the widest are `the_swarm_awakens` 10.1 dB and `dripping_stone` 9.8, which the realms' loop and the body's gain already leave quiet): the trim is kept as asked, and the three are the ones to listen to for thinness. **Playing:** fade in 2.5 s (0.8 s where the entry drives within 3 s), out 3.5 s, a handover to another theme 3 s, the takeover from a procedural score that began first 2.5 s; each later phase lifts 1 dB and 1 dB of highs (a shelf above 3 kHz, 1.5 s to settle), up to two; the move to the hot stretch one bar long; a horror's line ducks 5 dB (0.12 s in, 1.1 s out); the arrival scene holds 14 dB down (measured in the engine: the theme read 12 to 17 dB under its steady level while `cinema.active`) and rises over 0.4 s from the next bar line; two themes held, a failed file asked for again after 45 s, the nearest living horror within 160 m of its ring warmed. **Code:** `trackCache.ts` is the realms' fetch, decode, keep-two and retry, shared (`realmMusic.ts` uses it: unchanged to the ear, checked by a real session); `realmLoop.ts` gained an optional first-pass offset and `position`; `loudness.ts` `analyse` an optional target and ceiling; `GameAudio.update` an optional `cinematic` (main.ts passes `cinema.active`); `gameAudio.ts` stays at 304 lines (it was 303). Not heard by anyone: it is all measurement.
- **Round 45 (pad A/B, again):** no pad to hand, so the cause on the player's pad is not reproduced; the guard is structural. Every pad index now lives in `core/padMap.ts` (`PAD_BUTTON`) and `input`, `menuKit`, `cinema`, `intro` and `mapScreen` name buttons from it; `tests/padMap.test.ts` pins A=0/B=1, fails on any pad index written as a number in `src`, on the prompts' A/B names, and on `desktop/padWorker.js` losing its SDL by-place hint before `gamepad-node` loads. For a pad that still reports them swapped (Nintendo-style layout, a driver naming by printed label) there is a kept setting, Settings › Controls › Swap A / B (`padSwap`), applied where the pad is read (`core/pads.ts`) so every reader and the prompts follow it at once. Default off: swapping automatically by pad id would guess wrong for third-party pads.
- **Round 45 (Morgan, the mist):** Morgan's four recordings are processed again from the unprocessed takes (pitch x0.80 to ~88 Hz, pace 96%, +5 dB low shelf at 220 Hz, -3.5 dB high shelf at 5.5 kHz, tanh grit): round 42 had raised him to ~122 Hz and brightened him, which read as a thin sick old man. The menus' and the intro's mist is back to the discs of before f8b6999 (`skins/mist.ts`, `menuSkin.ts` restored from that commit's parent; the warped-noise layers and the held surge level of `seek` are gone).
- **Round 45 (Morgan's voice, again):** Morgan's four lines are re-recorded in the library voice Josef Hammer (`AFtA63zAzQAlNDuzSRKy`, chosen by the author from Julian, Josef Hammer and Jay Anderson) and used as recorded: the processed designed voice (lowered, then raised before it) was rejected twice, once for thin and sickly, once for unnaturally deep. It is a library voice whose licence the tools cannot show (round 42 had dropped it for that reason); the author chose it, and is to confirm its terms.
- **Round 45 (the opening's card transitions):** the intro's surge (mist brighter and closer, words fading) is replaced by ash, the author's pick of three previews (Unravel, Tide, Ash): a card's words break into fine grains in patches on a gust, leave a few specks and a faint haze of their shape, and the next card is the same grains returning and settling into letters; the ambient mist never changes. Cards are now drawn on a canvas (`ui/ashCard.ts`, so the words can be sampled; the canvas names them for a screen reader) and the numbers are those of the preview (`ui/ashFx.ts`, pure, `tests/ashFx.test.ts`). Dissolve 2.2 s, a 0.4 s breath, return 2.4 s; a card turned early dissolves from however far it had formed; each transition draws its own grains and blows the other way (odd seeds left). The grid is 0.75 cells per css pixel, redrawn about 30 times a second: a frame cost 13 ms at 1.1 cells and 60 fps in a Node benchmark, too much beside the world. `screen.stir` is no longer used by the opening (menus still use it on a page change).
- **Round 45 (stars, menus, fair foes):** (1) The rings about the stars were the bloom's twelve taps in two rings (`shaders/lighting.ts` `bloomAt`), which drew a circle of dots about any bright speck; taps that land on the open sky (depth 1: the sky writes none) now add nothing, so stars no longer spill and lamps still do. (2) A screen that closes now goes the way it opened, backwards (`menuSkin` `fade`, 0.8 s, the words blurring away, the mist rolling off, the backdrop clearing, over the game that is already let back in; `close(true)` where another screen or the loading follows: the title's start, the intro's end, the pause menu's Map). (3) A page change no longer slides the old page sideways and surges the mist: the words being left dissolve where they stand (blur, a lift, 0.26 s), the new page gathers a moment after, each part a little after the one above (0.42 s, 45 ms apart), and the mist drifts a little instead of swelling and brightening (`menuKit` `bring`, `skins/mist.ts`). (4) Fair foes, by rules and not one creature at a time: a ranged blow shows itself for at least 0.5 s and its bolt can be rolled from (projectile 26 to 40 frames and 16 to 13 m/s, fan 34 to 46 and 12 to 10, beam 40 to 52, spit 20 to 32, barrage 30 to 38, wind 24 to 30); melee openers show 0.3 s (bite 14 to 18, sweep 18 to 22, grab 22 to 28, lunge 24 to 28, tentacle burst 26 to 30, combo 16 to 20, its second blow 10 to 14); a foe rests 130 frames after a ranged blow (`AI.rangedGap`), never fires one into an opening of the investigator's, and only one foe at a time shoots at them with 30 frames of breath between (`AI.volleyGap`: a pack shoots in turn); the Elder Thing, which swung and shot as fast as a deep one with five times the health, rests 70 to 120 frames and hits a fifth less (damage 26 to 22); an invisible stalker shows as a faint shimmer (0.08 to 0.2). The same attack table builds the bosses', so theirs slow by the same frames (`docs/BALANCE.md` rewritten); their pace and numbers are otherwise as they were. `tests/fairness.test.ts` holds the floors. The strike audit (`foesStrike`) gives a foe forty seconds to land a blow, not twenty.
- **Round 45 (the tops of dungeons, the fog walk, the floors, the candles):** (1) *Mounds*: a notch of sky showed over every gate and a dashed slit ran along every ridge. The heap over a room (`cap`) ends at the middle of the wall's thickness and the bank against the wall began at its outer face, so half a wall's top lay bare along every edge (the dashes: the lumpy cap and the flat brim crossing above it), and over a doorway, where no bank stands, the wall stopped 0.9 m short of the heap's brim (the notch, through which the culled underside of the ceiling showed sky). The bank's profile now begins at the wall's middle (`profileOf`), a brow closes each doorway's top (`brow`), and a building's roof reaches its neighbour's wall only to its outer face, not over the next room's heap (`roof`: two shells a few centimetres apart fought). `tests/roofAudit.ts` / `roofView.test.ts` (in the audits) cast straight down over every roofed room's footprint (the first face must be the shell and face up) and slanted rays from above (no seam of two looks fighting; a crossing line of one or two rays is no seam). (2) *Fog walk*: the walk was a glide because `movementSystem` overwrote `prev` with the position the fog pass had already moved to, so the legs saw no ground go by; `fogPassSystem` now sets `prev` before it moves and movement leaves it (`tests/fogGates.test.ts`). (3) *Inlays*: the glitchy square in the middle of Charles le Sorcier's hall was the emblem's gilt frame laid over the rug it bordered, in one plane (inlays are drawn nearer than the slabs by an offset, and two of them over the same ground fight at the edge). Four plans overlapped their own parts (squares and rings and quincunx: fill under border; cross: the dark square over the arms; quincunx: the satellites into the frame): each fill now stops where its border begins (`roomStyle.ts`), and `tests/inlays.test.ts` holds that no inlay of any room lies over another. (4) *Candles* (Elden Ring's Stakes of Marika: statues before boss fog, taken by being near, a fall within reach wakes you there, no rest, no levelling; researched on the Fextralife wiki and others): a stub of tallow in a saucer, ringed in chalk, stands a few paces before each horror's fog on the way in (`FogWall.stake`: the pace back from a boss room's doorway, moved aside or nearer where a pit or pillar is; for an open arena's ring, nine metres beyond it toward the realm's nearest Elder Sign). Passing within 6 m lights it (nothing is pressed; kept in the save as `candles`); a fall within 100 m of a lit candle offers the choice of waking beside it, looking at the fog, or at the Elder Sign (`candleFor` in `death.ts`; the creatures come back as on any death). It is no place to rest or level: the Elder Sign remains that, and the map's travel carries the investigator back to one. The choice: under the veil that falls on UNMADE (without a place's name on it), a small menu, `ui/riseMenu.ts`, asks `At the candle` (the default) or `At the Elder Sign: <name>`; the fall waits (`Pilot.rising`, `riseAt`) and it is left only by choosing; without that screen (the tests and the bots) the candle is where they rise. The reach was 85 m, made 100 at the author's word. One hint at the first lighting. `render/candleViews.ts`, `tests/candles.test.ts`.
- **Round 46 (rests, and the horrors' crowds):** (1) *Foes did not all come back at a rest.* `reopen` cleared the killed, but the 60 alive had been refilled from posts further off, so the nearer ones that came back were past the count and stayed away in a crowded place (found by killing every foe about each of the 45 signs and resting: 11 of 60 missing at one, 23 at another). A rest or a fall now lets every foe standing go (bosses and allies kept) and the nearest posts fill afresh, whole and at home (`overworld.reopen`; `tests/restRespawn.test.ts`). (2) *Nothing to farm, and no empty dungeons.* A rest brings foes back, so foes beside the stone would be farmed by resting and killing again. 16 dungeon signs have their rooms' foes 5-14 m away (the cats at Ulthar 5 m, a ghast 7 m), and removing those would leave the dungeons near empty, so none is removed: the open ground keeps its 30 m (`WORLD.signClear`, now the one number), and in a dungeon the foes about a sign are *sanctum foes*: a rest (or a rising) at a sign does not bring back those killed whose post is within 30 m of where it is taken (`overworld.reopen(g, from)`; the post of each is kept in `ow.posts`); they return with a rest or a rising anywhere else. Killing them again takes a rest elsewhere and the way back, each time, so they are not a farm, and the first time through a dungeon is as it was designed. `tests/restRespawn.test.ts` kills a dungeon sign's foes and checks both. (3) *Horrors too hard for their crowds and quick blows.* A boss's blows follow each other no sooner than 60-110 frames (`BOSS.gap`; the hound struck every 30-70, Brown Jenkin 35-70, a dozen others 45-90); two servants at most (was three); a summoner calls no oftener than every 9 s (`BOSS.summonEvery`, held in the fight's `sig.calledAt`); a servant's own pace is 1.4x slower with a floor of 60-110 frames, its blow weighs 0.7 (`foeDamage`), and the servants share one of the investigator's two closing-in places (`assignTokens`) so the boss and one servant, not a crowd, press them. The audit's table held: Cthulhu's fight had doubled to 311 s with the floor at 70 (fewer blows, fewer openings), so the floor is 60-110; the Unnamable, now so slow to strike that a bot felled it in 5.5 s untouched, has 850 health (was 700).
- Credits (studio): the roll opens with the studio's name on its own card, set large (`title` on a `CreditBlock`: Digital Dro Studios, under "A GAME BY"), then its maker's name as "DESIGNED, WRITTEN AND DIRECTED BY Alessandro A. Foddis" with the copyright notice under it in both names; the second "A GAME BY" block that held the name alone is folded into it. `tests/credits.test.ts`.
- **Round 46 (towards a release: saves, breaks, speed, the first hour):** (1) *A save is kept twice.* The game already wrote saves whole or not at all on the desktop, drew a crash screen when a frame threw, and read a damaged save as none. Now the save before this one is kept beside it (`<key>-backup`, taken at most every five minutes and at a session's first save, and only if it still reads, so a damaged save never replaces a good one); a save that cannot be read is made good from it and the game says so once (*A DAMAGED SAVE WAS MENDED FROM ITS BACKUP*); `tests/save.test.ts`. (2) *Breaks leave a record.* `ui/crashLog.ts` keeps the last sixty console errors and warnings, uncaught errors and rejected promises; the crash screen offers *Copy report* (the version, graphics, window and that record) and, in the desktop shell, *Open logs*; the shell writes every report to `logs/crash-<time>.txt` in the user's data folder (the last ten), keeps its own uncaught faults and its page's process dying (once reloaded), and shows an error box rather than vanishing. The version comes from a new `vite.config.ts` (`__APP_VERSION__`). (3) *Speed can be measured and is set on a first launch.* `core/frameStats.ts` (average, 1% low, worst), F3's frame meter, `?bench` (eight places, a verdict in words) and the ladder `render/autoQuality.ts` run on a first launch only, and never over a player's own settings; `docs/PERFORMANCE.md` has the method and a table to fill in: no real hardware has been measured yet, and nothing here claims a frame rate. (4) *The first hour.* Hints for what teaches hardest came too late (the fight's was shown only once struck): a foe closing in, the breath spent, a wall of fog near and a person to talk to are now noticed (`ui/hintWatch.ts`) and shown before they cost, ahead of the hints waiting; a parry says what follows (a riposte); and within 140 m of the first Elder Sign only gentle foes stand (lesser, blows of 14 or less, in plain sight: no invisible stalkers, ambushers, casters or the like; `planSpawns.gentle`, `tests/firstHour.test.ts`). Packaging, Steamworks and localization are not begun: they need a dependency, an account and the author's choice.
- **Round 46 (the parry, the vitals, the Suno list, localization):** (1) *Parry.* The old pose swung only the off-hand out to the side (the cane did not move, the feet stayed put). Now the cane beats across the face in the first frames (the wrist snapping, the blade held diagonally over the hand, found by searching the arm's joints for a hand in front of the chest and a blade raised up and across), the body turns into the blow with the lead foot stepped in and the knees given, a small shudder rings through the window, and the arm lets go past the guard. `tests/parryPose.test.ts` builds the skeleton and holds the hand in front of the chest and the blade up through the window. The parried/interrupted reaction (a drop to one knee for 1.6 s, for every body) is a knock back on the heels, the weapon arm flung wide, then a stooped, swaying open stance (`poses.recoil`; checked as stick figures from the skeleton's world positions, since the headless renderer would not hold a pose still). (2) *The vitals* are a step less saturated (health, stamina, the sanity bands and their magenta, the pips, the gauges' filter .7 → .55); the map's own magenta is untouched. (3) `docs/SUNO_SOUNDS.md` and its generator are removed at the author's word; the planned recordings, with their prompts, live in `data/plannedSounds.ts` only, and the test that matched the doc to it went with it. (4) *Localization* (`docs/LOCALIZATION.md`): the interface's words are keys of an English table with German, French and Spanish tables typed to hold every key; the language is a button in Settings › Display (a slider would redraw under the hand), taken from the browser on a first launch. This pass moved over the menus, hints, HUD labels and notices, the crash screen and the buttons' names (about 230 lines in each language); the story's text (about 100 KB), the Elder Sign menu, the map and journal pages, and the voices (English recordings) are not translated, and the language setting's hint says so. The translations are mine, not a translator's: they should be read by a native speaker of each before a release. "You" is *ihr/euch* in German and *vous* in French (the old, formal register of the period), *tú* in Spanish.
- **Round 46 (the store, and 1.0.0-rc.1):** the version is 1.0.0-rc.1 (a release candidate, not 1.0: the list of what stands between is the foot of `CHANGELOG.md`: benchmarks on real machines, a native read of the translations, packaging and Steamworks, licences). `CHANGELOG.md` lists what a player would notice. `docs/STORE_PAGE.md` has the copy, tags, languages (interface in four, voices and subtitles English: said as it is), content notes and, because Steam asks for it, the AI disclosure (voices: ElevenLabs; music: Suno; code: Claude Code; the pictures are not AI: they are drawn by the game's code), with the licences to confirm before a commercial release (the Suno and ElevenLabs plans used; Josef Hammer's terms), which cannot be checked from here. `docs/PRESS_KIT.md` has the facts (counted from the data, not remembered) and no press contact, as none was given. `?trailer` is footage for a screen recorder (the picture alone, a slow turn about eight places, `&hold=n` to stay) and `tools/store_shots.mjs` makes the stills and the capsules from the game with Playwright, outside the game's dependencies, into `store/` (ignored by git: marketing pictures are not the game's assets, and a software renderer's are flat; the final ones belong on a real GPU). No trailer film is made: there is no encoder here, and a recording of `?trailer` is better made on the author's machine.
- **Round 46 (a voice's place):** the ElevenLabs workspace is at its limit of voices, so Brown Jenkin's, which spoke one line (his last, shown as a notice), is given up at the author's word: his line is gone from `speechBosses`, his cast entry from `speechCast`, his recording from `public/voice` and its index, and he is wordless like the horrors whose stories leave them so (the boss and its theme are untouched). The voice itself (`XAfV6hn6JT4LPatdDizz`) is still in the workspace until it is deleted in the app (the connector has no delete); that frees the place for the new narrator, designed for the game (a library voice the author found too recognisable as synthetic is to go).
- **Round 46 (the narrator's voice):** the intro narrator is now *The Archivist (Intro Narrator)* (`uqOnApI3a2RVj8rEwlAi`), a voice designed for the game in Voice Design, replacing the library voice Finley (found widely used and recognised as synthetic). Chosen by the author from six previews (A-C from the author's description, D-F from a fresh one of Claude's; F). The design model is `eleven_ttv_v3` (Voice Design offers only that and the older multilingual one); the five cards are made again in `eleven_v4`, one take each, under the same clip names (the speaker and the words are unchanged), kept as recorded; `room` is unchanged. Not heard by Claude and not read back by a recogniser: the author should listen to the opening in the game, and ask for a take again if one is wrong. This leaves Morgan's Josef Hammer as the one library voice in the cast.

- Round 46: intro cards' ash no longer cut at a box (panel overflow visible, canvas padded, edge feathered). Their pace kept as it was (2.4 s in, 2.2 s out; the first pass at 1.5/1.8 s was too quick); the empty wait between them removed instead: forming eases out (sine), and the next card gathers at 75% of the last one's blowing away.
- **Round 47 (release prep: audit, saves, controllers, accessibility):** (1) `npm run audit` re-run after the localization and store changes: 9 files, 216 tests pass. (2) *Saves across versions:* `systems/saveVersion.ts` holds the format (still 2), the oldest that can be carried forward and the steps between (none yet: a raised format adds its step), and every save now records the game version that wrote it (`made`). A save of a newer format is not read, is not "mended" from its backup (that would throw away the newer progress), and the title names its slot *made by a newer version* rather than empty: Continue and Load leave it, New game asks before overwriting it as for any full slot. `tests/fixtures/save-v2-rc1.json` is a save as 1.0.0-rc.1 wrote it (the map's fog left out), and `tests/saveVersion.test.ts` keeps it loading. (3) *Pads:* the prompts name a PlayStation pad's buttons (✕ ○ □ △, R1/R2/L1/L2, Create, Options) and a Nintendo one's (B A Y X by place, R/ZR/L/ZL, − +) from the id the browser or SDL gives (`core/padFamily.ts`; Sony 054c, Nintendo 057e); the rest, a Steam Deck's own controls among them (Steam presents an Xbox pad), keep Xbox names. Pad navigation of every menu was already there (d-pad, stick, A, B, bumpers for tabs). (4) *Steam Deck:* the shell passes `--steam-deck` to the page when Steam sets `SteamDeck=1`, and a first launch there takes UI ×1.15 and captions ×1.2 (`DECK` in playTuning); the smallest HUD text (8 and 9 px: the night's hour, the vitals' labels) is 10 px everywhere, over Valve's 9 px at 1280 × 800. Its 16:10 screen letterboxes the 16:9 picture, as before. Not tried on a Deck. (5) *Accessibility:* a fifth settings tab, Accessibility (the tabs made a step smaller so five fit one line, in German too): Caption size (×0.8–1.6) and Caption backdrop (CSS variables `--caption`, `--caption-bg`, `ui/captions.ts`) on a cutscene's caption, a talk's words, what is overheard and a horror's last words on the banner; Flashes (0–100%): lightning's brightness, and below full its three pulses melt into one swell (no strobe; `flicker` in `render/lightning.ts`, `tests/flashes.test.ts`); Screen shake and FX intensity move there from Play. The intro cards are drawn on a canvas and keep their size. Hold-or-toggle inputs were not added: blocking is held by design and lock-on already toggles.
- **Round 47 (the first hours' balance, and packaging for Steam):** (5) *The opening, as a new player meets it:* the balance table met every boss in order of health with Echoes from the whole world; a new player in the hub and Arkham has only those realms' foes and bosses. `tests/balance.test.ts` now also fights the ten horrors of those two realms at that standing (`standings(['hub', 'arkham'])` in `tests/balanceModel.ts`), each held to 1.5 lives, and writes a second table, *The opening*, into docs/BALANCE.md. It found one wall: the Colour Out of Space healed 30 hp/s, and against an early player's blows that cost 2.53 lives and 75 seconds (some four minutes for a person), ten times any other horror there. Its heal is 18 hp/s: 0.91 lives and 27 seconds at the opening's standing, still the hardest of Arkham, as its strongest should be; at its place in the whole table, 0.37. Elsewhere the curve rises with the realms and holds the bounds; the Great Ones, Yig and 'Umr at-Tawil cost more than some fights after them (2.2–2.6 lives against 1.2–1.9) but are their realms' heights and under the bound of 3, and are left as they are: the order a person meets the late realms in is theirs, not the health order the model assumes. (7) *Packaging and Steamworks* (the author asked for it; two dependencies added on that ask): `steamworks.js` (0.4: prebuilt for Windows, Linux and macOS, N-API) and `electron-builder` (26, a dev dependency). `desktop/steam.js` starts Steam's API when Steam is there and does nothing when not: achievements are passed on as earned and again at each launch (Steam ignores those it has), the overlay is asked for only when Steam started the game (its switches change how Chromium draws), and a release build started outside Steam relaunches through it once there is an app id. The app id is `steam.appId` in `package.json` (0: none yet), `STEAM_APP_ID`, or a `steam_appid.txt`. The achievements' Steam API names are the game's ids (`tests/steam.test.ts` keeps them valid and listed in docs/STEAM.md). Saves need no code: they are files in the shell's data folder, and docs/STEAM.md gives the Auto-Cloud roots. `npm run package[:win|:linux|:mac]` packs `dir` targets into `release/` (unpacked folders are what SteamPipe uploads; Steam installs them, so no installer); `npmRebuild` is off, the native modules are unpacked beside the archive, and each platform must be packed on itself (`@kmamal/sdl` downloads its build at install). `three` moved to the dev dependencies (Vite bundles it; it was being packed for nothing). Packed and launched here on Linux (under Xvfb): it runs, the pads' SDL loads, and Steam's absence is met quietly. Not done: macOS signing and notarisation (an Apple developer account), achievement icons, the partner-site steps (docs/STEAM.md).
- **Creature sprite review (the outside library's walks):** the side views looked wrong because every walk in the pack has two frames (the two contacts, no passing pose), not because of a few bad drawings, so a look-again review could only shuffle them. `tools/sprite_review.py` measures every set against a written spec (docs/SPRITE_QA.md: four-frame profile walks; feet on the idle's ground line within 1 px; height within 3 px or 8% of the idle; strides within 15% in area; upper body within 3 px; facing; nothing on the canvas edge). What a measurement cannot see is written by eye in `tools/sprites/notes.json`: the Deep One's LEFT (both variants) faced right, so its mirrored RIGHT faced left; seven sets off model (the Deep One twice, Joseph Curwen, the hybrid mummy, the shantak, the dhole, the whisperer); 35 with no stride marked `glide`, moved by the engine or given four keys. Limits picked so the clean humanoids pass (the first torso limit, 2 px, flagged 73 sets, mostly gliders' undulation and walkers' natural lean: 3 px, walkers only). `fix` mirrors, grounds and steadies on a copy (26 frames on this pack), never frames due to be redrawn; the pack stays outside the repository (no asset files).
- **Sprite review, same leg:** the author's screen recording of a v1 profile walk (a hooded robed figure) showed the back leg never coming forward: v1 walks are two frames, looped, so the far leg can only ever trail (frames 1 and 3 of the loop measured alike, 0.10 mean grey difference against 2.36 between the strides, which is the loop repeating). The four-frame redraw must not fall into it by copying: `same-leg` fails a walk whose `move_2`/`move_3` differ from `move_0`/`move_1` in under 10% of the legs' pixels, and the brief names the near and far leg and has them swap.
- **Four-frame walks without drawing (`tools/sprites/walk4.py`):** with no drawing budget left, the v1 two-frame side walks (measured: the two frames are both contacts, their legs' shading already swapped, front 73 / back 39 then 42 / 70 for Hypnos, so the eye reads one outline with a leg always behind) get a passing pose after each contact, made from its own pixels: each leg below the crotch found as one of two blobs that both reach the ground, rotated toward upright about its hip by 0.85 of its lean, the trailing one lifted 2 px, the lighter (near) one drawn over, the hip mended, the frame lifted until the standing foot is on the old ground line (rolling it a pixel up first left Wilbur 2 px off the ground). Accepted by eye on the v1 pack for 13 creatures (18 sides); four-legged ones (front and back pairs, not near and far) and some legs came out broken (Man of Leng's feet, Gyaa-yothn, Bokrug) and stay with the drawing. A side that already has `move_2` is never touched, so a redraw wins.
- **Walks drawn by an image model (`tools/sprites/fromsheet.py`, `deliver.py`):** the 47 sides still on two frames (and the Ghoul's, whose pilot frames were not kept) were made as one generation each of ElevenLabs `gpt-image-2.5-sunburst` (1:1, 1K, medium) from the creature's own contact sheet: a 2x2 sheet of the four walk frames on magenta, 62 generations in all (the 48 first tries, and 14 more for the nine that failed: Shantak, Night-gaunt (four tries; the one that passed the audit faced the viewer and was refused by eye), Venusian man-lizard, Beast in the cave, Gnorri, Black Man, Ephraim Waite's eldritch form, Bokrug, Nyarlathotep), about 21,000 credits. A model keeps neither the scale nor the place of its figure from cell to cell (set by the cell, the first conversions failed the audit's ground and torso checks for no fault of the drawings), so `fromsheet.py` sets each frame by its own body: one scale for the four, balanced between the tallest and the shortest (a scale by the tallest left the shorter frames of the winged ones outside the audit's 8%), the lowest pixel on the idle's ground line, the upper body over the idle's, a stride that would touch the canvas edge nudged in by the least it takes. The sheet is brought to the creature's own look: its tone (the model paints lighter than the pixel art, so a gain on luminance, held between 0.7 and 1.3), then each colour to the nearest of the creature's own. A colour under 0.4% of the creature's pixels is for pixels nearly it (one stray tan pixel in Gnorri's palette had put tan strands through its body), and its glowing accents (an eye, a claw tip) are kept where a blob of them is drawn at a height where the creature's own frames have one, since any looser rule let the magenta ground's fringe through (pink specks on the hem of Dagon's eldritch form) or brightened the Wamp's whole body by 13%. Six creatures list a right set beside their left and are symmetrical (Terrible Old Man, Zkauba, Tsathoggua, Rhan-Tegoth, Bokrug, Nyarlathotep): the right is their left flipped, so the pair cannot disagree. Keziah and the Outsider have rights of their own, drawn. The by-eye notes for the hybrid mummy and the shantak are dropped (the new walks are in profile, and on the ground at the idle's height). Audit of the pack: 194 findings on v1, 177 after `walk4.py`, 100 now, none new; what is left was there before (Codex's four sides, which this pack does not hold, the Deep One's and Curwen's notes, the dhole and whisperer notes, front and back views, 49 gliders on two keys). `deliver.py` makes the hand-over zip: the gallery page (host script dropped; Codex's four sides listed with `move_2` and `move_3` by the paths of their `move_0`) and the frames that differ from the published pack. `docs/SPRITE_REVIEW.md` is still the audit of v1: a report of this pack alone would count Codex's four walks as missing.
- **The walk hand-over after Codex's review (`walk_fix_v4.zip`):** Codex's review of the author's folder, with `walk_fix_v3.zip` laid over it, found 1,465 files the gallery lists missing across 61 entries, and the Zoog's and the Outsider's walks turning from left to right halfway through. Both faults were ours. The count is exactly what v3's 270 loose frames delete when laid over the folder by replacing each creature folder (Finder's "Replace") rather than merging, which v3 assumed: a simulation of the author's folder gives 1,465 missing in the same 54 folders. `deliver.py` now puts each creature folder it touches in whole (every file the page lists under it, the rest as published, which `fetch --all` copies), so a replacing unzip leaves the folder complete; files the page does not list are not on the published site and are the author's own to restore. Judgment call: whole folders rather than the whole pack, since only the touched folders were lost, and a whole pack would put v1's frames over Codex's Deep One and Curwen walks. The flips came from the prompt, which asked for cell 3 as "the mirror of cell 1": the model drew the bottom row of three sheets mirrored (the Zoog, the Outsider's left, and the Thousand Young's last frame, which is less plain on a blob), and neither the audit nor the look at each walk caught it. The audit now has a `turned` check: each side frame's head and upper body against its idle's, as drawn and mirrored, turned when the mirror matches better by over 10%. It flags exactly those five frames in v3 and nothing else in the pack or in v1; the threshold sits between the smallest real flip (-0.12) and the most negative glider (-0.05), and a near-symmetric blob scores near 0 either way, so the eye stays. The three sides were drawn again with every cell's facing pinned in the prompt and no "mirror" in it (one generation each, about 1,000 credits, all three passed), converted on a rebuild of the pack (only their twelve frames changed, every other file byte for byte the same), audited (100 findings, none new, none turned) and looked at beside their idles.
- **The leg exchange, and v1's free mends (`walk_fix_v5.zip`, `tools/sprites/v1fixes.py`):** Codex's review and its countercheck found 31 sheet-made sides whose second contact repeats the first; the author chose to redraw them all with the model rather than relight the legs in code. The prompt that worked names each leg by its colour (near light, far dark), says which edge of the cell each foot goes toward, and asks for cell 3 as cell 1 with the legs' colours exchanged (four legs: the feet counted from the front edge, light-dark-dark-light then dark-light-light-dark); a three-sheet pilot showed the two-legged and robed versions working and the four-legged one not, which was rewritten before the rest ran. Round one passed 20 of 31; a retry of the 11 that failed (adding "draw cell 3 by recolouring cell 1" and "cells 2 and 4 must not be the same picture") passed 7 more. Judgment calls: (1) the Ghoul, Brown Jenkin, Rhan-Tegoth and the Wamp failed twice and keep their v4 frames, since a failed redraw would only change the author's folder for nothing; a third try, a relight in code or a drawing is left to the author. (2) Simon Orne's and Ephraim Waite's new walks pass with the audit's `pair-size` note (contact against passing 18-19% apart in area, over its 15%): their legs exchange, and the passing pose is the narrower by nature. (3) Bokrug's hind legs exchange and its forelegs only half; kept, as better than v4's repeat. Judged by eye on each walk's contacts at 8x, brightened (the near/far shading of black robes and coats is a few levels apart); a lead-foot measure helped but was fooled by four legs and robes, so it is not an audit check. 43 generations (about 14,600 credits; 108 and about 36,800 in all). The v1 defects that need no drawing go in the same zip, by `v1fixes.py`: the Innsmouth Hybrid's base LEFT and RIGHT sets were swapped in v1, idles and walk alike (the eldritch form is right), so the two folders change places, walk4's frames with them; Cthulhu's turnaround views change places in the page; the gallery's load error now clears on a good load and on picking another creature, and its banner counts entries "accepted", which is what it reads. The tool refuses a pack it has mended (a second run would swap Innsmouth back). Checked: the 133 files that differ from v4 are the 27 sides, the 3 mirrored rights, Innsmouth's 12 frames and the page; over a simulation of the author's folder (after v4, and as v3 damaged it), merged or replacing, nothing listed is missing, Codex's frames are untouched and every listed image loads in Chromium.
- **The v6 walks (`walk_fix_v6.zip`; `tools/sprites/ground.py`, `legswap.py`, `fromsheet.py --frames`/`--flip-cells`):** asked to "fix all" with the ElevenLabs credits left, "all" was taken as Codex's review and measurements, the four walks whose legs still repeated after v5, and every finding `audit --strict` gates on. The two-key gliders it only advises on stay for the engine to move (30 sides), except the 28 whose keys were out of size against their idles, which get four keys of one motion. What needs drawing went to the model (74 sides; 135 generations and two image edits, about 49,600 credits); what needs none went to code: the ground line (`ground.py`, a direction moved as one so a leap keeps its height over the idle), the Ghoul's and the Wamp's second halves (each sheet's contact and passing pose were good but its second half repeated them, three sheets over: `legswap.py` exchanges the two legs' shades, or mirrors a crawler's legs about their hips), the Elder Thing's and the Mother Hydra's left (a sheet drawn facing right, and the right side's sheet, each cell mirrored where it stands), the Colossus's right (its left, flipped: v1 drew it apart, `move_0` squashed; `v1fixes.py`). Accepted rather than drawn a fourth time: the Shoggoth boss's back a little wider than its idle, the Dhole's back off-centre (its tail must clear the canvas), the Cat from Saturn's tail changing sides (a sway; it came from calling cells "mirror images" in the prompt), four floaters wider than their idles as their originals were. The by-eye notes on the Deep One and on Curwen were dropped once Codex's redraws, seen from the author's Drive, were found to mend them: the Deep One's facing note would have had `fix` mirror Codex's correct left. Its folder ships whole with Codex's frames copied byte for byte from the Drive (`deliver.py --theirs-from`), so it is safe to replace. Rebuilt from the published pack with the repo's tools alone, the zip comes out the same byte for byte (docs/SPRITE_HANDOFF.md).
- **Sprite walks by prompt (`tools/sprites/prompts.py`, `docs/SPRITE_PROMPTS.md`):** with Codex's budget spent and the other session's walks not yet seen, the author draws the walks themselves in ChatGPT's image generation, so each of the library's 105 entries (96 sprite sets, 9 colossi) has a self-contained prompt: a 2x2 sheet on flat magenta, left side only (the game flips it), the reference being its front sprite and its left idle (`refs_x8.zip`, 8x on magenta, made by `prompts.py refs`). The cells are written by body plan (`gaits.py`: biped, hunched, robed, quad, toad, many legs, waddle for the legged, whose cells 3 and 4 swap the leading leg as the `same-leg` check demands; slither, ooze, float, sway, drift, jelly, pulse, swarm, wingbeat for the 35 legless, one smooth four-cell loop; a slow lumber for the colossi) and the entries' looks were written from the sprites (`creatures.py`). 35 entries are legless (a move cycle) and 9 are colossi (128x128 frames): nobody has drawn those; the other 61 already have a four-frame walk and are in the book to redo only where the author is not happy. `fromsheet.py` takes the frame size from the idle and reads a colossus's `movement` frames (the merged version, ElevenLabs session).
- **Walk batches from v1 (`tools/sprites/batches.py`, `docs/SPRITE_BATCHES.md`):** the author judged every ElevenLabs-made walk unlike its reference and went back to the unchanged backup (v1, the published site). Every two- and four-legged creature (56) gets a four-frame walk in its front, back and left view, and its right where it is one-sided (6): 174 sheets in 19 ChatGPT requests of up to 10, a creature's views kept in one request, one body plan after another. The check behind it: in v1 every walk of these creatures is two frames in every view (a hop in profile); in front and back the legs of the robed and the toad-like hardly move (Dagon Priest, Zoog, Gyaa-yothn: 10 to 20% of the legs' pixels change), and some move frames are another drawing (the Serpent Man's front in other colours, the Terrible Old Man's front with another face). The project instructions carry the walk (each view's four cells, body types, cloth that follows through, movement measured in the sprite's pixels, each cell the reference at half size); a request lists its jobs. References are the view's idle_0 at 16x, transparent, with v1's facing mistakes mended in them (Innsmouth's swapped sides, the Deep One's left).
- **Notes on burnt paper (`ui/skins/paper.ts`):** the owner chose the mist for the menus but asked for the burnt paper on every note. Field notes, letters and reports (the `note` entries of `data/documents.ts`) now open on their own screen with that skin, whether read in the world or from the journal's Documents list (`dialogue.ts` `showNote`); tomes keep their plain page. The paper's torn edge is pushed back wherever a bite would reach the words, so no ember or edge crosses the text (the owner saw the title's "HOME" crossed by an ember). `createScreen` takes an optional `look` skin for a standard panel. The skin kit gained the noise, ragged edge, grain and clip functions the paper draws with.

- **Reviewed creature art integration (2026-10-13):** The author's explicit request to import the accepted library supersedes the original procedural-only/no-imported-assets scope for these creature visuals. The active library registrations supply all 105 enemy entries (93 base enemies and 12 variants), 2,577 losslessly packed PNG frames and four views, including the nine formerly procedural assembly bosses. Seven allies remain procedural because this library supplies no art for them. Whole native canvases, per-family scale/ground anchor, alpha, palette and authored order are retained; right views mirror only when registered. Accepted 2/4/6/8-frame walks share a one-second phase, idles a 1.25-second phase, and attack/hurt pose selection follows existing simulation windows. Front combat poses are used for directions without authored combat art. PNG transparency never acts as the procedural glow marker, and the procedural row distortion/void recoloring does not modify accepted art. AI, collisions, hit windows, visibility and saves retain their existing behavior. Four nearest-filter pages use 56.16 MiB of decoded RGBA and 9.53 MB of PNG data. The bestiary displays all 105 reviewed forms plus seven allies; the pack can be reproduced with tools/pack_reviewed_assets.py against the active source folder.

- **The interface review (`docs/UI_REVIEW.md`):** asked to review every menu and change only what showed a fault, with the mist kept exactly. No screenshots or recording came with the brief, so the baseline was the running game (Playwright, a software renderer, disposable `?fresh&debug` worlds); every screen found in source was opened. Nine faults were mended, each shown first: Settings › Controls' **Swap A/B** was built disabled in round 45 and never usable (enabled; turning it on made the A that did it read as a B, so `pollPad` now takes no edge from a held button in the frame the exchange changes); the Controls grid's right-hand columns were cut off at 1080p and lost in French and Spanish (label columns now give way); Esc while a key is awaited left Settings (it now cancels the wait, and the foot says which action waits); the credits roll was cut flat at both ends and set against Back (its edges fade); scrolling bodies cut a line in half and gave no sign of more (a body fades toward an edge with more beyond it: `ui/scrollCue.ts`); Elder Sign › Arms wrapped a lone "m" (the numbers take a line of their own, as Continue's does); a saved slot wrapped with a "·" opening its second line (same); the ending card's lozenge stood on its first letter; and the faintest readable text (locked achievements, done quests, the keys lines, the opening's hints, the credits' small print) went from 2.0–3.0:1 to 3.6–4.7:1 against the menus' ground. Kept as they were, on purpose: the framed reading leaf in the world against the mist page from the Journal, "Quit to title" asking nothing (it saves), a shop's reason shown on choosing and not on focus (showing it earlier moves the panel), the pages that stay English (docs/LOCALIZATION.md). The mist is untouched: no line of `ui/skins/`, `menuSkin.ts`, `veil.ts` or the transitions changed, and with `?debug` the skin's canvas drawn still at five moments on eleven pages hashed **identical** (55 of 55) to the unmodified code at the same panel size. Evidence pictures and recordings are kept out of the repo (CLAUDE.md: no picture files without the author's say) and go out as a zip.

- **The creature art audit (`docs/CREATURE_ART.md`, "In-game audit"):** asked to check every new enemy and boss form for major errors, bosses first, all 105 were rendered in the running game and read back (view, state, frame, source, flip) and the art measured against each body; none was wrong. The one defect was in the code around the art: the reviewed-art branch of `creatureViews.ts` drew hallucinations and decoys with no glitch, so they were indistinguishable from real creatures; they get the old `PHANTOM_WEIRD` again and nothing else changes. Not mended, because each is the author's call or needs art: attack and hurt art only from the front (all 105), the colossi's stoop no longer drawn (the hint and head zone still work), eyes in the mist and the outer gods' echoes absent on reviewed art, Yog-Sothoth's hit spheres not following the painted ones, two near-black figures on a dark floor.

- **Four mends after the creature art audit (echoes, rim, Yog's spheres, eyes):** asked to fix all but the stoop, each was done in code without touching a frame. *Echoes:* the reviewed branch draws through one `put()` and draws the outer gods' two echoes as the same art at `ECHOES`' lag, dither and drift (the drift one helper for both bodies); each echo shows its frame from the clock `lag` seconds earlier (the state, idle or move or a blow, is the body's own now), so it trails rather than copies. *Rim:* a per-instance value in the batch's `aInfo.w`, which the reviewed pages never used (the procedural batch's `outside` flag lives there, so the shader reads it as a lift only when `uReviewed` is set); the table is measured, not chosen by hand (`tools/reviewed_look.py`: median brightness of the front idle below 0.165 gets a rim between 0.55 and 1), and the strength was raised once after looking at 'Umr at-Tawil in the game, where the first was too faint to read. *Yog-Sothoth:* the seven painted spheres are read off the art by eye and kept as fractions of the height; each is ringed four ways about the axis (a flat picture cannot say which side of the body a sphere is on) and held within 1.15 body radii so a blade reaches it; the other seven colossi keep their columns, because a column is what they are drawn as. *Eyes:* the finder (small, bright or saturated marks in the head, against their own surroundings) was wrong too often to trust on all 105 (teeth, a visor, spots), so the list of forms is written by hand after looking at each (`EYED`, with a search box where two marks competed), and only front views glow. The Shoggoths, the rat swarm, the Wamp, the Haunter and the Unnamable were looked at and left out.
- **The colossi's stoop, from the author's drawn poses:** the 14 frames came as two zips (the PNGs alone, and the same PNGs with their manifest, hashes and comparison sheets). They were added as a later delivery rather than by rebuilding the pack, because `tools/pack_reviewed_assets.py` needs the author's whole library and its byte hashes, which this repository does not hold: `tools/add_reviewed_frames.py` puts them on a new page (`creatures-4.png`, so the four existing pages are untouched byte for byte), adds a front `stoop` action to the seven creatures in `index.json` and the source index, and the integrity test counts them (2,591 frames, 1,057 scopes) and requires the page to be the extra one. In play: `lookOf` takes the stoop over every state but death while `stooped()` is true (so a staggered colossus bows rather than shows its hurt pose, and one in the recovery of its own blow bows rather than shows the attack pose, which matches what the old 3D body did); the bow shows `FEEDBACK.bowSeconds` (0.3 s, by eye), then the hold until it stands, with no drawn rise (it goes straight back to its idle); the stoop is front-only art, so side and back views use it as they use attack and hurt; the outer gods' echoes bow a moment behind. The head hit zone (`ZONES.head.stoop`, 4.5 m) was not touched: the drawn head is at the ground in front, inside it.
- **Seven more arrivals of their own (`data/cutscenesBoss.ts`):** asked to write them for the bosses with a story hook still on the generic size-class scene: Ghatanothoa (the mountain looks at you; the last shot is from its eyes, and a monolith stands in it; its caption is the cover hint's advice), Mother Hydra (she sings; the Deep Ones answer), the Colossus Beneath the Pyramids (opens on black; the hands first, in a low shot, then the rest; the mummies only its servants), Tsathoggua (out of black, one eye opens), Yig (the grass moves, rattling, before the father is seen), Wilbur Whateley (whippoorwills, then the turning of a page), Joseph Curwen (the dead are consulted at his side: a violet flash and embers). Where the horror speaks (Hydra, Tsathoggua, Wilbur, Curwen) the scene has no caption of its own, so the director's line (`speaking`, at 0.9 s) is the only text on the screen: sounds, a flash, motes and the camera carry the idea; the silent three have two short captions each, spaced to be read in turn. Sizes and distances were set by playing each in the arena with the investigator at the edge, facing the horror: the first versions put the investigator's back across the shot and two close-ups into macro pixels (the drawn sprites are 64 px at 3 to 6 m), so over-the-shoulder shots start above the head and no close-up is nearer than the generic arrival's. Not done: the falls stay generic, by size; the Dunwich Horror (unseen) and Brown Jenkin (joins another's fight) have no scene by design. The other 28 scripted bosses that play a scene still arrive by size alone.
- **The colossi are struck where their picture shows them (`REVIEWED_BODY`, `assembly.painted`):** asked to fix the painted width against the combat radius. The first measure (the picture's whole width against the capsule: 1.1 to 1.65 times) counted claws and tentacles, so the bands were measured instead (`tools/reviewed_look.py`, the 75th percentile of a band's rows, the mass and not the farthest claw). Three forms were narrower in the fight than in the picture by 15 to 45% (Hydra's legs and torso, Dagon's torso, Cthulhu's torso and head), two fit within about 10% (Ghatanothoa, the Colossus), and the mounds are the other way about: Azathoth's and Shub-Niggurath's zones are wider than the low mass and their head zone is above empty air (the picture's top is motes). Chosen: widen only, and only the lathed bodies; the walking capsule, the arena and the mounds stay (a mound's head is what the revolver aims for at 2.5 times, so fitting it would change the last fights). A blow's damage from the capsule's edge is unchanged (`tests/colossusZones.test.ts`; the same eight blows on Cthulhu and Hydra with and without), and the audit holds. The balance table was regenerated and Cthulhu's row moved from 150 s to 250 s (and Yog-Sothoth's from 121 to 111, which was already stale from the sphere fit); that is the bot's fight with Cthulhu, which is chaotic: over six starting distances it takes 61 to 455 s, a mean of 216 s without the fit and 223 s with it, so it is path and not a cost of the fit (Hydra: 28 s to 26 s, Dagon unchanged). It stays inside the bounds (260 s, 6 lives), but near them.
- **Falls of their own for the seventeen, and ten more arrivals (`data/cutscenesFall.ts`, `data/cutscenesMore.ts`):** asked to fix "boss falls are still generic by size, and 28 bosses still arrive by size alone". *Falls:* each of the seventeen that arrive in their own way now falls in its own (the Haunter's lamps come back after two flashes of lightning; the Colour goes up out of the well and what it leaves is grey; Cthulhu sinks slowly and the screen goes black on his dream; Yog-Sothoth's gate shuts in violet; Tsathoggua closes the black over his eye; Nyarlathotep is not struck, smiles, and a door is shut softly; Curwen is a little dust). They are made as the arrivals are (data, shots then beats, the director laying the last words over them), and speakers carry no caption of the scene's own (the silent six have one). Two things were learnt from playing them in the arena (`Vanquished` emitted over a body in its death move, the arrival skipped first): a body takes `80 / (60 × slow)` seconds to sink and fade (4.4 s at 0.3 of the speed), so the camera has to stay on it that long, its aim sliding down with it (`over`), or the scene is a camera on an empty floor; and what comes after is a crane up over the spot it fell (`elegy`), not the investigator's back, which for a person-sized horror filled the frame for three seconds under a long last line. *Arrivals:* the ten with the clearest hook of the 28: the Black Man (the book held open), the Hound (baying on the moor), the Terrible Old Man (his bottles swinging), the High Priest (the piping behind the mask), 'Umr at-Tawil (the veil lifted), the Outsider (out of the ground, the hand first), the Gorgon (the gaze, looked away from), Lilith (a match struck, candles taking), Rhan-Tegoth (the ivory throne, then that it is hungry) and the Thing beyond Zann's window. Close-ups of a person-sized body are no nearer than 1.9 to 2.6 of its height at 44 degrees or wider (nearer, they are pixel blocks), and an opening shot from behind the investigator stands at 2.1 m or more and off the line, or the horror is hidden by their back. The other 18 scripted bosses with no arrival of their own (Simon Orne, Hutchinson, Waite, the Whisperer, the Unnamable, the Shunned House, Martin's Beach, Dr. Muñoz, Charles le Sorcier, Hypnos, Zkauba, the Daemon Pipers, the Other Gods, the Ancient Ones, Bokrug, Nug, Yeb, the Great Ones) still arrive by size where they play a scene at all, and the other 28 falls are still by size; the first seventeen's scenes were shot in the debug arena, not on their real grounds.
- **Three deliveries of the author's art: 'Umr at-Tawil, the stoop's halfway pose, the bosses' sides and backs (`tools/replace_reviewed_sprite.py`, `tools/add_reviewed_frames.py`):** *'Umr:* drawn again whole (21 PNGs, 64x64, a hooded figure, and a right view of his own where the old one was his left mirrored).
