They are put in place of the old frames on their page (each pasted over its old rectangle) rather than added beside them, so nothing is left in the pack that nothing uses and the frame names stay what they were; his four right-hand frames are on a shelf added at the foot of that page (it grew from 1,188 to 1,254 pixels, under the 2,048 limit), and his right no longer mirrors (mirrors 82 to 81). *The halfway pose:* seven frames on a page of their own (`creatures-5.png`); the head comes down through it for 0.1 s before the bow, and the colossus stands up through it for 0.18 s after the hold, which is the rise the stoop lacked (the bow and the hold are the same pose: idle to bow was a cut, and so was the way back); a form without the frame is drawn as it was. *The sides and backs:* 126 frames on `creatures-6.png` (the left and the back of each of the nine bosses' attack and hurt, as many frames as the front's); the right is the left mirrored once, by `reviewedClips` (a boss's right walk and idle are its own), so a boss swinging while it is circled is drawn from the side it is seen. What was checked beyond the importer: the frame counts equal the front's, no frame is the idle's or the one before, the halfway heights lie between the idle's and the bow's, and the ground lines stand on the idle's; the new frames of the two palette-limited forms (Cthulhu and the Dunwich Horror, 30 colours) keep that palette, and the others (thousands of colours, as the library's own) have fewer than the library's but the same look. The integrity test now counts 2,728 frames and 1,100 scopes, and the later deliveries' pages are those after the library's four. Seen in the running game: 'Umr from all four sides idle and mid-beam, and Dagon mid-swing from all four; the stoop's timing is held by the unit tests only (the harness freezes the render clock).

- **600×338 default, steady menu pages, and authored creature shading:** the default is 1.5× the render base, including first-launch quality tuning; current saved resolution choices stay intact. Shared menu transitions now rise and fade without moving sideways, keep the outgoing page's exact layout and scroll position, and leave pinned titles/tabs outside the scroll body. Removed the recently added reviewed-art rim and shading lift completely from enemies, bosses and their echoes, including its generated metadata; eye glows and painted body measurements stay.

- **Relics are worn two at a time (`RELIC_SLOTS`), chosen at an Elder Sign:** round 26's relics were all worn at once ("no equip screen: a menu was more than the round could hold"), so by the fifth horror every build was the same one. Five are found, two are worn; the Elder Sign has a Relics tab (a line for each slot, a page of the relics found to put in it, each with its gift and its price in numbers), and the pause menu's Arms page lists what is worn. Owned stays `relic:<id>` in `told` (the order found), worn is `slot:<n>:<id>`, so the save has no new field; a relic found with a slot free is worn at once, otherwise it is kept and the notice says to choose at a sign; a save from before the slots keeps the first two found worn and the rest waiting (`settleRelics`, when it is loaded). A relic worn in the other slot changes places with the one chosen. The numbers are unchanged: every pair of the five makes a build no other pair does (`tests/relics.test.ts`).
- **Reinforcement moves between weapons, free and whole:** star-stones were spent a weapon at a time, so a weapon found at the ninth hour could not compete with the cane. At an Elder Sign (Arms tab › Move reinforcement) a weapon with levels set into it opens a page of the others, each of which takes one level or all it has room for (`moveLevels`; `movable`). Nothing is lost or made (the total levels set in is the same), and it can be moved back, so there is nothing to charge: a cost would only punish trying. It does not touch the revolver (its levels are its own).
- **Insight says what spending it costs, and is shown early:** the hidden doorways and bridges of the dungeons (16, `systems/veil.ts` reads them from the dungeons' data) show to a mind with enough insight, and spending insight on Resolve or a Draught shuts them again, which the menu never said. The Mind lines at an Elder Sign now say how many passages insight holds open that have yet to be passed, and each upgrade it can pay for says which it would shut ("Spending 2 insight shuts The Witch House: burrow ... again, before you have passed it"); a passage whose tome has been read is no loss and is not counted. The first time the investigator stands within 10 m of a doorway only insight shows, two hints come, one after the other (`passage`, then `passageCost`). The demonstration is in the opening: Gilman gives the first insight and the closet on the Witch House landing is the first wall to open (see next). `hint.insight` said insight "buys strength", which Echoes have done since round 4; it now says what insight does.
- **The Witch House is five stages, not one (`quests.witch_house`):** the opening went from finding Gilman to killing Keziah. Now: read Officer Riley's report (a note in Arkham), take it to Gilman (who says what it means, and shows the investigator how to look: `gift: { insight: 1 }`, paid when the stage is closed by its own goal), reach the stair landing and its Elder Sign, read Keziah's Formulae in the closet only insight shows (its text now sets out the ritual), then the garret. Two new rules in `quests.ts`: a stage may carry a `gift`, and a quest may be `lenient` (what the world has already done is done: a later stage whose goal is met — a boss slain, a sign found, a page read — closes the ones before it at once, one change, and the gifts of stages passed over are not given). Keziah slain without the clues still ends the quest, as before. A new `read` goal (`ow.read`), placed by `lead.ts` at the tome's own place, in the open or inside a dungeon. Gilman has a word for each stage (ten new lines, performed with their audio tags in `speechNpcs.ts`, and recorded in his cast voice: docs/VOICES.md). Tests assumed the quest was one stage (`quests.set(id, 1)` for "done"): they now use the stage count. The saved stage of the quest changed its meaning (0 was the witch to be put down, 1 done), so the save format is 3, with a step from 2 (`saveVersion.ts`): 0 becomes the last stage, 1 becomes done, and a quest done is not paid a second time (a release candidate's save goes on as it was).
- **Hints are given once for each save, and read again in the Journal's Field Guide:** they were remembered in the browser, so a second save (or a new game) never saw them, and they stayed nine seconds. `hint:<id>` is now kept in the save's `told` (a world holds `hints:kept` from the start; a save from before takes up what the browser had been told, once, so veterans are not told again); a new journey carries them (`Carry.hints`); the browser's own set is still kept, for the lore lines under the veil (`loreLine.ts`). A hint stays as long as it takes to read (60 ms a character, nine seconds at least). The Journal has a Field Guide tab: every hint given, filed under four heads (`data/fieldGuide.ts`: what each is called is the journal's own English, like its tabs; the words are the language chosen). The hint watcher no longer waits for a frame that is a multiple of 30 (a slow picture steps the game in strides that never land on one).
- **A side quest can be followed:** the Journal's Under way tab has "Follow it on the map" for a side quest under way; the minimap and the map lead to it in place of the story (`currentLead`), the Elder Sign's NEXT line too, until it is done or let go (`Overworld.tracked`, saved; main quests lead by themselves and are not offered).
- **Peaslee's "Find him." is quiet and followed by a long pause:** it was performed `[urgently] Find HIM.`, a tag of urgency and a leaned-on capital together, and Eleven v4 shouted it (-16 and -14 dB against about -22 round it, peak 0.85), with 0.34 s of silence after it: round 42's treatment of his clips cuts every pause over 0.4 s to 0.22 s, so a `[long pause]` tag could not have helped. The performed line is now `[quietly] Find him. [long pause]`. The clip shipped is the author's own take (speed 100%, stability 50%, similarity 75%; the first re-make, through the connector, was not liked), treated as his others are and with the pause held at 1.0 s ("Find him." at -18 and -22 dB under the line's loudest words, 1.0 s of silence after it). That treatment had no script: `tools/voice_process.py` is it, fitted to reproduce a clip that shipped (same length, spectrum within 0.3 dB a band); without it a re-made Peaslee line would have come out higher and slower than his other ten. The take's own tags were not kept, so `speechNpcs.ts` may differ from it. docs/VOICES.md.
- **The pause menu's main page is centred, on plaques, grouped by space, over a lighter backdrop:** the title was centred over a left-aligned list in a 594 px panel (about 500 px of dead band right of the words, a highlight running far past them, a foot hint left of the lines). Its lines are now centred, each on a plaque of one width (`.pause` in `ui/menuParts.ts`, a diamond either side of the one chosen, a band at 10% across the plaque, and a glow; the old menu's own peak was 15%, and 23% and then 15% were each judged too much); the three faint 1 px rules are 14 px gaps; the ways out (Quit to title, Quit to desktop) are dimmer and light ash-red when chosen; the foot hint is centred. The pause screen's backdrop is 70% dark where the others' is 87% (`createScreen(6, '#050506b3')`): the world, the vitals and the minimap read through it. A blur of the world was tried and dropped: the vitals and the minimap are page elements under the screen, so a `backdrop-filter` blurs them too ("LUCID 100" became unreadable). Only the main page's list is changed (the other menus' look is as it was; the backdrop is the pause screen's, so its Journal, Arms, Settings and the rest sit on it too: the Journal read well on it). The panel is 21 px taller (three 7 px rules became three 14 px gaps) and the same width; the mist follows the panel and was looked at in the game, not re-measured as docs/UI_REVIEW.md did.
- **Stamina waits a short beat after a run, not an action's whole delay (round 48: "the regeneration pause is a little too long, especially when running and then trying to fight right after"):** every frame of a sprint went through `spend()`, which set the 48-frame delay of an action, so a run that ended with the bar empty (100 stamina lasts 5.6 s of sprint) left the investigator unable to act for 0.8 s before anything came back, just as a foe was reached. A sprint now sets `STAMINA.sprintDelay` (18 frames, 0.3 s) and `tickStamina` takes the longer of that and any delay an action has just set, so a blow followed at once by a run is not given back its time. Rolls, blows, shots and blocks keep the 48 frames: the round 22 rule that the delay outlasts a roll (so a chain of rolls draws on the bar alone) is untouched, and shortening that one as well is the other lever if this is still felt as too long. Regeneration's rate is as it was.
- **Input that sticks (round 49: "the character started walking on its own and I couldn't attack anymore, just block and dodge/roll"):** the cause of that one play was not found (it was on a Mac (M1) in the desktop shell with keyboard and mouse, after resting at an Elder Sign; resting, leaving its menu and then walking and striking was walked through in the running game with proper events and holds, and nothing in the sim would give it: `stolen`, the fog's walk, a talk, a cutscene and the stamina all end by themselves or take every button, the guard and the roll with them), so what was mended is every way the input could be left believing in a press that had ended, which is the one thing that fits: walking (a movement key or a stick held) and no blows (a press-only button "held", so no press is ever seen to begin) with the guard and the roll, which need no new press edge to read, still working. Found in `core/input.ts` and `core/pads.ts`, each with a test (`tests/inputStuck.test.ts`; ten of its fourteen fail on the old code): (1) on macOS a key pressed with Command held is never heard to rise, so a key taken for the game stayed down for ever: a key pressed with Command is no longer the game's, and Command rising lets go of every key (a key still held says so with its next repeat); (2) a mouse button let go over another window, or under a screenshot's overlay, was never heard to rise: every mouse event now carries which buttons are down (`MouseEvent.buttons`) and a button the page thinks is down and the mouse says is up is let go; (3) a button that acts on its press alone (all but the guard and the dodge, which are the only ones read as held) is let go after two seconds down, since only its rising edge is read and a stuck one hid every later press: this covers a pad's button that glitches as well; (4) a stick that has drifted off centre (up to 0.4) and lies still four seconds is at rest there (a stick seen at centre was read as it lay from then on, and the deadzone of 0.2 let a drift of 0.25 walk the investigator slowly for ever; pushed hard and held, a stick is never taken for drift); (5) a key that has been heard to repeat and then says nothing for 3.5 seconds is no longer down (a key held goes on repeating, within two seconds at the slowest setting; a key that has never been heard to repeat, its repeats being off or taken by the system, is never timed out), which lets go of a movement key or the dodge whose release was lost by any means; (6) pausing now lets go of every key and button and takes the sticks where they lie for their rest (`InputDevice.reset`, `recalibratePads`), so the way out of any of this is the pause menu, which is also what a player does. The frame meter (F3) now also says what the input hears (`keys KeyW · mouse light`, the pad's name, its sticks and held buttons: `InputDevice.describe`), so that the next report can say which of these it was. Not done: a key that has never been heard to repeat has no timeout, so it can stay down until it is pressed again or the game is paused. Likely, not shown: a Mac's screenshot shortcuts (Command with Shift and 3, 4 or 5) are made at a sign while the menu is open, and lose the releases of the keys pressed with them and the mouse button under their selection overlay: (1) and (2) mend those.
- **The title menu no longer shifts left for a moment when something is chosen (round 49):** a page comes in lifted (`bring` in `ui/menuKit.ts`: each part rises 6 px from a blur, a tab's body 8), and the title's panel is one that scrolls (`overflow-y:auto`, `max-height:60vh`) with no padding of its own, so the lift ran past the end of its content: 6 px of overflow, which put a scrollbar in for the 420 ms the rise lasts (about 10 px of it, on a Mac with a mouse and on Windows, where scrollbars take room; not with a trackpad's overlay ones, nor in Playwright's headless Chromium, which hides them) and the centred page moved 5 px left to make room, and back as it left. Measured in Chromium with scrollbars shown, the first choice's left edge during each change: 430 → 425 → 430 before, a constant 430 after, on every page change and way back (main, slots, difficulty, Settings, Credits; a tab's change never did it). The panel now takes `LIFT_ROOM` (`padding: 10px 0` in `menuKit.ts`, the lift and 2 px over), symmetric so nothing stands anywhere else (the first choice's top is the same to the pixel). Left alone: the rise menu has the same panel but only ever shows one page, so nothing is lifted in it; the standard panels have their skin's padding. A window too short for a page still scrolls, as before, with the scrollbar there throughout (no change of width as the page comes in). No Node test: it is layout, which only a browser has; measured with `shift2.mjs` in the scratchpad.
- **No place is walled in (round 49: two Exham Priory Troglodytes stood inside a ruin that "the enemies cannot get out of, nor I get in"):** a ruin is four runs of wall about a house's floor, each kept with a chance of 0.85 and each of its segments with 0.8, so about one ruin in seven came out with its walls meeting all round, and `planSpawns` posts a foe about every ruin it likes without asking whether it can walk out. Found by walking out of every point of the land (a search over a 0.6 m grid with a body of 0.55 m radius, against the real colliders: `world/escape.ts`, `tests/enclosedAudit.test.ts`): of 376,017 points, 14 pockets, ten of them ruins (three with foes in them, the hub's among them, at 98–103, 48–49, where the screenshot was taken) and four **farmyards**, which were shut by their own buildings (read from a map of the blocked ground: the house and the barn stood across the way out of the yard, between the ends of its side fences). No sign, gate, tome, arena or dungeon door was shut in, and no foe stood walled in anywhere else. Mended three ways: (1) a ruin's front wall is broken at its middle (`RUIN_GATE`, 4.8 m, facing the road as the graveyard's gate does) and no rubble lies in the way in, the rubble kept nearer the middle so it makes no nook against a wall; (2) a farm's house and barn stand beyond the ends of the side fences with the way out of the yard (the gate's width and a body's either side) running between them, and no tree or stump grows in it; (3) whatever else may come, `planSpawns` walks each post out (`escapes` over its own props, with the margin a foe keeps from them) and refuses one that cannot, so a foe is never put where it cannot come out nor the investigator go in. After: 0 pockets in 376,089 points, 1,075 foes posted of 1,073 before, none walled in; the foes of the hub's ruin come out of it by its doorway in the running game. The spawns near a mended ruin are not the ones that were (the walls and the draws shifted): that is the fix, not a change to the tables. Left: a nook of fewer than 100 cells (about 36 m², a hollow between a stone and a wall where nothing stands, and no foe is posted: the walk-out refuses it) is not counted, so `enclosed.test.ts` and the audit do not fail on rubble; the dungeons' inside is their own walls and doors (the audit stands off them), and a horror's ring is held by its fog.
- **The title's main page is centred as the pause menu is, and "PAUSED" stands on the middle (round 49):** the title's lines stood left-aligned in a 260 px box (their words 65 to 76 px left of the middle of the screen's panel, the keys line under them centred) while the pause menu's are centred on plaques of their own. The main page now takes the pause menu's own list (`.pause` in `menuParts.ts`): centred lines on plaques with a diamond either side when chosen, the groups told apart by space (Continue, New game and Load; Settings and Credits; and Quit in the desktop shell, dimmer, ash-red when chosen), the line that says where the dream was left centred under Continue, 420 px at most. And `title()` (every menu's name at its head) kept the letter-spacing of its words (7 px) after the last letter, so they stood half of it (3.5 px) left of the middle, over a rule that is centred: its padding at the left is that spacing now (the keys line on the title page, 2 px, the same). Measured on the ink of the words in Chromium: PAUSED 3.5 px left before, 0 after; the title's lines 65 to 76 px left before, 0.4 after (the plaques' own diamonds make the rest). Left: the headings of the title's other pages (slots, difficulty) are letter-spaced and centred too, 1.5 px off, which is not seen; they are left-aligned on the panels that are not the title's, so padding them would shift those.
- **No line of keys, no descriptors in the pause menu, and the title without its two lines (round 49: "this makes the player feel dumb"):** (1) the line at the foot of every menu page that named the keys ("↑ ↓ Choose · Enter Select · Esc Back", the pad's "D-pad · A · B", and "PgUp PgDn Tabs" on a page with tabs) is gone, from the title, the pause menu, Settings and its Controls, the Journal and its pages, Arms, the Achievements, the Elder Sign's menu and its relics and reinforcement pages, the shop and the rise menu: `ui/menuKeys.ts` is deleted and `footer(parent, hint, back?)` takes no keys. The mouse's way back was a click on the "Esc Back" of that line, so a page that has one keeps a plain "Back" there (clickable, in the same dim capitals, with no key beside it); the keys still do what they did. Not said any more: that the bumpers (LB RB) and Page Up and Page Down change a tab, which the tabs themselves, left and right on them, and the mouse still do (to be put back if a player is found not to look for them). (2) The pause menu's lines have no descriptors (the nine `pause.*Hint` lines, in all four languages) and its page has no foot at all, not even the rule: `footer` returns before drawing one for a page with no hint, no Back and no line with a hint of its own. The other menus keep what they say of a setting or a choice (Settings' hints, the rise menu's line, Arms' note): those say what is not plain. (3) The two lines under the name on the title ("Arkham, Massachusetts · October 1928" and "Eleven people in Arkham have fallen asleep…") are gone (`TITLE_LINES` too), and the name, which stood with them centred at 25 % of the screen, now stands midway between the top of the screen and the top of the menu under it, which it is measured for (`settle` in `titleScreen.ts`, on every change of the menu's size): so it is lower, by about 60 px at 720 p without a saved game and 30 with one, and a saved game's Continue and Load, which lift the menu's top, lift the name too and never meet it. Measured in Chromium (gap between the name's foot and the first line of the menu): 136 px without a save and 114 with at 1280 × 720, 170 at 1920 × 1080, 129 at 4:3, 51 with the interface at ×1.5; in the 600 × 338 window the menu left no room for the name at its size (the name overlapped it by 50 px, as the name and its lines did before), so the name is drawn smaller to the room there is (25 px between them). It glides, over .45 s, as the page changes (the slots, the difficulty), and does not on first standing.

- **Release for Steam, Windows and Mac (round 50):** (1) The shell takes one instance at a time (`requestSingleInstanceLock`, not in dev or the smoke test), sets the Windows taskbar id (`com.digitaldro.seventysteps`), has no menu bar and no developer tools in a release (macOS keeps its app menu with View › full screen, since a Mac without one cannot quit by the menu), and `--devtools` opens them for support. (2) `--smoke-test` is the proof that a packed build starts: it uses a throwaway profile, waits for the title's "New game" and the preload bridge, and prints `SMOKE OK` or `SMOKE FAIL` with the Steam binding and the controllers' worker (`SMOKE_REQUIRE=steam,pads` makes either a failure; the Linux runner requires only Steam, as its SDL worker may not find a display). `tools/smoke_packaged.mjs` runs it against the unpacked app of this machine. (3) The Mac build is two apps, Apple Silicon and Intel, because `@kmamal/sdl` downloads the native build of the machine it is installed on: `tools/package_mac.mjs` installs each architecture's with `CROSS_COMPILE_ARCH`, packs it, then puts the host's own back. `--universal` merges the two with `@electron/universal` (`x64ArchFiles` for Steam's binding, which carries both architectures' libraries); it is optional because one depot per architecture is the simpler route on Steam. (4) Signing is ad hoc (`codesign --force --deep --sign -`, verified) unless `MAC_SIGN_IDENTITY` is set, which uses `@electron/osx-sign` with the hardened runtime and `packaging/entitlements.mac.plist` and, with the APPLE_* secrets, notarises. `@electron/osx-sign` is not used for ad hoc: it looks identities up in the keychain and finds none for `-`. `@electron/universal`, `@electron/osx-sign` and `@electron/notarize` are electron-builder's own dependencies, not new ones. (5) `tools/steam_upload.mjs` writes the SteamPipe scripts (never to the `default` branch) and, with `--run`, calls steamcmd; the app id stays 0 in `package.json` until Steamworks gives one. (6) `.github/workflows/release-builds.yml` packs and starts the game on a Windows, a Mac and a Linux runner. Not done, and only the author can: the Steamworks app, the depots, the store page, a Developer ID and notarisation, and a run on real hardware with a real Steam client (`docs/RELEASE.md`).

- **Release round (round 50), what the runners taught:** (1) A step marked `continue-on-error` shows green when it fails: the Rosetta and universal steps did, twice, and only their logs said so; they are required now that they pass. (2) The Intel app under Rosetta never "hung": Rosetta translates the Electron framework on a first start, which took 3 to 6 minutes on a runner, and the first limit (4 minutes) ended it before the app ran; the step has ten minutes. (3) The universal app stood at Electron's own error box for the whole timeout, and a stack sample was needed to see it (`NSAlert runModal`, not the JavaScript loop first read): the launcher `@electron/universal` writes (`Resources/app.asar`, which picks `app-arm64` or `app-x64`) carries a copy of this `package.json`, `"type": "module"` and all, so its CommonJS `index.js` is read as an ES module and fails on its first line, before any handler of the game's exists. `tools/package_mac.mjs` unpacks the launcher, takes the type out of its `package.json`, packs it again and signs after; the game's own package keeps the type. (4) `mergeASARs` was tried to avoid the launcher and fails (EEXIST on the symlink in SDL's folder), and the stub is not avoidable while the apps differ, so the Mac apps are packed without an asar (`-c.asar=false`): the merge then needs the native files to differ and nothing else. (5) The merge is made from copies without their `_CodeSignature` folders: the seal files differ in each architecture and `@electron/universal` refuses files that differ. (6) In `--smoke-test` an uncaught error in the shell is printed and the shell exits 1, so a failed start says what broke at once. (7) A Mac ad hoc signature is `codesign --force --deep --sign -` and `--verify --deep --strict`; `@electron/osx-sign` looks the identity up in the keychain and finds none for `-`, so it is used only for a Developer ID.

- **itch.io before Steam (round 51), asked: "lead me through this, and make the game code ready for the pre-Steam release on itch.io; playable on Mac and Windows without problems":** (1) One codebase, a build-time marker: `channel` in the packed package.json (`-c.extraMetadata.channel=itch`; `package_mac.mjs --channel itch`), read by `desktop/steam.js`, keeps an itch build off Steam entirely, so setting the Steam app id later cannot make an itch build relaunch through Steam; the smoke report carries `channel` and `smoke_packaged.mjs --channel itch` fails a build that says otherwise. (2) `tools/itch_push.mjs` checks each pack's own package.json (out of the asar on Windows and Linux; the universal Mac app's `app-arm64`) and refuses one not marked for itch; it writes `.itch.toml` (what the itch app launches), pushes with butler (`--userversion` the package version, `--if-changed`), shows a plan unless `--run`, and downloads butler with `--get-butler` (the broth URLs for the four platforms were checked to exist; Linux butler ran). (3) The push is a dispatch option of the release workflow, each platform pushed by its own job after its smoke test, so nothing large travels through artifacts; without `BUTLER_API_KEY` it shows the plan and warns. (4) Linux is pushed too (free, and it is built anyway). No HTML5 build: the game's recordings are too many and too large for it. (5) The Mac cannot be made to open without a warning from a browser download without a paid Developer ID, which only the author can get: the workflow imports a certificate into a temporary keychain and signs and notarises when its five secrets are set (written, not run); otherwise the Mac is ad hoc and the page says how to open it. Windows is unsigned (an EV/OV certificate or Azure Trusted Signing would fix SmartScreen: not set up). Checked: the itch Linux pack starts and reports channel itch with no Steam output; the refusals and the plan on a fake pack; not checked: a real push.

- **The Elder Sign on the executable, the app, the tab and the store (round 52, asked: "the game icon (.exe, .dmg) should be the elder sign", and a profile picture and screenshots for the itch.io page):** (1) The packed icon already was the Elder Sign's five strokes (`desktop/icon.js`); what was wrong with it is drawn into the new one: a thin stroke (0.036) that an inward-only edge ramp thinned further, so that at 16 px it was a smear; a rounded square to the very edge, which in a Mac's Dock stands larger than every other icon (Apple's template is 824 of 1024, with a shadow); and the browser tab still wore the old stair. Now: stroke 0.047, edges anti-aliased about the true edge, a lit core along each stroke, the bone paler toward the crown, the ground darker toward its corners; `iconPixels(size, shape)` has `round` (Windows, Linux, the window), `mac` (set in to 80.5% with a soft shadow: `build/icon-mac.png`, named by `build.mac.icon`) and `plain` (no corners, the sign set in to 80% so that a round crop keeps it: `build/avatar.png`, for a store's profile picture, which crops itself); the favicon in `index.html` is the same five strokes as an SVG. (2) There is no .dmg: a Mac build is an `.app` (itch's app runs it; a browser download gets it zipped), and a disk image would add a step that only matters when the app is notarised; the icon applies to the `.app`. (3) Proof, not assertion: CI prints each shipped icon (the .exe's through `ExtractAssociatedIcon`, the .app's through `sips`) as base64 into its log, and fails the Windows job if the icon has no purple glow in it (Electron's own is teal) and the Mac job if an app names no `icon.icns`; the icon of the Linux depot is the window's (a `dir` target embeds none). (4) The stills for the page (`tools/itch_art.mjs`) are made like the store's (`tools/store_shots.mjs`): a software renderer, so flatter than on a GPU; the author's own photo mode (P) on a real machine will make better ones, and the tool takes `--from <folder>` to dress those in the same way.

- **The vitals at the upper left, the hints at the lower (round 52, asked: "I'm thinking about putting the vitals, etc. top left and the tutorial hints bottom left, permanently"):** the bars, the Lucid line, Reagent and Revolver (`hud.ts`: `vitals`) stand at the upper left (`top:14px`), where the hints stood; the hints (`hints.ts`) stand at the lower left (`bottom:14px`), in the place the vitals left, so that a picture of the game has its life in the corner the eye starts from and a hint is read from below, out of the middle of the picture. The hint box is at most `min(360px, 22vw)` wide, so that in a window of 1280 it ends before the boss's bar begins (24% in); the boss's Gaze and Petrification bars (`bossHud.ts`), which stood above the vitals, stand below them now (`top:136px`, `168px`). The debug panel (`?debug`) is at the upper left too and covers the vitals as it covered the hints (H hides it). Asked as a thought, done as a layout: it is three lines to put back.
- **The boss's bar lower, thinner and quieter (round 52, asked: "lower the boss health a bit and make it more subtle but still legible, because it clutters the screen too much"):** it stood 100 px from the foot, 52% of the width, with a 14 px bar, a 14 px name and a 13 px run of blows, at full strength: a slab across the middle of every fight. Now 38 px from the foot, 38% wide, the bar 7 px (its frame, ticks and chip as before), the name 11 px, the run of blows 11 px, the status 10 px, all at 85–70% over a block at 88%: the name and the bar still read in the dark and in the bright, as the picture of a fight in R'lyeh shows. A prompt (`E`, 64 px up, centred) can touch the bar's line of status when a signature has one.
- **The Reagent's diamonds and the Revolver's rounds in one quiet grey (round 52, asked: "the diamonds and dots from reagent and revolver are too colorful and distracting"):** they were lavender (#bdb1cc) and gold (#c2ae78), the brightest colours in a corner that is otherwise bone and a muted red; both are now one warm grey (#8a8678), the hollow ones a fainter outline (BONE at 23%, not 33%), and the row at 85%. The shapes (a diamond, a round) tell the two apart, as the label does; a full pip against a hollow one still reads at a glance, which is all they are for.
- **Better pictures for the itch.io page (round 52, asked: "make new and way better screenshots, the old ones are pretty boring and an unpleasant angle", then "screenshot without sanity loss as it distorts the screen too much and looks weird"):** the first set was the game's own `?trailer` tour: eight places, each the investigator on one knee at an Elder Sign seen from behind and above, the same framing every time. The tool (`tools/itch_art.mjs`) now photographs fights instead: it takes the investigator to a horror's arena, makes him unkillable, walks him into the ring and has him strike, a picture every second or two, and the author or I choose by eye (a fight's moments are not predictable and a score for "a good picture" would be a guess). Three things are set for a picture and not for the game: the sanity effects' cap is 0 (`settings.fxCap`) and sanity is held at 100 (the warp that Cthulhu's presence puts on the whole picture reads as a fault in a still), every hint is marked told (a hint over the picture), and the debug panel is hidden. The first fights were photographed with the vitals at the lower left and the boss bar across the middle; those were thrown away when the HUD was changed (the vitals, the hints, the boss bar and the pips: above), and the set was made again.

- **700 × 394 default (round 52, asked: "increase the default resolution to 700x394"):** the resolution scale's default is 1.75 (× the render base 400 × 225; was 1.5, 600 × 338), still 0.5–2× in Settings › Display. The settings version is 14, so a saved resolution from before (the old default among them) takes the new one once, as at each such change; choices made at this version are kept. The first-launch quality ladder's top three rungs stand at 1.75 (the default picture is its first rung), so a slow machine still gives up its shadows, then fog, before the sharpness (1, at the fourth); it costs about 36% more pixels than 600 × 338 to draw. `docs/PERFORMANCE.md` and `docs/SPEC.md` say 700 × 394.

- **Dungeon water and boss voice audit (15 October 2026):** the open sea's waves extended beneath every dungeon and crossed sunk floors. The sea now discards fragments within each room's exact footprint, using a static nearest-filtered single-channel mask on the existing 4 m dungeon grid (all 290 rooms, about 1.1 MB); ocean between dungeon branches, wells and boss floods keep their own surfaces. All 53 authored boss lines across 27 horrors have recordings. The Voice in the Tomb has no fight script, so its two lines never reached speech; it now speaks on first sight and when vanquished using its roster ID. Boss recordings are warmed on sight/engagement, and repeated pending requests for the same recording play only the latest request. The Outsider remains wordless as authored: its third-person narrative captions have no cast voice or recording to restore.

- **Laudanum and ammunition (15 October 2026):** a dose restores 50 of the investigator's 100 sanity, capped at full. The revolver carries 12 spare rounds (two cylinders) plus six loaded; existing saves clamp to the new spare limit, and pickups and merchants already enforce the shared cap.
- **Atmospheric weather (15 October 2026):** keep the existing regional rain, gales, clear intervals, wet ground, wind and weather sound. Lightning now requires a gathered rain/gale at 65% strength instead of flashing in clear weather. Roofs exclude precipitation and flashes, indoor thunder is muffled to 35%, and travel clears the previous realm's pending strike/thunder.
- **Dungeon illumination (15 October 2026):** choose and occlusion-test world lights from the investigator's chest so rotating the camera cannot switch a whole torch's contribution off; camera rays independently hide obstructed halos. Torch and brazier intensity, range and glow are reduced, dry stone sheen is restrained, and reflected lamp streaks share the diffuse compression so overlapping pools do not burn the floor white. Browser comparison in the Cavern of Flame retained legibility and greatly reduced bright floor glare.
- **Nasht's silent third line (15 October 2026):** all 133 authored NPC lines have recordings; the runtime-only third line was one of 18 generic rumours with no recordings in any of the fifteen voices. With no compatible voice-generation connection available, keep conversations entirely recorded and present the rumour as a separate written note after a completed talk. An interrupted talk does not consume the note, and merchant wares follow its dismissal. This preserves the news without presenting unrecorded text as spoken dialogue.
- **Dreamlands stair water (15 October 2026):** the room mask shipped in PR #104 already removes the open sea from the Stairs of Slumber. Add a regression through all eight stair flights, including steps below storm-wave height; no additional water surface or gameplay change is needed.

- **600×338 as the standard ceiling; sharper modes are an explicit choice:** the default and standard slider maximum are 1.5×, with 700×394 and 800×450 inside a collapsed Higher resolutions control. Opening it warns that a sharper picture may weaken the intended retro look and immersion; an active override keeps that warning and offers a return to 600×338. Settings version 15 returns older v13/v14 higher modes to the standard ceiling, preserves lower preferences, and remembers new optional choices. The first-launch quality ladder starts at 600×338 and only steps down. Warning and controls are localized in every interface language.
- **Round 46 (see-through mounds, Gug):** (1) *Seams of the earth.* A recording showed a dungeon's outer wall through the heap over it. The audits stood inside the rooms and looked down on the tops, so none stood outside on the ground. `tests/outsideAudit.ts` (`outsideView.test.ts`, in the audits) stands on a ring about every mound-shelled dungeon and casts 24 directions at five elevations; a first hit that is the outside of an outer wall above the ground is a wall showing through, and a lintel over a way in is left out (it stands bare by design). It found three: at a joint between two walls in line ('joined' ends), each bank stopped half a wall short of its end, so the seam was open by a whole wall's width. Joined ends now meet at the cell's edge, as the earth is built to (`dungeonShell.ts`: only a 'neighbour' end is still cut). The rays are nudged off the grid, since a ray that runs exactly along a shared edge slips through it. (2) *Gug.* He was the strongest brute of his tier: 1100 health and 44 damage, so a slam (power 1.6) hit for about 70 and his grab (1.4, unblockable) for about 60 that no guard stops. He now has 900 health and 34 damage, and a blockable `seize` (the grab's timing, a guard stops it) in place of his unblockable grab; the unblockable grab stays for the others that use it. The foe bot still beats every creature (59 tests).
- **Round 47 (the fights' feel: Bokrug, staggers, cadence, the Ghoul's eyes, the flying Shantak):** (1) *Bokrug:* too hard in almost every way: the 2nd phase stacked the flood (the drag on the feet), the camera warp, a summons and two charges. The 1st phase has no flood; the 2nd keeps the flood alone, with one charge, and no summons and no warp. Health 3250 → 2600, damage 74 → 60, sanity drained 12 → 8, into the band of Rhan-Tegoth and Nug. The balance bot's fight: 52 s and 1527 damage taken (1.55 lives) → 33 s and 914 (1.00 lives). (2) *Staggers:* a blow's poise is the attack's poise times the creature's damage, against the player's 30, so two bites from a shantak (26 damage) or a sweep and a bite from a ghoul staggered them, and a stagger held them for 34 frames plus a respite. Player poise 30 → 60, so about twice as many blows land before a stagger; the rest is unchanged. (3) *Cadence:* the frames between a foe's attacks (60 a second) were 50–90 for the flyers and 30–70 for the pack hunters (a pack of ghouls struck about every second): flyer_swoop 110–170, hover_ranged 90–150, pack_hunter 55–100, ambusher 45–90, skirmisher 50–95. (4) *The Ghoul's eldritch eyes:* the green glow is gone (`lesser.ts`); the three eyes stay. (5) *Flying creatures: reverted.* A moving flyer's animation was changed in this round and the owner asked for that to be undone: nothing about how flying creatures animate is changed here. The Shantak's walk, which plays in the air (the reviewed art's move frames are a walk on the ground), stays as it was until the owner draws a flight cycle for it. The bot audits (boss, foe) and `npm run check` pass; the balance table is rewritten.


- **Round 48 (the Shantak's flight, blocking, relic symbols, the Elder Sign menu):** (1) *Shantak flight.* The creature's moving frames were its ground walk, so it walked in the air. The eight frames the author drew for flight are a new `flight` action in the reviewed pack (32 frames: four views, four per direction), and `creatureViews` draws it only for a moving creature that has that clip. No other flyer's art or animation changes: the earlier hover-frame change to them was reverted (d0a0a5f). (2) *The flight frames' ground rule.* `add_reviewed_frames.py` checks that a walk's lowest pixel sits on the idle's ground line; a flight stands above it by design, so `flight` is exempt: it may lift up to 12 px and sink up to 2 px. The integrity pins follow (2728 → 2760 frames, 2334 → 2366 at 64×64, 1100 → 1104 scopes). Not yet seen in play: checked by the pack tests and the typecheck. (3) *Blocking does not slow stamina.* The guard's regen multiplier (0.35) is gone, with `guardRegen` and the `guard` stamina mode: a blocking investigator regains stamina at the same rate as one who is not, after the same delay. Blocking still spends stamina per blow. (4) *Relic symbols.* Each relic has its own mark, drawn in code in `src/ui/relicGlyphs.ts` and keyed by relic id (a new relic needs one: the type enforces it): a star (Hook of the Star-Stone), an ankh on a coin (Coin of the Black Pharaoh), an eye (Lamp of the Pale Watcher), a spiral (Seal of the Yellow Sign, a polyline on r = 1.1 + 0.6θ), salt above brine (Charm of Salt and Brine). They sit at the start of each slot and relic button, and the marks stay out of the data. (5) *The Elder Sign menu, simpler without losing anything.* Tabs read Level up, Weapons, Relics, Travel (were Grow, Arms). Each row carries its name and count; what it gives and costs sits on the smaller line beneath, and the detail of a revolver or weapon step (shot and reach, light blow) shows in the foot when the row is chosen. The arms and insight explanations are shorter, the rest note is one line, and every function is kept (levels, insight upgrades, the revolver, reinforcement, moving star-stones, relics, travel, the Court and the Sleeper's Sign).
- **Horizon silhouettes stay drawn as the investigator walks up to them (16 October 2026, asked: "the city on the horizon just disappears when walking close to it", in the Dreamlands by the water, and to check the other horizon props):** every silhouette (the towns, hills, ranges, peaks, reefs, titans and the Dreamlands' Celephaïs) was hidden whenever the investigator stood within 1.5 × its drawing distance (about 147 m). The far plane is 140 m, so no real geometry took its place in that band, and the city by the Dreamlands' sea vanished as it was walked up to. The silhouettes are drawn first, before the sea and the world, so anything real standing where a silhouette stands now draws over it. The distance hide is removed; only a feature exactly under the investigator, which has no bearing, is skipped. The titans' halos and eyes already drew this way. Judgment: removed rather than cross-faded, since a fade would reopen the gap wherever the real thing is beyond the far plane. Regression test: every silhouette of every realm, from 20, 60, 140 and 400 m (it fails on the old rule). Checked in a browser: Celephaïs at 140 m shows its lit towers; at 80 m its upper part shows, its foot in the haze as designed.
- **A controller connected after launch is found (16 October 2026, asked: "the application fails to detect Bluetooth controllers or gamepads if launched prior to connection; a restart or pre-connection is required"):** the desktop shell reads controllers natively (`desktop/padWorker.js`, gamepad-node over SDL2). SDL lists the devices it found when it started, and the library's hot-plug events did not bring in one connected later, so only a restart found it. The library has no rescan call, so while no pad is heard the shell starts the worker over (a fresh start lists the devices connected now): after 5 s, then 7.5 s, 11 s and so on, up to every 30 s (`desktop/padRescan.js`). A pad heard resets the wait. Judgment: restart the worker rather than reach into SDL, which would need a new direct dependency (asked first). The cost is a worker start a few times at first and then about every 30 s, which a keyboard player does not notice. Checked: the smoke test passes (title, bridge, worker ready); a run with no controller restarted the worker at 5, 7.5 and 11.25 s, each time starting it again; `npm run check` passes (216 files, 2134 tests). Not checked: a controller connected after launch on macOS or Windows (no hardware here). The browser build is unchanged.
- **The Shantak flies in the sky (16 October 2026, asked: "the shantak is still playing the old walking animation in the air instead of the flying animation"):** the Shantak's flight (round 48, eight frames from every side) was packed and drawn when it was on the ground, but the sky visitors (`render/skyLife.ts`) always asked for the walk clip. They now ask `flyingClip` (`render/sprites/directional.ts`): the flight where a creature has one, else the walk. Only the Shantak has a flight clip, so no other flyer changes. Tested against the packed pack (`tests/flyingClip.test.ts`). Not seen in play: a sky visitor appears every 100 to 200 s in the Dreamlands.
- **The Shantak flies its wing-beat in the arena and the sky (16 October 2026, asked again: "the shantak is still WALKING in the air"):** the earlier fix covered only the Dreamlands' sky visitors. The creature view (`render/creatureViews.ts`) asked `lookOf` only for the stoop's frame counts, so a flyer's `flight` state was never known and a moving flyer always drew its walk. The view now tells `lookOf` every state the creature has in the way it faces (`clipCounts`, `render/sprites/directional.ts`) before it picks the state. Checked in the browser with `?spawn=shantak&debug`: the Shantak is drawn in the `flight` state while it moves, with its wings spread, and in `attack` while it dives. `window.creatures` is exposed in debug mode so this can be read again.

- **Unexpected pauses from focus changes:** a window blur opened Pause immediately, even when the document still had focus (reproduced in Chromium). Confirm that focus is still lost after 150 ms; a focus return cancels the check, while a hidden page pauses immediately. Only an observed captured-to-free mouse transition can pause or retry capture; releases for menus and cutscenes are ignored, and a delayed retry cannot capture an unfocused, hidden or already-captured page. The loading guard checks `building` before the cutscene object, which is not initialized yet while sprites load. Escape and pad Menu still pause immediately. Handler-level regression tests cover these cases in `tests/pauseMenu.test.ts`.

- **Stamina gives room to defend and counter:** blocked hits now charge half their listed guard cost across melee, bolts and waves. The Other Gods’ actual quake costs 39.5 of the initial 100 stamina instead of 79, leaving a fully funded roll and light counter; its heavy slam still costs 62.5, and repeated blocks break the guard. Base recovery is 32/s instead of 24/s, blocked hits now wait only 24 frames (0.4 s) instead of 48, and sprint drain is 14/s instead of 18/s. Actions retain their 48-frame recovery delay: shortening it allowed a completed roll to refund enough stamina to repeat forever, because any positive stamina can start an action. Both fast and complete roll chains still exhaust the bar. Endurance continues adding 0.8/s per level, reaching 48/s; existing saves recalculate this rate when loaded. Actual boss-attack and depleted-guard regressions live in `tests/bossStamina.test.ts`.

- **Sanity builds over a boss fight instead of demanding constant doses:** passive aura drain is 10% of the roster’s rate (the strongest boss is now 1 sanity/s), landed blows and roar/gaze windows take half their listed sanity, and first sight costs 4/6/10/12 for greater/named/great old one/outer god. The separate gaze burst is halved to 6. Laudanum still restores 50, now protects for 20 seconds, and also prevents landed blows taking sanity and gazes building through that protection; health damage and normal hit reactions still apply. Resolve retains its full resistance, aura distance/crowd rules and volley grouping remain effective, and avoidable void-platform penalties remain. Without Resolve, a minute of full aura plus first sight leaves 70 sanity against the Colour, 54 against Dagon, 46 against the Other Gods and at least 28 against every scripted boss. A scripted two-minute Other Gods timeline with three hits, two roars and one dose ends at 28 and never becomes Unmoored (`tests/sanityBossBalance.test.ts`); this bounds resource pressure, not a guarantee for unscripted play.

- **Encounter and first-visit fixes (17 October 2026):** a joining boss shares its leader's death lifecycle, so Keziah's fall also eliminates Brown Jenkin, once, whether hidden, fighting or not yet streamed; older saves suppress the surviving joiner. Cutscenes protect health, sanity and position through slow motion, skip fades and queued scenes. Notices hold for at least 3.5 seconds (longer for longer text), then fade over 1.2 seconds; queued pickups retain that time and hidden HUDs pause it. Hints use larger, higher-contrast text and a 1.5-second fade; stepping 0.6 m beyond a fog gate's interaction range rearms its instructions. Exterior mound collision reaches the visible steep bank and its corners while leaving doorways open. Speaking allies, including the Cavern of Flame priests, wait unharmed at their authored position until their first conversation; targeting, hits, shoves and body separation cannot displace them before they begin following or fighting.

- **Mount update (17 October 2026):** The Borrowed Grey, a plain saddled mare from the university stables, is the included early-game reward. The Spectral Chariot and the Brassbound Motor Car are optional $0.99 cosmetic DLCs, each with its own Steam app id, ownership and installation check, and the same fail-closed rules. All three reference one frozen traversal object (11 m/s cruise, 18 m/s gallop), so a paid appearance is never faster. The Borrowed Reins still asks for Peaslee then the Sleeper’s Sign and now grants the grey; journal text, the completion notice, the Mounts tab and `hint.mount` say so. Peaslee’s recorded lines are unchanged, because a new spoken line would need a new recording. Only appearance preference persists; a lost entitlement falls back to the grey. Violet fire is parented to each spectral horse’s body, neck and hooves and kept dim. Mount foley is non-positional, like footsteps, and procedural (hooves by gait and surface, wheels, harness, idle breath or hollow wind, summon and dismiss). Notes default to the burnt-paper screen; rumours use it too; the world pickup shares a charred sheet. Store ids remain release setup.

- **Allied sprites (17 October 2026):** Nodens, the Tritons, the Cats of Ulthar, Pickman, Nasht & Kaman-Thah, the Light-Being from Algol and the Yithian Archivist use a separate lossless atlas at `public/creatures/allies/`, merged in memory with the 105-creature pack. The enemy atlas is not repacked. Right views mirror the left except the Yithian's, which is drawn. Algol and the Yithian glide on four frames; the others walk on six. Scales match the sprites they replace, so the procedural ally atlas is no longer drawn. The delivery's installer targeted an older `reviewed.ts` and `bestiary.ts`, so the loader was ported onto the current files instead of overwriting them.

- **Tooltip rollback (17 October 2026):** restore the exact pre-PR #116 HUD and hint styling, timing, queue behavior and one-time gate hints. Remove the added notice controller, tooltip tuning and replay tests; preserve mount prompts and all paired-death, cutscene, exterior-collision and NPC introduction fixes.

- **Fatal edges (17 October 2026):** only the investigator falls. The land's edge, the sea outside a dungeon, pits, wells, bridge gaps and a hidden bridge's seal are fatal; mound banks and the arena ring stay walls, and foes are still pushed out of those drops. Lost Echoes spawn on the ground at the last safe feet (the previous step, when that step could still be stood on), so the drop is not left in the void. A cutscene does not fall. A mount does not go off the land (round 50: it was written that its nose may overhang the void and the step off be the fall, but the world's containing wall pushes each of its footprint's circles back, so in play a ride stops a rider's length short of the edge, as at a wall; the fall path stays for a world with no such wall). Water and slopes stop the mount short too.
- **Boss attack colours (17 October 2026):** each scripted horror, including the Elder Shoggoth and the Polyp Swarm, tints its existing telegraphs, particles and beams. The blows' motion is unchanged: the same arcs, bursts, trails and rings, in that horror's colours, because the shared magenta and green made every attack read as one effect.
- **Shots (17 October 2026):** the void-green fireball stays Yog-Sothoth's spheres. The Colour keeps an orb in its own hue. Every other caster's bolt is a shard, a drop, a spark or a dart in that creature's colours, and it still flies on the same path. A lob, and a sea or ichor body, is a drop.
- **Area marks (17 October 2026):** a horror's ground effects keep their reach and their clock, and take a mark that fits the horror: tide, glyph, crack, flame, spore, coil, eye, root, star, frost, ash or stain. The same mark is drawn in the telegraph, the eruption, the quake and the pool. An ordinary fighter keeps the plain fill. Two horrors that share a kind still differ by the mark's seed.

- **Plain chariot (17 October 2026):** the included mount is no longer a saddled mare. It is one horse and a wooden chariot whose floor, walls, roof, wheels, shafts and tufted seat are the spectral coach's, with the curtains, crest, violet lamps and mourning black left off. The seat stays at body local `(0, 0.79, 0.08)`, so the seated rider is unchanged. The horse is built with the coach, not imported. The save id remains `grey`, so an old save is not rejected. Foley is one horse's hooves plus wheels and harness, with no idle breath and no spectral wind. The controller helper is not killed to rescan: replacing that utility process wakes the macOS dock and the unfocused game pauses. The same process builds a new `GamepadManager` instead. A crashed worker may still be started again, up to three times.

- **Plain chariot horse (17 October 2026):** a block prototype read as a toy, which is a cheap look and not the restraint the picture wants. The horse is a loft in metres, facing +z, with enough stations to read as a barrel, a neck, a head and a hoof. Its coat is a generated hide: hair and grain in one value, slightly warm, so the bay, the black points, the pale muzzle and the darker belly are vertex colour under the same world shader as the rest of the scene. The legs are two bones skinned on the CPU, one mesh each, so the knee folds and the hoof plants without a GPU skin or a second draw. No veins, no brand, no fire.
- **Mount prompt (17 October 2026):** the included mount is named Chariot. The summon line and the ride line stay five seconds, then the prompt is free for whatever is at hand. They are not a fixture of the picture.
- **Mounts indoors (17 October 2026):** a ride that reaches a dungeon room or a boss arena drops the mount, and the summon stays refused there. Water, slopes and walls still stop the chariot where it stands. The check is the chariot's own footprint, so the horse does not remain in the saddle against the door.
- **Kenney horse, dressed (17 October 2026):** the loft is set aside. The carriage horse is Kenney's CC0 animal-horse again, with that file's walk and run, and a coat so it is not one flat colour: vertex bay with black points, a pale muzzle and blaze, a dark mane and tail, and a little shade from the normal. The chariot and the seat stay the spectral coach's. No second horse asset. The hide is a soft mottle in one value, drawn large so it does not quantize into stripes, and the split-vertex normals are averaged so a face does not paint a panel. A sine of hair strands read as stripes once the picture was quantized, so that line is gone. The bay is dark. The eyes are two small spheres on the head, one mesh, with a catch of light.

- **Mount review, round 50 (17 October 2026):** what the review of the last round's mount work found, and what was done.
  - **The violet fire never drew.** Its fragment shader read `uSpeed` and never declared it, so the program did not compile (`Shader Error ... 'uSpeed' : undeclared identifier`, found by reading the console in the live scene), and three drew nothing; the fire had been "kept dim" in a test that never saw it. `uSpeed` is declared, the smoothsteps whose edges were written backwards (undefined in GLSL) are the right way round, and the presence is raised until the wisps read at night (the tongues are half again as tall). The three mounts and eight horrors' fights were then run in the live scene for shader and console errors: none.
  - **A ride could not be heard.** Footsteps are muted on a mount, and the mount's own sounds were a few short filtered ticks at rms about -35 dBFS, level with a human footfall and under the ambience; the speed they followed was measured from the simulation's 60 Hz position by render frame, which is bursty (a frame with no step read as standing still), and beats were played by distance only, not by the feet. Now: the simulation counts the gait's cycles (`MountState.phase`, `systems/mountGait.ts`) and a hoofbeat is the frame a foot comes down in that gait, so a horse that stops stops at once and the sound is the picture's; each beat is a layered recipe (thud, the hollow of the hoof, the clack of the shoe on road and stone, the splash in the shallows, at a gallop the ground itself), panned left and right by the foot, with the recorded footfall laid beneath it pitched down for the included horse; under the beats run beds that follow the speed and the ground (the chassis' roar, gravel and cobble rattle pulsing with the wheel's turn, the air rushing by, the motor car's engine climbing with its speed, the spectral chariot's cold drone); over them the harness' brass on each stride, timber creaks, wheel knocks, a snort at the gallop, an idle snort now and then. Every one-shot sits at -22 to -11 dBFS rms (a landed blow is about -18) and never over full scale, and a walk's hoofbeat is 5 dB under a gallop's (`tests/mountAudio.test.ts`). The gap a frame may be before a ride's sounds are dropped is a quarter second (it was a tenth: below ten frames a second nothing was heard).
  - **The horse is built here again, and well.** The Kenney block was a pony the size of a donkey beside a coach three metres tall, a sausage of a barrel on stubs, no harness, shafts that ended in air, and at the gallop the run clip folded the legs under it; at night its near-black bay was a hole in the picture. The new horse is a chestnut draft horse of sixteen hands (`horseBody.ts`, `horseLegs.ts`, `horseLoft.ts`): a lofted barrel with haunches and shoulders, an arched neck with a flaxen mane, a long head with a blaze, ears, eyes and nostrils, a flaxen tail, white stockings with feathered fetlocks, black harness with brass (collar and hames, back pad with shaft tugs, girth, breeching, crupper, bridle with blinkers, bit and rings). Chestnut and flaxen read in the dark where the bay did not; at 400 x 225 the big forms and the blocked colours are what show, so those are where the work went. The shafts now run to the collar. The rig (`draftHorse.ts`) hangs it on joints a horse has and places each foot by the shared gait: feet stand still on the ground while down (the skate measured over a cycle is a hundredth of a step at a canter), fold through the air and come down on the hoofbeat; each leg is a two-bone solve that places the fetlock, so a knee folds forward and a hock drives back and the hoof lies flat on the ground at every speed (never more than a millimetre under it, tested); the body crouches and rocks with the gait, the neck nods in the walk and reaches out in the gallop, breathing, ear flicks, a tail swish and, now and then, a pawed hoof stand it at rest. The skeleton horses are on the same gait (their feet no longer slide), their second horse a sixteenth of a cycle behind the first. The included horse's pace is a walk (1.5 m/s), a trot (5.5), a canter (10, the cruise) and a gallop (17+): a cycle is 1.8 m at a walk to 5.4 m at the gallop, a foot is down 64% of it at a walk and 26% at a gallop. The old Kenney data (`draftHorseData.ts`) is gone, and with it the credit it needed.
  - **Hoofbeats throw dust.** A puff where each hoof comes down (the gait's own footfall: `hoofFx.ts`), a spray in the shallows, a spark from an iron shoe on stone at the gallop, a violet ember from a skeleton's bone, and a thin dust behind each rear wheel on loose ground.
  - **The mount's words are in the four languages.** The summon and ride lines, every refusal, the quest's notice and the Mounts tab were English in a game that has four; they are keys now, the notices in the capitals the rest of the notices use.
  - **Reviewed and left as they are:** the fatal edges, the area marks, the attack colours and shots, the allied sprites, the burnt-paper notes, the controller look (a fresh manager on the living helper process reads `sdl.controller.devices`, whose getter polls SDL's events, so a pad connected after launch is listed), the dungeon and arena drop, the five-second prompts. `npm run audit` passed on the merged main before this round's changes.
  - **A fall that was saved loaded inside itself (round 50, found by a scan):** the investigator who falls is not marked dead, so the autosave ran through the death move and wrote feet in a pit, well or bridge gap; loading that put the body back in the fall, where it died again at once and the saved Echoes were lost. Autosave now waits out a death move, and a load whose feet cannot stand (`footingSafe`) wakes at the checkpoint, as one outside any region already did (`tests/falls.test.ts`).
  - **Two more from the scans:** an unthemed summoner's burst is the old green again (the default branch had gone purple); a gaze's burst takes the colour of whoever gazed (`GazeBurst` names them, as its lane's telegraph already did); a menu skin whose closing fade ended inside its own frame set its next frame after clearing the timer and drew, hidden, for good (`tests/menuSkin.test.ts`).

- **Attack effects, round 51 (17 October 2026):** every boss and creature attack effect redesigned (docs/ATTACK_FX.md). The blows themselves are unchanged.
  - **Legible before pretty.** Half the horrors' danger colours were near black (Cthulhu's 0x0b3a32 on a dark floor: the first look at an eruption showed a few pale specks and no mark at all). `attackLook` lifts the colours that must be found to a luma floor (0.42 for ground marks, 0.58 for lights) keeping their hue; the author's dark remains as the ink of scars and pools. The data file is untouched, so a horror's palette is still one place.
  - **One element per horror, not one effect per attack.** The twelve marks the ground already wore (`aoeLook`) became the twelve elements of everything else (the matter of a burst, impact, trail and charge; the timbre of the sounds), so a new horror needs its colours and a motif, and the effects are the same code for all. An unthemed creature throws what it is made of (palette to element).
  - **The simulation says when a bolt leaves and ends, and when a blow opens** (`BoltLoosed`, `BoltLanded`, `Swung`): each is one emit with nothing read back, so no timing, damage or RNG changed (`tests/attackEvents.test.ts`). The effects used to guess these from the bolts present, and could not know where a bolt ended.
  - **Budgets.** Peak particles were measured per move against stand-ins for the batches (`tests/bossFx.test.ts`, every creature in `bossFxAudit`): before the trails were thinned, a barrage's bolts kept 1 525 of the batch's 2 400 particles alive at the peak (and 12 000 to 15 000 were thrown over the moves of the horrors that have one); trails now thin with the number of bolts in the air. A colossus's blasts size their light by radius, capped, so a fifteen-metre ring is not a screen-wide glow.
  - **Sound is by event and by hum, not by animation.** A sweeping beam, a vortex and a gaze are kept up by short recipes laid end to end (the pause menu stops the world, so nothing is left sounding), a volley of five is one sound, and the gathering before a blow shares its frames with the light that gathers (`windup.ts`). Levels are held to a landed blow's.
  - **The first look at the effects in a browser was invalid.** The earlier claim in PR #127 that eight horrors' fights ran with no shader or console errors was made against the training Deep One: `&variant=boss` is only for creatures that have a boss variant, so those pages never spawned the bosses. The lab spawns the real ones (`?spawn=<id>`), and this round's checks are made so.


- **Mounts, round 52 (17 October 2026), asked: "replace or eliminate the metallic clatter", "address intermittent mount immobilization, particularly during directional changes", "enhance the sound design for the remaining mounts", "authentic equine turning mechanics (both chariot mounts)":**
  - **The clatter was the brass jingle.** Measured offline, 74% of its energy sat above 2.5 kHz, and it struck on every stride beside a bandpassed rattle at the wheel's rate. The jingle and the chain's rattle are gone, the roll under the ride is a low-passed noise (cut-off by ground: 380 Hz dirt, 600 road, 820 stone) that swells a little with the wheel's turn, and `tests/mountAudio.test.ts` holds the recurring one-shots (the wheel's thump, the hoofbeats, the leather's shift, the creak) to a small share of their energy above 2.5 kHz. The motor car's engine now climbs through four gears and drops at each shift; the spectral chariot has a cold drone with a breath of wind that rises as it quickens.
  - **Immobilization was two things.** A rotation the colliders refused left the rig at its old yaw and the next frame asked the same again, and a turn through a large heading error was paced down to zero ("stop to turn"). The rig now applies the turn in 1, 0.5 or 0.25 of the step, the footprint being five 1 m circles over 4.8 m, and after six frames of a jam it backs off (`backOff`) rather than wait. A scrape against a wall nudges the heading away only while the rider is steering (|error| > 0.12 rad): the first nudge steered round bodies straight ahead and broke the old test. The new tests were run against the old code and fail there.
  - **The turn is a circle.** `systems/mountSteer.ts`: the tightest circle widens with speed (`turnRadius`, speed^1.5), the yaw rate is capped by it (`turnCap = max(v/R, pivot(1 − v/4))`), the pace falls toward `creep` for a large heading error and never to zero, and the rate's acceleration is limited. A standing horse steps round on the spot (`pivot`); the car keeps its arcade steering with a creep in place of a stop. `data/mounts.ts` `MOUNT_TURNING` holds both sets of numbers; the horses lead the carriage into the turn (`bend`, drawn in `mountViews.ts`).
  - **Not checked:** how the new sounds feel by ear (only their spectra and levels are tested), and real play for a stall (only simulated rides).

- **Weather, round 52 (17 October 2026), asked: "increase the frequency of rain and thunderstorms (none seen in a two-hour test)", "rare, random ground strikes preceded by a brief but avoidable indicator, damaging the player and enemies", "enhance the visual and auditory fidelity of all weather phenomena":**
  - **Why none was seen.** The hub had no weather at all, calms were 150 to 420 s and the region tables gave rain and gale only. `WEATHER.calm` is 60 to 220 s, `clear` 1.3 (another calm follows a calm 1 time in 4), the hub has weather, and a new `storm` kind (heavy rain, wind and lightning) is in every region's table. `tests/weatherFrequency.test.ts` runs two hours of every region and asserts, for the start region and six others, that rain and storm together are blowing over a fifth of the time, storms gather at least three times, clear sky remains and no dry spell lasts half an hour, and that the first rain or storm in the start region comes within a quarter of an hour.
  - **A strike on the ground is a sim event with a telegraph** (`systems/skyStrikes.ts`, `SKY_STRIKE`): once a storm has gathered 0.6 of its strength, first strike 8 to 24 s on and then every 28 to 75 s, a spot 3 to 24 m from the investigator (30% of the time near a foe within 30 m) is marked, and 90 frames (1.5 s) later a bolt falls on it. Radius 3.4 m, 55 damage to the investigator, a base 40 plus 20% of health (capped at 320) to anything else, 90 poise. A body outside the ring is untouched and so is one in a dodge's i-frames; nothing is struck under a roof, in a dungeon room or on the sea. The sky's choices come from `g.rng` (a replay sees the same sky) and the hit goes through `strike()` from a transient transform-only "sky" entity, so the relics' price on blows taken applies and the roll's i-frames dodge it; it is unblockable and cannot be parried. `StrikeWarned` and `StrikeLanded` are emit-only.
  - **Visuals.** The warning is a ring on the decals batch (`GroundFx.warn`) whose crackle quickens to the fall; the bolt is a branching ribbon on the beams batch with a white-hot core, no fog mix, a width that grows with distance and a flash of the sky and light (`lightningPath.ts`, `skyStrikeFx.ts`). Rain is drawn in grains with splashes (a thunderstorm 680 streaks about the lens), and each spell takes part of the fog's reach (`haze`: rain 0.32, storm 0.5, gale 0.12). A first version's bolt was a thin line that vanished in the grade, and its halo showed as an arch in the sky; both were found in a browser and mended.
  - **Sound** (`data/stormSounds.ts`, `render/audio/stormAudio.ts`, `weatherBed.ts`): rain is three layers (the far hiss, the body on the ground, the near drops in grains made by two slow waves that never agree), the gale a low rush that swells in gusts with a howl that wanders, a storm both, and thunder is a crack and a roll that arrive later and duller with distance. A strike's crack was +2.2 dBFS at first and is below full scale and level with a landed blow's.
  - **Not checked:** the weather by ear, and a long walk in a storm for frame time (the benchmark was not re-run).

- **Menus, round 52 (17 October 2026), asked: "streamline the resting sign interface and all comparable menus: relic effects red for negative and green for positive, condense text, starstone quantities clearly visible":**
  - **One vocabulary for numbers.** `ui/menuChips.ts`: a figure under a choice's name (green gift, red price, dim aside), a price boxed at the row's right (bright affordable, red unaffordable, "MAX"), and the pinned strip of what is carried with the figure the tab spends lit. The colours are `GOOD` and `BAD` in `ui/menuParts.ts`. The figures carry their sign ("Damage taken −8%" is green) so colour is not the only signal, and the red and green differ in brightness as well as hue.
  - **Relic effects are data.** `relicEffects(id)` (`systems/relics.ts`) returns `{text, good}`; `good` is `dealt > 1`, `mend > 1`, `echoes > 1` or `taken < 1`. It replaced `effectsOf`, a string. `tests/relics.test.ts` holds each relic to at least one gift and one price and fixes three of the strings.
  - **Text that said what the rows already say is gone.** The Weapons paragraph ("Star-stones: 7. Each level …") is a foot line and the count is in the strip; the Relics paragraph and the Arms page's relic lines are figures; the long rest line is one short line, a different one per tab; Travel's instruction is its foot line; the Mind's insight explanation shows only when a passage is held open (the passages a purchase would shut are red on the row). The mounts' shared speeds were printed on every row and are one line in the foot.
  - **Left as they were:** the pause menu (already five plain lines), the rise menu (two options with a hint each), settings, the journal and the map. Not touched: the localisation of the sign menu (it was English, and stays so; the mounts tab is still keyed).

- **The itch.io version label (round 53, 17 October 2026), asked: push to itch "with the correct version suffix this time (not 1.0.0-rc.1 again)":** every build had gone out as `1.0.0-rc.1` because `tools/itch_push.mjs` hands butler `package.json`'s version (`--userversion`) and nothing had raised it since the release candidate. (1) The version is `1.0.0-rc.2` in `package.json` and the lockfile: the next release candidate, not 1.0 (the foot of `CHANGELOG.md` still lists what stands between), and the changelog's sections since rc.1 are now rc.2's. A save's `made` field records it (`tests/fixtures/save-v2-rc1.json` stays an rc.1 save, which a later build must still read). (2) A push can say its own label without a commit: the workflow's *itch_version* input, `ITCH_VERSION` or `--version` on the script, checked to be a version, so a mistaken label can be mended from Actions › Run workflow. (3) The rule is in `docs/RELEASE.md` step 5: raise `package.json`'s version (`-rc.3`…) with its changelog heading before each push that goes out. Not done: a check that the label is new on the channel (that needs butler's login and the page; `butler status digitaldro/seventy-steps:windows` shows what each channel carries).
- **Review of PR #130 (round 53), four findings, all real, all fixed:** (1) *A scrape past a wall rotated the rig by a fixed 0.1 rad a step, so at a gallop (two steps a frame) up to 0.2 rad, about 12 rad/s against a cap of about 1.* The nudge is a collision response, not a steer, so it is not folded into `mount.yawRate` (that rate feeds the next frame's steering and would carry the snap on); it is bounded to 0.04 rad a frame across all the frame's steps (`SCRAPE`), about twice a horse's turn rate at a gallop. The wall tests, which need the rig backed off and brought round, still pass; `tests/mountTurning.test.ts` holds a gallop's scrape to 0.07 rad a frame (it was 0.12). (2) *A body that stepped under a roof, or into a dungeon room, within the ring when the bolt fell was struck,* though `aim` never marks such ground: the landing now skips them too. (3) *The ring could be marked on the seabed or in the shallows near a coast,* where no one sees it: a candidate at or below `seaLevel + 0.4` is rejected (a region check was tried and left out: the arena the tests stand in is outside every region). `tests/skyStrikes.test.ts` stands on Innsmouth's shore and holds forty marks to land. (4) *A ground strike's thunder replaced the thunder of a flash still on its way* (one slot): the rumbles are a list now. The tests for all four were run against the old code and fail there (the scrape: 0.12 rad a frame measured, against 0.07 held).
- **Blocking balance (17 October 2026), asked to restore slower regeneration while blocking and fix other reasons it is too strong:** restore the pre-round-48 `guardRegen` multiplier of 0.35 on the actor's actual guard, composing with Endurance and Unmoored recovery as before. Keep the later base recovery, half guard costs and short successful-block delay: the Other Gods' actual wave still costs 39.5 stamina, leaving room for a roll and counter. Four further openings are restored: (1) `absorb` preserves a longer existing action delay instead of replacing it with the shorter block delay; (2) the guard-break reaction counts down that delay but cannot regenerate stamina while helpless, so it no longer refunds a fresh guard during the opening; (3) a block pays only for the fraction of HP damage its remaining stamina covers, with the unpaid fraction hurting through the guard and the full guard-break reaction retained (or death on lethal damage). Exact-cost hits still fully absorb and break; zero stamina absorbs nothing. This deliberately replaces the earlier rule that even a tiny or empty bar erased the entire breaking blow; (4) raising block or queuing a parry after a committed blow/shot waits for the move's own cancel frame, preventing recovery removal and same-step block-plus-attack restarts before that frame. Wind-up aborts remain. This revises the earlier instant recovery-cancel judgment; normal dodge cancels, guard arc and funded blocks are unchanged. Regressions cover the recovery modifiers, real guard-break duration, zero/partial/exact/lethal absorption, every weapon's recovery, shot recovery and actual Other Gods attacks.


- **Rain, round 54 (17 October 2026), asked: "replace the current rain sound effects with more subtle and aesthetically balanced alternatives (it resembles white noise or static and may conflict with other ambient elements)" and "review the frequency of rain events so that they are realistic and not excessive":**
  - **Why it was static.** The rain was a bandpassed white noise at 3.2 kHz (Q 0.4: a band of one to six kilohertz), a lowpassed one under it, and a high-passed crackle at 4.3 kHz gated by two slow waves: three sources of the same broadband noise, level with the crickets and the wind in the hiss region. `render/audio/rainTexture.ts` makes rain of two things instead, both as loops drawn once at start and closed on themselves (the ends are made to meet): a **wash** (noise rolled off twice at 1.1 kHz and 650 Hz, its rumble below 140 Hz taken out, a slow swell of a few seconds in it) and **drops** (a few to a few dozen a second at random times and places between the ears, each a soft sine 600 Hz to 2.6 kHz that rises a little and dies in a few hundredths of a second, with a one-millisecond tick; their loudness is spread evenly in decibels so most are faint, a third carry a low heavy note of 200 to 520 Hz, which is what a roof lets through). Light rain is the wash and the sparse drops; the dense set (`shower`) comes in above 40% of a spell's strength.
  - **How loud, measured.** Offline (`tests/rainTexture.test.ts`): the whole bed at its fullest is about -33 dBFS with under 4% of its energy above 3 kHz, its loudest drop below -14 dBFS; the drops' peak is more than ten times their average (a texture of events, not a level).
