In a real audio context (an analyser on the bed's bus, headless Chromium): clear night -29.3 dBFS, full rain -28.3, storm -27.0: the rain sits within about a decibel of the ambience instead of several above it. Not done: listening to it on speakers and headphones, which only the author can.
  - **How often, measured.** Six seeds over six hours in every region with weather, before: rain or storm 45% of the time, a wet spell every four to five minutes, a thunderstorm every twelve to twenty, the first spell within seconds of a game or load (the weather is not kept in a save, and its first spell was chosen at frame 0, with a three in four chance of a spell then). Round 52 overshot its own aim (a storm in two hours). Now `WEATHER.calm` is 240 to 840 s and `spell` 100 to 260 s, `first` (new: a clear 300 to 780 s at the start of a game or a load) and the regions' weights moved toward rain over storm (the hub 5 : 3 : 1 rain : storm : gale, Arkham 4 : 3 : 1, Dunwich 3 : 3 : 2, R'lyeh 3 : 4 : 1, the mountains 5 gale : 1 storm). Result: wet about 17 to 20% of the time, 3 to 4 wet spells an hour, 1.1 to 2.4 thunderstorms an hour (the mountains 0.7), the first wet spell 18 to 25 minutes in on average (45 at most), the longest dry run 31 to 40 minutes on average (48 at most). `tests/weatherFrequency.test.ts` holds those with margin over three seeds and four hours per region.
  - **Left as it was:** the strikes on the ground (their own timing: 8 to 24 s after a storm gathers, then every 28 to 75 s), the sound of wind and thunder, the rain's look.

- **The run that stopped by itself, round 54 (17 October 2026), asked: "during sprinting or galloping, the character occasionally halts without clear cause; it appears with mouse and keyboard and should be verified on a controller as well":** the cause was found in the input layer, not the simulation (a probe that sprinted a hundred runs across every region with stamina held full found only real collisions: houses, trunks, boulders, fences).
  - **The repeat silence (`core/input.ts`).** Round 49 let go of a key that "has been heard to repeat and then says nothing for 3.5 s", to mend a release that is never heard. The system repeats only the key pressed last, so a run held on W and then a sprint on Space leaves W held and silent: three and a half seconds into the sprint (or the gallop: it is the same key) W was let go and the investigator stood sprinting nowhere. It needed only a second key; a roll tapped over a held W did the same. Now a key newly pressed ends the repeating of the others (`repeats.clear()`: their silence is explained, and no longer says they were let go), and a key let go is forgotten. What is left of the safety net is the case it was made for: the last-pressed key that repeated and then fell silent.
  - **The blink of the window.** `blur`, `focus` and `visibilitychange` let go of every key and button (round 31); a notification, an overlay or a focus taken and given back (the same blips that once paused the game by mistake, PR #114, which the pause guard now ignores under 150 ms) took the run and the sprint with it, and only the key pressed last came back, on its own repeat. A window away for less than 400 ms is now returned to what was held (no fresh press: a sprint carried on, not begun again); longer, or paused in between, nothing is held on the return.
  - **The controller (`core/pads.ts`).** Its own stop was found by reading, not seen: the correction for a stick that has drifted (a stick lying still within 0.4 of its rest for four seconds is at rest there, round 49) could take a light push held steady through a sprint for a stick at rest, and the sprint went nowhere. It no longer applies while any button is down. The pad's buttons need no repeat and have no silence rule, and a pad's dodge is exempt from the stuck-button rule. Not verified: a controller in hand (only a made-up one in the tests, and the code read).
  - **Verified.** Four tests (`tests/inputStuck.test.ts`) fail on the old code and pass: W then Space for ten seconds, W after the sprint is let go, the blink, and the pad. In headless Chromium with real key events (W repeating, then Space repeating, nine seconds): the old code stood still at 7.3 s with the sprint dropped (z unchanged from 3.9 s), the new one was still sprinting and moving at 9.3 s.
  - **Not changed:** the sprint ends at an empty stamina bar, as it should (about seven seconds from a full bar at the start, longer with Endurance), and a gallop stops where the mount cannot go on (a wall, a dungeon door, a boss ring, the sea): those are rules, and they say so.

- **The edge is a fall, and UNMADE stands alone, round 54 (17 October 2026), asked as urgent: "the player character should not instantly die when reaching a ledge, the death should occur because you stepped too far over the edge and literally fall down, implement a good looking animation", then: "if you are stepping too far over an edge, there shouldn't be an animation that starts because the player should be able to save himself ... only a falling and camera animation when already falling past the possibility of saving by simply going back ... you should be able to balance on the very edge and only fall and die when there is actually no contact with the floor anymore" (and "the balancing should not be an animation, it was just a saying"), and "when dying UNMADE should always be the only text that's displayed":**
  - **The rule (`systems/falls.ts`, `FALL` in `data/playTuning.ts`).** The feet's hold is a ring 0.18 m about the body's centre (the centre and eight points on it). While any of it is on the floor (outside every `fall` collider) the body stands: it may hang its heels over a pit's lip and walk back, and nothing is drawn or begun for it (no teeter: the figure keeps walking as it was). When none of it is, and the body is on the land (a region, above the sea outside a dungeon), the fall begins: `g.player.falling` (frames, vertical speed, the run-up's speed clamped to 4.5 m/s and spent at 10 per second so the body goes 0.45 m past the lip at most, the tumble's direction, the last feet on the floor). The old capsule test (any touch kills) is kept only for saves (`footingSafe`: a save whose feet are in a pit still wakes at the checkpoint).
  - **The drop.** 1.1 s under 22 m/s² (a pit is 12 m deep, so the body is a metre above the floor of it at the end); the ground has no say meanwhile (`movementSystem` leaves the body alone; it stays where it fell, in the dark, until the waking, where `restore` clears it), input is not heard, a move in hand, a mount, a lock and a sprint are let go of at the first frame, the autosave waits, and no blow, pool or ground strike can reach it (`hostiles`, and `strike` and `shove`, the one boundary every source passes). At the foot of the drop the death move begins and `Died` is emitted with the Lost Echoes at the lip, on the ground there (the old rule, unchanged). `Fell` is the new event at the first frame (the sound, the HUD, the death line's note).
  - **What is seen.** The pose is its own (`poses.ts` `plunge`: the arms thrown up and out, the legs running on the air, the head thrown back, then the hips over and a head-first tumble at 3.4 rad/s about the hip; no ground is settled to). The camera follows 65% of the way down, and swings up to look straight down the hole (`stepCamera`'s `probe`: the boom's walls are read at the lip's height, since a pit's floor is the room's and a body that has gone through it would shut the camera against it; seen from behind the ledge, the fall was hidden by the ledge itself). The picture goes dark from 40% of the drop to its foot, edges first (`uFade` in the post pass, `fallDark`, held until the waking), so UNMADE comes over black. A new sound, `plunge`: a breath, the rush of air rising and thinning, a falling whistle, a weight below; UNMADE's own sound follows at the foot as it did.
  - **Left as it was.** The sea and the land's end (no region, or the ground below the sea's edge outside a dungeon) are the quick death they were: there is no ledge to see go over (the coast slopes away, the feet are on it the whole way). Mounts stop short of any edge. Foes are pushed out of drops as before.
  - **UNMADE alone.** On a fall or a death the HUD fades (the bars, Echoes, minimap, hints, notices, foe and boss plates, the interact prompt, the overheard line; the notices that were waiting are dropped), any other great words (a place's name, a horror's fall, a discovery) are not shown over it, and its two lines (`hud.lie`, removed from all four languages, and the death line) are no longer under it. The death line is read in the dark instead: the veil that comes over UNMADE says it under the name of where they wake (`journeys.ts`), a fall's own lines (`EDGES`) after a fall, where a lit candle's rise menu stands the menu says it itself, in a dim line under its title (the veil has no words there, which is where its place's name would be). The veil's words show only once it covers everything, after UNMADE has faded, so they are never on the screen together.

- **Things meet the ground, round 54 (17 October 2026), asked: "strengthen shadows where objects meet the ground ... contact shadows, houses, trees and rocks cast shadows onto the terrain, excessive shadow bias can leave a visible gap beneath objects; subtle ambient occlusion, SSAO or GTAO, avoid thick black halos; reduce excessive ambient/skylight, shaded areas readable but noticeably darker than lit surfaces; blend the bases into the terrain with patches of grass, soil, roots, decals; for an open world, good shadow detail near the camera, fading gradually into the distance":**
  - **What was already there.** Houses, trees and rocks already cast (`CASTS` in `propMeshes.ts`: stone, wood, leaf, clapboard, brick, shingle; a test reads it), the terrain receives, and the post pass already has a screen-space contact shadow (`contactAo`, eight taps, 0.75 m) and the props' own vertices are darkened toward their feet (`groundShade`). What was missing was the ground: the terrain about a foot was the same as the terrain far from it, so every base had a clean line.
  - **The ground takes the mark (`world/groundContact.ts`, `GROUND_CONTACT` in `data/fxTuning.ts`, `render/terrainMesh.ts`).** The footprint of every solid prop (its collider: a cylinder or an oriented box; slimmer than 0.2 m, a lamp post or a fence, leaves none) darkens the terrain's vertices about it by up to 62% at the edge, falling with a smoothstep to a reach of 1.1 m plus 0.55 per metre of its size (at most 3.4 m), and turns their tint toward bare earth (70%, over the inner 60% of the reach, its edge broken by noise 0.45 m either way: a patch, not a ring). Two things close together take the strongest mark, not the sum (no black pool). Baked at chunk build into the colours the mesh already has: no pass, no texture, nothing at run time. The footprints of the neighbouring chunks are gathered too (a house across a seam marks both sides alike), a neighbour at a time, in slices: a chunk's content is made the first time it is asked for. A test finds a house in the hub and compares the vertices within a metre of its walls (darker by 15% or more) with those six to nine metres out.
  - **The plants and roots (`render/baseCover.ts`).** About each footprint the ground's own cover (its biome's mix: tufts, ferns, tall grass, brambles, toadstools, pebbles; reeds on mud, dry grass on sand, only pebbles on stone) stands a few more to the metre of reach, seeded by the prop, never on a road or in a dungeon; about each tree, pine and stump four to six roots flare from the trunk into the earth, flat wedges as dark as the trunk's foot. Merged into the chunk's ground cover and so into the props' meshes: no draw call added. 3 to 11 ms a chunk, in slices of 24 props. `groundCover.ts` exports its builder and a `plantAt` the base cover shares (its output is unchanged).
  - **The shadow map.** 1536 texels over the same 68 m (4.4 cm, was 6.6), bias 0.035 m (was 0.07) and normal offset 0.06 m (was 0.12), which at the ground's slope under the moon were a lifted 0.2 m and a 0.12 m hold, a visible gap along a wall's foot on the lit side; the moon's strength 0.94 (was 0.9). Shadows fade over the far half of the map's width (`smoothstep(0.5, 0.98)`, was `0.7, 0.97`): near the camera they are the map's full detail, and they thin out into the distance. No acne was seen at the new bias (the lab at the gloaming and in the deep night, on grass, road and cobble). One map and not cascades: a second map would draw the casters twice for a distance at which the mist is already the picture.
  - **The ambient.** `LIGHTING.sky` 1.3 to 1.05 and `ground` 0.55 to 0.38 (the hemisphere's ambient: up-facing and down-facing), which also lowers the lit side by its ambient share. `LIGHT.nightAmbient` is left as it was: at night the moon is eight to ten times the ambient already, and the lantern's pool is what the player reads by. The contact shadow's strength 1.0 to 0.85 (its taps are range-checked, so a silhouette against the far ground leaves no halo; softer because the ground now carries its own).
  - **Seen** in the lab on the original code (a copy of the tree on its own server, for before) and on the new, at the gloaming and the deep night, with the lantern on the base: tree, rock, house. Not done: a screen-space GTAO (the contact shadow is the post pass's already, and the baked mark is cheaper and has no noise), decals as meshes (the vertices do it), per-prop shadow bias.

- **The shore, the rim and the wells, round 55 (17 October 2026), asked as urgent: "I just playtested walking over the edge of the map (where the ocean begins) and DIED INSTANTLY, you should have fixed that", then "I also tested it on wells now and it doesn't even make sense because the wells have walls which you shouldn't fall over":** round 54 had left the sea and the land's end as the quick death they were ("unchanged"), though the player had asked for no instant death at any ledge. `holdOf` (`systems/falls.ts`) now asks one question of everything the feet can stand on: a point holds if it is on a region's ground above the sea (the dungeon aside) and clear of a drop. The centre holding is the floor; failing that, the ring of eight points 0.18 m about it (each at its own ground, or the body's height if higher, so a bridge's deck is not read as the riverbed below it) holding any is the edge, which does nothing, as at a pit's lip; none is air, and air is always a fall (`beginFall`), the sea and the world's rim included. The fall's dark takes the body below the water as it does the pit's floor; the Echoes lie at the last feet on the floor. The well (`world/dungeonParts.ts`) is a wall now, not a fall: its curb stands knee-high above the floor, is drawn as a wall, and is solid to everyone, so it cannot be fallen over (pits and bridge gaps, which have no wall of their own, stay drops). Tests: `tests/falls.test.ts` (the rim and the shore balance and fall; the sea's drop ends in the death; every well is solid and not a fall).

- **Mounts are slower, round 55 (17 October 2026), asked: "the mounts are too fast in general, the galloping feels like a speedhack and ruins immersion, lower the normal and sprinting (galloping) traversal rate dramatically while still being a good option to travel around":** `MOUNT_TRAVERSAL` (`data/mounts.ts`) cruise 11 -> 7 m/s and gallop 18 -> 10.5 (a walk is 4.2 and an on-foot sprint 6.4, which drains stamina in about seven seconds: a ride is 1.1 and 1.65 times a sprint, and a gallop drains stamina as before (`p.sprinting` is set; unchanged); a mount was 2.6 and 4.3 times a walk). The rest follows from the speeds so the ride feels the same, only slower: acceleration 14 -> 8.2 and braking 26 -> 15 (a second and a bit to a gallop, as before), the car's fixed turn rates 1.8/1.15 -> 2.4/1.7 rad/s and the horse's tightest circles at a gallop 16 -> 9.5 m (the car's 12 -> 7.5), so a turn takes about as long as it did. The gait model (`systems/mountGait.ts`) keeps its keys as fractions of the gallop (`gaitScale`), so a cruise is still a canter and a full gallop still a gallop, in the picture and in the hoofbeats; the car's gears (`GEARS` in `render/audio/mountBed.ts`) are scaled the same. A stride is still metres a cycle, so the feet fall less often, as a slower horse's do. All three mounts share the one traversal, as before. Tests moved to the new speeds (`mountGait`, `mountTurning`, `mounts`, `mountAudio`, `hoofFx`); not played by anyone, so the numbers are a first guess to judge by feel (`cruise` and `gallop` are the two to move).


- **The well's hole, the rings, and the seam, round 55 (17 October 2026), asked with two screenshots: "fix the visible line where regions meet, since it ruins immersion and makes the game look cheap; fix the well not being an actual hole, it has visible stone textures that don't make it look deep at all", then "what even are those rings around the well, they look off, remove them":** *The seam.* A chunk's road ground came from `chunkContent(...).roads`, which held only its own region's plan's pieces, though a plan buckets a road over every chunk its margin reaches, a neighbour region's among them; a road that ran beside a border was cut there, straight, where the next region's chunks knew nothing of it (the cobble on one side, the mud on the other). `world/chunks.ts` `roadsOver` takes the pieces from the plans of the regions about a chunk (`tests/roadBorders.test.ts`: it failed on the code before: hub's road over Vermont). The ground itself also changed along the border (each chunk is textured and tinted by its region): `world/regionBlend.ts` gives each vertex beside another region a share of that region's ground by `aEdge` (the shader's fourth texture, `uMap4`; the tint is blended in the vertices), over `BLEND.band` = 20 m either side of the border, moved by a swell of noise (6 m). The two sides compute one function of where a point is (the region whose id comes first gives the share by `1 - smooth(t + n)`, the other by `smooth(n - t)`), so no step is left at the border (`tests/regionBlend.test.ts`). A chunk blends toward the one region it has most chunks of beside it; a corner keeps the other as it was. *The well.* The dungeon well's floor slab was whole under the shaft and its cap lay 0.6 m below the lip, so what showed was a shallow pan of stone. The floor of a well room is now slabs in 0.3 m strips either side of a circle between the shaft's wall (2.4 m) and the curb's face (2.75 m), so every edge of the hole is behind one or under the other (`world/dungeonParts.ts`); the shaft (`render/siteMeshes.ts` `well`, now given the part's `y0`) goes down the whole chasm (12.9 m) in six bands whose stone is 0.6, 0.34, 0.16, 0.07, 0.03 and 0.012 of the kit's, to a disc of black water. The open world's well (the Gardners', in an arena) stands on terrain and has no hole to open: it keeps its pan. The `ring` floor plan (a dark band and a pale ring on the floor about a well) is removed, with its test's list. `tests/wellHole.test.ts`.

- **The world's edge is the water, not the border, round 55 (17 October 2026), asked: "the world edge fall is still bugged.. you fall through the floor a few steps in front of the cliff instead of down the cliff into the water":** `falls.ts` `onLand` took the land to end at its region's border (`regionAt`), but the terrain does not: past a border `landHeight` sinks from the region's own height to the sea floor (-9) over `WORLD.coast` (24 m), the mesh draws it down to a metre under the sea (`terrainMesh.ts`), and on foot the body follows `ground()` with no slope limit (`movement.ts`). The fall (`beginFall`) therefore began on the border, with the body 0.5 m above a slope that fell away under it, and went down through the slope's surface. A point is now land when it is a dungeon's floor or its ground is above the sea (+0.4 m), whatever region owns it, so the body walks off the border and down the slope and the fall begins where it meets the water (measured in the game at 14 places along the east rim: crossing the border at frame 99, the fall at frame 231 to 284, 7 to 13 m on, at a height of -1.8 m, the death 66 frames after). The Echoes lie at the last feet above the water. `footingSafe` (saves) keeps its region test: a save outside any region still wakes at the checkpoint. `tests/falls.test.ts` walks the real ground west of Arkham past its border.

- **The borders' roads and patches, round 55 (review of PR 136, two findings):** the ground's blend toward the region beside it had left two things switching along the border: a road's texture (each chunk draws every road in its own region's style, so hub's cobble running over the border into Vermont came out as Vermont's mud) and a biome's patches (hub's leaves against Arkham's rot, each chunk laying its own over the blended ground). *Roads:* the road texture now cross-fades with the beside region's road style by the same share as the ground (`uMap5`, `uHasMap5`; only where the two styles differ, hub/Arkham/Providence/Innsmouth's cobble against Dunwich/Vermont's mud), so a road changes its style over the band, as the ground does, whichever region's plan holds it; segments carry no texture of their own. *Patches:* where the region beside has other patches (`uPatchFade`) they are faded by `4e(1-e)` of the edge share `e`, which is one value for a point from either side (`e` and `1-e` give the same) and nothing at the border itself, whole beyond the band. One material per combination (`groundMaterial`'s key: the ground beside, whether its road and its patch differ). `tests/groundBorder.test.ts`.


- **The sea, round 55 (17 October 2026), asked: "make all the water in the game look way more realistic", "implement logically accurate water physics / effects when falling into the water", then "make it so you get dragged in by a tentacle":**
  - **One surface (`world/seaSurface.ts`).** The wave table, `omega` and `seaHeight(x, z, time, chop)` moved out of `render/seaWaves.ts` (which re-exports them and writes the GLSL) so the simulation, the shader and the deep tentacles read the same water; a body afloat rides the swell the eye sees.
  - **The fall into the sea (`systems/waterFall.ts`, `WATER` in `data/playTuning.ts`).** `beginFall` sets `Falling.water` when the feet are at the water and clear of every pit (`overSea`), else the fall is the pit's as before. Air phase at 9.81 m/s² (the pit's own gravity is the game's, a body falling from the sea's edge has its own); at the surface the speed is kept in part (`entryKeep`, `entryAcross`), a `Splash { at, speed, size }` is emitted, and the body floats chest deep on a spring (`spring`, `damp`) for `seize` frames (40). Then the tentacle: `Seized { entity, at, root }`, a heading fixed from the swim, the body pulled down and drawn out along it toward a root kept `root` metres ahead (it never reaches it, so a coast's gentle slope cannot stall it), clamped to the seabed by `g.world.ground`, and the death comes `held` frames (96) after the seizing, wherever it has got to. A first model that drowned by depth never did: the coast is shallow for a long way and the body is held by the bed, so the time held is the rule, the depth is only the picture's darkness (`waterDark`, by frames held, `WATER.dark`). The Lost Echoes lie at the last feet on the floor, as at a pit.
  - **What is seen.** `render/dragTentacle.ts` (a bezier arch from root to tip over the swell, the coil about the waist, its radius from the deep tentacles' own profile, `createTubeGeometry` shared in `render/tentacleMesh.ts`), `render/splashFx.ts` (droplets and a crown on the Splash, bubbles and trail rings while held, `render/seaRipples.ts`: eight rings at 2.6 m/s that the sea shader reads), the pose (`poses.ts` `thrash`: the arms flung, the body twisting, in place of `plunge`), the camera (`followY`, a pitch toward the water, `WATER.follow`, `lookPitch`), the sound (`splash` and `seized` stingers, pitched lower and louder for a heavier entry; the world hushes under the water), and a line of its own on the veil (`SEAS` in `data/deathLines.ts`, 'drowned' by 'the sea' in `deathNotes.ts`).
  - **The look (`render/shaders/sea.ts`, `world/seabedWindow.ts`).** The sea reads the seabed's heights through a toroidal float window of 160 cells of 1.5 m about the investigator (rows filled sixteen at a time as it moves, `uSeabed`, `uSeabedWin`), so every fragment knows its depth in metres. It is drawn after the ground, transparent (rgb by alpha, the picture's alpha kept for the post pass), thinner where shallower so the bed shows through and nothing at the waterline; its colour goes from a pale turquoise to the black of the deep by `1 - exp(-0.36 d)`; shallows are calmer; foam is the crests' as before plus a wash at the shore that advances and slips back on a slow surge and a lace behind it; Schlick's fresnel for the mirror; the moon's glitter sparkles. The terrain's own shader (`#ifdef SHORE`) tints what is below the water by the absorption `exp(-d (1.15, 0.45, 0.36))` and plays two layers of caustics on it. Not done: the underside of the surface or an underwater camera (the camera stays above the water, as the fall holds it there), and the Beyond's `water` ground, whose texture is its own and not this surface.
  - **Tests.** `tests/waterFall.test.ts` (a walk off the real rim: the Splash and the Seized once, `seize` frames apart, the death `held` frames after, the dark rising only, never under the bed, a drag of 3 to 12 m with the root taut; a drop from a height splashes harder; a pit stays a pit; `waterDark`), `tests/seabedWindow.test.ts`, the sea case in `tests/falls.test.ts` and `tests/deathNotes.test.ts`.

- **The sea's review, round 55 (PR 137, two findings):** *Chop:* the picture raises the swell by the wind's `chop` (R'lyeh 1.5, Innsmouth 1.15, rain, gale and storm on top) but the simulation had read the waves at 1, so in those places the splash, the floating body and the tentacle's root sat above or below the water drawn. `seaChop(g)` moved to `systems/seaChop.ts` (pure, from the realm and the weather; the renderer still eases `SEA.chop` toward it, so the two meet within a second of a change) and `waterFall.ts`'s `surfaceAt` and `enterSea` take it. *Edges:* `smoothstep(high, low, x)` is undefined in GLSL (`edge0 >= edge1`), and three of the sea's were written so (the two shore foams, the rings' young foam): each is `1.0 - smoothstep(low, high, x)` now. `tests/waterFall.test.ts` (a body that falls in a storm meets the storm's water).

- **The sea's clock, round 55 (PR 137, a third finding):** the fall sampled the waves at `g.frame / 60`, but the shader's `uTime` is `main.ts`'s `simTime`, which runs on in every step including those a cutscene holds the world still for (`cinema.step` returns false after it has been advanced) while `g.frame` waits; after one such scene the two clocks stay apart for good and a body meets another wave than the one drawn. The renderer hands its clock to the simulation as it does `Game.lit` (`Game.seaTime`, set in `main.ts` to `simTime`); `waterFall.ts` reads it through `seaTimeOf(g)` (the frames' own when none is handed in: the tests and the lab), and `surfaceAt` and `enterSea` take a time in seconds, not a frame. The sample is the step's, a frame at most from the picture's interpolated time. `tests/waterFall.test.ts` (a clock offset from the frames', as a cutscene leaves it).

- **The sea's bug hunt, round 55 (asked after PR 137's reviews: "run a fast bug hunt that could be caused by the new water update"), three findings:** *After the death:* `fallSystem` runs the drop before its death check and `waterStep` stopped only at `WATER.max`, so the body kept sinking for the 50 frames after the Died event (1.56 m in a test, under a black screen, with the drag tentacle still drawn); a pit's drop ends at its own last frame. `waterStep` returns once the actor is in its death. *The tentacle's blend:* `dragTentacle.ts` took the picture's place between two steps as `time * SIM.hz - g.frame`, but the picture's clock runs on through a cutscene or a slow-mo scene that holds the frames, so afterwards the blend was pinned at 1 and the tentacle and the body it holds moved at 60 Hz, not smoothly; the frame's own `alpha` (the one the bodies are drawn at, `main.ts`) is passed through `worldLife.update` to the tentacle, as Codex asked (a blend rebuilt from the sea's clock would differ from the body's in a cutscene that stands the world still). *The chop:* the simulation read the wind's target while the picture drew the value `worldLife.ts` eased toward it once a drawn frame (so also at a rate that followed the display's), which left the splash and the float some tenths of a metre off the drawn water for seconds after a storm or a change of realm. The easing is the simulation's now (`easeSeaChop` in `stepGame`, 2% a step as before; `seaChopNow(g)`), and the renderer assigns that value to `SEA.chop` and the tentacles, so the swell drawn and the swell floated on are one. Left, unmeasured: the cost of the sea's fragment shader (ripples, rain rings, eight splash rings, caustics, foam, per pixel over the whole sea) on a coast in R'lyeh or Innsmouth (`?perf`). Looked at and found sound: the seabed window's slide and wrap, its refill after a jump (13 ms for the whole window), and the tube geometry the two tentacles now share. Tests: `waterFall.test.ts` (it lies where it died; the sea's clock), `sea.test.ts` (the chop comes up a step at a time).

- **The chop is set before the tentacles are placed, round 55 (review of PR 138):** `worldLife.ts` assigned `SEA.chop` from `seaChopNow(g)` after `deep.update` and `drag.update` had placed the tentacles' waterline, so for a frame (more when several steps came between two drawn frames) they sat on the previous chop while the sea was drawn at the new one. The assignment is the first thing `update` does.

- **The endings, round 55 (asked: "run a quick check on if all 3 endings are working correctly", three subagents, one a ending; then "fix all"):** the three work (trigger, `Ending` event, scene, card, save, afterwards); five things were wrong, each checked in the code before it was fixed. (1) *Told twice:* `courtEndings` ignored `overworld.ending`, so the Court kept offering `seal` and `herald` (the Guide's offer already stopped at it, `umr.ts`), and a second choice replayed the scene and card, overwrote the ending and counted `records.finished` twice. `courtEndings` returns nothing once `ended(g)`, and `endGame` itself returns if the dream has ended (a stale menu, or any other route, is told nothing more); without an overworld (the arena) it emits as before. (2) *Held after the Gate:* nothing let go of the Guide's fight on `silver_key`: its brain stays in `engage`, and `holdInVeil` set the investigator back at the rim every step, so the card's "walk on" was a stay in the ring until it was struck down (it never strikes again, `cooldown` is `Infinity`, and it can be killed, so not a softlock, but not what the card says). `parted(g, fightId)` (`endings.ts`: the Guide's fight, once the ending is `silver_key`) is derived from the saved ending, so a save keeps it; `steady()` (`brain.ts`) settles such a boss and returns at once, which leaves `hunting` false, so `fightSystem` resets the fight and lifts the fog in the same step, and nothing in the brain engages it again (a blow struck at it is not answered). It stays where it stands, struck or not; killing it opens the way on to Yog-Sothoth as before. The arena has no ending to read, so there it behaves as it did. (3) *Save timing:* the ending reached the disk on the 20 s autosave, on `pagehide` or on the next rest; `autosave.ts` also saves on `Ending`. (4) *Record timing:* `noteEnding` ran when the card opened (after the scene), so a quit during it lost the ending reached; the card now notes it on the `Ending` event and shows the records it got (it still notes it itself if opened without one, as the lab does). (5) *Gilman:* his undone line says he "did not wake" while the seal and herald cards say the sleepers woke; `Fate.undoneEndings` (per ending, for a favour left undone) gives him a line that wakes him for those two (`fateOf`), and the passage through the Gate, which says nothing of the sleepers, keeps the old one. Not changed: the cards' text is English in every language, which `docs/LOCALIZATION.md` lists as intended (ending card, shop, journal and the rest of the interface that is not translated); the court choice has no confirmation (one click); `herald` has no route through the Guide (`DECISIONS` Phase 5c: the Guide offers only the Gate, the Court the other two; one of the subagents took that for a missing route). Tests: `endings.test.ts` (told once, the Court's offer, `parted` and a save), `outerGods.test.ts` (the fog set the investigator back at the rim while engaged, and after the Gate nothing does and the Guide neither hunts nor strikes), `epilogue.test.ts` (Gilman's lines by ending), `endingSaved.test.ts` (the record and the save at the choice, with the card's screen and the browser stubbed).

- **The endings' cutscenes, round 55 (asked: "implement a fitting high-quality cutscene for each ending (narrated by the current intro card narrator voice)", then "make sure they are edited the same way as in the intro, with the same pitch and effects"):** an ending had been two shots and one or two captions, nine seconds (`ENDING_SCENES` in `data/cutscenes.ts`). They are now `data/cutscenesEndings.ts` (the scenes moved: the file was near its limit and they are three times the length): 43.2 s (seal), 38.4 s (silver key), 37.4 s (herald), five or six shots each, cut to nine new lines of the Archivist (`data/endingNarration.ts`, `ENDING_NARRATION`: three to an ending). *The words.* The second person, as the cards are; written to be said slowly with pauses, in what the opening's cards use (no new tags, none whispered); none repeats a sentence of a card, whose text is unchanged (the cutscene is the moment, the card is what came after: the seal's cutscene ends as the sleepers are about to breathe and the card begins "You wake"; the herald's as the knee bends and the flutes begin, the card's "The tall man thanks you" after it; the Gate's in the white). The seal says the key lets go of the Gate and the Gate of the investigator, the Gate's that the dream runs out of road and what lies beyond is wider than any stair, the herald's that the knee bends before the mind has agreed to it. *Recorded* through the connector, one generation each, in the voice and model of the opening's (`uqOnApI3a2RVj8rEwlAi`, `eleven_v4`; `docs/VOICES.md` has the flow, the cost: 1,463 credits, and the numbers), downloaded and kept as given. *The same treatment as the opening's:* his takes are not processed offline (docs: "only Peaslee is treated"), and the pitch and the effects are the playback's, by speaker: `CAST['narrator:intro']` is `room` with no `rate` or `echo` (the chamber of `voiceRoom.ts`, the pitch as recorded), `ui/intro.ts` and `render/audio/gameAudio.ts` build the same `createSpeech` on the same `shell.engine` (the same speech bus and its level), and the ending's `voice` beats name `NARRATOR`. The files are as the opening's are (44.1 kHz mono, 128 kbit/s, peaks 0.62 to 0.86, about -18 to -22 dB RMS against -18.5 to -19.8). `tests/endingNarration.test.ts` holds the speaker, the cast entry and each take's length (read from its frames) against the `seconds` the scene is cut to. *Timing:* each line begins where `NARRATION_AT` says, its words low on the screen for as long as it runs, the next no sooner than 0.8 s after (a test), and the beats of each scene (the key's glint, the ash, the Gate shut with a flash and a boom, a bell three times for the clock, the kneel, the embers, the flutes as a low choral) sit on the places in a line where it says them (measured by ear from the lines' segments, not by listening). *The engine, three small things:* `Beat.kneel` (the investigator goes down on one knee before the sign the scene is at, and the scene's end stands them up, under the black); `Shot.aim: 'sign'` (the lens may look at the Elder Sign from a stand of its own about it or from the investigator's side: `frameOf` takes the sign as an optional anchor); and the lens's line to the head of whom it circles is drawn from the investigator's head for a shot about the sign. That last one is a repair: the sign is a solid and the line began inside it, so every shot of the old ending scenes about the sign was drawn in against its glow. The Guide is the camera's `target` for the Gate (`cinemaDirector.ts` finds it by its roster id); the narrator's three lines are fetched as the ending is chosen (`gameAudio.ts`, `speech.ahead`). *Seen:* each scene was played in the headless lab at the Court's sign and in the Guide's arena, one frame every two and a half seconds, and the shots were reframed from the frames (the first versions put the camera against the sign, and two of the herald's inside the kneeling figure's coat); what these stills do not show is motion, the voice, or the sound beats, and nobody has listened to the takes (the recordings were not read back by a machine either). The lab holds the render clock when it holds the world, so the kneel was filmed live. *Left:* a pause for the player to read the card before a scene (the scene ends on black as the endings did); the cards' text (English in every language, as `docs/LOCALIZATION.md` has it); the realm music under the narration is not ducked (a horror's theme is; a person's line is not either).

- **Nyarlathotep drawn again, round 55 (asked: the author's `Seventy-Steps-Nyarlathotep-Redesigned-full` zip: "implement its new sprites and animations accordingly"):** both forms, as the author's manifest registers them: the pharaoh (`nyarlathotep`, 26 frames in place) and the Crawling Chaos (`nyarlathotep#eldritch`, 29 in place and 8 new). `tools/replace_reviewed_sprite.py` took the zip as the library's tree but would have refused it, so it was widened rather than the zip bent: a scope may now have more frames than it had (the Chaos's left and right walks, two made six: the old frames' places are kept and the four more of each go on a second shelf at the foot of `creatures-3.png`), a variant's key now finds its folder (`sprites/nyarlathotep/eldritch/64x64`), and a view the pack mirrors stays mirrored when the delivery's files are that other view turned round pixel for pixel (the pharaoh's right, checked, so nothing is added; had they differed they would have been drawn on the shelf, as 'Umr's right was); the source index's copies of a mirrored view's frames are kept in line with the frames they read (the integrity test caught that the first time). The Chaos's right stays its own files (it was, in the pack: `sourceDirection: right`), though the manifest calls it a mirror of the left: the same picture, and no registration changed. `scale` is as the author registered it (2.6 and 6, the same as before) and the capsules are not touched, so the fights are as they were; the Chaos's picture is now taller and narrower than the mass it replaced (54 px by about 39, where it was 37 by 53: some 5.1 m tall against 3.5 m, over a 4.2 m capsule, 1.2 times; the audit's range is 0.6 to 1.7). No eyes were read for either form (`reviewed_look.py` finds the same ten as before). Not verified: how the walks read in motion (the delivery's README says the same: its GIFs are put together from the PNGs); the arena was looked at from four sides for each form with the world held, no console errors. `tests/nyarlathotepArt.test.ts` pins the registrations, the delivery's own hashes, the one ground line (row 62) and the six-frame side loops; `tests/reviewedCreatureAssets.test.ts` counts 2,768 frames (2,374 of 64 by 64).
- **The replace tool, round 55 (review of PR 141, three findings, all real; each shown on the tool as it was with a copy of the pack and a delivery made from the pharaoh's own frames):** *A grown scope and the views that mirror it:* the new ids were appended to the source view in `index.json` but to every view that reads it in `source-index.json`, so the two disagreed and the mirrored view stayed at its old count (a left walk of 6 made 8 left the right's at 6: `count 6 vs 8`); both indexes now take them in every view that reads the direction. *A mirrored view that is drawn:* the tool put the view's frames that mirrored in `kept` and its drawn ones in `fresh`, then made the view anew from `fresh` alone, so a right idle that was the left's turned round was lost with no error, from both indexes (so the integrity test passed on the damaged pack); a view is one or the other whole, so the kept actions are put on the shelf as the delivery's own files for them (the same picture), and an action of the view that the delivery leaves out is refused. *A shortened mirrored view:* the minimum-count check ran only for a view that is its own source, so a drawn right of 4 frames over a mirrored walk of 6 was accepted; it runs for every view. Run again from the pack as it stood before, the tool makes the committed pack byte for byte (`index.json`, `source-index.json`, `creatures-3.png`), so the art is unchanged by any of this. The scenarios are not kept as a test: the repository has no Python tests and CI does not install Pillow.
- **The demo, round 56 (asked: "start now: the demo build"):** one game, two editions, chosen when packing: `-c.extraMetadata.edition=demo` (the Mac: `package_mac.mjs --edition demo`; CI: the release workflow's *demo* input) puts `edition: "demo"` in the pack's package.json, the shell passes `--edition=demo` to the page (`desktop/steam.js` `edition()`, `preload.cjs`), and `data/edition.ts` reads it (on the web: `?demo`). What it keeps: the university and Arkham (`systems/demo.ts` `DEMO.regions`, the first two realms of the story, six signs, the Witch House, every horror of the two), to Keziah Mason's fall, which opens the stair and is where the full game's story leaves Arkham; then `DemoEnded`, once (kept in `told`), and a card (`ui/demoEnd.ts`) after her fall's cutscene: the full game's page, or more of Arkham (a demo that stops the player dead at its end is a worse advertisement than one that lets them walk on). How it holds them: a walk over the edge of the two realms is set back to the last step inside, as a boss's fog holds one, with the reason said at most once in four seconds (an invisible wall, the simplest thing; a mist or a sign at each road would be better and is not done); a leap past it is refused where it begins, so nothing is half done (`travel`, `dream` and `passGate` in `checkpoints.ts` ask `demoAllows`), and the Sleeper's Sign says the stair leads into the full game instead of offering it. A save carries over both ways: the two editions share the product name, so the same save folder; the full game reads a demo's save as its own. A save from the full game opened in the demo wakes at the Quad and that sign becomes its checkpoint, which the demo's autosave keeps: the full game then wakes there too, with everything else as it was (a small cost, and only to a player who owned both and opened the demo). itch.io: the demo's packs go to the channels `windows-demo`, `osx-demo` and `linux-demo` of the same page (`itch_push.mjs` reads the pack's edition), whose uploads the author marks "demo" in the page's editor (not checked: no push was made). Steam: a demo there is an app of its own, with its own app id, and the "Get the full game" button would have to open its store page; not done, as there is no Steam app yet.
- **The demo, review of PR 142 (two findings, both real, both shown in the browser before and after):** *`?demo` was lost on the game's own reloads:* a return to the title, a crash's Try again and an ending's new game all go back to `location.pathname`, which drops the address's query, so a browser demo came back as the full game. The tab now remembers it: `?demo` sets a session-storage key that `editionOf` (`data/edition.ts`) reads with the address (a tab that refuses storage has the address alone; a packed demo never needed it, the shell says so on every launch). Rather than the query carried through every reload: there are four of them, and some mean to drop the rest of the query (`?fresh`). *The end card did not hold the world:* it gave the investigator no hands but the world went on stepping, so whatever was left in the arena could hurt them behind it, and the play time ran. It now holds the world as the pause menu does (main.ts: no step, and drawn still).
- **The crash report's graphics card, review of PR 142 (a third finding, real):** every crash report made borrowed a WebGL 2 context to read the card's name and never gave it back; past the browser's limit of live contexts it takes the oldest, which is the game's own, and the dream breaks. The name is now read once, on a context let go at once (`WEBGL_lose_context`), and kept (`graphicsName`, `ui/crashLog.ts`); a test makes twenty reports on one borrowed context, and fails on the code as it was (twenty made, none let go).
- **What a crash report may carry, round 56 (asked: that what a player pastes in public expose nothing sensitive):** a crash report is pasted in public, and its last errors are the page's own words, which can hold a path through the player's home folder (`C:\Users\Jane Doe\AppData\…`, `/Users/jane/…`, `/home/jdoe/…`), an e-mail address, or a web address whose query carries a key. Every crash report (and the crash file the shell keeps) now goes through `ui/scrub.ts`: the home folder's name becomes `<user>` (spaces and all, to the next separator), an address `<email>`, and an address loses its query but keeps its path and line; file names, line numbers, versions (`three@0.170.0`) and the game's words are kept (`tests/scrub.test.ts`; in the browser, errors planted with each kind came out clean, and what is shown is what is copied). What it still names: the game's version, the browser or shell, the graphics card and the screen's size. Nothing is sent by the game: the crash screen copies the text, and the shell keeps the file in its logs folder.
- **The demo and the crash report, review of PR 142 (two more findings, both real):** *A journey out of the demo was refused only under the veil:* a gate's journey (`ui/journeys.ts`), and a sign's from the map or an Elder Sign, begins by covering the screen and makes the jump only once it is covered, so a refusal came after a full fade and hold that went nowhere. The way is now asked first (`gateBeyondDemo`, `signBeyondDemo` in `checkpoints.ts`, which `travel` and `passGate` ask too): E at such a gate says why at once and no veil falls; the map greys such a sign and says IN THE FULL GAME in its prompt, as it says why when a fight bars a journey; an Elder Sign does not list it (it can only be one a full game's save found). A test fails on the code as it was (the veil was asked to cover). *A query with a colon was cut short:* `?token=abc:def`, or a nested address with its own key, left the rest of the key in the report, for the query stopped at the first colon (to keep a stack trace's `:line:col`). It is now taken whole, to the next space, quote or bracket, and only a `:line:col` at its very end is kept.
- **The demo's edge, seen, round 56 (asked: "visible mist please"):** the border was an invisible wall with a notice; it is now a bank of mist along every run of it where the demo's land meets land beyond (`world/demoBorder.ts`: chunk edges merged into straight runs, about a kilometre along the north and half that along the east; none where its land meets the sea, which is the world's own edge), standing a metre past the step where the investigator is held (`render/demoMist.ts`). It is the boss fog's own shader (`render/bossFog.ts`, its fragment now a function of options the boss fog takes the defaults of), in its churchyard grey, 26 m high with a ragged crown, following the ground. Three things were wrong at first and are mended, each seen in the browser: it glowed like a lit curtain at night (the boss fog's colours are not lit; this one is multiplied by the world's ambient and moon, with a floor so it is found on the darkest night), the trees behind it showed through (the post pass's haze and contact shadows read the depth, which the mist did not write: a depth-only copy is drawn with the solids where it is solid), and an open end stopped in mid-air (it feathers out over 60 m where it meets no other run). It thins with distance from the eye (140 to 300 m) and is not drawn beyond. Only the demo makes it.
- **A Windows home folder's whole name, review of PR 142 (a sixth finding, real):** the scrubber stopped a Windows home folder's name at an apostrophe or a bracket, which a folder's name may hold, so `C:\Users\O'Neil\AppData` came out `C:\Users\<user>'Neil\AppData`. The name is now every character Windows allows in one (all but `< > : " / \ | ? *`) up to the next separator; where none follows, up to a space, `;` or `,`. The price is that a bare `C:\Users\Jane, see /tmp/x` loses ", see " with the name (more taken, never less). macOS and Linux short names hold no space or quote, so their pattern is one word, which no longer runs on through the prose after a bare home path. The one case still open: a bare Windows home path whose name has a space and nothing after it (`… C:\Users\Jane Doe` at the end of a line) keeps the surname, for it cannot be told from the words after a name.
- **Nyarlathotep's true form, struck where it is drawn, round 56 (asked: "fix the hitbox of the new Nyarlathotep"):** the redrawn Crawling Chaos is a lanky figure about five metres tall (its shoulders at 3.75 m, a thin curl of tentacle over them to 5.06 m), its legs and body about a metre either side of its middle and long arms hanging to 1.8 m. Its body was still sized for the grey heap it replaced: the `blob` silhouette's capsule, 4.2 m tall and 2.16 m in radius, a ball twice its breadth, so a blade struck air a metre short of its legs and the investigator was held off as by a wall. A sprite recipe may now carry its own `body` (height and radius as fractions of its scale, before its silhouette's: `systems/creatures.ts`), and the true form's is `[0.7, 0.2]`: 1.2 m in radius, its legs' stance and upper arms (where 85% of its painted pixels fall, front and side), the hanging hands out of it; its height kept at 4.2 m, its shoulders and neck, so its blows' reach and areas (which scale with its height: `data/attacks.ts`) are what they were. The silhouette stays `blob` (it draws only the fallback figure). The human form was measured too and left as it is: its capsule (2.34 m, 0.47 m) stands to its crown and as wide as its robe's hem. `tests/nyarlathotepArt.test.ts` holds both forms' capsules inside their painted width in every view; `tests/outerGods.test.ts`, that the last phase takes the true form's body.
- **The demo's end told when seen, and a query's own brackets, review of PR 142 (two more findings, both real):** *The end was marked told before its card showed:* `demoSystem` added `demo:end` to the save's `told` as it sent `DemoEnded`, but the card waits on a cutscene, a journey or a menu (and a 400 ms poll), so a page hidden or closed in between was autosaved with the end marked told and the next launch never showed it, the card and its link to the game's page lost for good. `DemoEnded` is now sent once a session (a `WeakSet` of games) while the save's marker is unset, and the marker is added by the card as it comes on the screen (`demoEndShown`, called from `ui/demoEnd.ts`): a launch from a save made in between says the end again, and one made after does not. `tests/demo.test.ts` saves and reloads at each point, and fails on the code as it was (the marker was in the save before the card). In the browser, with a menu holding the card, the game's memory and its saved file held no marker; closing the menu showed the card and set it. *A bracket inside a query ended the address:* `…?redirect=https://private.example/(user)?key=SECRET` came out `…/x)?key=SECRET`, for `)` ended every query (to keep a stack frame's own closing bracket). Rather than another character, the query's end now follows how its address was opened (`ui/scrub.ts`): in a bracket or quote it ends at the mark that closes that (brackets of its own kind, opened and closed inside, are the query's), and an address nothing opened runs to the next space whole, a lone bracket, quote or apostrophe being as likely a key's as an end (`?name=O'Neil&k=SECRET` goes whole). Over-taking is the price (a prose bracket after a bare address goes with it), never under-taking; a `:line:col` at the end is still kept. `tests/scrub.test.ts` holds the reported case and its family, nesting, a lone bracket, an apostrophe, and quoted, bracketed and angle-bracketed addresses.
- **The demo's border, and what lives past it, review of PR 142 (a finding, real):** `demoSystem` holds the investigator at the border, and the mist is only a picture, but `populationSystem` streams in every spawn point of the 5 × 5 chunks about them (64 m each), so at the university's northern edge fifteen of Vermont's creatures were alive a few chunks away: they could see the investigator across the line, hunt them to it and come over, a full-game foe and its Echoes inside the demo. A spawn point beyond the demo's realms is now not filled (`demoAllows(g, s.at)` in `systems/population.ts`; the full game's `g.demo` is unset and nothing changes). The creatures of the demo's own realms stay; one that is knocked or chases across the line comes to no harm, for the investigator cannot follow. The other things that make creatures were looked at and need nothing: a wandering file's path must begin and end in the investigator's own realm (`wanderers.ts`), and the rest are apparitions, summons and arenas about the investigator. No sign, gate or tome beyond the border lies within twelve metres of it (reaches 3.2 and 5.2), so none can be used from inside. `tests/demo.test.ts` stands at the edge in both games: the demo has people on its side and none of the other's alive, and the full game brings them in as it always did; it fails on the code as it was (fifteen of Vermont's alive).
- **Sixteen creatures drawn again, round 56 (asked: the author's `Seventy-Steps-16-Creatures-Repairs-v4-Haunter-Wing-Fix-Full` zip: "implement them correctly, and adjust hitboxes if needed"):** Yig, the Haunter of the Dark, the High Priest's true form, Rhan-Tegoth, the Dhole, Yog-Sothoth, the Man of Leng, the Serpent Man, the Flying Polyp, the Ym-bhi, the Daemon Pipers, the Other Gods, Lilith, the Being of Ib, the Shunned House's entity and the Great Ones, put in whole (2,844 frames in the pack, from 2,768). Idle and walk in all four views, attack and hurt from the front only. Three choices:
  - **Attack and hurt are front art only (the author's standing rule, for review and for the game).** `tools/replace_reviewed_sprite.py` never packs a left, right or back attack or hurt, even where a delivery has one, and drops the old drawing's own with it: Yog-Sothoth's boss sides and backs (the left and back attack and hurt of the author's earlier delivery of the bosses' sides and backs) would no longer match the new body, so seen from a side it strikes and is struck as its front, the fallback `resolveDirectionalClip` has always given a creature without them. `tests/bossCombatSides.test.ts` now asserts that for Yog, and for the stoop on a creature that has its own. The tool was rewritten to take several keys or `--all`, a scope that shrinks (the cleared frames leave the index and the later ones move up a number), a 128 px boss among 64 px creatures, and a creature the manifest registers by its central body (no ground-line check for it); it fills the places the old drawing freed, then a shelf on one of the library's four pages. Checked byte for byte against the delivery. The delivery folder must be kept: a `pack_reviewed_assets.py` repack would lose these.
  - **Hitboxes: width from the picture, never height.** A capsule's height is what its blows' reach is scaled by (`sizeFactor`, `attackRange`), so only the radius was set (new `sprite.body: [height, radius]`, the height written as the silhouette's own and unchanged). Measured as the 85th percentile of the painted pixels' distance from the billboard's middle over the front, side and back idles, a radius was set where the capsule stood more than a quarter wider than the body (none stood more than a quarter narrower). The test holds every capsule to 0.75 to 1.35 times the painted body. Radius before to after, metres: High Priest 0.86 to 0.55, Rhan-Tegoth 1.8 to 1.5, Great Ones 1.0 to 0.6, Flying Polyp 1.30 to 0.79, Serpent Man 0.40 to 0.26, Ym-bhi 0.46 to 0.38, Lilith 0.38 to 0.29, Shunned House 1.08 to 0.84; Yig, the Haunter, the Dhole, the Man of Leng, the Daemon Pipers, the Other Gods and the Being of Ib are as they were. Left alone, since their heights couple to their blows: Yig (capsule 3.0 against 4.6 painted), the Dhole (3.0 against 4.0) and the Shunned House (2.1 against 1.66).
  - **The Shunned House at 0.84, not the drawn 0.64.** The balance bot's fight with it is 5.6 s at 0.69 across, under the table's floor of 6 s (`tests/balance.test.ts` caught it); 0.84 gives a fight no shorter than before. The body's picture is a little narrower than its capsule for this reason.
  - **Yog-Sothoth's seven hit spheres are refitted to the seven painted in the new front idle**, as fractions of its painted height from the lowest sphere's foot. Its picture floats about a metre and a half over the ground (the lowest sphere is above the bottom row), and a blade cannot reach that high: the reach test (the sword-cane must touch every creature) and the hurt-zone test (the lowest sphere rests on the ground) fail with the spheres where they are painted, so they stand on the ground and the picture levitates 1.55 m over them. `reviewed_look.py` was run again (its painted body, `[0.431, 0.374, 0.204]`, is the only change).
  - `docs/BALANCE.md` was regenerated (`BALANCE_WRITE=1`): it had drifted from the code before this round, and rows moved; every fight is within the table's bounds (330 of 330 in `npm run audit`). Not verified: how each walk reads in motion at the frame rate (the in-game read-back checks which frame and which view the renderer draws, not how it plays).
- **PR 143's review and its known gaps, round 56 (Codex P1; then asked: fix the two gaps the PR listed):**
  - **A variant that draws another silhouette drops the base's fitted `body` (Codex, real).** `applyOverride` merges a variant's sprite over the base's, so the Serpent Man's eldritch form (`silhouette: 'serpent'`) kept the man's `body: [0.9, 0.12]`: a 1.98 m by 0.26 m capsule over a serpent drawn 1.1 m by 0.55 m, nearly doubling its height-scaled blows. The fix is in `data/registry.ts` rather than the one entry: an override that names a different silhouette and no `body` of its own loses the inherited one, so no later fit can leak the same way. Nyarlathotep's and the High Priest's true forms bring their own and keep them; the Flying Polyp's boss variant changes only scale, which a fraction follows. `tests/redrawnCreatures.test.ts` holds both.
  - **Capsule heights now follow the painted bodies, and blows keep the height they were balanced at.** `reachOf` (creatures.ts) is the height blows are scaled by (`sizeFactor`, `attackRange`): the silhouette's own, whatever `body` says; it travels on the combatant and the body component as `reach` (read by `bossFight`, `realityTricks` and Nyarlathotep's borrowed moves, `body.reach ?? body.height`). So Yig stands 4.5 m (was 3.0), the Dhole 3.94 m (3.0) and the Shunned House's entity 1.69 m (2.1), and every move of theirs is compiled at 3, 3 and 2.1 m as before: what is struck follows the picture, how far a blow reaches does not. Lock-on and shots keep their aim (0.6 of that same height): aimed at the picture's 0.6, a taller Dhole, which Yog-Sothoth summons, made the bot's fight with Yog 14 s longer and 6.5 lives against the colossus limit of 6 (it was 5.5), so what is struck moves and where it is aimed at does not. Nothing else with a `body` changes (their heights were the silhouette's).
- **Variant bodies, review of PR 143:** the redrawn Serpent Man's humanoid `body` was inherited by its serpent form, making it 1.98 m high and 0.264 m in radius instead of 1.1 m and 0.55 m; the Flying Polyp's narrower new body also shrank the unchanged Polyp Swarm boss from 2.88 m to 1.76 m in radius. Each variant now names its previous body fractions explicitly (`[0.5, 0.25]` and `[0.7, 0.36]`), keeping the base redraws' fitted hitboxes and the registry's existing merge behavior. Regression tests cover the Serpent Man's sanity swap, fractured spawn and recovery with its bite geometry, and spawning both Flying Polyp forms.
- **Artwork and body alignment, follow-up to PR 143:** Yog-Sothoth's alpha bounds ended at row 119 because of a detached spark, while its connected sphere cluster ends at row 111. `reviewed_look.py` now measures that physical ground row into `REVIEWED_GROUND`; the renderer lowers the unchanged canvas by 8 pixels (1.553398 m), matching the existing hit spheres and retaining melee reach and authored pose registration. All 23 unique poses share row 111. Yig, the Dhole and the Shunned House now use the tallest painted idle height across every view: 4.5 m, 3.9375 m and 1.6875 m, retaining radii 1.5 m, 1.5 m and 0.84 m. Blow geometry and aim points keep the heights they were balanced at (`reachOf`; see the entry above); damage and timing stay unchanged. Focused bot comparisons keep Yig near 31 seconds and the Shunned House near 7.9 seconds, so no balance retune or separate attack-height field is needed. Regression tests fail on the old grounding/heights and compare actual rendered registrations and spawned bodies with the source art. Browser checks confirm all four creatures, Yog-Sothoth from four sides and in an attack, and the Shunned House at its taller idle breath, with no console errors. The full check (2,520 tests), audit (330 tests) and production build pass; docs/BALANCE.md is regenerated.
- **640 × 360 is the default internal resolution, round 56 (asked: "I'm extremely unsure about what's the best default resolution, decide for me"):** the picture is drawn small and blown up with hard-edged pixels, so it looks its best when the screen holds it a whole number of times over: every low-res pixel is then the same size, and none of them is a row wider than its neighbours, which shimmers as the camera turns. 640 × 360 is the one size that does that on every common 16:9 screen: 720p and the Steam Deck (2×), 1080p (3×), 1440p (4×) and 4K (6×), also at Windows' display scaling, since the browser maps the box onto physical pixels. 600 × 338, the default before, is 3.2× at 1080p; 700 × 394 and 800 × 450 are 2.7× and 2.4×. It is 7% sharper than 600 × 338 on each axis (13% more pixels for the fog to march), so the look approved in round 52 is kept. The scale now moves in steps of 0.4 of the 400 × 225 base, 160 × 90 at a time: 320 × 180, 480 × 270 and 640 × 360 in the standard range (320 × 180 is the floor, where 200 × 113 was), and 800 × 450 and 960 × 540 behind the warning (960 × 540 is 2× at 1080p and 4× at 4K; 700 × 394 is gone). The first launch's quality ladder steps 640 → 480 → 320. Settings version 16: a choice kept since version 13 is snapped to the nearest new step (600 × 338 to 640 × 360, 400 × 225 to 480 × 270: a tie snaps up), and a version 15 choice above the standard range, made after the warning, is kept. Not done: snapping the window's box to whole multiples (black borders in a window of any other size); in a window the picture is scaled as it fits, as before.
- **The plain chariot is open, round 56 (asked: "improve the chariot's (the default one's) overall design, because the top of the chariot should be gone, it covers so much"):** the roof and its four posts stood 2.7 m up over the box behind the rider's bench, so the follow camera, which hangs behind and above, saw a slab where the rider should be. `carriageArt(…, plain)` no longer builds them (the spectral coach keeps its roof, posts, curtains and lamps: the request was for the default one). The box is lowered to waist height (sides 0.5 m, was 0.73; tail 0.46, was 0.68) so the deck and bench read from above, and `render/chariotFittings.ts` dresses what is left: a dashboard before the rider's feet with its brass cap and a lit lantern each side, armrests running from the box to the front of the bench, iron fenders arching over all four wheels (nine slabs along the arc, tilted to follow it, a stay to the body), and a step and a brass rail at the tail. The bench, deck, foot-boards, wheels, shafts, seat origin and reins are where they were, so the rider, the horse, the turning and the sounds are unchanged, and so is the capsule the world sees (its height is a collision number, left alone: a lower roofline could pass under things it could not before, which is a change to the ride and not asked for). Looked at in the mount gallery (`?mounts`) from behind and above, in front and to the side, before and after; not looked at with a rider on it in play. The lamps' glass glows (an emissive box, as the spectral coach's does) but lights nothing about it.
- **Nine creatures from the author's v7 delivery, round 57 (asked: the `Seventy-Steps-21-Creatures-v7-Profile-Inbetweens-Full` zip: "implement them correctly, and also check for hitboxes that need adjustment"):** the zip's 21 forms are round 56's sixteen with the Horror at Martin's Beach, the Polyp Swarm, Zkauba and the two Ghouls, and what it says is new is the Ghouls' eight-frame side walks. Each frame was compared with the pack by hash (the right of a mirrored view through its left, since the pack holds none of its own): 12 forms were already what the game held; 9 differed and were put in with `replace_reviewed_sprite.py` (the Dhole's back walk 6 to 4 frames, Lilith's left walk, the Serpent Man, the Great Ones, Zkauba, the Horror at Martin's Beach, the Polyp Swarm, both Ghouls: 2,856 frames, from 2,844), `reviewed_look.py` run again with nothing changed. The delivery holds no left, right or back attack or hurt; the standing rule held without the tool having to drop any. Not imported, since the pack already held them: the 12.
  - **Frame counts differ across views and need nothing:** the Ghoul's side is eight frames beside a four-frame front and back, the Dhole's back four beside six elsewhere; `loopFrame` runs one phase over a 2-, 4-, 6- or 8-frame view (the pack's tests hold the counts and the hashes).
  - **Hitboxes, five refitted (height from the picture, width where it was off; blows unchanged through `reachOf`):** drawn on each painted idle (front, side, back) the capsules showed the head and neck of the Horror at Martin's Beach (5.1 m) standing 1.6 m above a 3.5 m capsule, the serpent Serpent Man's hood 0.55 m over its 1.1, the Polyp Swarm's narrower new body inside a capsule 1.84 times its width, Zkauba and the Great Ones under 0.4 to 0.5 m of empty capsule above their heads. Metres, before to after: Martin's Beach 3.5 by 1.75 to 5.14 by 1.75 (the width was right, 1.00); Swarm 5.6 by 2.88 to 5.84 by 2.0; Great Ones 4.75 to 4.22 high; Zkauba 2.21 to 1.83; serpent Serpent Man 1.1 to 1.65 (its art is unchanged since round 56, and was found short beside the rest). Left: the Dhole, Lilith, the Serpent Man and the Ghouls, within a tenth of their pictures' height and 0.79 to 1.19 times their width (the Ghouls' 0.79, a hunched body with arms spread beyond it, is the narrowest and no change of this delivery: their front is as before).
  - **A capsule must clear the roof of the hall it stands in (a real catch of `npm run audit`, `bossStand`):** the Swarm's picture is 6.25 m, its vault's roof is at 6, and at 6.0 m or more `resolveCapsule` pushed the boss 26 m out through the wall, as it did Father Dagon and Mother Hydra in round 24. The capsule stops at 5.84 m (probed: 5.9 stands, 6.0 is pushed). The test names the rule. The tip of the tentacles above that is not struck.
  - Not verified: how the new walks read in motion at the frame rate.
- **The chariot's lanterns on the sides, the driver's hands, and the horse's voice, round 57 (asked, after a look at the lanterns on the open chariot: "put the lanterns on the sides and animate them slightly wiggling when riding"; "animate slapping and pulling the reins for galloping and halting accordingly, logically and visually pleasing"; "give the horse according sound-design, not only when slapping/halting the reins, but also occasional noises while idling"):**
  - **The lanterns (replacing round 56's dash lamps).** Seen up close, each stood on a stub 4 cm thick that stopped 1.25 cm short of its base, so it floated, with no socket or bracket: not how a dash lamp is held. They hang now from a forged crook (a strap bolted down the side of the box, an arm rising and curling out of it to a hook with a brass eye) beside the driver, out at 1.0 m (the wheels' width) and between the two fenders; the lantern hangs below the hook by its own loop (`render/chariotFittings.ts`, tests hold the loop at the hook, the width and the clearance). It is a damped pendulum about two axes (`render/lanternSway.ts`: 1.75 Hz, a seventh of critical, limited to 0.32 rad) pushed by the box speeding up or braking (it lags, then swings on), a turn (thrown out of it), the box's own pitch and roll (it hangs plumb against 70% of them) and the road (a tremble of 0.03 rad rms, 0.05 at its peaks, at a gallop, from the stride's beat and a rougher one, the two lanterns out of step; a third of that at a walk). The spectral coach keeps its lamps on its posts.
  - **The hands are state, not animation (`systems/mountReins.ts`, on `MountState.reins`, stepped by `moveMounted`).** The picture and the sound both read it, as they read the gait's cycle count, so a slap is heard as it lands and never twice. At a gallop (sprint held, above 8.6 m/s, kept above 8.0) the driver slaps the reins on the horse's back: once at once and again half a second on as the gallop is taken, then every 1.15 to 2.5 s (a hash of the count: the same ride is the same). Slowing faster than 2.5 m/s² is a pull, but whether it is a haul depends on what the rider asks for (`want`, the stick's pace from `moveMounted`, 0 for a rig held against something): a pull to a standstill rises to a haul (both hands to the chest) at the sim's braking of 15, and a halt is counted when it passes 0.55 of a haul and the rig was above 2.5 m/s (so a walk stopped is not one), held a quarter second after the rig stands and let go over half a second more. Slowing that is not a stop (the sprint let go at a gallop, the stick eased, a turn taken slow) draws the hands in by at most 0.4 and is never a halt: the sim brakes a gallop let go to a cruise at the same 15 m/s² as a stop, so the slowing alone cannot tell them apart (Codex, review of PR 145, real: it hauled both hands and played the halt, the scrape and the harness while the rig was still at a cruise). A wall that takes the speed in one step still hauls (`blocked` asks for nothing; a decaying peak of recent speed, `recent`, stands in for the speed it had). A turn draws the inside hand, the outside hand giving a little. No slap while pulled up; none at a cruise; the motor car, which has a wheel, never steps it.
  - **The arms and the reins (`render/riderPose.ts`, `render/reinLines.ts`).** A slap winds up (hands up and forward, wrists cocked, the body back a little), snaps down on the strike at 0.38 s (arms down and in, wrists flicked, the body thrown forward), and a ripple runs from the hands to the bit in a third of a second; a haul folds the forearms to the chest, sits the body back and braces the feet on the footboard; a gallop leans the driver into it and keeps the eyes level (the head counters the lean). The reins are drawn from the real hands (read from the fist after the pose, `MountVisual.holdReins`): slack they sag and sway, taut they run straight, a slap takes them taut and lets them loop, and the ripple travels them. The horse answers at the bit: a haul brings its head up and in, sits it back on its haunches and lifts its tail; the ripple's end pops its nose up and pins its ears (the spectral horses the same, lighter). The slap is carried between two steps of the simulation by the loop's alpha, so a 144 Hz picture is not stepped at 60. Looked at in the browser with a rider at a gallop frozen at the wind-up, the strike, the ripple, a haul and a left turn, from the side; not at all hours from behind (the seat back hides the driver there).
  - **The horse's voice (`data/mountSounds.ts`, `render/audio/mountAudio.ts`; levels held by `tests/mountAudio.test.ts`).** `REIN_SLAP`, `HORSE_URGE`, `HORSE_HALT`, `hoofScrape` (by ground), `HARNESS_SHAKE` for the hands; `HORSE_BLOW`, `HORSE_WHICKER`, `TAIL_SWISH`, `LIP_FLUTTER` for a standing horse, one every 6 to 14 s, never the same twice running; and a hoof put down on each of the two landings of its pawing, on the pawing's own clock (`systems/horseIdle.ts`, now shared with `draftHorse.ts`; the audio reads `Game.seaTime`, the render's clock). The skeletons get the slap, the scrape, the shake and a whisper, and idle with a shake of the harness; the car none. The new recipes are procedural placeholders like the rest of a ride's: how they sound against the music has not been heard, only measured.
  - **The driver's lantern no longer shines through the seat back (asked: "change the wooden plank behind the character so it covers the light from the character's lantern shining through").** Seen from behind, the lantern at the driver's hip (the flame's box, its halo and its pool of light) showed under the black leather back, which floated 0.4 m over the deck, and the halo and the light came through the back itself: the halo is drawn 0.8 of its radius toward the eye so the surface it sits on does not cut it (`halos.ts`), and the lantern's shadow map looks only the way the camera looks, which for a camera behind is away from the plank. Three mends, each tested: a board under the leather back, to the deck (`mountCarriage.ts`, both coaches: `SEAT_BACK` in `render/lanternCover.ts` is the one slab the board and the back make); the halo is taken by what the slab covers of the line from the eye to the flame (`slabCover`, soft over 0.3 m at the slab's edge, so an orbiting camera fades it and never pops: `worldLights.update`'s new `lanternCover`, `ActorViews.flameCover`); and the lantern's light is eased off whatever lies close behind the slab, by an analytic test in `lanternSlab` (`shaders/shadow.ts`, world and sprite shaders: the line from the point to the lantern crosses the slab's plane inside its edges). **Softened after the author saw it ride ("the shadow veil is way too harsh and unrealistic", with a night screenshot):** the first version took all the light from everything behind the slab with a 6 cm edge, a black wedge with a hard curved edge across the cobbles behind the chariot, which nothing else on the rig casts (the wheels, the box and the horse throw no lantern shadow). Photographed at night from above with the shadow on and off, it is now a blur that widens as it goes (`0.08 + 0.45 m` per metre behind), strongest next to the seat back, gone by 1.5 m behind it (full to 0.45 m), and never more than 85% of the light: the rear deck is shaded and the road is not fed by `uLSlab`/`uLSlabInv`, set by `mountViews` while a chariot is ridden and zero otherwise. `tests/lanternCover.test.ts` casts rays from a grid of eyes behind each coach at the posed flame and asks that the coachwork is in the way of every line that crosses the slab (it fails without the board), and holds the uniforms' lifecycle. Looked at in the browser from behind, low and high, before and after: the glow and the lit patch are gone, the front of the seat back is lit as before. The fog's glow about the lantern (`glowAlong`) is still drawn through it: a faint haze, left.
- **The draft horse stands in four white socks, round 57 (asked: "one foot of the horse is not white like the others"):** the right fore had `sock: 0` in `render/horseLegs.ts`, so three feet were white and one dark; it has 0.3 now (the others 0.28, 0.5 and 0.42: no two alike). `tests/mountArt.test.ts` holds that every leg has a sock and that the lowest vertices of every cannon bone are white alike.
- **The tips are gone, the notices stay longer, and the lantern goes out with the mind, round 58 (asked: "remove the tips/messages that appear bottom left entirely"; notices "presented for an insufficient duration… remain visible longer with a gradual fade-out", and again on re-approaching the gate; "when sanity drops unmoored the lantern should flicker for a brief moment and go dark after, until sanity gets refilled again to a certain threshold (1 state higher than unmoored)"):**
  - **No box at the lower left (`ui/hints.ts`).** The hint box and its queue, and the line that said where a journey taken up again had led, are removed. The hints are still *given* (the events that gave them still mark them in the save, silently, and in the browser's list the lore lines go by), so the Journal's Field Guide fills as before and is where a tip is read again if wanted; nothing is written on the screen. Judgment call: the Field Guide is kept rather than removed, as the only place a player can still read what the game would have said; say so if that too should go.
  - **A notice stands for as long as it takes to read (`ui/noticeFade.ts`, `ui/hud.ts`).** The line at the middle of the screen was up for 1.1 s with a 0.3 s fade, a second or so more in practice (`bossHud.ts` listened to `Notice` as well as the HUD, so each was queued behind itself and put up again 0.7 s later, which is why it stood nearer two seconds; the second listener is removed). It now comes in over 0.18 s, stands in full for 1.6 s plus 55 ms a character (up to 8 s: the gate's line, 54 characters, stands 4.6 s), and fades out over 1.7 s. The next notice waits its turn until the one up has stood in full (up to three wait), a notice said again while it is up stands afresh instead of twice. This is every `Notice` and everything said through the HUD's `say` (parries, Echoes, quests, a first sight, a sealed door, the demo's edge).
  - **A sealed door says so again on coming back (`systems/seals.ts`).** It said so at most once in 20 s wherever the investigator was, so a player who looked away at the wrong moment, walked off and came back found nothing. The memory is dropped once they have been out of reach (6 m of a sealed door, or out of a sleeping horror's arena) for `SEALS.away` = 3 s, so the next approach is told at once; staying near still repeats it every 20 s, and going out and in again within 3 s (the edge of the reach) does not make it stutter. The demo's refusals are said when a gate or stair is used, which is the player's own act, and are left as they were.
  - **The lantern gutters out when Unmoored, and kindles at Fractured (`render/lanternLife.ts`).** On `SanityBandChanged` to `unmoored` the flame stutters for 1.8 s (dipping lower and staying down longer each time, then gone); it stays dark until the band is no longer Unmoored, that is, one band up, with the band's own three points of hysteresis (sanity 18, not 15) so it does not kindle and gutter at the edge, and then catches over 1.6 s (stuttering up, steady). It is a pure function of the game's own time since the band changed, so a pause holds it and a save loaded Unmoored begins dark.
