It dims the light itself (`uLanternColor`: the world, the sprites, the fog's wash, the post pass), the halo (as if covered), and the flame on the belt (smaller, dimmer, then hidden; it is `Figure.flameMesh` now, under `flame`, so it scales about its own centre). The chariot's own side lamps are the vehicle's and burn on. The mending of the mind never counted the lantern (`lightAt`), so a dark lantern costs no mending: the dark is the cost. Not verified: how it reads at the frame rate on a real machine (checked in a headless browser with the game held at chosen frames).
- **Darker nights, a harder mind, a finer warp, round 58 (asked: "reduce ambient lighting in selected dungeons and corridors so that navigation relies primarily on the player's lantern"; "the open-world environment during nighttime lacks sufficient darkness as well"; "recalibrate [the sanity system] to increase difficulty without introducing frustration"; "the current screen wobble and distortion effects … appear visually unrefined"):**
  - **Why the night was not dark: the mist lit it (`render/volumetricFog.ts`).** The volumetric mist's own light was the realm's *whole* mist colour at every hour (`fogOf` took `lookOf(region).mist`, not the night's), so the open world's picture, and the haze band across it, was mostly a bank of mist lit from nowhere; the night's own floors (`NIGHT_DARK`) only dimmed the sky, the ambient and the moon beneath it. Measured on stills (mean brightness of the middle of the picture, headless): Arkham 0.171 → 0.077, Dunwich 0.154 → 0.067. The mist now keeps `NIGHT_DARK.fog` = 0.2 of its light at the deepest of the night (`mistTone`, by the eased darkness `RealmLook.dark`), never black: the horizon is a faint line to walk toward. The floors were lowered as well (sky 0.16, mist 0.11, far 0.13, ambient 0.09, moon 0.11; were 0.24, 0.28, 0.3, 0.2, 0.28).
  - **The lantern is a pool, not a corridor (`LANTERN`).** Intensity 1.15 (was 1.5) and range 8.5 m (was 11): with the room dark, the old flame still lit every wall of a two-metre corridor. A fight is still read within six metres of it. `decay` is left (the lamps and torches share it, and they are where the mind mends).
  - **Dungeons: a gloom per kit, by the room's kind (`data/kits.ts` `gloom`, `world/gloom.ts`).** The roofed room took 0.35 of the night's darkness and 0.55 of the moon at every dungeon alike. Now each kit has a gloom (the crypt 0.92, the mine 0.95, the cellar and the drowned and basalt vaults 0.9, masonry 0.88, brick 0.85; the library and church 0.72, timber and townhouses 0.76, the marble hall 0.62, which have their candles); a corridor adds 0.1, a stair 0.06, a pit and a well 0.1, a hall takes 0.08 off and a great hall 0.08 more; a room with an Elder Sign is at most `DUNGEON_LIGHT.refuge` (0.35) and a horror's arena at most `arena` (0.5), so a fight and a rest are read. The room takes `lerp(dark 0.35, deep 1, gloom)` of the night's darkness and `lerp(moon 0.55, moonDeep 0.06, gloom)` of the moon, eased as the camera passes a door. The torches and the braziers are untouched, and are what one walks toward (and, for the mind, where it mends). Judgment call: *selected* dungeons are chosen by kit, not by name, so that every dungeon built from the same stone has the same dark; open-roofed ruins are under the sky and keep the night's.
  - **A lantern gone out (Unmoored) is not a blindfold.** The eye widens: `adapted()` takes `DUNGEON_LIGHT.adapt` = 0.15 off the darkness where `lanternGain` is 0, in a room and on the road alike (a crypt corridor, lantern out, still shows the wall and the foe's shape: mean 0.05, measured).
  - **The sanity numbers (`SANITY`, `LAUDANUM`; `systems/sanity.ts`).** The great horrors and the gods were weighed right; the small and the middling were not (a deep one's blow took one point; a named horror's fight of forty seconds, first sight and all, some twenty). `SANITY.weigh` now weighs each tier's auras (lesser 1, was 0.6; greater 1.5; named 1.6; the great old ones and the gods 1, unchanged) and blows, roars and gazes (lesser 0.9, greater 0.8, named 0.85, were all 0.5; the great old ones and the gods 0.5, unchanged). First sight costs 6, 9, 13 and 15 for the greater, named, great old one and outer god tiers (were 4, 6, 10, 12). Mending: 0.08 in the dark (was 0.25: every wound of a dungeon was healed by the walk to its next room; the lamps, at 1.2 from 1.6, are where it is mended now) and 8 s after a blow (was 6); *Unmoored the mind clings harder*, 0.3 in the dark, so that from nothing to Fractured, where the lantern kindles, is at worst a minute and no trap. The hallucinations' blows (5 each, fixed) are untouched. **Laudanum:** 35 a dose (was 50), steadying for 10 s (was 20), three doses still; and *it dulls*: each swallow within 60 s of others restores 0.4 less, to 0.3 of a dose at least (35, 21, 10.5), so three in a row are worth about two and a dose a minute after the last is whole; a notice says so. Why not frustration: a dose is still whole once a fight; a lamp, a torch, an Elder Sign and a rest still mend; Resolve still shortens every loss by its 15% a level; the horrors' own numbers are not made worse; and `tests/sanityBossBalance.test.ts` still holds that every boss leaves at least 25 sanity after first sight and a minute of its full aura (it was 28: only first sight rose, by three). The same Other Gods fight, two minutes with three hits and two roars, that one dose carried now takes two, a minute apart, and ends with 45.
  - **The warp is a picture, not a wobble (`render/shaders/post.ts`, `fx.ts`, `warpWaves.ts`).** It was two sines over the whole screen, a colour split that turned about, a zooming lens and a shearing one, at one rhythm, for as long as the mind was low: a filter. Now: *flow* (two octaves of slow noise, in patches that come and go) at the edges of the picture only, the middle holding where a fight is read; a *lens split* of the colours outward from the middle; a faint *second picture* a little to one side (the mind seeing double: `FX.ghost`); at the edge of Unmoored (stress 0.8, sanity 20) *tears* in slices of nine rows, rare and only at a surge's height; a *ring* sent out of the middle as a band is lost (`FX.shock`, over `pulseSeconds` 1.1); and all of it in *waves* (`surgeAt`: two slow waves that never line up, so there are calm stretches and swells, the calm growing short as the mind goes and never quite gone), the world's far walls swimming and the lens drifting (`driftAt`: unevenly, 5° and 0.05 of shear at most, were 7° and 0.07) with them. The accessibility cap, the warp's toggle, `FX.calm` and `warpCap` hold as before. Judgment call: the ghost and tear are under the *warp* toggle, not new toggles. Not verified: how it reads on a real display at 60 Hz (checked in a headless browser, with the surge, tear and ring pinned).
  - Measured by `window.pipeline` stills and the mean brightness of the middle of the picture; the sheets are not kept (scratch).
  - **The balance bot now drinks (audit, round 58).** `npm run audit` failed one row after the sanity changes (Nyarlathotep, 3.4 lives against a bound of 3): the bot (`tests/botHelpers.ts`) never drank Laudanum, so a harder mind left it Unmoored in the long fights, taking x1.35 and striking x0.85 for the rest of them, which no person would let happen. With `mind: true` it drinks when the HUD calls for it (`callsForLaudanum`: Fractured, a dose left), as the table's person is told to; the table is regenerated and every row is within its bounds (60 of 60). The long fights read shorter and cheaper than the old table, which had the bot Unmoored for most of them (Cthulhu 227 s, 4.6 lives, now 89 s, 1.5; Yog-Sothoth 176 s, 5.5, now 84 s, 2.4): that older figure was the bot's mind, not the horror's. It does not model the Elder Sign's candle before each fog, the Draught upgrade's extra doses or Resolve, which a person has.
  - **Codex's review of PR 146 (two findings, both real).** *A volley took the toll of the wrong tier:* `tollOnce` compared and stored the unweighted `blow` and only the returned delta was weighed by the current attacker's tier, so a high raw, low-weight blow could suppress a later one whose actual toll was greater, and the reverse order overcharged. The weighted cost goes in now (Codex pushed the fix, `5c86473`, with tests for six mixed-tier pairs and for a Laudanum-protected hit not spending the next volley; read and kept). *A long notice came back at the waking:* with a notice standing 4.6 s and the fall 2.5 s, `Died` and `Fell` cleared only the queue and the HUD's root hid the rest, so the notice that was up showed again after the respawn until its hold and fade ran out. The notice clock is now `createNoticeBoard` (`ui/noticeFade.ts`, so that it can be tested) and `Died` (the investigator's) and `Fell` call `clear()`: what is up goes at once, what waits is dropped.
  - **The open world's mist and moon are as they were; the default picture is 800 × 450 (asked, after seeing the round's stills: "I liked the fog on the open world way more like it was before"; "why is the moon gone?!"; "I want the default resolution to be the 800 × 450 setting").** *The mist:* round 58 dimmed the volumetric mist's own light by the night's depth everywhere; that glow, in its realm's colour, is what the open world was liked for, so `mistTone` now keeps all of it in the open air (`NIGHT_DARK.openFog` = 1) and dims it only under a roof (`NIGHT_DARK.fog` = 0.2), where it is part of the dungeons' dark, which was liked. *The moon:* the disc was never gone (`uMoon` and its colour were not touched, and it is drawn when the camera is turned to it), but at the deep of the night it stands 58° up, out of the follow camera's view, and what had gone was its light: the floor for the moon's light on the land had gone from 0.28 to 0.11, so the slopes it silvered and the shadows it cast went with it, and the glow in the mist's moon quarter. The floor is 0.28 again, and a room's moon is held where round 58 first put it by `DUNGEON_LIGHT.moon` 0.5 (was 0.55) and `moonDeep` 0.024 (was 0.06), so that the dungeons did not move (measured on stills: the library's hall and its vault corridor, mean 0.12 and 0.14 as before). What stays darker in the open world is the ambient (0.09), the sky's floor and the lantern's tighter pool: the ground away from the lantern. *The resolution:* `SETTINGS.resolution` = [0.8, 2, 0.4, 2], so 800 × 450 is the default and the standard maximum, and `HIGH_RESOLUTIONS` is 960 × 540 alone (its warning and its restore button now name 800 × 450, in four languages). Judgment call, said plainly: round 56 chose 640 × 360 because it is a whole number of times over on 720p, 1080p, 1440p and 4K, so every pixel is the same size; 800 × 450 is 1.6, 2.4, 3.2 and 4.8 times over on them, so some rows and columns are drawn wider than others there (what shimmered as the camera turned in the old 600 × 338). It was asked for, and it is logged so that it can be changed back in one number. Saves of settings are version 17: one kept at 640 × 360, which every save of settings held whether chosen or not, goes to 800 × 450 once; a lower choice (a slow machine's, the first launch's) and a sharper one are kept, and a 640 × 360 chosen since is kept. The first-launch ladder starts at 800 × 450 (800 × 450 twice, with and then without shadows, then less fog, then 640 × 360, 480 × 270 and 320 × 180).

  - **Codex's review of PR 147 (one finding, real): the first-launch tuner could not reach the new bottom rung.** The default's move to 800 × 450 added a rung to `LADDER` (seven now), but `createAutoQuality` still allowed four adjustments (`MOST = 4`), so a machine that stayed slow without being poor (above 28 fps, below 50 at every measurement) stopped for good at rung 4 (480 × 270, half fog) where it had once reached 320 × 180. The cap is now `LADDER.length - 1`, so it follows the ladder whatever its length; `tests/frameStats.test.ts` keeps a launch at 40 fps stepping down to the last rung (it fails with the old cap).
  - **Codex's second review of PR 147 (two findings, both real).** *The mist's tone jumped at a threshold:* `mistTone` took the raw `enclosed` flag, so crossing a dungeon's door in the deep night swung the mist's light between `openFog` (all of it) and `fog` (a fifth) in one frame, while its density and colour were eased. The fog now keeps a `roof` share, eased at `FOG.ease` like the rest of the mist (taken at once on the first frame, as the place's own mist is), and `mistTone(dark, roof)` blends the two by it (a boolean still reads as 0 or 1). `tests/fog.test.ts` steps through a door and holds the tone to k of the way, then to the room's. *The `?mounts` exhibit's two sizes were one:* its "Inspect at 800px" button rendered at scale 2, which is now the game's own picture; the inspection is the first optional higher step (`HIGH_RESOLUTIONS[0]`, 960 × 540) and its label is read from the scale.
  - **Codex's third review of PR 147 (one finding, real): the 640 × 360 → 800 × 450 move must not reach a slow machine's tuned profile.** A save from before version 17 holding 640 × 360 was moved up whatever else it held, and the first launch's first two steps down (shadows off; the fog thinned) leave the resolution at 640 × 360, so those machines, which had already missed the frame target there, were given 56% more pixels. A profile with its shadows or fog below the defaults is now a tuned one and keeps 640 × 360; an untouched one moves up once, as before. The same mark holds for a save from before version 13, which takes the default of the day (it keeps the old default if marked, as it did before this round, unless it had chosen something sharper). The cost: a strong machine whose person thinned the fog or turned shadows off by choice stays at 640 × 360 until they raise it themselves, which is the safer side to miss on.
  - **Codex's fourth review of PR 147 (one finding, half right): a room's moon was only matched at the two ends of its gloom.** When the open land's moon floor went back to 0.28 (from 0.11), the room's share of the moon was changed to 0.5 and 0.024 (from 0.55 and 0.06) to keep a room's moon as round 58 first made it. The finding's own example was wrong (a room with no gloom takes only 0.35 of the night's darkness, not all of it, so it is within 1% of what it was) but the middle was not: the product of the darkened moon and the share is `(1 - dark (1 - floor)) x share`, a different curve for a different floor, and a room at gloom 0.9 (the crypts, the mines, the cellars) kept 28% more moonlight than before, 0.0234 where it had been 0.0183. `DUNGEON_LIGHT.moon`, `moonDeep` are 0.55 and 0.06 again and `moonFloor` (0.11) names the floor a room's moon is darkened to; `moonShare(gloom, night)` takes the land's floor out and the room's in, so the light that reaches the stone is what it was at every gloom. `tests/gloom.test.ts` holds the curve to round 58's own numbers on a grid (it fails on the old pair). A lantern gone dark widens the eye a little (`adapt`), which moves the dark in both and is left out of the share.
  - **Notices stand half as long (asked: "the recently updated info text (e.g. at the gate, or when trying to summon a mount inside) stays too long").** `NOTICE` in `ui/noticeFade.ts`: `base` 1600 to 800 ms, `read` 55 to 27.5 ms a character, `max` 8000 to 4000, `fadeOut` 1700 to 850; `fadeIn` (180) and the queue (3) are as they were. The gate's line (52 characters) stood in full for 4.5 s and faded over 1.7 more, 6.2 s in all; it now stands 2.2 s and fades over 0.85, 3.1 s. It still stands for as long as its words take to read, still comes back when the gate is approached again, and every notice uses the one board (the sealed gate, a mount refused indoors, the rest).
  - **The horse's idling and summoning are recordings (round 59, asked: "dramatically improve the sound design of the horse idling and summoning, use free and fitting external sfx").** The Chariot's horse was a synthesised nicker, breath and snort. It is now nineteen takes cut from nine CC0 recordings on Freesound (found by searching its CC0 filter for nickers, whinnies, snorts, breath and harness; 30 candidates cut to the ones whose spectrograms showed what they were named for: harmonic glides for the whinnies, a low voiced call for the whickers, broadband bursts for the snorts and blows, bright metallic bursts for the harness): four whinnies, three whickers, five snorts, three breaths out and four harness rattles, high-passed, faded, levelled to a peak of −1.4 dBFS like the rest and encoded as the rest. I could not listen to them: each was chosen from its loudness profile and spectrogram, and the levels were measured (`tools/audio_levels.py`) and checked by decoding every file in Chromium, so a listen is still owed. *Found, not made:* only the HQ preview (128 kbps MP3) of each recording is served without a login, so the files are cut from those and say so in `CREDITS.md`. *Alone, not under:* the planned recordings kept a third of their recipe beneath them (right for a door, where the recipe lends what a cut lacks), which would put a sawtooth nicker under a real whinny; `Planned.under` (default a third) is 0 for these five sets, and `recipeShare` in `data/samples.ts` is the one place that says so. The recipe plays where a file has not loaded. *The arrival:* the summon is instant (the rider is on the horse at once), so a lone call was all it had; it now runs `settle` (the harness taking the weight, 0.2–0.3 s), `stamp` (a hoof planted over the recorded footfall, about a second) and `greet` (a low whicker, about two seconds), for the included horse only. *The toss:* after a breath out or a snort a head goes up now and then (a quarter or two-fifths of the time) and the harness answers 0.3–0.45 s later, under its own key so no shake follows a shake. *Also recorded:* the slap's huff (`urge`, from the snorts) and the haul-up's blow (`halt`, from the breaths), so no real breath in a ride is answered by a synthesised one; the weights of the idle round were moved toward the recorded voices (blow 3, whicker 2.5, shake 2, snort 2, swish 1.5, flutter 1). *Left as recipes:* the tail, the lips and the paw's hoof (the one lip-flutter found was a single take, and a set of one plays the same take twice running; no clean stomp could be told from a handling thump by its spectrogram), the skeletons' team, the car, and the dismissal.

  - **Codex review of PR 148: keep hoof sounds on visible landings.** The arrival stamp scheduled a hoofbeat and recorded footfall about a second after summoning, with no matching hoof animation. Removed that cue rather than add another animation clock; the arrival keeps its whinny, harness settle and low whicker, and the existing gait and shared `pawLanded` paths still sound on visible hoof landings. The arrival regression rejects both the stamp and its recorded footfall.

  - **Codex follow-up review of PR 148: make the head toss visible.** The idle blow/snort branch scheduled a harness rattle but never signalled a pose. `MountState.idleToss` now holds one transient start/peak cue: audio and the draft horse use the same render clock, the head and neck rise to the rattle and settle over 0.5 s. Moving off, dismissal and long frame gaps clear the cue and cancel its pending sound; no save format changes. An integration regression compares the real rendered head/bit with a neutral twin at the same time when the actual harness callback fires.
- **A cutscene frames a horror as it is drawn, round 59 (asked: "in some of the boss cutscenes the camera goes too high up and boss isn't centered in frame"):** a shot's `dist`, `up` and `look` were scaled by the capsule's height (`body.height`, what a blow strikes), and its feet were the anchor. The capsule is the silhouette's share of `sprite.scale`, but the reviewed art is drawn at its own scale and its figure fills only part of the frame: the Haunter's capsule is 2.8 m and its picture 1.75 m (hovering 2 m up besides), the Colour's 3.5 and 2.3, the Unnamable's 2.2 and 1.55, Nug's and Yeb's 4.9 and 3.3–3.6, and the Hound's picture is a third taller than its capsule. Framed by the capsule, nine bosses' close-ups aimed over the head, and the horror sank out of the bottom of the picture. Now `creatureViews.figure(e)` gives where the drawing stands above the feet (its idle front frame's painted rows, `reviewedSpan`, plus a flyer's hover), the cinema anchors the horror on that span, and the director picks a scene's size by it (`cinema.heightOf`); a creature with no reviewed art keeps its capsule. `tests/cinemaFraming.test.ts` holds every boss's arrival and fall to it: each shot aimed at the horror looks at its figure (but the Colour's last look up out of the well) with at least a quarter of it in the picture. The colossi's face shots (Cthulhu's lens 24–28 m up, at his face) are the same as before: they look at the face they mean to. Two more rules came of looking at the scenes in the arena (`?spawn`): a close-up of a coarse picture stands back until one of its pixels covers at most a thirty-second of the picture's height (`keepGrain`, `GRAIN`; the Haunter is painted in 28 rows, and framed by its true height its close-up was a wall of blocks: it now stands about 2.4 m off, where the capsule had it at 3.1 m with its head out of the top of the picture; the person-sized bosses, at 50 to 57 rows, keep their close-ups), and a shot over the shoulder at the other party turns off the line between them until the line of sight passes 0.6 m beside the one it stands behind (`SHOULDER`; at 180° to 176° the investigator stood in the middle of the picture and hid a horror of their own size), unless it looks over their head at a taller one.
- **The rain and a bolt on the ground are recorded, round 59 (asked: "improve the weather sound design (especially rain, because the new dripping sounds unrealistic (before the patch it sounded like white noise/static which wasn't good either) and the lightning bolts hitting the ground just sound like blips (the roaring thunder in the background is fine though), use free external fitting sources"):** the rain is two CC0 recordings of rain on open country by one recordist (jmbphilmes on Freesound: a light rain and a heavy one, cut to 32 s loops with a 2 s crossfade, high-passed, `public/audio/amb/rain_light`, `rain_heavy`), played by the weather bed (`weatherBed.ts`): each read twice, half its length apart and panned apart, softened above 6.5 kHz (`RAIN_SOFT`), the downpour coming in over the light rain past 35% (`levelsOf`'s `light` and `heavy`), and under a roof the bed's low-pass as before. Fetched with the first rain; until both have loaded the made rain of round 53 stands in. Chosen by their spectra and level over time (no traffic, voices or gusts in them; the heavy take's one low thud at 44 s left out). A bolt on the ground plays `strike` (four CC0 close strikes, loganzsound, TRP, foad and csengeri: the crack, the air tearing and the crash, cut at their onset to 4 s), with the low boom beneath it; the round-52 recipe only until the recordings have loaded. The thunder that rolls after a flash is unchanged.
- **Attack effects for every boss and every foe, round 59 (asked: "review and improve all attack effects (vfx) of all bosses", then "also review and improve the attack vfx of all other enemies as well"):** reviewed by playing each kind of move in the arena (`?spawn`) under a fixed side camera, a frame every few steps, with the AI held. What was weak and what was done: a horror's beam shot (`Shot`) lingered 0.26 s and thinned from its first frame, so at fighting distance it was gone before it was seen: it holds 0.42 s, full for its first 30%; a sweeping beam opens from a thread over a tenth of a second; both keep a least width on the screen (1.2% of their distance), as the lightning's bolts already did (`beams.ts`). A quake's front threw four motes a frame however wide it had grown, so a colossus's ring was a scatter of specks: it throws up to sixteen, by its circumference, on the ground it runs over (it used the height of where it began). A pulse of dread (a `sanity` move with no gaze) was only a ring on the ground: its front now throws up motes in the mind's colour (`attackFx.ts`). A slow, heavy blow (26 frames or more to its first active one, 30 or more poise) glints where it will come from a quarter of a second before it falls (`meleeFx.ts` `TELL`), once however the pictures fall against the steps. And an ordinary foe's swing, slam, lunge and the blow it lands threw nothing at all (`meleeFx` drew only the horrors with colours of their own): every foe now throws a share of it by its size (`strengthOf`: 0.3 + 0.35 × its radius, at most 0.8; a horror all of it), in the colours and matter of what it is made of (`attackLook`), with a scar and a lit arc on the ground only under the largest (radius 1.1 m or more). Telegraphs stay a boss's alone (spec §3E: an ordinary foe is read by its body). `tests/foeFx.test.ts`.
- **A horror's blows are recorded, and a foe swings with its own whoosh, round 59 (asked: "also improve the sound design of all enemy attacks (including bosses) as well"):** `data/attackSounds.ts` made every element's bolt, landing, burst and throw of sines and filtered noise alone (the same family of sound as the bolt on the ground that was heard as a blip). 34 CC0 takes from Freesound now stand in front of those recipes through the planned recordings (`data/plannedSounds.ts`, `atk(...)`: the recipe stays beneath, a third as loud): water for the sea's blows, wet squelches for ink, spores and flesh, stone and debris for stone, ash and the plain, a fireball and fire whooshes for fire, ice breaking for frost, energy impacts and spell whooshes for the glyph, the eye and the star, a whip's crack for the coil; and a heavy low whoosh for every physical throw. Each landing, burst and throw of every element is recorded (`tests/plannedSounds.test.ts`), each take levelled in a window of -34 to -14 dBFS. An ordinary foe's swing played the investigator's own heavy swing: it plays `swipe` (four CC0 creature whooshes), pitched by its size as before. Chosen without hearing them (no speakers here): by title and rating on Freesound, their spectra and their level over time, each cut at its onset; worth a listen in the game, and a take swapped if one is wrong.

- **The walk through a boss's fog is the fog's, and the horror wakes after it, round 60 (asked: "the character keeps walking in after entering a boss room through the fog, and sometimes the boss can hit you immediately while the user is locked in an animation"):** played headless at all 51 walls, the walk (`systems/fogGates.ts`, input taken away) lasted ~3.2 s, and the horror woke ~1 s before it ended: its ring reaches past the fog, and `arenaSight` takes anyone inside the ring. Waking set off its arrival cutscene, which holds the world still, so when the scene ended the rest of the walk played out with the horror free to strike, and some did (Sentinel Hill, Y'ha-nthlei, the Colossus of the pyramids). Now nothing may reach the investigator on the walk (`outOfReach` in `targets.ts`: not seen, struck or shoved, as in a cutscene or a fall), so the horror wakes on the step it ends; the walk ends 1.8 m past the wall, not 2.4, and its pace runs from rest to rest (`walkAlong`: 0.3 s to get under way, 2 m/s, 0.5 s to stop; it set off and ended at ~1 m/s, so an arrival scene that held the world on its last step showed the investigator striding in place: asked, "the characters walking animation should stop after entering"), ~2.5 s from the edge of E's reach; and a boss holds its first blow `BOSS.wake` (45 frames) once it wakes (`bossFight.ts`), since without it the Outsider seized whoever stood at its feet the frame after. I-frames on the walk were the other way; out of reach is the rule a cutscene already keeps, and it also keeps the horror from waking early.
- **A parried or shot horror is a punish taken at once, not a free crit, round 60 (asked: "there is an extremely gamebreaking bug where a player for no apparent reason does huges amounts of damage to bosses with normal/heavy attacks"; on human-sized bosses, a big chunk of the bar):** measured by a bot at every boss in the arena and in the open world, and by random play (mashing, circling, rolling, parrying, shooting) at twenty human-sized ones: a light or heavy blow landed at 1.0–1.2 of its listed damage (the mind's band, the relics), except a riposte, which landed at x2.5 (up to x2.95 with the band) and set the horror reeling, and every one of those came 40–98 frames after a parry or a shot that broke a wind-up (none without). A parried or interrupted foe stood open 96 frames, so any ordinary blow within 1.6 s of it was a riposte; with Might and reinforcement one took half of Dr. Munoz's 400. With Shift held for the heavy blow, the right button parries rather than blocks, so a parry can come unmeant. Now a boss stands open `BOSS.opening` (40 frames) and a riposte weighs `BOSS.riposte` (x1.5) on it; common foes keep 96 frames and x2.5 (the parry and the revolver's interrupt are still the way into a horror's guard, Phase 1). Played again: one riposte in sixty fights, at x1.77, 40 frames after a parry.
- **The revolver breaks a horror's wind-up and chips it; it does not fell it, round 60 (asked: "it also happens with firing the revolver ... because the revolver makes an immense amount of damage at the boss, and you can just spam it and cheese it that way, if yes nerf the revolver"):** measured with the revolver alone at ten human-sized bosses from 3 m: a shot landed 30–45 (a heavy blow's weight, from range, every half second, more than the sword-cane's light chain), each one in a wind-up broke it, and two quick shots made the second a riposte; it felled eight of the ten in twenty seconds, and a full carry (the cylinder and the twelve spare) was enough for most. Now a boss takes `BOSS.shot` (0.3) of the investigator's shot and of its poise, a wind-up a shot has broken cannot be broken again for `BOSS.rearm` (240 frames), and no shot ripostes (`Blow.ranged`: the opening is the hand's to take, on any foe). Common foes take a shot whole, as round 22 made it. Played again: a full carry, alone, took 14–31% of a human-sized horror's bar with the gun as found, about half with it fully set with star-stones, and broke at most two wind-ups in forty seconds.
- **Criticals and weak points weigh less, round 60 (asked: "if the issue stems from critical or weakpoint damage output, the character is excessively powerful, undermining the intended challenge of the soulslike experience even at the standard difficulty setting"):** every oversized blow measured was a critical (a riposte, a backstab) or a colossus's head, or both: a riposte into a stooped head was six times a blow. A riposte or backstab on a common foe now weighs x1.75 (`COMBAT.riposte`, was 2.5), on a boss x1.25 (`BOSS.riposte`), and a colossus's head x1.5 (`ZONES.head.damage`, was 2.5): worth seeking, never the fight. Two findings of Codex's review on PR 150 with it: the boss's opening is `BOSS.opening` 64 frames, not 40, since the parry's own recovery (36 frames) and a heavy blow's wind-up (24) left no riposte a parry could take at 40 (a test now drives the parry and the heavy blow frame by frame); and a boss that joins a fight (Brown Jenkin) takes `BOSS.wake` at the join, since its first blow was chosen before its fight was marked engaged.
- **A sign walked up to says it is a journey's end, round 60 (asked: "automatic activation of an elder sign that is caused by walking up onto it should also be making it available to fast travel, instantly, instead of having to rest on it first"):** it already was. A sign is found within `WORLD.discover` (6 m), and the map (M) and every sign's journey list offer every sign found, rested at or not. Played in the browser: walked up to the Sleeper's Sign, away again, opened the map, and it offered "TRAVEL TO THE SLEEPER'S SIGN"; Enter took the investigator there. But nothing said so: the line on finding one was its name, and the only hint about signs is about resting. The line now ends "TRAVEL HERE FROM THE MAP (M)" (the map's button for the device in hand), in all four languages, and a test walks up to a sign and journeys to it without a rest.
- **The gods are tuned to where the open world lets a person meet them, and the levels come faster, round 60 (asked: "God bosses like Father Dagon and similar are way too overpowered and make way too much damage"; "prioritize the colossi and all the strong bosses ... way too hard ... even at like level 20. This should be an urgent fix"; "the primary observation from this playthrough is the minimal level progression ... moderate grinding ... leveling balanced in alignment with area exploration and boss encounters"; the observation was "in R'lyeh at level 26"; then "I might just have been underleveled and overexaggerate ... but still decide for me"):** decided on a middle course. Not the cut measured at level 20 (it took the gods' damage to a fifth and halved two of their healths, which a person at the level the world means would walk through), but three things. (1) **The model**: `tests/balanceModel.ts` met the bosses in order of their health, every god after the others, and had Cthulhu met at level 44; R'lyeh is two gates from the start, and a person reached it at 26. The bosses are now met ring by ring as the world opens (the university; Arkham; Vermont, Providence, Dunwich and Innsmouth; the Dreamlands and the realms through the waking world's gates; Beyond the Gate), each ring's lesser foes (60%) in step with its named horrors and colossi, and a god, which may be met in any order, halfway through its ring with no god's Echoes in hand: the waking world's gods at 28, the second ring's at 40, Beyond's at 46, on the new curve. (2) **The levels**: `LEVEL_COST` 250 + 90n + 9n² became 200 + 60n + 4.5n², about half from the twentieth level on: at 26 a level cost 35 of R'lyeh's foes and costs 19, the same run comes to R'lyeh at about 32, and at each ring's standing a level costs at most forty of that ring's foes (`tests/levelPace.test.ts`; the second ring's took 48 on the old curve); the whole world's Echoes still buy all sixty levels, and Beyond is met short of them. (3) **The measure, then the gods**: the bot stood a minute behind a star-spawn and the Unmoored mind's phantoms (they vanish at a blow; it now strikes a servant or a phantom at its elbow), walked into Cthulhu's 12 m body on its way to the Alert, which always came in at the arena's north (she now comes in on its far side from Cthulhu: a game fix, a person crossed under the god too), fought every colossus among the Phase 1 arena's middle pillars and fixed dummy, which threw a body that wide metres to and fro every step (they are set aside for a colossus: its arena in the world has nothing in its way), and stood in the void under the Great Ones' and 'Umr at-Tawil's hidden platforms that a person with insight keeps to (waived, as the gaze and the roots are); and each fight is now the mean of three starts. Measured so, Cthulhu (0.78 lives at its standing), 'Umr at-Tawil and the lesser gods were within a person's Reagent; ten were not, and were cut on their damage, the three longest also on their health: Shub-Niggurath health 5450 → 3800 and damage 100 → 34 (4.13 lives where a person first meets it, in Dunwich at 28), Yog-Sothoth 6100 → 4400 and 62 → 38 (2.56), Nyarlathotep 5150 → 4000 and 100 → 42 (2.01), Father Dagon 80 → 38 (1.87), Mother Hydra 76 → 46 (1.40), Tsathoggua 52 → 30 (1.36), Ghatanothoa 90 → 58 (1.29), Hastur 90 → 56 (1.19), Yig 70 → 50 (1.12), the Great Ones 70 → 50 (1.00). The ten now cost 0.62-0.78 lives at their standings, and no god more than Nug's 0.84 (the bound, `tests/balance.test.ts`, is 1); at the 26 of the playthrough, R'lyeh's cost 0.99 and 1.12 (3.85 and 1.95 before) and Innsmouth's 0.85 and 0.84 (2.62 and 1.26). A horror's heaviest blow takes at most a third of the health a person has where they meet it (`tests/bossBlows.test.ts`; Shub-Niggurath's charge took 51%, Father Dagon's slam 44%). Shorter fights with blows that still weigh were chosen over longer ones of light blows: Shub-Niggurath at 3800 and 34 costs about what 4200 and 30 did, in 38 seconds instead of 44.
- **The settings' sound mix, round 60 (asked: "the game sounds alot better at sound volume settings: 50% ambience, 100% effects ... volume 50, voices 50, music 50% ... micro decide around these values yourself to calculate the best mix with only a slight off by -/+11%"):** volume 0.5, music 0.5, effects 1, ambience 0.45 and voices 0.55 (0.7 and four at 1 before). The sliders are linear gains, a step at a half about 0.8 dB: the ambience a step under, as a bed that never stops masks the steps, the calls and the speech most, and the voices a step over, so a line stays clear over the blows with the effects full. A level a save of settings kept at the old default goes to the new once (version 18); a level chosen is kept, the mix asked for among them.
- **Every creature's blows are its own, round 60 (asked: "regarding the last attack VFX improvements of every boss and enemy ... make it very distinct from each other, and also with more fidelity"):** a blow's element (what it is made of, `render/aoeLook.ts`) was shared by up to six horrors, and every common foe of one hide threw the same matter in the shared magenta and green. Each of the 99 creatures now has a signature (`data/attackSignatures.ts`): a form of 18 (tendrils, bubbles, runes, eyes, forked arcs, fangs, feathers, a swarm, crystals, petals, voids, a prism's sparks, sand, chains, hands of smoke, notes, cinders, motes), a motion of 6 (a fountain, a spiral, drawn in, a rain, an orbit, scattered) and an accent light, drawn over the element at every flash, burst, landing, trail, gathering and column (`render/fxForms.ts`). No two creatures of one element share all three, and no two horrors of one element share a form; a common foe's colours come from its accent and its hide. Eight particle shapes were added (rune, eye, fang, bubble, a forked bolt, feather, petal, note), and what falls lands and lies on the ground a moment (a particle's floor: it springs a little, skids, rests) instead of sinking through it. The particle batch holds 4 096 (2 400: a colossus's burst in a crowd took the oldest away mid-flight), and a signature adds at most 64 particles to a burst and one to a bolt's step (a burst of tendrils, seven to the stalk, had been 280 over the element's own; a bolt's step had drawn whole chains). The element tests keep testing the element alone; the signature's own are `tests/attackSignatures.test.ts`. The floor that debris lies on is the ground under the blow, not its height (Codex, on the PR: a wound's spray lay in the air at the height of a torso; `Particles.ground`).
- **The gates between the realms are walked through, and look the part, round 60 (asked: "alls traversable portals (except boss fogs) should be accessible via straightforward pedestrian movement and feature more ambitious, visually compelling animations that enhance aesthetic appeal"):** the portals are the seven pairs of realm gates (the descent into dream is a choice at the Sleeper's Sign, and a dungeon's doors already open as a body nears; the boss fogs are left as they were, as asked). A free investigator on foot who steps into a gate's veil, between its jambs, from either side, is drawn on into it about a metre, the walk no longer theirs and nothing reaching them (as the boss fog's walk-through, round 35), and comes through at its twin (`systems/gateWalk.ts`); E still passes a gate, a boss fight still holds every gate shut and the demo's edge still turns them back, said as they step in (and not every step after). The veil of the journey falls over the walk, the world still stepping, from the middle of the screen out behind a ring of the far realm's light (`ui/veil.ts`, `ui/journeys.ts`), made to close as the walk ends. The flat purple pane between the jambs is now a door of light onto the realm beyond (`render/portalViews.ts`): that realm's sky and lamps wound into a slow vortex about a bright heart with its stars drifting in it, waking as the investigator nears (it turns faster, its heart opens, motes are drawn in), blazing as they step in with motes torn in after them, and, once the veil over the journey lifts, the twin bursts outward, its motes streaming on past those come through (who stand facing away from it) into their sight. Played in the browser: the realm's grade keeps only how bright a thing is, so a door of the far realm's colours read as a flat cream pane; the vortex is now drawn in light and dark, its arms the far sky brought to one brightness and Cosmic Purple (which the grade keeps) between them, and no prompt to press E stands over the walk in. Riding, a gate is not passed by walking into it: the chariot stops at the jambs, and E dismounts and passes as before.
- **The gods' one heavy blow, round 60 (asked, of the gods cut a few hours before: "are you sure the gods are not too easy now?", then "do what you suggest"):** the cut had made them attrition fights: Cthulhu's heaviest blow took 16% of a person's health where they meet it, Tsathoggua's 14%, Yog-Sothoth's 15%, Shub-Niggurath's 17%, Nyarlathotep's 18%, softer than the horrors of the first hours (the Dunwich Horror 28%, the Outsider 24%), so a good dodger was never in danger. Each now has one heavy blow given a long warning: `crushing_blow`, lifted a full second (60 frames) before it falls, and `great_mark`, one mark under the investigator that breaks 1.3 s after the cast begins (Nyarlathotep's from afar; the bot never steps out of a mark, so Yog-Sothoth, which stands still, has the crushing blow on any at its edge instead). Its recovery is a slam's (30 frames): at 44, a colossus stayed stooped long enough to have its head struck down a third faster, and Cthulhu cost the bot less with the blow than without it. The rest is set so that each god costs the balance bot about what it did (`docs/BALANCE.md`): Cthulhu 4600 health and 37 damage (0.82 lives, its heaviest blow 25%), Shub-Niggurath 4300 and 31 (0.81, 25%), Nyarlathotep 4000 and 34 (0.76, 23%), Tsathoggua 3000 and 31 (0.78, 21%), Yog-Sothoth 4200 and 32 (0.75, 20%). Each was tuned against a mean of six starts as well as the table's three, the bot's path through a fight being chaos.
- **The reins' slap is no longer heard, round 60 (asked: "remove the chariots reins slapping sound entirely as its annoying over time"):** `REIN_SLAP` and its mix level are gone, and nothing plays when the reins land at a gallop, on the grey horse or the skeleton team. The driver's hands still slap in the picture (`systems/mountReins.ts`, `render/riderPose.ts`, `render/reinLines.ts`) and the counters are kept; the grey horse's huff a beat behind (`HORSE_URGE`, three slaps in four) is left, the chariot's team having none.
- **The mouse taken again is let settle, round 60 (asked: "when walking normally, opening the map, closing the map again, and then look around, the camera then seems to glitch and reset on a different viewpoint"):** opening the map lets the mouse go and closing it asks for it back at once (`onMenusClear(capture)`). Played in the browser (headless Chromium) the camera did not move across a walk, the map and its closing: yaw and pitch stayed put, the position ran on, and the same mouse movement turned it the same before and after. What can turn it to another view is the first movement a browser reports with a freshly granted lock (Chromium on Windows and macOS, and Electron, can report the cursor's jump to the middle of the window as a movement of hundreds of pixels), read as a look, and the pitch pinned at its limit. So `core/input.ts` now reads no mouse movement for `INPUT.lockSettleMs` (250 ms) after the browser grants the lock, and forgets what had built up before it; a hand on the mouse a moment later turns the camera as ever (`tests/inputLock.test.ts`). It could not be seen happen here, only the cause that fits it removed: if it still happens, the platform and whether the pad or the mouse was in hand are what is wanted.
- **A horror is not seen from afar, and comes into view when roused, round 60 (asked: bosses, Cthulhu and Father Dagon above all, were seen from a long way off, which spoiled them):** a boss of the open world stands in its ring from the moment the chunk streams in, and was drawn, locked on to and beheld (the sanity it costs) from as far as the mist let the eye run; the fog wall before it is a few metres high and the horror forty. Now `isUnroused` (`systems/components.ts`: an open-world game, a `Fight` not yet `engaged`, a script that does not hold the boss unseen already) makes `isUnseen` true, and everything that asks it (`lookOf`, the shadows, `lockOn`, `insight.inSight`, the foe bars) leaves the horror out until it wakes as the investigator steps into its ring. `render/bossArrival.ts` then brings it into view over `ARRIVAL` seconds by its height (1.1 s and 0.055 s a metre, at most 3.4 s), by the render clock, since an arrival scene may stand the simulation still: dithered in (`look.opacity`, the colossi's `uGhost`) and, for the great bodies of earth and sea (a lathe or a mound), up out of the ground (`look.sink`). Left as they were: the arrival scenes of the horrors (`data/cutscenesBoss.ts`, `cutscenesMore.ts`), which already open on the horror as it comes, and their stinger; the dread the horrors give off (their hum, the sanity drain of an aura) and the far titans of the skyline (`render/skyline.ts`), which are not the horror but the idea of it, and fair warning of where it is. The combat arena, the bestiary and `?spawn` are not open-world games (`g.overworld` is absent), so every creature there is shown at once; a summoned boss fights as a servant (no `Fight`), is never veiled. `tests/bossArrival.test.ts`.
- **The map is made clear, round 60 (asked: "the average user seems confused on reading the map, make it way more clear, less cluttered and more user-friendly/intuitive, while still looking good, make all the icons more thematically fitting as well, e.g. the elder symbol as the resting sign"):** what confused was, in the order met: (1) a new game's map opened on the whole realm, a small pale blot of seen ground in a field of black, which read as broken, so it now opens on what has been seen with the investigator in it (`ui/mapFit.ts`: charted box + margin, no nearer than 300 m across, no closer than ten times the realm's fit; a charted realm opens whole as before); (2) the key was a list of type glyphs (★ ◯ ∩ ◉ ◆ ♙ ◇) that were not the figures drawn, and two marks were both diamonds (Echoes and the story's lead), so the key now draws the map's own figures (`ui/mapChrome.ts` `KEY_ROWS`, `ui/mapIcons.ts` `drawMark`) and the Echoes are a drop of brass light; (3) every mark was a 5–6 px line drawing on pale ground, so each now stands on a small dark token ringed in its colour: the Elder Sign's five strokes as in the shrine and the app icon (bone and haloed once lit, grey and plain before), the gate's stone frame with its violet veil, a dungeon's arched door with steps down, a magenta slit-pupilled eye for a horror (a closed eye with lashes once it is slain, not a stroke through it) with a small eye on the door of a dungeon whose horror has been met, a bust for a person, the investigator's arrow in a faint halo; (4) everything was drawn at every zoom: on the full map people and found places now show from `DETAIL.people` and `DETAIL.places` pixels a metre (divided by the UI's scale), the names of gates and dungeons from `doorNames`, of people and places further in; the signs, gates, dungeons, horrors, Echoes and the lead are always there, and the minimap shows all it has room for and names nothing (`ui/mapMarks.ts`); (5) nothing said what a mark was: hovering one opens a card with what it is, what it is called (a horror's lair unnamed until met) and what can be done with it, and the places that were only a dot get their name; (6) the story's lead was lost when off screen: an arrow at the screen's edge points to it (`leadMark.ts`); (7) the controls were a run of dim text: each key is now in a box with its word beside it (the map's own key as bound), with a compass for north and L to hide the key. The heading is the region's name with the charted share and places found beneath it, the travel prompt a boxed line. Travel is as it was (a lit sign clicked twice, Enter, Tab, the pad), the failing mind's map still slides (`mapWrong.ts`). `tests/mapIcons.test.ts`, `mapFit.test.ts`, `mapMarks.test.ts`; captured in headless Chromium for a new game and a fully charted save.
- **A gate has no key, round 60 (asked: "remove the option and indicator for pressing E to move through portals/gates (except boss fogs of course), the notice should just say where the portal leads to when coming close to it"):** a realm's gate is passed by walking into its veil (`systems/gateWalk.ts`, earlier this round); E at one no longer also passes it. `interactable` takes only signs, people and a boss's fog (`Interactable.kind` has no `'gate'`), `checkpointSystem` and `ui/journeys.ts` no longer treat a press near a gate as a journey, and the HUD's line over the picture no longer reads "E · pass through …" there. Instead, within `GATE_WALK.notice` (9 m) of a gate and with no sign, person, fog or fight action to offer, it says `Leads to {where}` with no key beside it (`gateNear`, `leadsTo`; `hud.gateLeads` in all four languages): the region beyond the gate (Pnakotus, K'n-yan & N'kai, Mu & R'lyeh…), or, where its twin is in the same region (the Dreamlands' two), the twin's name. A fight that holds the gates shut still says so as the step in is made, and the demo's edge still turns the step back and says so. Riding: E dismounted the rider on the way (`playerControl.ts`); with no E, riding into the veil sets the rider down at the veil (`enterGate` → `dismissMount`) and draws them on as one on foot, which they come out as, as before; it was not left as a dead end (a gate that does nothing for a rider). The boss's fog keeps E (`fogToPass`), as asked. `tests/gateWalk.test.ts`, `checkpoints.test.ts`, `journeys.test.ts`.
- **Weapons and relics say they are found, and what is left to find, round 60 (asked: "enhance the weapon acquisition and collection mechanics: many users obtain them without sufficient awareness of the process or inventory status", and "same with relic acquisitions"):** a weapon was picked up by walking over it, with one line in the notices that faded, and not taken up (it waited in the pause menu's Arms, which nothing said to open); a relic was put on by itself when a horror fell, with its price on the mind, in a line over the banner; the Arms page listed what was owned and "N more lie somewhere in the dream", and the relics only once owned. Now both announce themselves with `Found` (`systems/finds.ts`, from `takeUp` and the Vanquished listener in `relics.ts`) and a card opens over the held world (`ui/findCard.ts`; the world stands still, as under the pause and the map) once the investigator is free (`free`: no scene, no boss fight, no foe hunting close, not fallen or walking through a gate or fog; it waits `FINDS.wait`, 0.9 s for a weapon so that the pickup is felt first, 5.2 s for a relic so that the horror's fall, banner and Echoes are read first). A weapon's card: its mark, name, note, who gave it, its figures against the weapon in hand (green where better, red where worse: light and heavy blows with Might and reinforcement counted, how soon the blow lands, reach, stamina), its temper, how many weapons are carried of how many, and Take it up now / Keep it for later. A relic's: its mark, name, note, who left it, its gifts green and prices red, whether it is worn (slot I or II of two) or kept because both slots are full, how many are found, and, if worn, Keep it worn / Take it off, else Understood; relics still wear themselves while a slot is free (the balance bots and saves from before rely on it), but the card now says so and offers to take it off. The collection is the Arms page (`ui/armsPage.ts`): `MELEE · 2 OF 5 FOUND` with each weapon's mark (`ui/weaponGlyphs.ts`), a NEW tag until the page has been seen, the ones not yet found as sealed lines that carry the rumour of where (`WEAPON_WHERE`, checked against where weapons lie and the quests that give them), `RELICS · 1 OF 5 FOUND · 1 OF 2 WORN` with each found relic's figures, worn or kept, and a line for those yet to be left by horrors slain for good (which relic a horror leaves is not told). The pause menu's Arms line carries `N NEW` until the page is opened (the Elder Sign's Relics tab also clears the relics' mark). New marks are kept among what has been told (`new:<kind>:<id>`), so a save holds them and an older save has none. The Notices stay, reworded (`… · A NEW WEAPON · TAKE IT UP FROM {pause} · ARMS`, `RELIC · … · WORN`), for a game with no card (the arena has none). `tests/finds.test.ts`.
- **A marker may be set on the map, and the minimap points to it, round 60 (asked: "add the ability to set a marker on the map, that has its own minimap arrow indicator"):** one marker, kept in the save (`Overworld.marker`, `SaveData.marker`; an older save has none), set and cleared from the full map: right-click on the map, F (at the pointer, or the middle of the map if there is none) or the pad's Y (at the middle, which shows a small cross for it); on the marker itself it clears. It must be on the ground of the realm shown (`systems/marker.ts` `markable`, and the map checks the realm). Coming within `MARKER.arrive` (10 m) clears it, with a word (`n.markerReached`, in all four languages). It is shown in the realm it was set in only (`markerHere`); in another it is kept, not drawn. The picture: a pin in verdigris, a colour no other mark has, its point on the place and a pennant in its head (`ui/mapIconsLive.ts`); on the full map it is a mark like any (a hover card says how far it is and how to clear it), and the map's edge shows an arrow toward one off screen. On the minimap the arrow is its own, a verdigris pointer with a pin's dot behind it where the story's lead is a bone plain one, on the round rim when the marker lies past it, and the pin itself where it lies within the minimap's reach (`ui/leadMark.ts`: `beaconPlace`, `drawBeacon`). The minimap turns with the camera and a pin turned with it would hang sideways, so the beacons are now drawn upright, after the turned picture, at the places the turn puts them (the lead's diamond too). `ui/mapIcons.ts` had reached 330 lines and is three files (`mapIconKit`, `mapIcons`, `mapIconsLive`). `tests/marker.test.ts`.
- **Moving reinforcement costs Echoes, round 61 (asked: "implement a substantial Echo cost for transferring weapon reinforcements to alternate weapons, thereby incentivizing the upgrade of additional armaments and the utilization of surplus Starstones"):** moving levels from one weapon to another was free (round 52), so a bare weapon was always best made by a move from the cane and the stones the horrors leave went unspent. Now each level moved costs `REINFORCE.moveEchoes` (600) Echoes for each star-stone it would cost to set in afresh as the level it becomes in the weapon it goes to (`systems/arms.ts` `moveCost`): 600 for the first level, 1200, 1200, 1200 and 1800 for the next four, and 5400 to move five into a bare weapon, against a level's own cost of 200 to about 3000 and a horror's 12,000 to 30,000 Echoes. A move stops at the levels the Echoes carried will pay for (`affordableMoves`), a move back costs again (so shuffling bleeds Echoes), and stones set directly into a weapon cost no Echoes, so the cheaper way to arm a second weapon is to set stones into it. Nothing is lost or made in a move, as before. The Elder Sign's move page shows the Echoes carried and each move's price in a chip, a move that cannot be paid for says why when chosen, and the tab's line and the Field Guide's hint (all four languages) say it is for Echoes. `tests/reinforce.test.ts`.
- **Insight to spare is offered for Echoes, round 61 (asked: "determine an optimal resolution for excess Insight points once all Mind attributes are maximized; currently, a lot of Insight points are accumulated with Mind fully upgraded, whereas a lower threshold reportedly grants access to all secret doors"):** counted from the data, a whole run brings about 170 insight (first sight 127, tomes 29, quests 16) against 14 for the Mind's two upgrades (Resolve 4 × 2, Draught 2 × 3) and 3 for the highest hidden passage (nine want 1, seven want 2, one wants 3), so some 150 points were gathered for nothing. The options weighed: more upgrade tiers (a sink, but each wants its own balance, and sanity resistance is already at 60%); converting it to star-stones (the opposite of what was asked the same day); a passive bonus from insight held (a hidden number nobody reads); selling it for Echoes. Chosen: Echoes, at an Elder Sign, by hand. What is *kept* is what every passage wants (`PASSAGE_NEED`, 3) plus the cost of each upgrade not yet bought (`insightKept`), so an offer never shuts a passage and never puts an upgrade out of reach (the upgrades are bought first, and what is then over is the surplus, `surplusInsight`); the rest is offered at `LORE.echoes` (200) Echoes a point, all at once or five at a time (`offerInsight`, `ui/signLore.ts` under the Mind's upgrades), so a run's spare 150 or so is worth some 30,000 Echoes, a few levels' worth against the 430,000 that sixty levels cost, and what pays for moving reinforcement between weapons (also this round). It is by hand and not automatic so that the insight stays the player's until they choose, and it is not made to bite by raising the passages' thresholds or the upgrades' costs, which would only move the problem. The Field Guide says so once five points are spare (`lore`, all four languages). `tests/lore.test.ts`.
- **Secret passages are framed, and open as they are approached, round 61 (asked: "enhance the visual design of secret passages and ensure they activate via a contextually appropriate animation upon approach"):** a hidden doorway was three plain blocks banded with one glowing stripe every half metre and a slab of plain wall, a hidden bridge one plank, and either was simply there, open, from the moment the insight was held, however far off, with a half-second flicker as the only event. Now (the look) a doorway is a door of cut stone (`render/passageShapes.ts`): a plinth and a capital to each jamb, a lintel with a cornice and a keystone, runes of four kinds carved down the jambs and an eye on the keystone, on both faces of the wall; the slab that fills it is wall, and carries, once the mind is ready for it, a faint outline of the door and the same eye; a bridge is a row of slabs, each edged with light and every other one set with a rune. And (the opening, `systems/passages.ts`) a passage that is shown stays shut, a wall or a gap, until the investigator comes within `PASSAGE.wake` (7 m; a bridge's from its middle and half its length further, so that it is formed before the brink is reached) and then opens over `PASSAGE.frames` (a door 1.6 s, a bridge 1.2 s, the sealed door of the Beyond 2 s), `Layer.open` running 0 to 1, begun it is finished though they step away, and open it stays until the mind loses what shows it (then it is a wall again, shut). What it is physically follows the picture: a door's seal leaves the world and a bridge's deck comes into it (and its gap, which a body falls into, is stopped) at `solidAt` (62%) of the way, not before, so no one walks through a slab or onto a bridge that has not formed. A passage stood at when the world is made (a load) is open (`PASSAGE.stood`), so nobody is shut into a wall. The picture (`render/passageViews.ts`): the runes flare as it wakes, the slab draws aside into the wall (a little thinner as it goes, so it passes within the wall's faces without fighting them) in dust and grit from the edge it clears, and a stone door's grinding is heard (`data/doorSounds.ts`, made for the seconds it takes, from where it stands); a bridge's slabs rise out of the dark one after another from the brink the investigator stands at, each coming in as a dither, flaring as it is set and giving up three motes of light. Insight now only decides whether the passage can wake; spent below its want, it shuts at once. `tests/passages.test.ts`, `passageShapes.test.ts`.

- **Every open arena is composed from its horror's own story, round 61 (asked: "the current boss arenas, particularly those in the open world, lack compelling visual direction and design: each arena should be completely reimagined to align more closely with thematic and narrative elements, featuring distinct, high-fidelity aesthetics both externally and internally; external assets such as models/props, textures and animations may be sourced from available royalty-free resources"):** the nineteen arenas of open ground (the Colour's heath, the sabbat, Orne's burying ground, Hutchinson's manor, Shub-Niggurath's grove, Medusa's plantation, Martin's Beach, the pyramids' court, Yig's mound, the Mound of Nug and Yeb, Bokrug's lake, Hatheg-Kla, R'lyeh, Carcosa, the Threshold, the Court of Azathoth, Yaddith, the pipers and the Ancient Ones' floor) were each a ring of the same dozen props about bare ground. Each now has a set (`data/arenaSets.ts`, `arenaSetsEarth.ts`, `arenaSetsBeyond.ts`): a ground, pieces by layout and an air. (1) *Ground* (`world/arenaGround.ts`): inside the ring and ten metres past it the land is the arena's: its texture comes in over the land's through the road's slot of the ground shader (no shader change: a chunk with a road crossing it keeps its road and takes only the arena's colour), its colour and bands and rays of dark and light baked into the terrain's vertices; the pad that levels it widened from the ring + 3 m to + 8 (and its blend from 12 to 14) so what stands in the court before the fog stands on level ground. (2) *Pieces*: a baked model (below), a built one (`render/setShapes*.ts`: glyphs glowing in the floor in an anomaly colour, totems, skull poles, horned trees, pylons, sphinxes, trilithons, the stepped mound, R'lyeh's leaning slabs, hexagonal pedestals, the idol of Bokrug, the pipers' pipes, banners, the fallen star) or a world prop, as a prop of the new kind `set` through the same prop job (so they are tinted by the region, shadowed, lit and merged as everything else is). Where each stands is in the arena's own frame: degrees from its *front* (toward its realm's nearest Elder Sign, the way a person comes and where its candle burns: `world/arenaFront.ts`, which the fog's gate plan now takes too) and shares of its radius, so a composition holds at any size, and what is inside the fog (to 1.0 of the radius) is seen from within and what is beyond it (from 1.2) from the way in. Three rules, enforced in `world/arenaSet.ts` and tested (`tests/arenaSets.test.ts`), keep a fight and a way in: nothing solid within the fight's circle (0.78 of the radius) but a piece marked a heart (and none within 3 m of where a horror is raised), nothing solid across the lane the way in runs down, and no two solid pieces within 2.5 m unless both are joined (a wall's, a colonnade's), so none of the audits' flood fills is shut. (3) *Air* (`render/arenaAir.ts`): ash, embers, spores, motes, mist, spray, dust or flakes, born about the investigator within the nearest arena that has any. (4) *Models, from outside the game*: the user allowed royalty-free assets, and CLAUDE.md said none are used, so the exception is recorded there: seventy-odd pieces of Kenney's Graveyard, Nature, Castle, Pirate, Survival and Fantasy Town kits (CC0; `src/data/models/CREDITS.md`) are baked by `tools/models/bake_models.py` into data, not shipped as files: points welded and held as 16-bit values within each model's box, each face flattened to the colour of the colour map it stands on and sorted into stone, wood or leaf, the metres it stands fitted per model (the kits were drawn to no common scale), about 300 kB, read once as the game loads (`loadModels`, a module of its own so the first script does not carry it) and drawn flat-shaded with the region's own tints laid over the light and dark of each face, not Kenney's cartoon colours (the realm's grade would take the hue anyway); `tests/arenaSets.test.ts` keeps every baked model stood by some arena, so none rides along unused. What was not taken: textures (the game's own grounds do the work) and animation (what moves is what the particles and the lights do, and the glyphs' glow). The three anomaly colours are the glows' only vivid hues (the rest of the picture is the realm's grade), so a glyph is magenta, purple or void green by what it means, as the passages' are.
- **Every horror that fights has an entrance of its own, round 61 (asked: "ensure that all bosses currently lacking a dedicated entrance sequence or animation manifest in a manner consistent with the established lore, utilizing visually compelling and thematically fitting effects; revise the cutscenes accordingly, only if really needed"):** of the fifty-odd horrors the world raises, twenty fought from a script (or, the Shoggoth and the Polyp, by their boss variant) and arrived by their size alone, the same three shots at a different distance. Each now has an arrival of its own in the same data (`data/cutscenesRest.ts`: the waking world's eleven, `data/cutscenesDeep.ts`: the realms' nine), found by `arrivalOf` after the seventeen and the ten before them: Dr. Muñoz in the cold of his room and the hum of its pump (a pale-frost fade, frost settling about him), Charles le Sorcier in his fumes (gold, smoke and embers, a low bell), the Unnamable as the old beams of its attic creak and the dark takes the picture twice, the thing at Martin's Beach as the tide goes out too far (brine and bubbles, the sea's deep call), Ephraim Waite as the mind changes eyes (two violet flickers), Simon Orne in three cuts for his three names, Hutchinson gathered out of his grey salts by a bolt (ash drawn up, a white flash), the Shunned House's mould taking a man's shape in a sickly-green fade and its spores, the Whisperer's buzzing and the stars it offers, the Shoggoth's *Tekeli-li!* down the tunnel and eyes opening in its dark, the Flying Polyp as a wind where there is none and the lamps going out; Nug as the Mound breathes (red embers, smoke, the ground quaking) and Yeb as the answer from the far side of it, Bokrug as the lake of Ib goes still (a green fade, bubbles, spores), the Other Gods as the cloud on Hatheg-Kla dances (violet, stars and runes, thunder), the Great Ones as the carved faces of Kadath turn in a dawn of gold, Hypnos as a long slow blink with the sleepers' eyes opening about him, Zkauba's borrowed body flickering between a man and Yaddith, the Pipers' thin piping made visible as notes, the Ancient Ones' open Gate and nothing coming through it. Words: those whose horror speaks (`data/speechLines.ts`) carry none of the scene's own, the director laying theirs over it; the silent have two lines to read, each from their story. What this needed that did not exist: two fades (a sickly green, a pale frost: `Tint`) and seven bursts (`BurstKind`: frost, brine, spores, smoke, runes, notes, eyes: particles the world already draws, with the shapes round 51 made: `render/cinemaBursts.ts`). Left as they were, on purpose: Brown Jenkin (who joins another's fight), the Dunwich Horror (heard before it is seen: it is not drawn), the Thing in Curwen's Pits (a lesser thing in a pit), and the Voice in the Tomb (a voice, with no fight), and the falls, which were not asked for. `tests/cinemaRest.test.ts` keeps the lot to the rules the ten before it keep (lengths, sounds that exist, a name with time to be read, the screen let clear, a finite lens at any size of horror, captions only where the horror is silent) and that no two entrances share their effects.
- **The arenas do not stand in a cutscene's way, round 61 (asked: "make sure nothing newly implemented is blocking the view of the camera in the boss cutscenes", then "by blocking I mean a significant portion of the screen and/or boss sprite"):** every open arena's arrival and fall were played in the browser from the way in and looked at shot by shot. The lens is held clear of anything solid between it and the head of whoever it circles (`render/cinema.ts` `clear`), so a solid piece never stands between the lens and its subject; what could still cover the picture is what has no collider or is born near it: the trilithons (a posts-and-lintel of stone, drawn tall at the rim) were walked-through pieces, so a lens could be set among their posts: they are one collider across their breadth now; the arena's air (mist, spores, ash) could be born a metre from the lens: none is born within 7 m of it (`render/arenaAir.ts`); and the sabbat's fire stood on the Black Man's feet, its flame across his lower body: it is at his back, where it lights him from behind.
- **An arena's focal pieces and lights are placed first, and a lane's avenue stands clear of the way by its own breadth, round 61 (found by counting what each composition asked for against what the rules left standing):** before this, twenty-odd of the set's own named pieces were silently never placed in some arena: Yog-Sothoth's twelve hexagonal pedestals and all of every lane's broad pieces (the pipers' pipes, R'lyeh's slabs, the rocks and urns and fences of an avenue: a lane's `side` was a middle's distance, and a piece broader than the way's 2.6 m half width less that was inside it, so the lane rule dropped it), the throne of Azathoth (beaten by the pipes ringed before it), the stools of the sabbat (by the monoliths and the skull poles at the next ring), the Colour's meteors (inside the fight's circle), Orne's urns, Medusa's and Hutchinson's gates, the obelisks at Carcosa and the manor. Now a put that holds the heart or lights the ground is placed before the dressing, whatever order the set names it in (`byPriority`; ties as named); a lane's piece is set no nearer the way's middle than the way's half width, a half metre and its own breadth (`flank`), so `side` is a least; and the compositions that still lost a focal piece were moved (the sabbat's stools before its monoliths, the pedestals outward, the urns to the rim, the gates wider). Dressing that a ring or a scatter loses to its neighbours (some of a tree line, a fifth of the skull poles) is left lost: that is the 2.5 m rule doing what it is for. `tests/arenaSets.test.ts` now asserts a heart's piece and a lit one stand in every arena that names them, and a lane's broad ones are not lost.
- **Every sound level defaults to full, round 61 (asked, with a picture of Settings › Sound at 100% on all five sliders: "I changed my mind, I want the default sound settings to be like this"):** volume, music, effects, ambience and voices are 1 (round 60 had 0.5, 0.5, 1, 0.45 and 0.55, and before it 0.7 and four at 1). The sliders are linear gains on the mix that data/sounds.ts, the sets' gains and `CLASS_GAIN` fix, so this is a louder game by default (the master multiplies each class's slider: against round 60's defaults the effects are 6 dB louder, the voices 11.2, the music 12, the ambience 13) with the balance among the classes that of the sliders' ends, which is the balance before round 60 that a player found too forward in the music, the ambience and the voices, not round 60's choice of them; a player who wants round 60's mix has only to set the sliders to it (0.5, 0.5, 1, 0.45, 0.55). Settings kept at a default of either earlier time are moved to the new one once (version 19: the round 59 levels (version 18's migration) and round 60's), a level a person set is kept; the master's compressor and the sounds' own levels (`tests/soundMix.test.ts`) were not touched, the test of which measures at its own levels, not the settings'.
- **The candle before a fog is set aside the way, never on it, round 62 (asked: "visually understated and contextually appropriate, in a fitting place near the intended area rather than centrally on the stairs, while maintaining proximity to its functional placement"):** the stake where the fallen wake is where it was (`gatePlan` `stake`, at the way in; the rise and the rest of `candles.ts` unchanged: a fall within 100 m of a lit one still offers waking there), but the flame itself stands on something and beside the way: before an open arena a few paces to the way's side (`world/candlePlan.ts` `ringCandle`: 3.4 m aside, clear of the set's solid pieces and the ring's stones by 1.2 m, never more than 4.6 m from the stake, so that passing the stake still takes its flame), in a dungeon hall's approach at the foot of a wall or a pillar (`hallCandle`: scored for a gap of about half a metre from a solid part, as far aside as the span allows, a span too narrow for it keeps the least step to one side). What it stands on is the place's own (`CandleLook` in each arena's set, `data/arenaSets*.ts`): a cairn of stones, a skull, a gravestone's foot, a piece of driftwood, a votive in a dish (`render/candleShapes.ts`), and the flame and its halo are small (a candle, not a beacon) and a little the brighter while lit so that it is still found from the way in. `tests/candles.test.ts` asserts the aside is within reach of the stake, clear of the set's pieces and valid by look.
- **A way to each open arena, round 62 (asked: "a distinct, thematic discovery process featuring cohesive pathways, unique visual identities, and original puzzles or hazards tailored to each encounter, rather than placing them arbitrarily"):** the nineteen open arenas were rings set down on open land with nothing leading to them. Each now has a `Way` (`data/arenaWays*.ts`; `world/arenaWay.ts` lays it): a trail from the arena's fog, straight down its way in for 26 m (where its candle burns and the set's lane runs), then wandering to the nearest road of the region (never shorter than 120 m, bowed when the road is near), 2.6 m wide and drawn as a road of its own in the region's road texture; a trailhead where it leaves the road, named when found (`WayFound`: the great words, a line under them, `data/arenaWays*.ts` `name` and `line`); waymarks placed by distance from the fog and the side walked (`mark`: the set's own pieces, growing thicker and stranger toward the fog, kept clear of the trail by half its width and their own reach, and of the other ways); omens said as places are passed (`WayOmen`, with a sound and a tremor); and, for each, a hazard of the horror's own kind (`systems/ways.ts`: a plate that wakes and bursts a moment after, a vent in its round, a wave down a stretch of the trail, ground that wears at the mind or the breath, a field of plates of which one in each row is safe, the one that bears the rune on the tablet at its head) and for some a rite (lights to be lit in the order the place tells; the wrong one puts them all out with a flare of the way's own kind; the right order is paid in Echoes and sometimes a point of Insight, and may still the hazards for good). The hazards are all outside the ring and cost what a blow of the way's size costs (`blasts.ts`, the same as the sky's strikes, which now use it): none is in the fight, and each warns first (a ring that thickens, a glow, a crackle of its own matter) so a step aside or a roll clears it. A rite and the hazards are optional: the way is walkable whole without either, and a rite that is done is kept in the save (`ow.told`); nothing else of the way is saved. Everything draws from the game's own random stream and frame count, so a replay sees the same ways. **Left as is:** the trail takes its region's road texture (a beach's way is cobbled where the region's roads are), where a ground of its own for each way would need a second road slot in the terrain's shader.
- **A boss hall has a set of its own, round 62 (asked: "enhance and maximize the visual fidelity and design of all boss arenas"):** the open arenas had their sets in round 61 and the dungeons' twenty-seven boss halls a heart, braziers in two corners and two rows of one prop each (`data/arenaStyles.ts`). Each hall now has a set in the same terms as an open arena's (`data/hallSets*.ts`, `world/hallSet.ts`: the arenas' own `arenaSet`, in the hall's frame, 0° being its way in), drawn from its own story (the Witch House's garret of chests, a pallet and a bowl; the sunken temple's hulls and idol heads; the onyx castle's columns; Akeley's study of Mi-Go cylinders…); it stands in place of the style's two rows, and keeps the heart and braziers. A piece is kept off the walls and buttresses, the pillars and the doorways (a pace of floor before each: 2.8 m either side, 8 m in), the room's own Elder Sign, rest, gate, tome and cache (4.2 m from the sign and the gate, 2.2 m from the rest), and the style's dressing; none stands in the fight's circle (0.78 of the hall's half breadth) and none is taller than the roof allows (`tests/hallSets.test.ts`). The curwen pit is not a hall and keeps its style. No air in the halls: an arena's drifts are put away indoors (`render/arenaAir.ts`).
- **Four faults a review found in the round 60–62 work, mended, round 62:** (1) a horror that has fallen is no longer "unroused": its death ends its fight (`engaged` false), which made it unseen on the killing blow and left its fall cutscene looking at an empty arena; `isUnroused` is now false for a boss with no health left. (2) A load reads the save after the world is made, with the body at the start sign and the mind not yet its own, so a bridge the investigator had been standing on stayed unformed, its gap a fall, and the footing check sent them back to their checkpoint: `openAround` (`systems/passages.ts`) opens every shown passage within its waking distance of the saved position (as it was when they left it: no animation, they had seen it open) before `applySave` judges the footing. (3) The map's right-click marker also ran the primary-button pointer handlers (`preventDefault` on `contextmenu` does not cancel pointer events), so right-clicking a chosen Elder Sign travelled: those handlers now take the primary button only. (4) `render/wayViews.ts` made a way's plates, tablet and lights anew each time it came within range and only took them out of the scene when it left: it now disposes their geometry and the materials made for that run (the stone, body and flame materials are the views' own, kept). `tests/bossArrival.test.ts`, `tests/passages.test.ts` and `tests/wayViews.test.ts` hold the first, second and fourth; the third is in a screen with no test harness (no DOM in the suite) and was mended by reading.
- **Round 63: a house about each open horror, and a discovery to open its gate.** Asked (the round 62 answer was read as roads): "a distinct, lore-accurate discovery experience for each encounter", not literal pathways; "based solely on the screenshots, players could bypass the designated route and enter the boss fog from an alternate angle, rendering the current road design ineffective"; and "expansive, visually striking, immersive structures that house the boss arenas".
