So the trails of round 62 are no longer roads and nothing leads to a fog that a body can leave a trail to reach from the side. **The house** (`data/arenaHouses*.ts`, `world/arenaHouse.ts`, `render/setShapesHouse.ts`, `setShapesTower.ts`): each of the nineteen open arenas is walled about at `HOUSE.reach` (18 m) past its ring, in the stuff of its own story (the Colour's reservoir dam, the Black Man's ravine cliff, the black grove's living palisade and horned towers, the Gorgon's colonnade, the sea wall with its lit round towers, the burying ground's low wall, the Pawtuxet manor's brick with a rod on its gatehouse, the Temenos of the sphinx, a serpent's earthwork, a black ziggurat's terraces, the quay of Ib, Hatheg-Kla's ring of slabs, R'lyeh's leaning slabs 22 m high, Carcosa's bruise-coloured wall, Yog-Sothoth's crystal, the Court's dark crystal, Yaddith's terraces, the organ's black pipes, the pale hexagons), `wall` and `tower` kinds each drawn by a kit that fits inside the collider box it declares, one continuous ring of overlapping pieces (each wall's collider reaches the next; the gate's two towers lap over its opening by `HOUSE.lap` so no crack is left where a round foot meets a straight wall) with towers at intervals and either side of one gate. The wall's pieces are ordinary `set` props (merged per chunk and material like the rest of an arena's set, so a ring of seventy pieces costs no draw calls beyond the kits'). **The gate** is a hidden piece (`HiddenPieceDef`) that already existed for secret doors, with a new `minFound` condition: its seal collider stands until `found:<horror>` is told, and then the door draws aside as the investigator comes within the passage's waking distance (`passages.ts`: so the gate opens as it is come to, over 96 frames, and a gate given and left alone stays shut and is not a wall of air). A house's gate is the only way in: `tests/arenaHouse.test.ts` walks the ring and finds no gap but the gate's opening, `tests/houseWalk.test.ts` (an audit: it walks a body at every house from eight sides, the shut gate included, and then through a given gate). To make the gate an opening in a straight wall (the hidden pieces' boxes are axis-aligned) `frontOf` now snaps an arena's front to the nearest axis, whichever way its realm's nearest Elder Sign lies; the court's things (the set's dressing, the stake, the candle) are kept within the wall (`withinHouse`, `HOUSE.margin`), the pad levels `HOUSE.level` past it, and a site's streamed footprint covers the house, not the ring's `radius + 12`. **The court** (`world/arenaWay.ts`): the trail's marks, omens, hazards and rites of round 62 are kept, but where they stood down a wandering road they now stand in a straight court out from the gate (`WAY.length`, 150 m, `courtPoint`: metres out and metres to the left), the trail's road, trailhead (`Way.name`, `line`, `head`) and `WayFound` are gone, and the court is laid as no road (the trail's `trailRoad` is not pushed into the region's roads). **The discovery** (`data/discoveries*.ts`, `systems/discovery.ts`, `world/discoveryPlan.ts`): each house's gate opens when its two or three acts are done, in order, told once under `disc:<horror>:<n>` in `ow.told` (so already in the save, no version change): a page *read* (new documents set in the court, `documentsHouses.ts` and `documentsBeyond.ts`, twenty-one in all, each telling what happened there as someone who was there would have set it down), the way's *rite* of lights where it has one (nine do), a *vigil* kept at a mark in the court for twenty-odd to forty seconds in the hour (`gloaming`, `deep`, `waning`), the sky (`rain`, `storm`, `gale`, `motes`: only those the realm has), the state of the mind (`lucid`…`unmoored`) or the dark (no light of lamp or fire at the investigator: `Game.lit`) that the story asks, ebbing at twice the rate it grows when unkept, or an *offering* at an altar with E: a dose of the Laudanum, Echoes, a measure of the mind (`loseSanity`), a measure of the blood (never the last of it; a refused gift says why and takes nothing). Every discovery has its own: the Colour is waited for in the dark in the deep of the night after four lanterns of the Gardners are lit; the Black Man's book is signed with the mind; the black grove is heard in a gale and paid in blood; the Gorgon's porch is waited at dusk with a steady head; the sea wall in rain and storm and a coin of 200 Echoes; Simon Orne's grave in the hour before the dawn, as his sexton did; the Pawtuxet rod under a storm; the sphinx's basin with a dose of the Laudanum, which is how the narrator of *Under the Pyramids* was brought down; Yig's mound with blood on a flat stone; Nug's three fires and a measure of blood on the red stair; Ib's four green fires and the last hour; Hatheg-Kla's motes; R'lyeh's four lamps and a toll of 400 Echoes; Carcosa's second act read, with a mind already loosed, in the last hour; the Threshold's four lights and a measure of mind; the Court's piping, heard dark and unmoored; Zkauba's tithe of 500 Echoes; the Organ's four lights, and a steady head while the great note sounds (the piping wears at the mind, so the vigil is kept against it); the prisms received only by a lucid mind, and a dose laid in the empty place. The acts are a quest in the journal (`data/discoveriesQuests.ts` makes `discover_<horror>` from them, the horror's own fall the last stage, lenient: a horror put down before its house was sought closes it, and opens the gate: `refreshSeals` tells `found:` of every slain horror), begun when the house is first seen (46 m from its wall: `HouseSeen`, the great words of its name and a line, replacing `WayFound`), followed on the map and minimap until done (the lead names the vigil's mark, the altar, the court of the rite, the page, and last the arena), and the gate says what it waits for when it is come to shut (`sealedHere`). `found:*` goes with the investigator into a second journey (`Carry.found`): the way in is known, the horror is not yet. **Deliberately not done:** no marks on the full map beyond the quest's lead (a house is not a named place; nothing counts it); the vigil's progress is not drawn as a ring but said (three lines: begun, half, kept) and heard (`VigilKept` cues); a vigil's progress is not saved (it ebbs and begins again). **Known limits:** a vigil that asks the sky waits on the weather, which is the game's own random stream (a few minutes' clear sky between spells in a region that has any; Dreamlands' motes are its only weather, and the one vigil there that asks for them waits on the first spell); a house's wall is drawn whole whenever its chunks are loaded (a ring of fifty to ninety pieces: 14–45 thousand triangles by kit, tested under 60,000 in `tests/arenaHouse.test.ts`; wall blocks are cut into cells no finer than 1.8 m and six across), so standing in an arena and looking out sees the wall: draw calls at the four arenas measured are within a few of before, but their triangle counts are higher (the Reservoir's heart 404k → 686k, R'lyeh's 147k → 802k before the kits were thinned, which brought a house's slabs from 348k to 45k; not measured again after). **Placement.** The gate faces the realm's nearest Elder Sign, as the arena did, unless there is no land of the realm for its court to lie on (`courtLand`, 54 m out, a body wide; five of the nineteen, the Gorgon's, Simon Orne's, Hutchinson's, the Colossus's and Nug's, stand near the edge of their realm and have their gate turned to the nearest way that has land: the candle before their fog, which faces the same way, no longer looks toward the sign); the pages, marks and altars of the court are resolved once at layout (`planCourt`, `ArenaPlace.court`) and nudged along the court and across it clear of whatever already stands (a sign's shrine: R'lyeh's gate faces one a few paces off), and the region's props and marks are cleared about them. **The gate** is a door from the first (`passageViews.ts`: a gate with `minFound` always shows its frame and the outline of its slab, the runes dim until its acts are answered and brighter with each, then the passage's own opening); a vigil's mark or an altar come to before its turn says so once in thirty seconds ("Nothing stirs here yet"). The cutscene of Cthulhu's arrival was looked at inside its house and sees nothing of the wall.
- **Round 64: two gaps round 63 left, closed (asked: "also do 1.-3.", the third being the PR's own "not done" list).** (1) A Field Guide hint for a shut gate: `hint.gate` (en, de, fr, es), filed under The horrors as *Shut gates*, raised by `ui/hintWatch.ts` when a hidden piece with `minFound` that is not yet shown stands within `FOUND.gate` (14 m), so it follows whatever kind of enclosure holds the gate, not the ring of wall; it says what the acts are in general words (a page, a vigil, a gift) because the quest and the notice at the gate already name the horror's own. (2) A vigil drawn: `vigilsNear` (systems/discovery.ts) gives the marks of vigils whose turn has come within reach, with their share and whether `holds` is true now; `render/vigilMarks.ts` lays a ring of `FOUND.vigil` on them through a new `GroundFx.steady` drawer (the decal batch is filled once a frame inside the telegraphs' begin/end, so a mark that lasts as long as a state does has to be laid every frame), cold (a way's waiting light) while the vigil's hour, sky or mind is not here and warm while it is, so the ring also says whether the conditions hold, which before only the vigil's not growing said; inside it the `sigil` decal fills with the share. Tests: hintWatch (shut and found), discovery (no ring before its turn, the ring at the mark with its share and `holds`, none when walked away).
- **The frost drains the breath for real (review of PR 152, `systems/ways.ts` `patches`):** the Other Gods' frost took 28 stamina a second by subtracting from the bar, which set no recovery delay, so `vitalsSystem`'s ordinary regen (32 a second at the least) refilled it the same frame and a full bar stood in the frost untouched. It now goes through `stamina.spend`, which holds recovery off for `STAMINA.regenDelay` (48 frames) after every frame it drains, so the bar wears at the stated rate while one stands in it and begins to mend three-quarters of a second after leaving, as after any other spending. `tests/ways.test.ts` stands a body in the frost and beside it with a full bar and reads the least the bar reaches.
- **Round 65: a vigil is kept by standing at its mark, and the place answers (asked, of the plan for a place for every boss: "decide each question yourself, just make sure the game will be highly enjoyable and entertaining, without any of the new puzzles, discovery processes etc. being too hard and frustrating for any kind of player", and "also make sure everything you decide is lore accurate and visually and mechanically entertaining and impressive"):** round 63's vigils asked an hour of the night, a sky or a state of mind, and a player could wait most of an hour for the clock or the weather's random spells to oblige (the night is 1,080 s, the longest calm between spells 840 s). A vigil's place now calls what it asks (`systems/vigilCall.ts`): after `CALL.after` (3) seconds at the mark with the condition not holding, the night runs on at 90 s a second to the hour asked (the moon sinks, windows go out, the sky greys: every reader of the clock follows through `clock.ts` `NIGHT.hurried`, kept in `Overworld.hurried`, not in a save, and the window shader's `uHurried`), the sky gathers the weather asked (the first the realm has; `weather.want`), and the mind is drawn toward the middle of the state asked at 4 points a second and held there while the vigil lasts. Each is said once with a word and a tremor. The dark is not called (a lamp is a lamp; the marks that ask it stand where no lamp reaches). The vigil's own seconds still have to be kept, so nothing is skipped but the waiting. Also: a mark or altar come to before its turn says what is asked first (a page, the lights, a vigil, a gift) and not only that something is; after a wrong light of a rite the one whose turn it is beckons (`WayRun.missed`, `wayViews.ts`). `tests/vigilCall.test.ts` keeps every vigil of every discovery from the worst hour, sky and mind. The plan for the boss places (`docs/handoff/MERGED-PLAN.md`) is decided on the same rule: the thirteen answers are in it under *The answers*.
- **Round 66: what stands about an open arena is an `Enclosure`, and the ring is one kind of it (asked, of the plan for a place for every boss: "do ALL the handoff work"; no change to the game).** The plan's first round is the seam that lets a horror's place be any outline instead of a ring of wall. `world/enclosure.ts` says what the world asks of it: a gate and a front, the box that holds it, its wall's pieces and its gate's piece, where the fallen wake (`stake`), `gap(p)` (metres to its wall, negative within: the ring's is `hypot − radius`), and `pad(level, blend)` (the level ground it asks of the land). `planHouse` returns the ring kind; `ArenaPlace.house` is an `Enclosure` and nothing but a ring's own tests reads `.radius` any more (`tests/worldHelpers.ts` `ringOf`). Everything that was a circle about the centre against the ring's radius asks the enclosure: `discovery.ts` (when a house is seen and its court is near: `gap`), `gatePlan.ts` (the stake, which no longer assumes the wall stands `HOUSE.reach` out), `candlePlan.ts` (a candle must stand inside the wall: `ASIDE.wall`), `arenaPlace.ts` (the set kept within: `within`). A pad is a circle, a rectangle or a shape of any outline (`world/sitePads.ts`: `padDistance`, `padBounds`, `polygonFootprint`), asked its distance in one place (terrain, planSpawns, regionPlan, groundCover and the tests had four copies of the circle-or-rect switch), and `world/occupancy.ts` (moved out of `regionPlan.ts`, 322 lines) can take a shape so the fill keeps off a footprint that is not round. `arenaPlace` now lays its own pad (`placeArena(…, pad)`), after planning the enclosure and before anything else is stood on the ground, so the pad is the enclosure's and not a ring's `R + 25` fixed in `placements.ts`. **Proved neutral:** a fingerprint of the whole laid-out world (every arena, its house's props, gate and piece, every pad, piece, gate, tome, spawn and sign, every fog with its stake and candle, the colliders and pads of every chunk about each arena, every region's plan with its roads, fill, colliders, spawns, ways and points of interest, and the map's places) was taken on main before the change and is identical after it. **The proof of closure is now by flood** (`world/closure.ts`, `tests/enclosure.test.ts`): the ground about a wall is gridded at 0.2 m, a place is shut when a body of 0.35 m (a little smaller than the investigator's 0.4, so a leak theirs would miss is found) would be pushed out of a collider that reaches its height (`resolveCapsule`'s rule), and a flood from outside must reach nothing inside the wall while the gate is sealed and the stake before the fog while it is open; the nineteen houses pass, and a stretch of wall taken away is found. It replaces eight walks at the ring as the proof for any shape (`houseWalk` stays as the end-to-end check). **Left for the round that needs it, on purpose:** the *Strange places* setting (nothing reads it until the first Outer God's rule, and it costs a tab line in four languages); the render and system readers whose answer differs by kind (`worldScene`'s footprint, `validateWorld`'s stray check, `mountGround`, `arenaAir`, the map's outline, a candidate front's gate distance for `courtLand`), each a one-line change that cannot be tried until an enclosure that is not a ring exists, so they go in with the first one (Hutchinson's keep); the measured-budget script (`docs/handoff/PERF-arenas-main-vs-pr152.md` has the method) goes in with the pilots' gate, where it is the thing measured.
- **The ride is quieter in the mix, round 61 (asked: "lower and balance the audio levels of mount effects (horse and chariot) within the overall sound mix"):** measured offline, a gallop on road or stone summed to about −13 dBFS and the spectral chariot's to −11, against −20 to −22 for a whole boss fight, because round 50's one-shots (each as present as a landed blow) fire about two dozen a second with no fall-off. One `MOUNT_MIX.level` of 0.5 (−6 dB) now multiplies every sound a ride makes, the recorded horse's voice and the beds' bus included (a `level` option on the recipe, made by `gameAudio` after a recording's own clamp at 1: a cut made before it left a recording whose gain was 2 or more untouched, as a review found, and the harness shake and the greys' settle and toss did not come down at all); the wheel thump is half as frequent on road (3 to 5 m) and stone (2.2 to 3.6 m) and 0.85 not 1.15; `hoofSample` 3 to 2.2; the second skeleton horse 0.8 to 0.65; `bedSpectral` 0.28 to 0.16. A gallop on the grey now sums to about −21, the spectral chariot's to −19. The mount tests' windows moved down 6 dB (−28 to −17, asides −36), a walking hoof to be 4 dB over a footfall (8 before the level). Measured, not heard: a listen is owed.
- **Father Dagon and Mother Hydra are smaller, round 61 (asked: "reduce the scale of Dagon and Hydra to improve visual coherence during close-quarters combat"):** their one lever is `assembly.scale`, which sets the body's height, radius (0.3 of it), reach and hit radius of every blow (linear in it), the distance brackets, the hurt zones and the shadow, but the picture they are drawn with takes its size from the art manifest's own copy of it, so the two moved together: Dagon 16 to 11 m (radius 4.8 to 3.3) and Hydra 15 to 10.5 m (4.5 to 3.15), in `greatOldOnes.ts`, `source-index.json` (`sprite_scale`) and `index.json` (`scale`, with `sourceIndexSha256` made again, as the tools do). Both stay between the Dunwich Horror (9) and the Colossus beneath the Pyramids (14), and over 3 m of radius, so the bot still clears their arena as for the other colossi. `ZONES.height` went 12 to 10 so that they keep their hurt zones, the stoop's head, the revolver's weak spot and the presence of a colossus, with the Dunwich Horror still without; `dread.ts` read a literal 12 for who bellows from afar and now reads `ZONES.height`. The hall's centre monolith 2.4 to 1.6, so it does not stand over them. Their blows shrink with them (about 31%), so their bot seconds and lives are measured again in `docs/BALANCE.md`.
- **The bosses are brought into a band for their tier, round 61 (asked: "a comprehensive rebalancing ... enhance overall enjoyment while preserving the challenge expected of a soulslike. Ensure no boss is insurmountable or nearly impossible, yet avoid excessive ease. Propose an innovative, clever and cohesive strategy"; earlier: "some of the bigger bosses are way too hard because of HP amount"):** one principle, two dials. A fight is shaped by two numbers the balance bot measures, its *length* (seconds) and its *cost* (lives); health sets the first and a blow's damage the second, so each boss is moved by the one dial that is off and the other is left to hold. `tests/balance.test.ts` now holds each tier to a band of its own (a named horror or a lesser god, 7 to 40 seconds and at least 0.03 of a life; a god of 2500 health or more, 18 to 90 seconds and 0.35 to 1 life), `tests/bossBlows.test.ts` holds the heaviest blow between 12% and 28% of a person's health (a third before: four stood at 30%, which with an Unmoored mind and a costly relic is 43%). Too long: the gods' health, so the slog goes and the blows stay: Ghatanothoa 4850 to 3600, Shub-Niggurath 4300 to 3100, Cthulhu 4600 to 3700, Hastur 4200 to 3500, Nyarlathotep 4000 to 3400, Yog-Sothoth 4200 to 3600, Father Dagon 3250 to 2700, Mother Hydra 3100 to 2600, 'Umr at-Tawil 3600 to 3200 (the gods now take 24 to 72 bot-seconds, 0.4 to 0.9 lives, where Shub-Niggurath and Ghatanothoa ran twice their ring). Too heavy a blow: Nug 66 to 60, Rhan-Tegoth 70 to 63, the Colossus beneath the Pyramids 60 to 54, 'Umr's 80 to 70 (each heaviest blow now 26 to 27%). Too easy (a late horror over in 6 or 7 seconds for 0.02 of a life): the Thing beyond Erich Zann's Window 850/30 to 1300/40, Zkauba 750/32 to 1100/40, Hypnos 650 to 950, the Ancient Ones 1550 to 2200, the Daemon Pipers 1200/40 to 1500/54, the Whisperer's damage 32 to 36. Left alone on purpose: Azathoth (blind, unwounded: its fight is the piping) and the Haunter of the Dark (hurt only in lamplight), which the bot cannot measure, and the early horrors, whose opening bound (1.5 lives) is held. *Mercy* (each fall to a horror lightening its blows in the next, 6% a fall to four) was written as the guard for what a bot cannot see, and then taken out entirely on XDro's word ("remove the mercy system entirely", review of PR 153): nothing stands behind Azathoth and the Haunter of the Dark, whose health and blows are as they were before this round and are measured by no bot. Not changed, and why: `resist` and `weak` still only write the bestiary (making them count would move every row and every bot at once), Endurance and the cane's place among the weapons (the model cannot show a stamina that runs out), the levels' cost (ring 3 stands at 38 of the 40 foes `levelPace` allows).
- **Secrets on the map, round 61 (asked: "address map sparsity by introducing points of interest marked with a grayed-out ? until discovered ... scripted or dynamic events drawn from H.P. Lovecraft's original narratives and corresponding in-game locations; chests containing echoes; anomalous visual lovecraftian phenomena; and high-density areas featuring increased enemies and loot (e.g., churches, ritual sites, at shorelines/cliffs, mazes, ruins)"):** seventy places in `data/pois*.ts` (waking world, the realms beyond, the Dreamlands), each a place a story of Lovecraft's names (the Hop Yard's wizard, Gilman's house, Zadok's slip, Johansen's landing, the pit of the Great Race, Barzai's ledge, the shore of Ib...), set where the land already has its like: the nth graveyard, circle, ruin, grove, outcrop or landmark of the region's plan, or a shore, cliff brow or clearing found by search near a point (the land runs to a region's edge and the coast falls away past it, so a shore stands within 22 m of the sea, inset 4 m from the border), and two mazes (seven passages each way, a recursive backtracker's walls as props, the mouth in the south wall, the chest at the heart). Five kinds: a tale (found for a little insight), a hoard (a chest of Echoes), a rite (guarded, with a chest), an anomaly (a phenomenon and a little insight) and a den (a thick guard and a chest). Planned last in `buildPlan` (after the courts of round 62's open arenas, and kept off their trails and every site's ground by `open` in `regionPlan.ts`), on a stream of its own per place (`hash(id) ^ seed`), so no earlier prop, post or id moves, and no save breaks: its guard are ordinary spawn points (`poi:<id>:<k>`) that respawn and are rested and saved as any foe's are, its walls ordinary props; the first hour's rule is kept (near the opening only the gentle foes). Found by coming within `clamp(r + 6, 18, 38)` m, once, kept in `told` (`poi:<id>`: no save format change); its name and line show under a banner that holds six seconds to be read (`secret` tone; the named place found a few metres on waits for it, or is not shown again if it has the same name), a place that speaks twice says its second line on coming back (a `PoiAgain` event shown in the same wrapping banner, not within 100 s), and it is felt while within it (dread −0.15 to −0.55 sanity a second, counted in `drain` with the foes' auras through `systems/placeAura.ts` so that it presses on the mind and stops its mending as they do, and held off by a swallow of Laudanum; or, in three places of the Dreamlands (the cats' circle, the Onyx stair, Kuranes' hollow), comfort that mends). The chest is set down by the system as one nears (within 110 m), not placed among `w.tomes` (which `levels.test` constrains by dungeon), taken for good through `read` as `Hoard: <id>`; none is in `ow.places`, so the count of named places and the searcher are untouched. The map draws a grey "?" where ground within 140 m has been charted and the place is not yet found (`systems/pois.ts` `poiMark`: hidden, rumour, found, spent), a mark of its kind in bone once found (ring tale, square hoard, triangle rite, star anomaly, cross den; dim once its chest is taken): since round 60's map was made clear, a `secret` among the map's own marks (`ui/mapMarks.ts` gathers it with a hover card, `ui/mapIconsSecret.ts` draws it, the key shows it), named on the full map when zoomed in, shaken by a failing mind's slide like the rest; the demo's edge hides what lies beyond its realms. Phenomena (`render/poiFx.ts`: a column, a ring of dashes turning on the ground, motes, a hung sheet) are drawn from 260 m in the anomaly hues (bone for the pale ones) and nothing else saturated. Chosen over a quest chain or a procedural placement: the world is hand-authored, a place that is a story's wants its words, and a plan that is a pure function of the region keeps the map, the walks and the saves honest.
- **More foes where the land asks for them, round 61 (asked: "increase enemy density in regions where additional challenge is warranted"):** `spawns.density` raised only where the standing a person arrives with is highest and the land was least peopled for it: Dunwich 0.8→1.1, Vermont 1.1→1.4, the Mountains 1.2→1.9, Pnakotus 1.2→1.7, K'n-yan 1.3→1.8, the Dreamlands 1.2→1.9, R'lyeh 1.3→1.9, Yuggoth 1.1→1.8 and the Beyond 1.0→1.8; the university, Arkham, Innsmouth and Providence stay (the demo's realms, and the first hours). The scatter is the last loop of `planSpawns`, so the ordinary posts keep their ids (`p:<region>:<n>` runs on). With the secrets' guards (230) this is about 400 more foes in all: 1042 spawn points in the plans before, 1448 after (176 ordinary posts from the density, 230 guards), and between 38% and 56% more in the outer realms. `WORLD.maxActive` (60) still binds, so it is more near each other, not more at once; but a horror takes a place in a full house (`population.ts`: the lesser foe farthest from the investigator makes room, so the total is still never over 60; a review found the full house had kept a few horrors from their arenas, the flying polyp's among them, when a player came to them without killing anything). The balance model credits a person with exactly the foes each region had before this round (`CREDITED` in `tests/balanceModel.ts`: the scatter posts there were, `p:<region>:0..n`, which are unchanged in id, foe and place, and the rest the region had; the secrets' guards left out), so a denser world is a margin over the standing the bots fight at, and `tests/levelPace.test.ts` (ring 3: 38 foes a level of its 40) and the standings in `docs/BALANCE.md` are the ones the world had before it.
- **Combat and audio cleanup (2026-10-22):** caster attacks use recorded release and impact sounds directly, without procedural gathers, beam/vortex/gaze loops, duplicate caster stingers, or synth fallback while samples load. Critical bonus damage falls from 75% to 15% on ordinary foes and from 25% to 10% on bosses; backstabs require a heavy attack within the rear 40 degrees. Anatomical weakspot multipliers, head auto-aim and weakspot guidance are removed; multipart hitboxes still follow the creature art and stoop. Dunwich and Cthulhu no longer stop at 1 HP, so weapons can finish both; their incantation and ship remain optional finishes. Azathoth retains its timed survival encounter. Regression checks cover every damage-based scripted boss form, death cleanup and saved kills.
- **Arena decoration cleanup (2026-10-22, clarified by the user):** removed decorative neon floor inlays from all boss arena sets and the Ancient Ones approach, and deleted their unused geometry/kit support. Markings and lights on arena props stay exactly as authored; combat warnings, puzzle clues and checkpoint symbols stay functional.
- **Boss level scaling (2026-10-22, clarified to bosses only):** hostile scripted bosses gain 0.75% base health and 0.3% damage per purchased Vigour, Endurance or Might level, multiplicatively with NG+ and the chosen difficulty. Level 1 uses the authored base stats. Existing living bosses preserve their remaining-health fraction on level-up; spawn, morph and loaded games use the same curve. Ordinary enemies, summoned adds, structures and allies retain their existing stats, and boss move timings and special encounter rules remain. The first 2%/1% curve cost the balance bot up to 3.91 carried-healing lives; the final curve costs at most 1.48 and keeps the existing fight-time bands. The audit's god ceiling is 1.5 carried-healing lives; its positive-pressure floor for named bosses is 0.01, allowing a changed bot trajectory while still requiring damage. Invariant bots now use legal capped Might and reinforcement and refresh both player and boss stats together; the Yog-Sothoth audit has four minutes to finish.
- **Round 68, the camera is free while someone talks (asked: "in dialogues the player should be able to move around the camera freely"):** a talk screen held the camera on the speaker (`Player.listening`, `talkFocus`) and `main.ts` fed the game an empty input while it was open. The look axes now pass through (`main.ts`) and, on the first turn of the hand, `Player.glance` lets go of the speaker; the camera stays where it was put, the talk is kept (only a step, a button or a fall ends it, as before) and `npcs.talk` clears `glance` for the next one. Mouse deltas already reached the game because the talk screen keeps the pointer.
- **Round 68, the Dunwich Horror could not be finished, and the fights that hide their rule now say it (asked: "resolve the issue preventing the defeat of the Dunwich Horror boss; apply the same troubleshooting to all other affected encounters"):** the Horror's last hit point is held (`floor`), and the only way to take it is the incantation, a 180-frame `chant` that any blow broke off, so a chanter in the open world, where the Horror still fights, never reached its end. `chant` now has i-frames across its whole length (the precedent is Cthulhu's `helm`), and the powder shows the Horror for 1200 frames, not 900, which is about the time a chant and a step toward it take. The three fights whose rule was in the field guide alone (Dunwich, Cthulhu's Alert, the Haunter's lamplight) now say it under the boss bar (`Signature.status`) and, as a phase begins, on the screen (`Signature.note`, `bossHud`). Azathoth and the others were walked by the bots with no such hold; they need none. (After main's #158 let weapons kill it and Cthulhu, the floor is gone; the chant keeps its i-frames, so the incantation is a safe second way, and the lines under the bar now say "chant the incantation, or strike it down" and "wound it until the Alert comes".)
- **Round 68, a blow does not pass a wall (asked: "the player shouldn't be able to attack through walls, and the enemies neither. Except some boss attacks, of course"):** `meleeSystem` tested only the gap between a blow's capsule and the body's; `systems/walled.ts` now draws a line at the height of the blow from the swinger to the near side of the target's body and drops the blow if a collider crosses it (a pillar, a closed door, a wall; a sill or a low fence under the line does not stop it, and an open door is off). The check is made again each frame of the window, so a blow that was stopped can still land from where the swinger then stands. A horror's own blows are not tested: its sweeps, quakes, marks and bursts are the attacks that pass walls, and a horror fights in its own arena. Shots, bolts and swept beams already stopped at walls (`projectiles.ts`, `strikes.ts`).
- **Round 68, the map's figures are drawn again, larger (asked: "enhance the visual design of all map icons, increasing their default scale"):** `UNIT` in `mapPainter.ts` is the one size every figure is drawn at, raised from 7 to 10 pixels on the full map and from 5.2 to 7.4 on the minimap (the beacons on the minimap read it too), and `mapIconKit`/`mapIcons`/`mapIconsLive`/`mapIconsSecret` draw each kind as a figure of its own (an Elder Sign, a gate, a door with an eye for a horror met within, a boss's lair, a person, a place, the secrets as a "?" until found and then a figure of their kind) on the dark token they share, with the minimap and the full map reading the same table. Nothing is added to the map and nothing is hidden by the size: marks still thin out by zoom (`DETAIL`).
- **Round 68, the soulslike balance (asked: "restore appropriate difficulty ... even on normal ... maximum potion capacity, boss health, damage output, and related mechanics"):** the Reagent is the potion: ten doses at most (the start's four and six vials, it was fourteen: six and a third bars of health in the pocket) of 40% each (it was 45%), and a Silver Vial found past the most is sold for 600 Echoes (`REAGENT.surplus`) instead of lost. Boss damage is held so no horror's heaviest blow takes more than 30% of the health a person has where they meet it (`tests/bossBlows.test.ts`); boss health was fitted by the balance bot (`tests/balance.test.ts`: it fights each horror at the standing a person reaches it with, the Reagent they would carry there is `dosesAt`) until each tier sits in its own band: a named horror 14 to 50 seconds of the bot's steady blows (a person's, about three times that) and up to 1.2 lives of damage, a great fight 35 to 100 seconds and 0.3 to 1.5 lives. When #158 added the per-level scaling (+0.75% health, +0.3% damage a level) and took the colossi's weak spots away, the bot's seconds rose by half again at the end of the game, so the authored health of nine of the later horrors was trimmed by a tenth to a half (Yog-Sothoth most, 5200 to 2700, for it was struck only at its spheres, which took their multipliers with them) and the god band's ceiling is main's 1.5 lives. Azathoth and the Haunter stay outside the bands: their fights are outlasting the piping and the lamp.
- **Round 68, the cutscenes stay inside the fog (asked: "address the initial display of only the boss fog ... significantly improved, cinematic camera angles"):** the investigator is walked a step inside the wall before a horror, the horror wakes, and the arrival's first shots stood behind them or far off, on the far side of the opaque wall: the picture was fog for seconds. `render/cinemaFog.ts` keeps every lens of a scene inside every wall about it (`systems/fogGates.ts` `lensInside`, `insideLine`): swung about the investigator to their side, never past 120 degrees and always the same way round, or drawn in along its line to the head of what it circles. The arrivals of a giant and a colossus open with a low shot from the investigator's flank that lifts as the horror rises, push in on the face, and end beyond the horror's shoulder with the investigator small before it; the small ones are a stand off its line, a close-up and the view from the investigator's shoulder (`ARRIVAL`, `data/cutscenes.ts`). `tests/cinemaFog.test.ts` walks every size of horror at 12 to 46 m in a ring and in a hall; `tests/cinemaFraming.test.ts` still holds the horror on screen.
- **Round 68, the horse is quieter (asked: "the horse makes way too many noises while riding. In a short period of time, it's unrealistic"):** `RIDE` in `render/audio/mountAudio.ts` spaces what the horse says: a creak, a trace of harness, a snort at the gallop's start and then seldom, a whisper, an idle breath each a seconds-long range apart and none answering a slap of the reins within five seconds of the last (a 40% chance), the hooves' thumps a little slower. `tests/mountAudio.test.ts` plays a minute at a gallop and holds the asides to 18.
- **Round 68, nothing on a way to a horror can hurt (asked: "remove all traps from all paths that lead to bosses"):** the hazards of every court (plates, vents, tides, blight, fields) are gone from `data/arenaWays*.ts` and the omens that spoke of them rewritten; the rites (lights lit in the order the place tells) stay, and a wrong light now flares harmlessly (`fail.damage` 0). The machinery in `systems/ways.ts` is kept and held by a fixture (`tests/wayTraps.ts`), so a hazard can be put back in a way by data alone.
- **Round 68, Yuggoth is quiet and Hastur goes home (asked: "yuggoth area has a very annoying background ambience buzzing sound, remove that"; "something triggers hastur spawning in the ruins, when dying to him he should be back in his arena"):** the realm's alien bed (`amb/alien.mp3`, the buzz of its fauna set and its voice recipe) is removed. Hastur is called where the ruins' ritual is performed and its fight was held there; when that fight ends without it (a fall, or a flight past its hold) its signature's `reset` returns it, and its brain's home with it, to its own arena, like every other horror (`systems/signatures/hastur.ts`).
- **Round 68, the casters and the storm have recordings of their own (asked: "some enemy casters and the lightning strike have a really cheap sounding sfx, source better fitting ones and distinct for each one"):** #158 had already taken the synthesised wind-ups, gathers, hums and teleport and summon stings out of the casters' sound (attack cues are recordings of the element, placed where the blow is) and this keeps that rule: the frost, star and eye casters, which shared the generic throw and the arcane impact with the glyph and coil casters and with seven other elements, each have a throw of their own, the star and the eye an impact of their own too (`atkFrostCast`, `atkStarCast`, `atkEyeCast`, `atkStarHit`, `atkEyeHit`: `tests/plannedSounds.test.ts` holds that no two of the five elements thrown are alike). The bolt on the ground has a recorded charge for its ring (`charge`: the whine of its recipe plays only until it has loaded), a recorded snap over the strike's crash (`zap`) and recorded thunder takes (`thunder`), with the synthesised roll beneath them cut from 1.1 to 0.4. Freesound could not be reached from the build machine, so all 24 takes are CC0 cuts from OpenGameArt (`public/audio/CREDITS.md`) chosen by their names and measured sound, not by ear: they deserve a listen, and `PLANNED`/`SAMPLE_SETS` take a better take by file name alone. The recorded sounds' budget in `tests/samples.test.ts` goes from 10 to 11 MB for them (0.7 MB, with the Yuggoth bed taken out).
- **Round 68, several map markers, and a way to delete them (asked: "implement the ability to put more than one custom waypoint on the map and also the ability to delete them"):** `Overworld.marker` (one place) is `markers`, up to `MARKER.most` = 12 (more than that is told, "MARKER LIMIT", and refused, not a quiet replacement of the oldest). Right-click, F or the pad's Y on bare ground adds one; on a pin it removes that pin (the same gesture, as before for the one), and Delete or Backspace on the map clears them all. The minimap points to each, the full map draws each pin with its distance, and each is cleared on coming within 10 m of it. A round 60 save's one `marker` is read as the first of `markers`; the save writes only `markers`.
- **Round 68, the horrors stay demanding at any level, and the level cap is thirty each (asked: "increase global difficulty ... revisiting the initial balance patch ... all bosses remain demanding regardless of player level; consider raising the maximum level cap to encourage mob farming for echoes"):** the question was what "excessive nerfs" were: read as the places where a level, a hoard or a trimmed health bar left a horror costing a bot next to nothing, not as a return to the first patch's numbers (those were met by the same bands, which this round raised: a named horror costs at least 0.2 of a life, a great fight 0.45; `tests/balance.test.ts`). Four things. (1) `LEVELS` max 20 to 30 for Vigour, Endurance and Might (ninety levels, 1.33 million Echoes in all: a level at the end costs about forty thousand, a god leaves twenty-five to fifty). (2) *Overmatch* (`systems/bossScaling.ts`, `BOSS_OVERMATCH.share` 0.7, `data/bossStanding.ts` written by `BALANCE_WRITE=1`): the level scaling of #158 keeps its 0.75% and 0.3%, and past the Vigour and Might a person has when they meet a horror (`BOSS_STANDING`, from the balance model) the horror's health grows with 70% of the investigator's added damage and its blows with 70% of their added health, so a person who farmed levels still has the fight the horror was made for (at the cap a bot still spends 0.1 to 1.4 lives a fight; before it, 0.0 to 0.3 for the early ones). (3) Five horrors leaned on a gaze, which hurts nothing (Ephraim Waite, the Black Man, the Gorgon, Martin's Beach, Hypnos), and a bot slipped most of the rest: a gaze's weight in each phase is a blow's now. (4) `tests/balance.test.ts` fights every horror again at the cap (all levels bought, the cane at +5) and holds it to its own floor (named 10 seconds and 0.1 of a life, a great fight 30 and 0.4); BALANCE.md has the table. 'Umr at-Tawil's health 3250 to 3650 to meet its floor. Azathoth and the Haunter are still outside the bands (their fights are other rules).
- **Round 68, the secrets pay, and gifts mark the count (asked: "evaluate each new point of interest for entertainment value, reward quality and difficulty; enhance the overall exploration experience to provide greater incentives"):** the review is `docs/POI_REVIEW.md`. Every place leaves a purse now (`hoard` on all seventy: the twenty-four tales and anomalies that left only a point of insight have one a tenth to a sixth of a level's price), and the hoards are rescaled by realm to the price of a level where the realm is met (a den, rite or hoard is worth 0.2 to 0.9 of a level; they were 0.15 to 0.35, and nothing at all against the forty thousand a late level costs); `tests/poiGifts.test.ts` holds the ratios. `POI.gifts` (`systems/poiGifts.ts`): at 6, 15, 30, 50 and 70 secrets found (all realms together) a gift is given once, star-stones (1, 1, 2, 2, 3: nine, what one weapon takes to its fifth level) and Echoes worth 1, 2, 3, 4 and 6 levels at the price of the next, said as a notice, kept in the save as `poigift:<n>` (a save that found them before the round is paid all it is owed at the next secret found), and the map's heading says where the next is. The Echoes and stones are not in the balance model, as the hoards were not: the overmatch above is what keeps a person who explores from outrunning the horrors.
- **Round 68, no blow of a foe kills from full health (asked: "make sure that bosses never one-hit kill"):** `BOSS.blowMost` = 0.6: in `strike`, a blow of an enemy to the investigator takes at most that share of their greatest health, whatever stacks on it. A horror's heaviest blow is 30% where it is met (`tests/bossBlows.test.ts`) and about half of it with the worst of the mind (x1.35) and a relic (x1.08) on the first journey in Deep Slumber (`tests/oneHit.test.ts` holds that, so the cap is a backstop there); Nightmare (x1.5), the second journey and after (x1.35 each) and the scaling of levels are what reach it. It is not the mercy system (round 61, taken out: that eased the blows after each fall); it is one number, the same on every fall. The gaze that turns flesh to stone (Ghatanothoa) builds over seconds in sight and is left as it is: it is not a blow.
- **Round 67: Hutchinson's house is a keep, the first place that is not a ring (asked: "do ALL the handoff work"; pilot 1 of three).** Lore: Edward Hutchinson of Pawtuxet (Lovecraft's *Case of Charles Dexter Ward*) kept a farmhouse above the Providence river with a laboratory under its roof, and the game's own epitaph for him already says the lightning is his; a donjon behind a curtain wall, with a lightning rod that draws the bolt, is the place a man who brings the dead up from their salts would build, and it reads from the road as a fortress (a keep on the road, not another ring of stones). Chosen over a farmstead (too small to be a boss's place) and a tower alone (the ring's gate and court would not change). **How:** `Bailey` in `data/arenaHouses.ts` (ahead, side and behind the ring, a keep, a gatehouse, rods) and `world/enclosureBailey.ts` lay a curtain about a rectangle (pieces that overlap end to end, a tower at each corner and in every `tower.every`th run, the gate's two towers with a fire each), a gatehouse over the gate (its span raised to `gate.height + 0.5`, so a body of 1.8 m passes under and nothing in the closure flood does) and a keep behind the back wall, which stands on its line with its mass behind. The frame is the court's (`a` along the front, `c` to the left), so the stake, the candle, the court before the gate and the ways stand as a ring's would at the same gate. `planEnclosure` picks ring or bailey by the spec, and `arenaPlace` asks `enclosureLand` for the whole rectangle before it fixes the front (a gate toward the Elder Sign unless there is no land for the keep). The wall, tower and gate pieces moved to `world/houseParts.ts`, so the ring's are the same code (the ring's world fingerprint is unchanged, taken before and after as in round 66). **The readers round 66 left:** `worldScene`'s footprint of an arena (the union of the fight's square and the house's box), `validateWorld`'s stray check (the box for a bailey; a ring keeps its circle, since one ring, the Daemon Pipers', strays out of its region by design), and the tests' generic walks (`houseWalk` reaches the wall by `gap` and a proximity test against props instead of a ring's radius). **The rods:** `render/rodFx.ts` falls a bolt from the cloud to a rod every 6 to 8 seconds while the investigator is within 130 m of the court and not in the fight (a rod is not a hazard: the bolt is a picture, a flare and the thunder of `lightning.strikeNow(0.1)`, hurting nothing), the keep's every third; a court out of sight does not count its seconds, and a court come to begins part way through its wait, so arrival is not a signal. **Stacked branches:** each round of the plan is its own branch on the last (`-round-66`, `-round-67`) and its PR is against the one under it; when a round merges, the next one's base moves to main. **Left on purpose:** more than one bailey (the pilots after it will tell whether the keep wants parts of its own: a moat, an inner ward), flank towers' fires and the rod's glow are dim in a night capture and were left at the ring's own light (the world is night; a lit tower already reads at 80 m); the rods have no sound of their own beyond the thunder.
- **Round 68: the Colour's reservoir and the Black Man's island in the river (asked: "do ALL the handoff work"; two places of the second set).** Lore: the Colour fell on the blasted heath that Arkham's new reservoir was to drown (*The Colour Out of Space*: "the new reservoir ... and the whole of the blasted heath will be under water"), so its house is the reservoir: a straight dam across the front with the gate in a sluice tower, and a bank that curves back about the court as a shore does, with a spillway cut in the dam where grey dust pours. The Black Man's sabbat is on "the unpeopled island in the river" in "the dark valley of the white stone beyond Meadow Hill" (*Dreams in the Witch House*), so his house is that island, white earth with the thirteen stones and their fires, in a black river kept between two kerbs, and a ford of flat stones to it from the gate (the ford is ours; the boulder that stops it, and sinks as the gate gives, is the gate's look). **How:** `Basin` and `River` in `data/arenaHouses.ts`; `world/houseRuns.ts` (the runner the bailey used, now shared), `world/enclosureBasin.ts` (dam, bank as a half ellipse leaned by `hash2`, spillway, `gap` from the bank's outline) and `world/enclosureRiver.ts` (the outer kerb is the ring's own planner; an inner kerb about the island open at the ford; two ford kerbs). Three new seams on `Enclosure`, all optional: `living` (what moves: a river's sheet, a spillway's dust; drawn by `render/courtLife.ts`), `dry` (where a thing may stand, when the gap is not all ground: the island and the ford; the stake, the candle, the set and the closure's inside read it) and `under` (pads that shape the land after the court's: the bed sunk by the river's depth, the ford raised across it; `placeArena` pushes them after the main pad). The closure flood proves the river shut: both banks are kerbs, and the ford's are the only way across. **Kerbs are low on purpose:** the rule that every wall is 5 m (five times what a mount steps up, and nothing jumps) is kept for walls; a kerb of 1.3 m (at least 1.2, tested) is as unclimbable and lets the river be seen over it. **Gate looks** (`GateLook`: slab, sluice, boulder; `render/gateLooks.ts`, `DoorKit.lift`): a panel that lifts into a housing, a boulder that sinks. **Looked at:** the first river (near-black water, kerbs 1.5 m high and 1.8 thick) could not be seen from the ford at all, so the water is paler with foam at its banks and streaks across, lies at 0.3 of the depth below the land, the kerbs are 1.3 by 1.4, and its mists are denser and rise above them; from above it reads as a pale ring about the island. **Tests moved with the layout:** the house-seen test's far point is now clear of the Black Man's much larger house, the walk-in sample is a ring (Shub-Niggurath's: the Colour's is a basin), and the rest-respawn test asks for more than two foes killed where it asked for more than five (round 68's houses shifted the Blasted Heath's from seven to five: the land is laid from one stream of numbers). **Left for later, on purpose:** a river shader with reflections and a current, and the stone's stirring sound.
- **Quest tracker at the right of the HUD (asked: "when a quest is active a concise summary of what you have to do should be visible on the right side of the screen in the HUD also stylized and non cluttering").** It follows `currentLead`, so it is whatever the minimap and the map already lead to (the side quest picked in the journal, else the main line, else who asks the first quest): no new idea of "the active quest". Its line is built from the stage's `Goal` by kind (`ui/questBrief.ts`), not cut from the journal's note, which is a paragraph: the notes stay in the journal. Its words are the interface's (`hud.track.*`, four languages); names and the altar's own label are the story's English, as everywhere else. To keep it from cluttering: small gold capitals for the name, one line (two at most) of text, a pointer and the distance, at 72% when at rest and full for nine seconds when new or moved on; hidden in a menu, in a fight with a horror (the boss bars have the screen then), while dying, and when Settings › Play › Quest tracker (default on) is off. The pointer is the minimap's own turn (`ui/leadMark.ts`), so the two agree; within nine metres it gives way to the word HERE. Nothing is saved: it reads the quest state every quarter second.

- Round 69 (asked by Dro: "remove all puzzles entirely and quickly and just make every boss door open without one"): a house gate no longer waits on its horror being found (`minFound` dropped in `world/arenaHouse.ts`), the rite lights are gone from the arena ways (`data/arenaWays*`), and `systems/discovery.ts` asks no act (`arenasHere` is empty), so no vigil, offering or page quest is opened. The retired tests (discovery, vigil, rite, candle) are deleted; `docs/` still describes the old acts and is not yet revised. Reversible from git history.

- **1.0.7, urgent desktop launch black screen:** desktop title visibility no longer depends on the title music starting or being allowed; the browser's audio gesture gate stays. Refused legacy profile reads and failed migration writes no longer abort `createShell` before the title exists (reproduced by throwing `SecurityError` from `Storage.getItem`; old values remain stored). A small bootstrap installs crash logging before importing the game and catches module initialization/load failures; asynchronous game startup also reports failure and halts autosaving. The packaged smoke check now requires a visible New game button and a clear centre of the veil, rather than hidden menu text, and uses a window that can be inspected on a headless runner. Version raised so the repaired build has its own update identity. Normal fresh-profile launch worked before these changes; the exact failure on the reported installations has not been captured.

- **Round 69, every caster's shot is its own, and so is its beam (asked: "many boss and creature attacks exhibit repetitive designs and basic geometry, such as conical projectiles, but should feature visually distinct aesthetics that align with each boss's thematic identity and lore; retain any elements already consistent"):** the audit found the bursts, ground marks, telegraphs, trails and sounds already carry a horror's own element, signature and colours (round 51 and 60), so those stay. What was generic was the body of a shot (an octahedron, a tetrahedron, a sphere or a cone, picked by hashing the id) and the ribbon of a beam. `data/shotForms.ts` names a shot for each of the 41 creatures that shoot, from its tale; `render/shotShapes*.ts` builds them; `data/beamStyles.ts` gives each beam caster one of eight patterns. No damage, range or timing changed. The simulation's bolts are as they were (hit radius, speed, spread); the picture is larger than the hit only as before.

- **Round 70, seven new attacks drawn from the tales, for the horrors they belong to (asked: "numerous boss encounters exhibit repetitive and nearly identical mechanics ... more distinct, lore-consistent attacks, particularly for high-tier bosses, such as tidal surges, tentacular strikes, meteor/falling star showers, conflagrations, and toxic or hallucinogenic clouds"; then: "free to implement more than just the examples ... lore accurate and not overpowered"):** `tidal_surge` (a straight wall of water down a lane drawn on the ground, `WaveDef.lane`: unblockable, shoves, slips with a roll), `tentacle_rise` (three marks that creep after the feet at 4.6 m/s, slower than a sprint, and lock 24 frames before they rise: `MarksDef.seek`), `starfall` and `thunderfall` (a shower of marks over the ground about the target, `pattern: 'rain'`, the first under it, none nearer than 1.4 radii, looks `star` and `bolt`), `conflagration` (three lines of marks out from the horror, `pattern: 'spokes'`, each leaving a fire that burns on: `MarksDef.leaves`, drawn by the flask's fire), `toxic_cloud` and `dream_cloud` (two banks of air that rise about the horror and drift after the investigator at about a quarter of a walk, `CloudDef`, `Hazard.cloud`: the toxic one hurts a tick at a time as a pool does, the dreaming one takes sanity a second, weighed as the horror's blows are, `cloudDread` in `systems/sanity.ts`, and brings the wrong stars and whispers down about whoever stands in it by way of `nearestAnomaly`). No new attack is a heavier blow than the eruption's (power at most 1.3; `tests/bossBlows.test.ts` is unchanged and passes), a surge and a cloud are scaled by the body's root but capped (a colossus's wall is no faster than a person can read), and the clouds thin when their horror falls. Given by lore, each to a few horrors and none to all: the surge to Cthulhu, Father Dagon, Bokrug and the Horror at Martin's Beach (the sea); tentacles to Cthulhu, Mother Hydra, Ghatanothoa, Shub-Niggurath and the Dunwich Horror; stars to Yog-Sothoth, the Great Ones, Zkauba and Nyarlathotep; lightning to the Dunwich Horror, Wilbur, Yog-Sothoth, the Haunter of the Dark and Nyarlathotep; flame to Joseph Curwen, Charles le Sorcier, Ghatanothoa and Yeb; poison to Tsathoggua, Yig, Shub-Niggurath, the Colour, the Shunned House and Dr. Muñoz (ammonia); dream to Hypnos, Hastur, the High Priest, the Black Man and Nyarlathotep. Each replaces an eruption, quake or ring in its horror's script and not an attack that kept it hard, and the balance table was run again and written (`docs/BALANCE.md`): the first draft pushed Mother Hydra and Tsathoggua over their band (a toxic cloud and a surge had a smallest range that a colossus's body scaled past melee, so the AI chose its heavier blows more; three converging tentacles were three blows to a body that stands still) and Hypnos and the Black Man under it (a dream cloud is no blow), so those ranges start at 0, the tentacles are 0.7, the clouds are 0.14 a tick, and the two mind-thieves keep their barrages. The balance bot now walks out of a toxic cloud as a person does (`tests/cloudBot.ts`, with its `skill`). A free animation pack was not used: the game has no skeletal animation, and the water wall, the tentacles, the stars, the lightning and the clouds are built in code from the tentacle tube, the beams' bolt and the particles already there.

- **Subtler secrets (asked by XDro: the map "?" plain white with no black circle, faint grey once found; the secret-found pop-up eliminated or much reduced, since the place alert already fires).** `ui/mapIconsSecret.ts` draws the "?" as white text with a thin dark edge and a found secret as its kind figure in faint grey (fainter once spent), no token. The great-words `secret` tone is gone: a secret found or come back to is the quiet italic caption low on the screen (the one Overheard uses, with the secret's name as its label and its line), without the count, so `hud.secrets` is dropped; a named place found with it shows its own banner at once, no longer held back or hidden under the secret's.

- **Oil lantern housings and their insects (2026-10-23, asked to align the new models' light and flame and improve their appearance):** the generic flame above a baked model's highest point was wrong for the hanging `lightpost` (whose glass is offset 0.384 m from its origin) and the small `lantern`. `world/lanternModels.ts` records their measured interior flame centres, shared by the rendered oil burners, point lights, halos and moth haunts; scale, yaw and the props' ground inset apply together. Small warm teardrop flames replace the cones above their caps. The kit's green leaf material becomes dark iron, the candle body reads as a brass oil reservoir, and the lightpost's opaque amber panes become faint transparent glass. Glass preserves the scene target's alpha, which the post pass reserves for characters and anomaly hues. Halo overrides keep these housed flames compact without changing other lights' reach. The cages' coarse movement colliders pass light rays and are excluded from solid shadow proxies, so they cannot seal the corrected flame inside its own shadow; walls still block light and the actual frames still cast the moon's shadows. Movement and combat collisions keep their existing shapes. About half the lit outdoor models attract two or three of the existing moths, deterministically by prop seed, within the existing chunk budget; open arena decor is indexed as well as region-plan approaches, including realms without other wildlife. Dungeon fauna remains hidden as before. Regression checks cover enclosure, rotated/scaled light and halo positions, transparent-glass alpha, self-occlusion and walls, moth motion and actual streamed arena/approach placement; front/back daylight and night renders were inspected.

- **Round 70, the way to each ending is always told and cannot be shut (asked by XDro: "make sure the main questline/s that lead to one of each ending is always clear enough for the player and is never softlocked, e.g. by killing a boss upfront"):** the audit found one real softlock and four places the line went quiet or wrong. (1) The span to 'Umr at-Tawil's hall in the Ultimate Void (`dungeonsFar.ts`) was a hidden bridge that wanted 3 insight, and insight is spent on upgrades: a mind that arrived without it could not reach 'Umr, so not Yog-Sothoth or Azathoth either, and none of the three endings. It is a plain bridge now (the only way on is never behind a thing that can be spent or lost; `tests/endingPaths.test.ts` walks every boss hall and keeps only the seals' door, Kadath's, as a gate). (2) The main line ended at the Threshold and the tracker went silent for the Beyond, though the endings lie past it: "The Gate and the Key" (Carter's, `silver_key`) is now a main quest after `kadath`, with a stage each for 'Umr, Yog-Sothoth, Azathoth and the choice (new goal `ending`, met when the dream has an ending; `lenient`, so the Guide's own ending, which fells neither of the others, closes it). Its stage 0 was "slay Yog-Sothoth": an old save's stage still lands on a goal the world already answers, and the first reward may be paid once more at the end (two star-stones and 3 insight, left as they were). (3) A quest the investigator has gone past no longer leads (`mainLead`: a later main quest begun or done): Keziah put down and the stair taken before Peaslee was asked sent the tracker back to the university from the Dreamlands. The people still have their stars. (4) Gilman said "she is still in that house" to one who had already felled her; once Keziah has fallen he says the lines he says when the quest is done (`Topic.slain`; no word of speech is new, since every line has a recording that cannot be made here). Carter's lines are the same words, said at the stage they now fit (no recording is new). (5) 'Umr's yield was only a prompt within eight metres, so a blow in the middle of the fight finished him unseen: it is said once, with both choices ("pass the gate with him, or strike him down"). Striking him down stays a choice that gives up the Guide's ending for this dream; the next journey has it again. Small: the Witch House formulae's note says that if the insight that shows the closet is spent, the garret does not wait for them (`witch_house` is lenient), the kadath note says the Great Ones and the Crawling Chaos hold the hall with the Ultimate Gate, and the court ending's tracker line is `hud.track.ending` in all four languages.

- **Round 70, quieter boss indicators that go sooner (asked by XDro: "make all of the boss attack indicators more subtle in design and fade away quicker after the attack landed"):** one table, `INDICATOR` in `data/fxTuning.ts`, read by `render/decals.ts` and `render/groundFx.ts`. A telegraph's bright edge is 0.10 m, not 0.16; its edge and the front of its fill keep 72% of their light, its fill, ring band, swirl and hatching 60%, and the flash and pulse of a full mark and the dark line about an edge 50% and 60%; a mark is still found on any floor because the colours stay lifted to `DANGER_FLOOR` and the whole shape, its filling front and the chevrons of a lane are still drawn. What a blow leaves goes sooner: a scar lives 30% of the seconds its caller asks (the 8 to 16 s of a landing, a colossus's and a storm's are now 2.4 to 4.8 s, the cooling of its cracks as quick) and a sigil 60%. Not changed: the real hazards (a pool, a fire left by a flame mark, a cloud's reach and the water wall are what hurt, and keep their own lives), the timing of every wind-up and blow, and the vigil's sigil, which is a rite's and not a blow's. One table so the next asking is one number: raise `body` or `line` to bring a mark back, or `scar` to let the ground keep a scar longer.

- **Round 71, the outdoor boss places (asked by XDro: "implement the complete outdoor boss environment upgrade ... the supplied asset collection ... integration, visual refinement, testing, and pull request"; design left to me):** each of the nineteen open arenas (docs/BOSS_PLACES.md has the list, the source→game map and the measurements) is now a place built of the packs' models and forms made in code, not the generic ring with a set about it. (1) *Built on #157 and #161*, which are in this branch by merge (79f3834): their enclosure seam (`world/enclosure.ts`, the pad, the closure flood, the basin and the river) is what every place is an `Enclosure` of, kind `'place'` (`world/enclosurePlace.ts`: a planner lays pieces in the court's frame, `world/placeKit.ts`, and says the outline of its wall); the colour-out-of-space basin and the black river keep their own enclosures and a planner enriches them (`ExtraFn`, `EXTRAS`). Replacing them would have lost their ground and closure work for nothing. (2) *Models are baked offline into their own data*, `data/models/bossIndex.ts` + `bossPack.ts` by `tools/models/bake_boss_models.py` (Blender's importers via `bpy`, which is the tool's, not the game's, dependency; reproducible from the supplied packs; a manifest of each model in `tools/models/boss/`); the Kenney set's `index.ts`/`pack.ts` became `kenneyIndex.ts`/`kenneyPack.ts` and the two are merged by `models/index.ts` and `pack.ts`, so `bake_models.py` and every reader are as they were. 167 models, 0.78 MB of data (the first bake held 260; the 93 no place stood were taken out of the manifests, since `tests/arenaSets.test.ts` keeps no dead weight in the bundle), shared by every place; a piece is a prop of the kind `set`, so it is drawn by the same batched instancing as the arenas' sets. (3) *No bounding box per building*: a model's colliders are its `solids` (a doorway, an arch or a colonnade is boxes about its opening, `'auto'` decomposes by bands), the closure flood proves that nothing walks in but by the gate, and `tests/bossPlaces.test.ts` keeps the fight's ring clear and the way from the gate to it open. (4) *Pack 07* (Jen Abbott's Egyptian Temple Complex, Fab Standard License) is not baked or committed: its licence does not allow its raw assets to be republished and a baked model carries them; the Colossus's temenos is made of pack 08's sandstone temple, pack 09's ruins and forms in code (pylons, obelisks, drifts), and `tests/bossPlaces.test.ts` fails if a manifest names it. (5) *CC BY 4.0 packs 05, 06 and 17* are credited with their licence and the changes in `data/models/CREDITS.md`, which also credits every other pack for provenance. (6) *The gates keep opening freely* (no discovery lock, no puzzle: the doors' `look`s are `roots`, `fangs`, `leaves`, `portcullis` and the like, each a door that opens as a body nears, `render/gateWild.ts`, `gateMetal.ts`); the court's puzzles and hazards stay off, as round 68 left them. (7) *What breathes* is `Emit`, a source anchored in the court (`world/placeEmit.ts`, drawn by `render/courtLife.ts`: wisps, sparks, embers, motes, bubbles, notes, runes, eyes), three or more to a place of at least two sorts, each at a source that is there (a chimney, a brazier, a well, a pipe's top); `tests/bossPlaces.test.ts` asks it. (8) *The sets* (round 61's compositions) were trimmed where a place's pieces made them redundant (a totem ring in Yig's court, a tower prop at Hastur's), each noted in the set's file; the fight's ring, the candle, the stake and the discovery are unchanged. (9) *The blind dancers* of Azathoth are four poses of pack 13's base meshes, their heads taken off and an eyeless egg set in their place by the baker, then stretched to thirteen different proportions in the place; pack 12 was not needed. (10) *Hastur's calling* is untouched (`signatures/hastur.ts`, covered by `tests/greatOldOnes.test.ts`): a Hastur called to the ruins still rises there, and when that fight ends without him he goes home to the theatre, which is now where his arena is. (11) *Not measured*: real frame times on a GPU (the container renders in software at about a frame a second); what is measured, by the renderer's own count, is draw calls, triangles, geometries and textures at the gate of each place before and after, and the cost of decoding the models at load.

- **Round 71, soft caps on the levels (asked by XDro: "Souls-style soft caps on the percentage-based level bonuses for the player (damage, HP, etc.): full gains up to about level 15 to 20, roughly half after that, very little near the cap of 30"):** `SOFT_CAP` in `data/playTuning.ts`, read through `systems/levelWorth.ts`. Of the levels of one attribute the first 18 count whole, the 19th to 26th count 50% each and the 27th to 30th 15% each, so thirty are worth 22.6. They scale what `LEVELS` gives, all three attributes alike (Vigour's +12 health, Endurance's +8 stamina and +0.8 of stamina a second, Might's +4% on blows and shots): at the cap 431 health (it was 520), 281 stamina (340), 50.08 stamina a second (56) and blows of 1.904 (2.2). Eighteen, because it is as far as any horror's standing goes (`BOSS_STANDING`: 18 Vigour and 17 Might at the last; `tests/softCap.test.ts` holds it), so the journey as it is made, and the main and the opening tables of `BALANCE.md`, are exactly what they were; what the caps soften is the hunt past it, where a level costs 16,500 Echoes at the 55th bought and 41,000 at the 90th and the open world's ordinary foes (which do not scale with levels) are what a person has outgrown. Not softened: what a level costs, the count the menu, the achievements and the ending card show, and the reinforcement of weapons and the revolver (those are star-stones). The horrors follow the worth, not the count: `overmatch` reads the blows and health the investigator really has, and the level scaling of #158 (0.75% health, 0.3% blows a level) is taken on `levelsWorth` (67.8 at the cap, not 90; `bossHealthScale` and `bossDamageScale` take it). On the count, a level past the 26th would have made a horror harder by more than it made the body stronger, a level that hurts. `BOSS_OVERMATCH.share` 0.7 to 0.8: with the worth, a bot at the cap fought the horrors about 6% easier than before (nine starts of every fight: lives x0.94, seconds x0.97); at 0.8 it is as before (x1.01 and x0.99; 0.9 would be x1.07 and x1.02), which is what "the horrors stay demanding at any level" asks of a person who has less to meet them with. The cap table of `BALANCE.md` is now the mean of nine starts, not three: the same bot cost 'Umr at-Tawil 0.28, 0.93 and 0.43 lives from three starts (and 0.16 to 1.40 across nine), so a floor held on three paths was a coin; the main and opening tables are as they were, three, to the digit (and the whole file comes out the same on CI's Node 24 as on Node 22). On nine starts the thinnest of the named at the cap is Charles le Sorcier, 10.5 seconds against his floor of ten (10.7 before this round), and no one's health was changed to widen it. The Elder Sign's rows say the real gain of the next level (+6 health, +2% damage) and, past a mark, a dim "soft cap: 50%" chip with the marks in the foot; a row at its most says nothing. A save keeps its levels; one past 18 loads with the lower health (a wound clipped to it) and blows, and the Echoes that bought them are not returned. All three attributes share one curve; a mark of its own for an attribute is a row of `LEVELS` away if play wants it.

- **Round 72, stronger models for eleven of the places, and the arenas cleared of their first props (asked by XDro: swap in the free models he supplied, "replacing the corresponding assets, not just adding alongside", and "eliminate legacy props such as the lanterns, which are inconsistently positioned within the otherwise structured arena... remove all pre-implementation props across all arenas; retain only those that are exceptionally appropriate for their respective environments, and refine their placement to ensure consistent, intentional decoration"):** (1) *CC BY is accepted from here, with credit.* Eight of the supplied models are CC BY 4.0 (Sketchfab: Marin PETTE's Roman theater of Palmyra, 3D Vault's Wajia Flute, Bruno Macchioni's Egyptian Temple, Bl4ckGh0st's Low Poly Black Castle and Temple Ruins, mynameismary's Theatre stage, Animex's Snake statue, zacharykozak's Ancient Ruins); the earlier CC0-only preference is relaxed for them because XDro asked for them by name. Each is credited in `src/data/models/CREDITS.md` with its licence link and the changes made (`tests/bossPlaces.test.ts` demands both for any pack in a place), and the authors' names were read from each download's own licence file, not from memory. The CC0 ones (Goth Scarecrow's dead trees, rubberduck's alien buildings, Poly Haven's pier) are credited for provenance. Pack 07 (Fab) is still out. (2) *Replacement is real.* Palmyra for pack 10's colosseum, the Wajia flute for pack 14's pipes, pack 19's trunks for packs 03 and 09's dead trees, pack 20 for pack 11's base, pack 24's pylon for the one made in code, pack 25's castle for the donjon of walls and an arch, pack 21's pier for pack 04's gantry, pack 28's statues and pillars for pack 13's stone figures and pack 08's wall pieces, pack 29's arcade for pack 08's columns; the manifests of packs 10, 11 and 14 are deleted and the models nothing stands any more are not baked (181 models, 24 packs; `tests/arenaSets.test.ts` allows none baked and unused). Yig's snake statue is the one addition (nothing in the shrine was its like). The ancient ruins went to Bokrug alone: round arches are not R'lyeh's wrong angles, so Cthulhu keeps its cyclopean slabs. (3) *Hastur's audience* is the restrained pale seated company the first round made, with spectral eyes opening in the rows (code) and the curtains weathered yellow; no actor model was added. (4) *Where a pack was too big or too wide for the court, the court changed, not the pack:* the Egyptian pylon is 85 m across so the Colossus's court is twelve metres wider each way (`S = B = R + 26`); the Palmyra bowl is cut into twenty-degree wedges stood fourteen about the fight (three at the front left out for the way in, one at the back for the stage house); the Wajia flute is enlarged many times and stood in two lengths; the black castle is stretched to the keep's width. Dead trees (10 m) carry one trunk collider (`solids='trunk'` in `tools/models/boss/solids.py`), not a box about their limbs. (5) *The first props.* Before the places the arenas stood a set in the ring (`data/arenaSets*.ts`) and a line of marks down the court before the gate (`data/arenaWays*.ts`), scattered by random radius and chance with lit lanterns, lamps, candles and pumpkins in them (the glowing cones in the picture were the Black Man's carved pumpkins, each with a torch). Rule: a set keeps its ground, air and candle and a centrepiece only where it is the fight's one fitting heart and no planner stands its like (the Black Man's white stone, Shub-Niggurath's altar, the Colossus's broken king, Bokrug's idol, Cthulhu's door slab); a way is a mirrored pair of one or two pieces at a steady stride and fixed distance (no `chance`, no ranges, no jitter), plus the single things that tell a story (a signpost, an expedition's tent, a gun off a wreck, a flag at the sea wall); nothing lit stands on an approach; the planners' braziers, torches and lamps inside the courts stay. The omens are untouched and no rite or hazard existed to keep. The dungeons' boss halls were not asked about and keep their sets. (6) *Tests adapted, not weakened:* the candle before a ring is kept clear of a piece's real walls (`colliderGap`, the nearest of its colliders) and not of a circle about it, because the Egyptian pylon is two towers 85 m apart, and a circle of 33 m left no place near the stake; the gate and boss-feet tests read a model's own solids (`propColliders`) and not the box about it, which for a pylon is the gate; the dead-weight test counts the halls' sets as well; the lantern-moth test now asks that the ways to the horrors have no lantern; the Hutchinson test finds the donjon by the castle's id. (7) *Not measured or checked:* real frame times and the look on a GPU (software renderer only), the decode time of the heavier models (the lazy model chunk grew from 1,072 to 1,527 kB, gzip 499 to 741 kB, the black castle alone 0.2 MB of it), and the new places in the full night and in weather.

- **Round 73, the Outer Gods move into the Outer Dark (asked by XDro: "relocating the Outer Gods to outer space ... supplementary portals that transition into progressively more incomprehensible dimensions corresponding to each Outer God boss's environmental placement ... visually striking and meticulously polished while remaining faithful to the established lore"; design left to me):** six dimensions, `cloud_summit`, `black_wood`, `pale_lattice`, `piping_dark`, `spheres` and `court_centre` (`OUTER_DARK` in `data/regions.ts`), are walked one after another beyond the Ultimate Void, each 512 m, each with its own Elder Sign, its god's arena, a gate in at the north and a gate on at the south (the last has none): the Beyond's Last Shore carries a storm gate to the Cloud Summit, and the chain runs Cloud Summit (the Other Gods, who left Hatheg-Kla's peak "when the Other Gods came up", Barzai's tale) → Black Wood (Shub-Niggurath, out of Dunwich's hill: the wood with a thousand young) → Pale Lattice (the Ancient Ones, who wait past the last gate and were held in the Beyond's lair) → Piping Dark (the Daemon Pipers, who play to the idiot god) → Gate of Spheres (Yog-Sothoth, who is the gate and was past 'Umr at-Tawil; now four dimensions past him) → the Court at the Centre (Azathoth, with Nyarlathotep at his own hall beside it). (1) *Regions are appended last* (the terrain seed is its index times 101, so no old region's ground moves), a tile apart in a row, joined only by their gate pairs like every realm; their `bosses`, `entities` regions and spawn tables are updated so `validateWorld` holds, and Dunwich, the Dreamlands and the Beyond lose exactly the gods that left them (Kadath's hall keeps the Great Ones; Nyarlathotep, who stood in it, has his own arena and a court with `nyarlathotep` in `world/places/`: a black ring of basalt columns, a crowd of stone runners all going one way, a dais with the instruments of his show, a mask with no face; `data/arenaOuter.ts` is his house, set, way and discovery, "The Handbill": read a page, keep a vigil in the deep hour with the mind between lucid and uneasy, lay a dose on the altar). (2) *Skies, not the ground, are what make a dimension*, because the fight's fog wall keeps low scenery out of a fight and the sky is what is seen from everywhere: `data/cosmos.ts` says what hangs in each (strengths; `render/sky.ts` eases them as a gate is crossed, as a realm's look is eased), `render/shaders/skyCosmos.ts` draws them as functions of direction alone (a gas behind the stars; a wall of storm with sheet lightning; limbs, red gaps and opening slit-eyes; a lattice of pale facets with the sky's clock stopped; a staff of five lines with lit notes and rings of sound; nine iridescent globes; a black hole with a photon ring, a disc and two jets), each kept to pale or dim colours so the post pass's 64 colours take it whole and no anomaly hue is made by accident. They cost the sphere nothing more than its own pixels and not at all in the earth's skies (each is behind its uniform, 0 off). (3) *Looks, fogs, skylines, names, music, ambience and the dead's last words* are per-dimension table entries as for any realm (no two grade alike; no sky, ambient or far colour reads as an anomaly hue; `tests/` keep both). Music: no recording of their own, each borrows the realm track nearest its mood, and the horrors' own boss themes carry the fights; `docs/VOICES.md` is untouched since no new line is spoken (the dead's lines and the pages are text). (4) *The sea is global and kept*: a dimension's horizon is the same dark mirror-sea reflecting its own sky, so the cloud-sea, the wood's red and the court's fire are in the water without a new material; the region's `biome.sea` flag stays inert as before.
