(5) *Not frustrating to reach*: every gate is a plain walk-in door with its Elder Sign at once beside it (Kadath's seals are `exempt` for all six, and nothing in the chain is behind a spendable thing: `tests/endingPaths.test.ts` keeps the quest's lead following the chain from the Gate to the Court, the Storm Gate first), each dimension has three secrets (18 `poisOuter.ts`, so the POI gifts moved to 17, 35, 60 and 88) and thirteen pages (`documentsOuter.ts`), the pages said in the voices of Barzai, Atal, a drover, Akeley, Carter, a bandmaster and the rest. (6) *Balance*: one horror's blows were changed and no other horror's health, blows or script: Shub-Niggurath's damage 36 to 41 (she is met in the Black Wood, the last ring, so her standing rose to 18/9/17 and what she grows past it at the cap shrank; the bot cost her 0.35 lives at the cap against the 0.4 floor, and 0.47 after, 0.52 where a person first meets her, as before). The gods stand in the last ring of the table (`RINGS`, `OUTER_DARK` after 'beyond'), their standing is the Echoes of every ring behind them, `data/bossStanding.ts` and `docs/BALANCE.md` were written again (`BALANCE_WRITE=1`) and every band holds; three tests' bounds moved by what left the old rings and not by what the horrors do: `tests/levelPace.test.ts` 40 → 42 foes a level in ring 3 (the Other Gods and Nyarlathotep, lower-standing horrors of its last tier, are gone from it, so it is the Dreamlands' own foes alone) `tests/gunStock.test.ts` 350 → 400 rounds (twelve of the notes have a box beside them: 48 rounds), and `tests/soak.test.ts`: an entity may be 9000 m from the origin, not 6000 (the Outer Dark runs east to 8192 m), and a body going over an edge is not a body that floats or is stuck (the Elder Sign at Zin has a drop within two metres of it to the east, on main too: a random teleport there is a fall, and it was only luck that the old sequence never chose it). The boss never one-hits (`BOSS.blowMost` still holds all of them). (7) *Old words that named the old places* were mended: the journal's two stages for Yog-Sothoth and Azathoth ("The Gate and the Key"); the people's lines say only that Yog-Sothoth waits "past 'Umr at-Tawil", which is still true and is spoken on recordings, so they stand (speech is never edited here). (8) *Not done, left for play to ask*: a milky look of the sea for the Cloud Summit, a tint of the sea for each dimension, a fauna for each (the court has a few night-gaunts, beings from beyond and a Yekubian), and a photograph of every arena's own hall (the previews are of the skies; Nyarlathotep's hall is checked by `tests/bossPlaces.test.ts` and `enclosed*`, not by eye). *Merging with the outdoor places' upgrade (#172, round 72)*: it also edits `data/arenaSets*.ts`, `arenaWays*.ts` and `world/places/`; mine touch only the registration tables and add files, so a conflict should be a union of lines.

- **Round 74, the interface's typefaces (asked for: IM Fell English for the interface and Special Elite for the intro's telegram, after a comparison of eight free faces rendered at the game's sizes against its colours).** The stack was system fonts (Iowan Old Style, Palatino, Book Antiqua, Georgia), so the game looked different on every OS, and on one with none of them (Linux, SteamOS) it set its words in whatever serif was installed. `public/fonts` now holds the two fonts as WOFF2 subsets (Basic Latin, Latin-1, Latin Extended-A, œ, punctuation and the few symbols; 69 KB + 72 KB + 58 KB), each with its licence (IM Fell English: SIL OFL 1.1; Special Elite: Apache 2.0, whose text is kept and whose change, the subsetting, `CREDITS.md` states) and a credit, one more exception to "no asset files", beside the models, the music, the recordings and the voices; the credits screen names them. `ui/fonts.ts` loads them with the FontFace API before `main` is imported (boot.ts), because the canvas cards (the logo, the intro's ash cards, the banners, the map's words) draw once and would keep a fallback if the font arrived late; it gives up after four seconds and never rejects, and `hudKit.SERIF` and `TYPEWRITER` keep the system faces behind the new ones, which also supply what IM Fell English lacks (→ ≥ − ▲ ◇). Known costs, accepted: it has one weight, so the title wordmark's `bold` is a synthesised bold; its figures are oldstyle only (its `lining-nums` settings do nothing; the HUD's numbers read as oldstyle); and it is rough below about 12 px. Courier Prime was the first choice for the telegram and was replaced by Special Elite when asked (the aged, uneven typewriter against Courier Prime's clean one; it has no arrows or ▲ ◇, which the telegram does not use). The crash screen and the startup line keep system fonts on purpose (they must show when nothing else loads). Not changed: the monospace of the debug panel, the bestiary page and the frame meter. The desktop shell's static server learned `.woff2`.

- **Round 75, six thin places rebuilt, and ground and water for five (asked by XDro, "do 1 and 2", after he asked why only the Colour, the Black Man and Hutchinson came out well):** (1) *Why those three were the good ones, and what was done:* they alone had shaped ground, water or a mechanism (#157's basin and Colour, #161's river and island, the bailey's rods); the six thinnest were a wall and one repeated piece. Each of the six now has six to nine kinds of model and one thing it does that is seen from afar, as Hutchinson's lightning is; the other five places get the ground and water their lore asks. (2) *Signals, not new fights:* a `Signal` (`world/enclosure.ts`, drawn by `render/signalFx.ts`) is a chase of lights from point to point with a thread between, ending in a burst, or an arc that leaps between two points; it plays while the investigator is within 150 m and not hidden, fight or none, counts no seconds while out of sight and begins part way through its wait (the same rules as `rodFx`), so arriving is never a signal and the fights' timing is untouched; it does no harm (no blast, no sanity, no sound that a boss's attack makes). The Other Gods use the existing lightning rods (four of them: `rodFx` now strikes any rod when a court has more than two). (3) *Water that a body wades:* `Pool` (`render/poolMesh.ts`) is a sheet at the surface over ground a place sank (`under`), at most 1.2 m deep (the arenas stand at 2 to 11 m and the sea's standing limit is -1.6 m), its surface 0.3 of its depth under the level, so a body is wet to the knee at most; it has no drowning, no slowing and no foam of its own beyond its bank's, since the investigator's feet already follow the ground; the fight's circle and its fog are never touched (`tests/placeGround.test.ts` samples the fog's ring against every pad), the stake, the candle and the gate are clear of every pad, and `dry` keeps the court's own set, the stake and the candle off the water and the pits (a causeway across a pool is dry). Only the Black Man's river was ever sunk before; the pools are the same kind of thing and share `under`. (4) *Where the court had no room, the court grew:* Bokrug's lake needed more than the 8 m between the fight's fog and its back wall, so its court is a third deeper (`B` 34 to 44; the front, the gate and the wall's land are unchanged: the arena's front is still the Elder Sign's side, and nothing strays out of its region); Zkauba's pieces beyond the back wall are no further than the region allows (`outer` 30, centres within `B + 24`); the Pipers' ring has no room inside it but at the gate, so its rafts stand in the wall and its drums at the gate's bearings. (5) *What was replaced:* Martin's hull, canoe and rowboat moved into the shallows of the basin (the hull on its bed), the derrick onto the causeway's head, the sheds to the back corners; Cthulhu's flat dark tile was a pool made of a floor, now water, and the solid 1.5 m block before the Great Door is a platform of the ground with steps, so it can be climbed (the block could not); Yig's raised floor was likewise a solid block with stairs laid beside it that no body could climb, now a plateau of the land itself; Nug's trough runs became channels cut in the ground, the door's own trough kept at each door. (6) *New models:* thirteen forms made in code (`proc_rites.py`; the licence is this repository's) and nine pieces of pack 20 (CC0); no CC BY pack was added, so `src/data/models/CREDITS.md` is untouched; the models are 203. (7) *No balance change:* no horror's health, blows, script, arena radius or reward moved; only what stands in the court and what the ground does outside the fight's circle. (8) *The first pictures drew the water black:* a sheet with no normal lights as black in the world shader, so `poolMesh` gives its vertices an up normal; the Black Man's river (`courtLife`) has no normals either and draws as black as its name, which is the look it was accepted in and was left alone. The pools' tints are dark by design (a lagoon, a lake, a tide pool), lit by their own `glow`. (9) *Not done, left for play to ask:* a sound for the signals and the pools (the courts are silent; the recordings the game has fit neither), a splash where a body wades, the tide of Martin's moving with the moon, wading slowing a foe, the pits being climbed out of by a foe on its own (foes are not posted near them; the brains' paths follow the ground), and a photograph kept in `docs/` for each place (the pictures were reviewed from the game and not committed).

- **Round 77, the camera's looks (asked by XDro: whether a more cinematic camera was possible and worth it, then, after pictures of five looks, "leave the current as default and make A, B and D the optional ones under lockon/camera setting").** Settings › Play gained *Camera*: **Classic** (the default; the boom exactly as it was), **Low hero** (A: pivot 1.05 m, boom 4.4 m, 0.75 m over the shoulder, lens 76°), **Long lens** (B: pivot 1.5 m, boom 11 m, lens 32°, telephoto) and **Tight shoulder** (D: pivot 1.45 m, boom 3 m, 0.8 m over the shoulder, lens 60°). The high three-quarter look (C) was not asked for and is not built: it needs a threat indicator for what is off-screen, which the game has none of. The numbers are `CAMERA_MODES` in the new `data/cameraModes.ts`; `CAMERA` stays Classic's (three tests and the arena bots read it), and `createCameraRig` takes Classic unless told otherwise, so the simulation and its audits are what they were; `main.ts` sets the look from the setting every step, so a change in the pause menu shows behind it. The setting is `camera` in `SETTINGS` (0 to 3, default 0), kept with the others, not in the save, no settings-version change. Why it matters at all, when the camera can be turned: while locked on the game steers it and ignores the look input, and Classic's boom, tilted up to a tall foe's aim point, sits under a colossus with the investigator in front of its picture. So **Low hero and Long lens frame the lock**: the point the boom hangs from (the anchor) moves half the way to the foe (at most 9 m) and up toward its middle (`aim`), the boom lengthens with the foe's height (`back`, to 12 m, or 22 m for the long lens; the height is the taller of its body and the height its blows are scaled by, clamped to 24 m, so a 40 m god is framed as 24) and the pitch is set so the lens hangs low under what it looks at; the lens narrows a little for a tall foe, and *widens to hold the whole foe* (`fitFov`) where a wall or a near foe leaves less room. All of it eases in and out at 2.5 a second, so a lock taken, lost or switched is a drift, not a cut, and it is asked only of a lock (not a talk, not a rig driven). **The boom stays on the line the picture looks along (`rig.yaw`)**, which the sticks, the lock's bearing, the hurt edge, the minimap and the bots all read: only the anchor, the length, the pitch and the lens move (a code audit found that giving the lens its own heading would break each of them). The anchor stops short of a wall as the boom does. Judgment calls: Tight shoulder is 3 m and 0.8 m where the picture was 2.5 m and 0.95 m, because the boom hangs over the right shoulder only and the picture left a third of the left side unseen (it is still the narrowest view, and has no framing of a lock); the Long lens widens (up to 68°) where walls cut its boom short, so that indoors it does not become a close-up of the figure's back, and ends up about Classic's; the world's distortion is centred, and the sounds heard from, at most 6.5 m from the investigator along the boom (`nearLens`, `CAMERA.near`), so that a long boom keeps the clear zone about the fight and the volume of a blow that Classic's lens had (Classic's boom is shorter, so it is unchanged); the mounted boom is each look's (`mountDistance`). `?trailer`, the look test, the benchmark and the cutscenes are unchanged (a cutscene takes the lens over as before). Not done: the previews were taken in a software renderer, so none of the numbers has been felt on a real display; the lens's height for a very tall foe and the Long lens's pull-back are the first to adjust by play. Tests: `tests/cameraModes.test.ts`.
